# Kiwi — Bug Log

**Created:** June 22, 2026 (WS7-8b, mid-servings-arc)
**Purpose:** A standing triage list for bugs surfaced during testing, so they get captured without pausing the active build thread. Distinct from the deferred-decisions log: deferrals are *consciously postponed decisions*; bugs are *things that are broken*. When testing surfaces a bug, log it here, triage it (priority + target), and keep moving — don't stop the world.

**How to use (per working agreements §28):**
- Surfaced during testing → add a `BUG-NNN` row immediately, don't pause the current build to fix unless it blocks commit.
- Each bug: ID, date, surface, symptom, status (🔴 OPEN / 🟡 IN-PROGRESS / ✅ FIXED), priority (P1 blocks-commit / P2 ship-soon / P3 later), target (which block/WS), and root cause once known.
- When fixed, mark ✅ with the commit + keep the row (history).
- A bug that turns out to need a *decision* (not just a fix) also gets a deferred-decisions entry; cross-reference both ways.
- Chat-Claude reviews this log when commissioning a build chat so open P1/P2 bugs on the touched surface get folded in.

### ⚠️ STATUS VOCABULARY — EXTENDED SEPTEMBER 5, 2026 (Hans-ruled)

**Hans's ask, verbatim:** *"if we have stuff that's going to get addressed with [D-WS9-]191 we can put it in an 'assigned to upcoming phase' status. I think that's the place I really want to get to — assigning this stuff to future work that's already planned. and if it's genuinely new or separate we can address that or bucket some orphaned ones until they get a friend or we knock out random stuff at the end."*

**The list had 88 live rows and no way to tell a bug that is WAITING from a bug that is UNPLACED.** Both read 🔴 OPEN, so every triage pass re-read all 88. These three statuses split them:

| Status | Meaning | What it stops |
|---|---|---|
| 🔴 **OPEN** | Broken, unplaced, nothing scheduled will touch it | — |
| 🔵 **ASSIGNED — `<D-WS9-NNN>`** | Broken, and a **named, already-planned** piece of work will fix it as a side effect of its own scope | Re-triaging it every pass. **It is not a queue item; it rides.** |
| 🟠 **ORPHAN** | Broken, real, and **nothing planned touches it.** Parked until it gains a neighbour or a cleanup block | Pretending it has a home. **An orphan waits for a friend — it does not get scheduled alone.** |

⚠️ **`ASSIGNED` NAMES ONE ENTRY AND MAKES A CLAIM ABOUT ITS SCOPE — SO IT CAN BE WRONG.** A cross-reference is not an assignment. Assign a bug only where the named entry's own scope would fix it **without being widened.** If the phase ships and the bug survives, that is a finding about the assignment, not about the bug: **re-open it as ORPHAN rather than re-assigning it to the next phase.**

⚠️ **ORPHAN IS NOT A LOWER PRIORITY.** A P1 orphan is still a P1. The status says *nothing planned covers this*, which for a P1 is an argument to plan something, not to wait.

### BUG-054 — Grocery list renders partial, then full after a refresh 🔴 **OPEN — P2 → Block 3e. NOT REPRODUCIBLE FROM CURRENT SOURCE; do not pattern-match to BUG-051**

- **Surface:** mobile `app/grocery-list/[id].tsx` (the list read) + `groceryLists.ts:264-327` (generation).
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN.** Surfaced by Hans during device testing, July 30, 2026. Probed as 3d Part 3c **A4**; fix **B4 deliberately not shipped**.
- **Symptom:** generating a grocery list initially rendered only what appeared to be **recurring items**; tapping through to review unclear items caused a screen refresh/reload, after which the **full set** of groceries and ingredients appeared.
- **⚠️ THE PROBE FOUND NO RACE IN CURRENT SOURCE, AND THAT IS THE FINDING — not a dismissal.** Generation is a **single atomic transaction**: plan-derived items and recurring/staple items are folded in **one in-memory pass** and written in **one `createMany`**; navigation happens **only after the POST resolves**; and the list read is a **bare `useEffect`** — no React Query, no streaming, no SSE. **There is no mechanism in the current code for a partial render followed by a fill.** The only read-time mutation is `reconcileGroceryListIfStale`, which is **revision-driven** — and the DB shows all 10 of the dev user's lists **revision-consistent**, so it is not currently firing.
- **⚠️ CC STOPPED RATHER THAN SHIPPING A SPECULATIVE FIX, per the scope guard, and that was correct.** Any real fix would need on-device reproduction and would touch the grocery generation/reconcile flow — **Block 3e's surface.** Growing 3d to chase an unreproducible symptom is exactly the churn the guard exists to prevent.
- **⚠️ ROOT CAUSE UNVERIFIED. CC's hypothesis, explicitly labelled as such:** the on-device symptom came from a **409-already-exists path returning an older list** that reconcile-on-read later filled. **Not reproduced. Do not record this as the cause.**
- **⚠️ THE SHAPE MATCHES BUG-051 AND THAT RESEMBLANCE IS A TRAP.** BUG-051's *originally recorded* root cause was **wrong** — the query *was* invalidated; it fired at the first streamed card, before the server wrote the batch. **Probe this on its own evidence:** what fires the read, what the server has written at that moment, and whether recurring items genuinely come from a different source than the rest.
- **What Block 3e needs to do first:** reproduce it on device with the server log open, capturing the POST response code (200 vs 409), the list `revision` at read time, and the item count at each render.
- **Priority:** **P2.** **Target:** **WS9 Block 3e (Groceries).**
- **Cross-ref:** BUG-051 (same shape, different cause — the warning, not the template) · D-WS9-067 (grocery-list consumption of components) · D-WS9-090 (archived grocery lists staying reachable — same screen, different defect).

### BUG-051 — "See Previous Options" served the *previous* batch until a remount ✅ FIXED (`b2da12d`, device-confirmed July 29) — ⚠️ **the originally recorded root cause was WRONG; corrected below**

- **Surface:** Mobile client, `["wizard","last-batch"]` query cache. Server is not implicated.
- **Status:** ⚠️ **CONTRADICTORY — UNRESOLVED (flagged August 5, 2026).** **This entry contradicts itself.** Its heading reads **✅ FIXED (`b2da12d`, device-confirmed July 29)**; this Status line reads **🔴 OPEN**. ⚠️ **NEITHER SIDE IS ADOPTED HERE** — a doc-only chat cannot read the repo, and canon has been wrong in both directions this arc. **3f-4 Phase 0 owns the grep** (`b2da12d` and the `["wizard","last-batch"]` cache path). Whichever way it resolves, correct the losing side rather than silently dropping it.
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED — `b2da12d`, device-confirmed July 29, 2026; INDEPENDENTLY RE-VERIFIED IN CODE August 5, 2026** (commit exists, 9 files; `onComplete` fires only at true stream completion, `useBuildWizardPlansStreaming.ts:67-76,147,176`; `["wizard","lastBatch"]` invalidated at `wizard-results.tsx:283,357` and `tellkiwi.tsx:205`). ⚠️ **The heading and the Status field disagreed for a week — heading ✅ FIXED, field 🔴 OPEN.** Settled in favour of FIXED, from code. ⚠️ **The cache key is `["wizard","lastBatch"]` — camelCase. The hyphenated `last-batch` form is the HTTP path only** (`GET /wizard/last-batch`); a doc that greps for the hyphenated key in client code will find nothing and may wrongly conclude the invalidation is missing.
- **Status as originally logged:** 🔴 OPEN. Logged July 28, 2026 — surfaced by Hans during the Block 4b-3 device test, twice, before anyone was looking for it.
- **Symptom (user-visible):** after generating a new set of plans, backing out and tapping **See Previous Options** shows the *prior* generation's plans, not the ones just generated. Hans: *"I think there's a timing issue or something going on, it should have returned me to the latest previous results."* First occurrence looked like a one-plan glitch that resolved on re-entry; the second was unambiguous — a Brazilian/pescatarian/vegan generation returned the earlier round's plans.
- **⚠️ ROOT CAUSE — VERIFIED by a 304/200 pair, not inferred.** After the 20:06:45 generation completed, **no `GET /api/wizard/last-batch` appears in the server log at all** — the client answered from cache without asking. A full app reload then produced `GET /api/wizard/last-batch` → **200** (not 304), returning the Brazilian batch. **The server had stored it correctly the whole time; the client never refetched.** Last-batch invalidation was wired on the Tell Kiwi screen in 4b-3 (`tellkiwi.tsx`) and **not** on the wizard generation path.
- **⚠️ Same class as the contributor noted on BUG-047** — the `["wizard","drafts"]` query is likewise invalidated in exactly one place. Two caches, one missing invalidation pattern; **fix them together**, and prefer invalidating on the generation *mutation's* success over adding screen-specific calls.
- **Why it matters more than it looks:** it makes the 4b-3 feature *appear* broken on the exact interaction it was built for, and it produces a plausible-but-wrong "the server didn't save it" reading — which is precisely the misdiagnosis that cost this block a cycle (see BUG-049). Cheap to fix, expensive to leave as a confusing symptom during testing.
- **Priority:** P2. **Target: WS9 3c** (Hans's ruling, July 28) — 3c rebuilds the wizard and plan-options flow and will rewire this invalidation as a matter of course, so fixing it now risks writing a line 3c deletes. It cannot reach a tester before then: friends-and-family gates on WS9A hosting, which follows WS9.
- **⚠️ CORRECTION, July 29, 2026 — THE ROOT CAUSE ABOVE IS WRONG AND IS KEPT VERBATIM AS HISTORY.** The claim that last-batch invalidation was *"not wired on the wizard generation path"* is false. **It was wired** — at `wizard-results.tsx:320-321` and `tellkiwi.tsx:203-204`. **It fires too early.** The streaming hook sets `isSuccess = "success" || hasCards`, and `hasCards` flips true at the **first streamed card**, while the server persists the batch at the **end** of generation. So the background-mounted `WizardPreviousOptionsLink` (`staleTime: 0`) refetches, reads the **old** batch, caches it — and the once-per-attempt `invalidatedAttemptRef` guard prevents a second invalidation after the real write.
- **⚠️ WHAT THE ORIGINAL 304/200 EVIDENCE ACTUALLY PROVED.** It was real, and it was **under-determined**: it established that the client did not hold the new batch, not *why*. "Never invalidates" and "invalidates too early" both fit that observation. The entry recorded the first as VERIFIED. ⚠️ **The evidence was verified; the mechanism inferred from it was not** — and the entry did not distinguish those. **A verified observation does not confer verified status on the story told about it.**
- **✅ WHY THE WRONG MECHANISM COST NOTHING, AND WHEN THAT LUCK RUNS OUT.** The fix — invalidate at the hook's **true completion branch** (`useBuildWizardPlansStreaming.ts:118` done, `:150` buffered fallback) — is correct under *both* readings, so building before settling the mechanism was safe. ⚠️ **Contrast BUG-052, logged the same day, where the two candidate mechanisms required different fixes and a DB probe was mandatory.** **The test is not "how confident is the story" but "does one fix serve every candidate story."** If yes, build. If no, instrument first.
- **✅ FIX + DEVICE CONFIRMATION.** Fixed at `b2da12d` (WS9 3c Part C). The hook now exposes `onComplete`, fired only at genuine completion; regression proved by moving the call into `onCandidate` and watching the first-card and partial-keep tests go red. **Device-confirmed July 29:** `GET /api/wizard/last-batch` returned **200 at 09:28:04.8**, immediately after the generation completed at 09:28:04.7 — where the pre-fix mount had answered from cache with a 304.
- **The sibling `["wizard","drafts"]` invalidations were removed, not fixed.** After WS9 3c deleted the dead resume-interstitial chain, `draftsQuery` was gone and the key had **zero observers** — verified by grep, not predicted — so its invalidation was a no-op. Dropped in the same commit.
- **Cross-ref:** D-WS9-072 (the feature this degrades) · BUG-047 (the sibling drafts-cache gap — resolved by removal, not by a matching fix) · BUG-049 (the misdiagnosis this symptom fed) · **BUG-052 (the same-day contrast case: two mechanisms, two different fixes, probe mandatory).**

### BUG-048 — Aborted generation requests do not cancel the upstream AI call: full latency and full cost burned on a result nobody receives 🔴 P3

- **Surface:** `POST /api/wizard/build-plans` (and by inspection any streamed generation route). Server-side.
- **Status:** 🔴 OPEN. Logged July 27, 2026 — surfaced from Hans's own terminal output during the Block 4b-2 device test, not from a report.
- **⚠️ EVIDENCE (verbatim from the run):** request `id: 33` logged **`request aborted`** at 15:01:02 with `responseTime: 1394` — Hans navigated away ~1.4s in. The upstream Anthropic call **ran to completion anyway**: `ai_call_stream` succeeded at 15:01:20 with `latencyMs: 18784`, `outputTokens: 1000`, **`costEstimateUsd: 0.0315195`**. **18.8 seconds of model time and 3.2 cents were spent on output that was discarded.**
- **Root cause (UNVERIFIED HYPOTHESIS — needs a read):** the HTTP request abort almost certainly does not propagate an `AbortSignal` into the SDK streaming call, so the generation continues detached from the client that asked for it. **Verify before fixing** — the standing rule is that a plausible mechanism is not a finding.
- **Why P3 and not higher:** invisible and harmless at one developer. **It scales badly in exactly the wrong way** — abandonment is most likely precisely when generation feels slow, so the cost and the concurrency burn concentrate on the users already having the worst experience. At launch volume this is real money and real throughput spent on nobody.
- **⚠️ Note the interaction with the streaming work:** the latency block made first-card arrive at ~9s while total generation still runs ~19s, which **widens the window** in which a user can reasonably navigate away believing they are done. Streaming did not cause this, but it makes it easier to hit.
- **Priority:** P3. **Target: unscheduled** — not 4b-3 (different surface), reasonable to fold into WS9A hosting work when server-side cost and concurrency start to matter.
- **Cross-ref:** the latency block (`32c28e3`) · D-WS9-081 (cost-estimate cache-token fix, same cost-accounting surface).

### BUG-046 — Household servings not applied: split source + build-path non-enforcement ✅ FIXED (`08b0e04`)

- **Surface:** Wizard plan generation → save/materialize. Any user whose household ≠ the meal's authored default.
- **Symptom (device test, July 25):** user set household to 2 via the wizard, generated + saved a plan, meals came out **serves 4 / 4-serving quantities**. Household of 2 not applied.
- **Root cause (VERIFIED):** two disconnected household sources — catalog-**forked** meals read stored `UserPreferences.householdSize` (via 4a); **live-built** wizard meals read the per-run `candidateContext.householdSize`; and the build/materialize path set `servingsDefault` verbatim from the AI's `m.servings` with **no deterministic household step** (trusted prompt adherence). So a mixed plan can't scale uniformly, and the per-run value never reached forks. The user's stored value was 4 (per-run "2" was set in the non-persisting wizard stepper) → everything deterministically came out 4. Not a save-overwrite; generated/forked at 4.
- **✅ FIX (`08b0e04`):** resolve one `effectiveHousehold = perRun ?? stored` per plan at materialize; apply it deterministically to BOTH forked and live-built slots (`servingsDefault = effectiveHousehold`, `authoredServingsDefault` preserved as the authored anchor). Per-run threaded via the transient draft payload (Option 1, no migration); NOT persisted to `UserPreferences` (D-WS7-035 intact). Build path no longer trusts AI `m.servings`. Money check: household=2 on 4-authored meals → `servingsDefault=2, anchor=4, multiplier=0.5` on both branches. +7 tests. Resolves the **D-WS9-070 build-path half**.
- **Priority:** was P2 (functional, blocked catalog wiring). **Device-tested clean across scenarios.**

### BUG-045 — Fork drops `authoredServingsDefault` (latent anchor-drop) ✅ FIXED (`08b0e04`)

- **Surface:** `cloneMealInto` / `forkMealForUser` — any meal forked to a user.
- **Root cause (VERIFIED):** the clone copied `servingsDefault` but never set `authoredServingsDefault`, so the fork's anchor landed NULL and fell back to `servingsDefault`. A forked catalog meal was correct only **by accident** (copy servings == anchor → multiplier 1); a latent correctness bug the moment household scaling was introduced.
- **✅ FIX (`08b0e04`, part of the 4a two-field write):** the fork now sets `authoredServingsDefault: source.authoredServingsDefault ?? source.servingsDefault` on both meal and dishes, on both clone branches. Closes the drop.
- **Priority:** was P2 latent. Fixed as part of the servings unification.

### BUG-044 — `prep.narrate_steps` measured 21,356 input tokens / 91s / retryCount 1 against a post-trim expectation of 4,213 — a ~5× overshoot, mechanism unknown 🔴 P2 — UNVERIFIED, NOT YET INVESTIGATED

- **Surface:** prep narration on plan open (`prep.narrate_steps`). User-visible as wait time on a plan's prep screen.
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN — CAPTURED, NOT INVESTIGATED.** Logged July 20, 2026. Carried across at least two chat handoffs without being chased; written here so it stops riding in chat memory (§23).
- **Priority:** **P2** — not a correctness defect and **not a scale-run gate** (this is plan-generation latency; catalog writing never calls this path). But 91 seconds of waiting is squarely a *"this is amazing, I'd tell people"* problem and a tester notices it before anything else.
- **Target:** after the pre-seed gates and the ~1,133-meal scale run. Does not block either.

**The observation (single sample, device test):**
`prep.narrate_steps` returned **21,356 input tokens · 91s wall clock · retryCount 1** on a **3-meal plan**, against CC's measured Phase A reduction of **9,470 → 4,213 tokens**. Input came in roughly **5× the post-trim expectation**.

**⚠️ WHAT IS AND IS NOT EVIDENCE (§27) — read before chasing this:**
- **The 21,356 / 91s / retryCount 1 figures are a real measurement** from a device test.
- ⚠️ **A "mean of 67 seconds" prior baseline is UNVERIFIED.** It was quoted forward from an earlier chat and **has not been confirmed against `LLMCallLog`.** It may be right, but a figure carried through a handoff is a claim about a conversation, not about data — and **three separate figures have already been falsified this way in this arc** (the 44-meal store count, which was 25; the "2 appliance entries dropped," which were never dropped; the `STABLE_GENERATE_PREFIX` 2,453, which exists nowhere in the code and is really 2,888). **Do not build a regression narrative on 67 until it is queried.**
- **The good news: no reconstruction is needed.** Every narration call already writes latency, input tokens, `retryCount` and `promptVersion` to `LLMCallLog`. The baseline is a query, not a memory.

**Three candidate mechanisms, distinguishable by one read-only pass:**
1. **The Phase A trim doesn't reach this path.** If live requests take a different code path than the one CC measured, the reduction is real but not in production. **Concrete bug; the token count would say so plainly.**
2. **Plan-shape variance.** Dedup saves more when dishes repeat, so a 3-meal plan with many distinct dishes and steps could legitimately dwarf the measured case. **Not a bug — a measurement that didn't span the range.**
3. **The retry is the whole story.** `retryCount 1` means a first attempt failed or timed out. If 91s covers a failed call *plus* a successful one, that is a **reliability** problem with a completely different fix, and it would explain a jump from any prior mean **with no token regression at all**.

**The investigation (read-only, one pass over logged history — NOT a device test):**
Query `LLMCallLog` for every `prep.narrate_steps` call with `inputTokens`, `outputTokens`, latency, `retryCount`, `promptVersion`, `createdAt`, then:
- **Split on `promptVersion`** — if Phase A moved the prompt body there is a version boundary in the data. Pre- vs post-trim latency and token distributions side by side. This confirms or kills the 67s figure *and* shows whether the trim landed at all.
- **Attach plan shape** (meal count, dish count, total steps per call). If the 21,356 call was a 12-dish plan and the 4,213 measurement was a 4-dish plan, there is no regression.
- **Isolate `retryCount ≥ 1`** — mean latency with and without retries.
- **Confirm the live code path** is the one Phase A measured.

⚠️ **A device test is the wrong instrument here.** It yields one sample and a stopwatch; the table yields every run with plan shape attached.

**Expected outcomes:** the boring one is that the trim landed, 91s was a large plan plus a retry, and the mean is fine. The bad one is that Phase A's reduction never reaches production — a real bug, and the token counts will show it immediately.

- **Cross-ref:** D-WS9-049 (Phase A prep-input trim — the 9,470→4,213 measurement this is tested against; **Phase B** carries the remaining latency work: prep-completion invalidation cascade and wizard generate caching) · §27 (verify before adopting as a finding — the 67s figure is the live instance) · §23 (this entry exists because the anomaly had survived two handoffs in chat memory alone).

### BUG-043 — Cuisine preferences ship a polluted 10-element mixed-format array: invisible dev-seed values ride along with real selections ✅ FIXED (Fix A, `c7532f5`) — structural half → WS10

- **Surface:** Preferences screen + Wizard (shared `CuisinePicker`) → `POST /wizard/build-plans`. Affects **every plan generated**.
- **✅ FIX A APPLIED (`c7532f5`, July 25, 2026):** the immediate/seed half. Dev seed (`devData.ts:713-720`/`731-738`) now emits the exact `domain.ts` display labels (`Mexican`, `Italian`, `Mediterranean`, `Thai`, `Indian`, `Tex-Mex`) instead of lowercase slugs; the one polluted account row (`hans+8`) was normalized slug→label directly (dev data, no migration). Device-verified: chips render terracotta, count reflects real selection, floor-at-6 gone. Picker code untouched — was always correct; this was bad seed data. ⚠️ **The commit message mislabels this "BUG-047"** — a mis-attribution in the fix prompt (the bug was treated as new when it already existed as BUG-043); left uncorrected in git per Hans (option 1). **This log is the source of truth: the correct ID is BUG-043.** ⚠️ **STRUCTURAL HALF NOT DONE:** canonicalize `cuisines` at the Zod boundary (single enforced enum, reject unrecognized at the API boundary) per the D-WS6-013 precedent — tracked to **WS10** (see D-WS9-074 / roadmap WS10). Without it, a future writer can reintroduce slug/off-catalog drift. Field note: the column is now `cuisines` (renamed from `cuisinePreferences` referenced in older canon).
- **Status (historical):** 🔴 was OPEN — logged July 19, 2026 (post-Block-3 device test). **Root cause VERIFIED end to end via live DB query + code trace.**
- **Priority:** P2 — not a crash, but it **silently corrupts every generation** by feeding the AI cuisines the user never chose. Hans saw Thai, Indian, Tex-Mex, and Mediterranean meals against selections of American/Italian/Mexican/BBQ-Grill.
- **Target:** unscheduled; small fix, but see the canonicalization question below.
- **Symptom (verbatim from the mobile submit log):**
  `cuisines: ["mexican","italian","mediterranean","thai","indian","tex_mex","Mexican","Italian","American","BBQ/Grill"]`
  Two formats in one array; `Mexican`/`mexican` and `Italian`/`italian` each appear twice in different casings.

**Root cause — VERIFIED, and it is neither "stored row polluted" nor "read path injecting" as first hypothesized:**
- **The stored row holds six lowercase snake_case values the user never chose** — `["mexican","italian","mediterranean","thai","indian","tex_mex"]`. These are the **verbatim dev-seed default** written by `prisma/seeds/devData.ts:713-720` / `731-738` (the sole origin in the repo, in the exact order observed at the front of the payload).
- **Those six match NO mobile chip.** Mobile canon (`kiwi/lib/domain.ts:52-81`) is Title-Case-with-punctuation: `"American"`, `"BBQ/Grill"`, `"Tex-Mex"` (hyphen, capitalized) — so `tex_mex` / `mediterranean` / `mexican` render as **invisible, undeselectable chips**. The user cannot see or remove them.
- **`toggleInArray` APPENDS** (`components/preference-pickers/shared.tsx:53-55`, `[...arr, item]`). The invisible six persist in `form.cuisines`; the four real taps append on top → **exactly the 10-element array observed**.
- **The wizard never PATCHes prefs**, so the DB stays at the six seeds — consistent with the live query. The write path is a clean REPLACE (`me.ts:781-784`), not a merge, and the read path unions nothing external (`wizard.tsx:134` / `:278`).
- ⚠️ **`discoveryMealsPerWeek:0` in the payload is a red herring** — CC checked explicitly; no discovery/variety expander touches the cuisines array on either generate path.

**⚠️ THE UNDERLYING DEFECT — `cuisines` was never canonicalized (VERIFIED):**
Three layers disagree and **only two of them enforce anything**: Prisma is free-form `String[] @default([])` (`schema.prisma:299`); server Zod is `z.array(z.string().max(60))` with **no enum and no normalization** (`me.ts:168`, `schemas/wizard.ts:39`); only the **mobile chips** and the **AI-generation prompt** (which mandates Title Case: *"Match the casing… no lowercase"*) constrain format. There is **no normalizer anywhere on the prefs-write or wizard-submit path** — which is exactly why `"BBQ/Grill"` and `"American"` survive with casing and punctuation intact.
**This is the same class of defect as D-WS6-013** (which reconciled the `weeklyPacing` enum across mobile/server/Prisma to a single canonical form). **`cuisines` was missed by that sweep.**

**Fix has two independent halves — do not conflate:**
1. **Immediate:** stop the dev seed emitting pre-canonical lowercase, and/or normalize on read so unrecognized values can't ride along invisibly. Clears the live pollution.
2. **⚠️ Structural:** canonicalize `cuisines` the way `weeklyPacing` was canonicalized — a single enum enforced at the Zod layer, so an unrecognized value is **rejected at the boundary** rather than flowing to the AI. Without this, any future writer can reintroduce the same drift.

**Also worth ruling:** an invisible-undeselectable-chip class of bug can recur on **any** free-form array field rendered as a constrained picker. Consider whether the picker should surface unrecognized values as removable rather than silently carrying them.

- **⚠️ Test-account note:** the device was reading account **`hans+8` (`76f9d078`)**. Two other accounts hold clean Title-Case rows (`b8553fe1`, `30d91400`). Worth confirming which account device tests should use.
- **Cross-ref:** D-WS6-013 (the `weeklyPacing` canonicalization this field was missed by) · WS6 6c-1 (prior "cuisine-case drift" observation) · D-WS9-049 (the diagnostic session that surfaced this) · `devData.ts:713-720` (the seed) · `shared.tsx:53-55` (the append).

### BUG-041 — Tell Kiwi (`/wizard/build-from-text`) intermittently 502s ("Kiwi got distracted"), then succeeds on retry 🔴 P2 — TRACKING FREQUENCY

- **Surface:** Wizard **Tell Kiwi** / build-from-text entry (the free-text plan request path).
- **Status:** 🔴 OPEN — logged July 17, 2026 (Plan-Gen Arc Block 2 device test, Test 5).
- **Priority:** P2. **Target:** monitor; fix if frequency warrants (retry/timeout config) — not Block 2 scope.
- **Symptom:** First tap returned the "Kiwi got distracted" error screen; retry worked; a later tap failed again. Intermittent, not deterministic.
- **Server evidence:** `POST /api/wizard/build-from-text` → **502**, `responseTime: 2883ms`; immediately after, `wizard.directed.parse_intent` (Haiku) **succeeded with `retryCount: 1`** (`latencyMs: 5179`). The fail-then-succeed-on-retry pattern points to a **transient upstream 502 from the model API**, caught by the retry layer on the subsequent call — **not** a code defect introduced by the Block 2 fix pass (the parse path is unchanged; the `parse_intent` classifier is flat/Haiku per D-WS9-019).
- **⚠️ UNVERIFIED (§27):** "transient provider 502" is the leading read from one log sample, not a confirmed finding. **Track frequency before acting:** if it's ~1-in-20 it's provider noise (accept + ensure the retry/error-surface is graceful); if it's ~1-in-3 it's a retry-count/timeout config problem on the build-from-text path worth a real fix. Do NOT fix blind — gather frequency first.
- **Cross-ref:** D-WS9-019 (AI cost+latency program; the retry/timeout surface) · Fix 4 (retrieveShelf wired into build-from-text — verify the shelf retrieval isn't adding latency that trips a timeout, though the 502 is upstream-shaped) · BUG-036 (the *other* surprise/build error-screen guard, FIXED — distinct root cause).

### BUG-033 — `lime zest` portion/density: a whole 67 g lime standing in for 2–4 g of zest (~20×) 🔴 P2
- **Surface:** `conversionRef` / the B2 conversion-table arc. **NOT the nutrition arc.**
- **Symptom:** `lime zest` points at `[168155] Limes, raw` — a **whole 67 g lime**. Actual zest from one lime is **~2–4 g**. Any quantity derived from that portion figure is **~20× too large**.
- **Why it is NOT a nutrition-pointer bug (and why BUG-032 deliberately left it alone):** the **macro join is defensible** — zest *is* lime peel, and per-100g lime macros are a reasonable stand-in. The error is entirely in the **portion/density** figure. Two different consumers, two different fixes. **Do not paper over a portion bug with a nutrition-pointer change.** USDA has no zest/peel record, so raw lime is the only whole-food option on the macro side.
- **Status:** 🔴 OPEN. Logged July 12, 2026 (surfaced by the BUG-032 sweep; split out at Hans's ruling).
- **Priority:** P2.
- **Target:** the B2 / `conversionRef` surface — likely a `curated` override row (this is exactly the case hand-curation exists for: an ingredient whose purchase/portion unit has no honest USDA analogue).
- **Cross-ref:** BUG-032 (surfaced it), BUG-025-1 / D-WS7-197 (the conversion table), D-WS6-024.

### BUG-036 — Surprise-me lands on the "Kiwi got distracted" error screen: the no-input guard was never exempted for the surprise path 🔴 P2
- **Surface:** `app/wizard-results.tsx:525` (the no-input early-return guard).
- **Symptom:** tapping **✦ Surprise me** on home always lands on **"Kiwi got distracted. Try again? — Plan input wasn't passed through."** — never on the 3 candidate plans. Seeding + full server/app restart do **not** fix it (Hans confirmed, twice).
- **Root cause (diagnosed, §27):** the guard is `if (!wizardInput && !tellKiwiPayload) return <error/>`. The surprise path carries **neither** operand (Tell Kiwi passes `tellKiwiPayload`; the wizard passes `wizardInput`; **surprise passes neither** — it fires `useBuildSurprise` on mount instead). So the guard is **permanently true for surprise** and returns the error screen on **every** render, **before** the candidates can show. The hooks run before the early return, so `POST /wizard/surprise-me` **actually fires and can 200 — the result is just never rendered.** A CC self-regression from the 3c build: surprise handling was added to the derivations, mount-fire, and candidate gate, but **not** to this early guard; the 3c tests were pure-logic/route and never mounted the screen with `source=surprise`, so nothing caught it.
- **⚠️ Entitlement RULED OUT (checked both sides):** `subscriptionService.ts:27-33` is a stub returning `{allowed:true}` for everyone (Stripe deferred); `kitchen_wizard_just_say` is **not** trial-gated server-side; the "Trial expired" home badge is cosmetic; Hans reached the results screen (client `isLocked` was false). **Not the cause.**
- **Fix (one line, NOT yet applied):** `if (!wizardInput && !tellKiwiPayload && !isSurprise)`. **Trivial, and it survives the Part B rebuild unchanged** (the guard stays; it just needs the exemption).
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN.** Diagnosed July 15, 2026. **Ship the one-liner as an immediate hotfix** to unblock Surprise-me device-testing, independent of the paused plan-options rebuild (see D-WS9-032 / the new plan-lifecycle arc).
- **Cross-ref:** D-WS9-032 (the paused plan-options flow this screen also hosts).

### BUG-085 — Profile → Account info: **editing the phone number does not persist on save** 🔴 **OPEN** P2

- **Date:** August 11, 2026 · **Surface:** `app/(tabs)/profile.tsx` → Account info (phone field)
- **Found by:** Hans, on device, clicking around outside any commissioned test. **Not found by any suite.**
- **Symptom:** the phone field reads *"Not set"*. Hans edited it, entered a number, saved. **The value did not persist.** Whether the save reported success is **NOT YET KNOWN** — see below.

⚠️ **THE FIRST DIAGNOSTIC QUESTION IS NOT "WHY DIDN'T IT SAVE" — IT IS "DID THE UI CLAIM IT DID."** A save that fails loudly and a save that reports success while dropping the field are different bugs with different severities. **2b shipped a delete alert that navigated as though it had succeeded**, so this project has the silent-success failure mode on record. **Establish this before anything else.**

**UNVERIFIED HYPOTHESES — labelled, none paired with "no change required":**
1. **The field-drop class.** ⚠️ **This has the exact shape of BUG-068 / BUG-076 / BUG-083** — a client sends a field that the server's update input, `select`, or Zod schema silently omits. It typechecks, passes CI, and fails only on device. ⚠️ **`lib/api/me.ts` (10 schemas) is on D-WS9-152's never-field-diffed list**, and profile reads from it.
2. **A deliberate omission.** ⚠️ **PRECEDENT: `lib/meal-builder-state.ts` omits `servingsDefault` from `buildUpdateMealInput` ON PURPOSE** (D-WS7-169 / BUG-002) and reads as a bug. **If an update-input builder omits `phone`, check for a comment explaining why BEFORE restoring it.**
3. **The column may not exist.** Phone may never have been persisted at all, making this a **missing feature wearing an editable field** rather than a broken write.

**⚠️ DO NOT ASSUME SCOPE. Hans's read — *"hopefully it's just some wiring into the profile and not throughout"* — is the right question and it is OPEN.** The answer is one query and one grep: does `User` carry a phone column, is it non-null on any row, and how many surfaces read or write it. **Sweep for the SHAPE of the defect, not the one field** — if an update builder drops one field it may drop others.

**SEPARATE PRODUCT QUESTION, NOT PART OF THIS BUG.** Hans: *"that should be collected from signup."* ⚠️ **Whether phone is collected at signup is UNKNOWN and is a product decision, not a defect.** If signup does not collect it, **mint a deferral then** — do not fold a feature gap into a bug fix.

- **Priority:** **P2** — does not block the current commit. ⚠️ **Escalate to P1 if the save reports success**, because silent data loss on an account field is worse than a visible failure.
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN.** · **Target:** **3g** owns `profile.tsx` and `manage-account.tsx`. ⚠️ **If the field-drop class is confirmed, the sweep is D-WS9-152's, not 3g's.**
- **Cross-ref:** BUG-068 · BUG-076 · BUG-083 · BUG-002 · D-WS9-152 · D-WS7-169.

### BUG-084 — `Typography.letterSpacing.wide` is **0.04**, which is visually zero — the token looks authored in `em` and applied as points 🔴 **OPEN** P3

- **Date:** August 11, 2026 · **Surface:** app-wide typography token
- **Found by:** CC, WS9 Block 2c commit 7, while matching the card eyebrow's tracking to the existing precedent at `PrepCookHubView.tsx:419` rather than inventing a literal.
- **Symptom:** `letterSpacing.wide = 0.04`. ⚠️ **React Native's `letterSpacing` takes POINTS, not `em`.** 0.04pt is imperceptible at any size. **CSS `letter-spacing: 0.04em` at 12px would be ~0.5pt — a 12× difference.** The value reads as if authored for the web and carried across.
- ⚠️ **IF THIS IS RIGHT, EVERY `letterSpacing.wide` SITE IS INERT** and every uppercase eyebrow in the app has been rendering with no tracking since the token shipped. **That is a visual-consistency bug across an unknown number of surfaces, which is why it is logged rather than fixed inline.**
- **Not touched.** CC flagged and used the existing precedent rather than changing a shared token mid-commit — correct call.
- **Next step:** count `letterSpacing.wide` consumers, then compare a rendered eyebrow against one with a real point value (~0.5–1.0). ⚠️ **Fix belongs with a typography pass, NOT a Home block** — it reflows every consumer.
- **Priority:** **P3.** Nothing is broken; things are merely not doing what the token name promises.
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN.** · **Target:** 3g or a WS9-close typography pass.
- **Cross-ref:** BUG-035 (the other token-vs-render mismatch in this family).

### BUG-035 — Serif headlines render DIFFERENTLY on iOS vs Android: `fontWeight:"700"` over a Fraunces face that was never loaded 🟡 P2 — ⚠️ ⚠️ **COUNT NOW DISPUTED A FOURTH TIME — SEE THE AUGUST 11, 2026 NOTE AT THE END OF THIS ENTRY BEFORE ACTING ON ANY FIGURE HERE.** Figures in circulation: 8 fixed / 19 remain / 15 closed / **21 remain** (August 5–6, re-verified twice) / **1 remain** (August 11, Block 2c Phase 0, under a redefined test). **Trust a fresh grep, not this log's history — including this line.**
- **Surface:** app-wide — **25 sites across 20 files.** Every serif headline that asks for bold.
- **Symptom:** `fontWeight:"700"` applied over a **`Typography.face.serif[600]`** face. **`_layout.tsx` loaded Fraunces at 400/500/600 only — there was NO `Fraunces_700`.** So the platforms diverged: **iOS synthesized a faux-bold; Android rendered plain 600.** The app's serif headings have been rendering **differently on the two platforms**, unnoticed because each site looked individually plausible — it took a root-cause sweep to see the pattern. *(Per FLAG 3, RN/Expo resolves weight by family NAME on Android, so a bare `fontWeight` over a named 600 face silently does nothing there.)*
- **Root cause:** the font was never loaded. **Fixed at the root July 14, 2026 (WS9 3b, `67ccab0`/`4738f59`):** `Fraunces_700Bold` + `Fraunces_700Bold_Italic` now in `useFonts`; `Typography.face.serif[700]`/`serifItalic[700]` added per the FLAG-3 convention.
- **⚠️ Why P2 not P1 — the remaining 19 are INERT, not broken-differently.** They **name the `Fraunces_600SemiBold` family explicitly**, so loading the 700 face changes nothing for them until each is migrated to `serif[700]`. **No collateral change, no big-bang migration** — the fix lands incrementally, block by block.
- **Status:** 🟡 **IN-PROGRESS. 8 of 25 fixed** ⚠️ **— COUNT CONFLICTED (flagged August 5, 2026).** Three figures live in canon: this line says **8 of 25 fixed**, this entry's own heading says **19 of 25 remain** (= 6 fixed), and `kiwi_ws9_plan.md` records 3f-1 closing BUG-035 **at 15 sites**. **Do not trust any of them.** The real number is one grep for `fontWeight:"700"` over `Typography.face.serif` — 3f-4 Phase 0. — 3b fixed 6 (`sign-in:84` · `welcome` brand · `sign-up:286` · `verify-email:140` · `first-run:144`* · `first-run:176`*), 3c fixed 2 (`wizard-results:849` · `wizard-plan-details:443`). *(*`first-run-destination` was subsequently deleted in the 3b follow-up — those two sites are moot.)*
- **📌 THE REMAINING ~17 (the fix plan — each owning block folds its own):**
  - **`components/` sheets** (→ mostly **3d**): `ChangeMealSheet:331` · `DishChooserSheetView:470` · `AddMealToPlanSheet:282` · `AddDishChooserSheet:142` · `AddDishToMealSheet:251` · `FindSimilarSheet:368` · `AddMealsSheet:348` · `PlanDateRangeEditor:398` · `PlanPreviewModal:205` · `WizardResumeInterstitial:227`
  - **`app/`**: `wizard-plan-details:443`✓(done 3c) · `wizard-results:849`✓(done 3c) · `dish/[id]:261` · `meal/[id]:736` *(→ **3f**)* · `(tabs)/profile:626,:645,:754` · `manage-account:91` · `deactivate-account:153` *(→ **3g**)*
- **The fix per site is one line:** bare `fontWeight:"700"` → `Typography.face.serif[700]`. ⚠️ **A bare weight over a Fraunces face IS the bug** — don't just bump the weight token.
- **✅ NOT the bug (leave alone):** sans-face 700 sites (profile avatar, `Stepper`, `AvatarChip`) — `DMSans_700Bold` IS loaded; they resolve correctly.
⚠️ **AUGUST 11, 2026 — BLOCK 2c PHASE 0 REPORTS THE REMAINING COUNT AS 1, NOT 21. READ THIS BEFORE TRUSTING EITHER FIGURE.**

⚠️ **THIS IS A REDEFINITION, NOT A FOURTH RECOUNT — and that distinction is the whole point.** The original defect was `fontWeight:"700"` over a face that **was never loaded**. **All eight Fraunces faces (400/500/600/700 × roman/italic) now load at `_layout.tsx:85-92`, so the "never loaded" half of this bug is GONE.** Under the entry's own original test, Phase 0 reports **zero** surviving sites: *"Zero sites are 700-over-a-non-700-face."*

**What it reports instead** is one site failing a **stricter** test — declared `fontWeight` ≠ the named face's own numeric weight:

| file:line | style | face | declared |
|---|---|---|---|
| `HomeHeader.tsx:91` | `greeting` | `serifItalic[500]` | semibold (600) |

Method: block-parse of every `StyleSheet` entry carrying a serif `fontFamily` (**137 entries**, 1 mismatch), cross-checked with `rg -n "Typography.face.serif" -B5 -A5`.

⚠️ **DO NOT CLOSE THIS ENTRY ON THAT REPORT ALONE. TWO REASONS.**
1. **The claim is that the ~17 named sites below were migrated incrementally, block by block, exactly as this entry predicted** — plausible and probably right, but **nobody verified it site-by-site.**
2. ⚠️ **The report's own wording is soft where it matters:** the 31 raw `fontWeight: 700` occurrences app-wide are *"**almost all**"* on DM Sans faces. *"Almost all"* is not *"all."*

✅ **Block 2c fixes `HomeHeader.tsx:91` (it is on Home) AND spot-checks the named list below** — `ChangeMealSheet:331`, `dish/[id]:261`, `meal/[id]:736`, `(tabs)/profile:626`. **Four greps settle a count that has now been wrong four times.** Status moves only after that.

- **Cross-ref:** D-WS9-030 (the ruling that loaded the 700 face) · D-WS9-151 (2c scope).

### BUG-013 — Demoted prep steps shown muted instead of omitted (+ render-hide alone breaks the server rollup)
- **Surface:** Week Prep / Screen 3 render + (coupled) server `isPrepped` derivation.
- **Symptom:** Steps that should be demoted out of prep (e.g. a lone cooking-fat measure) are shown muted rather than omitted.
- **Status:** 🔴 OPEN — **both halves are Block 2** (tightened July 4: the earlier "render-omit = Block 1" framing was imprecise — per this bug's own verified root cause, render-hiding a demoted step ALONE breaks the Cook gate, so the render-omit and the server-exclude must land together in Block 2; implements the already-logged D-WS7-184). The narration-side demotion rule (BUG-014) DID land in Block 1 (`da857bc`) — `skipSuggested` is now set correctly; this bug is the omit + exclude consumption of that signal.
- **Priority:** P2 (with a P1 facet: the naive render-hide-alone fix would break the Cook gate — see BUG-015).
- **Target:** WS7-8b Block 2 (render-omit + server exclude together).
- **Root cause (VERIFIED, CC Phase 0):** render-hiding demoted steps ALONE breaks the server rollup — `isPrepped = every contributing stepKey checked` (`prepCompletion.ts:47-84`, esp. `:69`); the step set from `loadPrepStepSet` recomputes with NO AI (`prepStepSet.ts:44-61`) → blind to `skipSuggested`, treats demoted steps as required. Filtering them from the VM means their keys never get written → the meal can never reach `isPrepped` → Cook gate (`cookSession.ts:204-210`) nags forever.
- **Fix (ruled):** omit demoted steps from the prep list (render, Block 1) AND exclude `skipSuggested` steps from the server `isPrepped` required-set (Block 2). The narration-side demotion rule that decides what's demoted is BUG-014.


### BUG-015 — Vinegar stranded in its own prep step (assembly grouping gap) + server rollup exclusion
- **Surface:** Week Prep assembly (`prepWeekAssembly.ts`) + (coupled) server `isPrepped` derivation.
- **Symptom:** A sauce's wet part (white wine vinegar) routes alone to `sauces_marinades` as a lone step, stranded from the rest of the sauce, with no signal where it combines.
- **Status:** 🟡 PARTIAL (July 4) — grouping + `blendSpiceDish` linkage + blend reword ✅ FIXED in `da857bc` (Block 1); the server-rollup exclude for demoted steps stays OPEN → WS7-8b Block 2 (implements D-WS7-184).
- **Priority:** P2 (Block 1 grouping) + P1 facet (Block 2 server exclude — the forever-nag; see BUG-013).
- **Target:** WS7-8b Block 1 (grouping + linkage) + Block 2 (server exclude).
- **Root cause (VERIFIED, CC Phase 0):** only `seasonings_dry` collapses; every other phase is one-step-per-ingredient (`prepWeekAssembly.ts:222-226`). The engine groups by `ingredientId` globally, never by which sauce an ingredient belongs to — no sub-recipe grouping key → vinegar can't group with its siblings.
- **Fix (ruled — 5a, corrected scope):** group `sauces_marinades` by `dishId` so a sauce's wet components land in one "Make the Special Sauce" step; dry spices stay in the collapsed blend (cross-phase pull = 5b, deferred D-WS7-186). **CC dry-run finding:** with real seed routing, ketchup+mayo are Pantry/no-sauce-hint → they route to the blend, so only vinegar reaches `sauces_marinades`. The stranded-orphan outcome is still avoided via the **`blendSpiceDish` linkage** (code-owned field, present only when the sauce's dry spices truly survive into the blend → prompt emits "combine with the Special Sauce spices from your blend"). Plus a blend-step **reword** so wet condiments (ketchup/mayo) read naturally alongside dry spices. stepKey shape change: grouped sauce steps key on `sauces_marinades#dish#${dishId}` (stable; orphan-prune handles old per-ingredient rows). **Server-rollup exclusion (Block 2):** the AI-free `isPrepped` derivation must EXCLUDE `skipSuggested` steps from the required-set — REVERSES the WS7-8a B3 "flag-don't-drop" ruling → **logged as D-WS7-184.** Mechanism (persist per stepKey vs. read cached `structureJson`) scoped in Block 2's Phase 0.

*(Stale duplicate entries for **BUG-001** and **BUG-002** were removed here on August 2, 2026. Both were 🔴 OPEN/P1 copies that had been superseded by richer ✅ FIXED entries (`52775f8`) appended later in this file, and the two copies contradicted each other. ⚠️ The contradiction actively misinformed a chat-Claude audit on August 2 — a grep of this file returned the stale copy and the P1s were reported as live. **Status updates belong on the existing entry; appending a second entry for the same ID creates a heading collision that heading-grep cannot adjudicate.** Full history for both bugs is preserved in their FIXED entries below.)*

### BUG-005 — Timer chips too short (mis-tap ✕ vs +1 min)
- **Surface:** Cook Mode → per-step timer chips (the ✕-dismiss / "Add a minute" controls from Block-3 #4 + the new strip controls).
- **Symptom:** Chips are short enough that during testing a +1-min tap hit ✕ instead. Hans wants them ~2× taller for easier/safer tapping.
- **Status:** 🔴 OPEN — small mobile tweak, not built.
- **Priority:** P3 (ergonomics, no data risk).
- **Target:** WS7-8 (fold into a small mobile pass — pairs naturally with the strip-controls follow-on or the next Cook Mode touch).
- **Notes:** applies to both the per-step chips and the new top-strip controls (keep the two surfaces' touch targets consistent).

### BUG-008 — Compost button → stub; needs real soft-delete + context-aware routing
- **Surface:** Meal Detail Compost button — both plan-instance (from a plan) and Meal (from My Recipes → Meals).
- **Symptom:** Compost routes to a stub (no-op) instead of deleting.
- **Status:** 🟡 PARTIAL — **case 2 FIXED** (`af27018`); case 3 OPEN (net-new backend).
- **Priority:** P2 (case 3: real CTA dead-ends in Library; deletion is core).
- **Target:** WS7-8 (case 3 — sequence with Hans; net-new backend).
- **Spec (Hans, June 25, clarified June 26):** context-aware soft-delete + routing. Three cases:
  - **Case 1** — Plan Review → meal **card** → Compost: removes the meal from the plan. **Already worked** (`handleCompostFromPlan` → `removeMealFromPlan` → `deletePlanItem`). Reference only.
  - **Case 2 (✅ FIXED `af27018`)** — Plan Review → meal-in-plan → **detail screen** → Compost: same as case 1 (remove the plan item), route back to the same plan's Plan Review (`/plan/[planId]`). Wired to the existing `removeMealFromPlan(planId, planItemId)` with case-1-matching Alert copy ("Compost {title} from your plan? You can add it back later."). The meal stays in My Meals — only the plan item is removed.
  - **Case 3 (🔴 OPEN)** — Recipes → Meals → **detail** → Compost: soft-delete the base **Meal** from the user's library, route back to Recipes → Meals. **Net-new backend** (see below). Left as the existing stub this block (untouched), gated to Library context.
- **⚠️ Case 3 (Library) = NET-NEW backend.** `Meal` has `isArchived Boolean` but NO `compostedAt`/`deletedAt`, and there is no `DELETE /me/meals/:id` soft-delete route (only `DELETE /me/favorites/:mealId` unfavorite). Needs a meal soft-delete route (+ likely a `compostedAt` column for Plan-parity) before the Library Compost can wire. **Soft-delete (not hard) per Hans:** plan items reference the base meal, so a hard delete could orphan a meal still in a plan; soft-delete (`isArchived=true`) is the safe call. Confirmed by the Surface A audit (June 26).
- **Related:** Hans's D-WS7-040 direction (May 21): "next workstream will handle not showing deleted or composted recipes." Dish-deletion dangling-link concern is a separate logged item (deferred log).

### BUG-009 — "Save to my [Meals/Dishes/Plans]" action not exposed
- **Surface:** Non-owned item review/detail pages — plan review page (non-owned plan), meal detail, dish detail.
- **Symptom:** The save *paths* exist (a non-owned meal → add-to-plan already saves it to the user's meals; the plan save mechanism exists) but aren't callable as a deliberate user action. There's no explicit "Save to my [X]" door. Surfaced via the BUG-003 canonical-Save gate, which correctly **refuses** to save a non-owned item canonically — but there's no save-to-own affordance to redirect the user to.
- **Status:** 🔴 OPEN — not built; **AUDIT-FIRST** (map the existing callable save paths per entity before wiring).
- **Priority:** P2.
- **Target:** WS7.
- **Approach:** Phase 0 maps the existing callable save paths per entity (meal / dish / plan), then wire explicit "Save to my [X]" actions on the non-owned item's review/detail page. UX: action on the non-owned item → on success, toast "Saved to your [plans/meals/dishes]".
- **Notes:** Interacts with BUG-010 (a saved/edited Kiwi-Crafted item should move into "My Meals").

### BUG-010 — "Kiwi Crafted" vs "My Meals" filter split
- **Surface:** Meals + Dishes list views (My Recipes).
- **Symptom:** Wizard-generated meals save to the user's meals and pollute "My Meals" — there's no separation between meals the user actually created/edited and meals Kiwi generated for them.
- **Status:** 🔴 OPEN — not built.
- **Priority:** P2/P3.
- **Target:** WS7.
- **Approach:** Add a filter chip on the meals + dishes list views: **My Meals** = created-or-edited-by-user; **Kiwi Crafted** = wizard-generated, unedited by user. Requires an edited-by-user signal — **verify it exists at build** (Phase 0); if absent, add one.
- **Notes:** Interacts with BUG-009 — saving or editing a Kiwi-Crafted item moves it into My Meals.

### BUG-058 — "Swap for Similar" returns meals that are not similar, and returns the same meal multiple times
- **Surface:** `SwapMealSheet` — the **Find Similar** mode (plan-context meal swap).
- **⚠️ DEVICE REPRO (Hans, August 2, 2026):** swapping **Chicken Parmesan** on a live plan returned **BBQ chicken drumsticks**, **three separate instances of air-fryer chicken fingers**, and **a whole roast chicken with Italian seasoning.**
- **Status:** 🔴 OPEN — reported from device, **root cause NOT diagnosed.**
- **Priority:** **P2** (ship-soon). Does not block a commit and loses no data — but Swap for Similar is a primary plan-editing affordance and it is currently returning results a user would reject on sight.
- **Target:** **WS9 Block 3f-4** (the swap-sheet / Ask-Kiwi sub-block) — pairs naturally with D-WS9-102, which rules what this surface *should* return.
- **⚠️ THIS IS AT LEAST TWO INDEPENDENT DEFECTS. Fixing either alone leaves a visibly broken feature:**
  1. **Similarity is matching on a shared ingredient, not on dish character.** Every result contains chicken; none resembles Chicken Parmesan. Hans's own calibration, verbatim: *"Chicken parm could be similar to chicken picatta, spaghetti and meatballs, chicken fingers, baked chicken cutlets, etc. but not a whole chicken and not a bbq anything."* ⚠️ **Note that his similarity set crosses the protein boundary** (spaghetti and meatballs) **and excludes same-protein items** (whole roast chicken, BBQ). **The operative axis is preparation and eating experience — breaded//saucy/Italian-American weeknight — not the primary ingredient.** A fix that tightens ingredient matching will make this worse, not better.
  2. **The same meal came back three times.** Independent of relevance. Either the result set is not de-duplicated, or the corpus itself holds near-duplicate rows. **Both are plausible; neither is verified.** ⚠️ Note the catalog holds **1,124 `batch_generated` meals** produced by one generation harness — near-duplicate titles are a live possibility, and if the duplicates are *in the data* this is a catalog-quality finding, not a swap-sheet bug.
- **⚠️ MECHANISM IS UNVERIFIED — the fix location is not yet known.** Hans's read was *"the prompt instruction must be a little too specific or not specific enough."* **That is a hypothesis, not a finding, and it presumes an AI call.** Phase 0 must first establish **what Find Similar actually does**: an AI prompt (→ `aiPrompts.ts`, and §7's prompt-iteration rules apply, including the diff-driven version bump and the cache-boundary caution), an embedding/vector query, a keyword/`ILIKE` query, or a tag/cuisine DB filter. **Do not open `aiPrompts.ts` on the assumption that a prompt is the problem.**
- **Corpus question, open:** Hans — *"If it's searching the user's meals it should probably do user and catalog meals."* **Ruled in shape → D-WS9-102.** ⚠️ Establish the current corpus before changing it; a user with few saved meals and a catalog of 1,124 would produce exactly this thin, repetitive result set **if the search is user-scoped** — which would make corpus, not similarity logic, the dominant cause. **Measure before tuning.**
- **Related:** D-WS9-102 (what this surface should return + Ask-Kiwi as an alternate path) · D-WS9-018 (Ask-Kiwi escape hatch) · the catalog's Phase C/D work (roadmap row 7) if duplicates prove to be in the data.

### BUG-059 — The seeded catalog's `unit` field contains junk values, including quantities and non-units

- **Surface:** `dish_ingredients.unit` across the seeded catalog (the ~1,124-meal `batch_generated` corpus and its 3,450 dishes).
- **Discovered:** August 3, 2026, WS9 Block 3f-2 follow-up 4 Phase 0 — a **DB query**, not an inference. Surfaced while answering Hans's question about whether the system understands "1 Pinch Salt."
- **Status:** 🔴 OPEN — **not diagnosed beyond "the generator emitted it," and not yet scoped.**
- **Priority:** **P2** (ship-soon). Blocks no commit and loses no data, but it degrades grocery consolidation — a stated product selling point — on whatever fraction of rows it touches.
- **Target:** unassigned. **Natural home is the catalog work (roadmap row 7, phases C–E)**, or whichever block resolves D-WS9-105.
- **VERIFIED symptom — 37 distinct `unit` values, of which these are not units at all:**
  - **`""` — empty string (×4).** ⚠️ Note this passes `z.string().min(1)` **only if the rows predate the constraint or were seeded around the API boundary** — worth establishing which, because the answer says whether the boundary is currently leaky.
  - **`"6 oz"` (×3)** — **a quantity sitting in the unit field.** The quantity column presumably also holds a number, so this row is doubly-counted or nonsensical.
  - **`"15-ounce can"`** — same defect class, packaging-size text in a unit slot.
  - **`"second"` (×2)** — a time unit on an ingredient.
  - Judgment-call values that may or may not be defects: `"large"`, `"inch"` / `"inches"`, `"loaf"`, `"sheet"`.
- **⚠️ DISTINCT FROM ALIAS DUPLICATION, WHICH IS NOT THIS BUG.** The corpus also carries `teaspoon` 10,410 / `tsp` 15, `tablespoon` 7,165 / `tbsp` 50, `pound` 1,481 / `lb` 40, `ounce` 1,922 / `oz` 26, plus `cup`/`cups` and `clove`/`cloves`. **`normalizeUnit` already aliases these for conversion, so they are survivable and are NOT part of this bug.** ⚠️ **Do not bundle an alias-normalization sweep into this fix without ruling it separately** — that is a much larger change touching a working path.
- **⚠️ ROOT CAUSE UNVERIFIED.** The catalog was AI-generated, so the working hypothesis is that the model emitted these and nothing validated them on the way in. **That is a hypothesis. What has NOT been checked:** whether the seed path bypasses the Zod boundary, whether the generation prompt constrains units at all, and whether a currently-live user path could still produce these values. ⚠️ **If the ingress is still open, cleaning the data without closing it means the junk returns.** Establish ingress before scoping a cleanup (§27).
- **Downstream effect:** `groceryMerge` buckets by `(canonical, unit)`. A row with `unit = "6 oz"` or `""` cannot merge with the same ingredient measured properly, so it produces **a separate grocery line**. ⚠️ **Multi-meal grocery items are a product selling point, not an edge case.**
- **Related:** **D-WS9-105** (units are free text and mostly not understood — the *feature* gap; this entry is the *data* defect, and they are deliberately separate) · **D-WS9-106** (no-quantity / "to taste") · roadmap row 7 (catalog phases C–E).

### BUG-066 — A plan can be created with no name and no template, rendering as a blank row 🟡 **FIXED — P2**

- **Date:** August 8, 2026. **Found by Hans on device** (Plans tab, My Plans — first row rendered with no title text at all).
- **Surface:** Plans list. **Root cause is DATA + a permissive API, not a render bug.**
- **Root cause (cited):** `POST /plans` body schema has `name: z.string().min(1).max(120).optional()` (`plans.ts:626`) — **optional, no required-title guard** — and the create writes `titleOverride: body.name ?? null` alongside `mealPlanTemplateId: null` (`plans.ts:681-682`), a state an inline comment at `:614-616` explicitly permits. Every resolver computes `titleOverride ?? template?.title ?? ""`, so null + no template yields an empty string and the row renders nameless.
- **Scope:** exactly **1 record** in the live DB. Ten instances carry `titleOverride = null` but nine inherit a template title.
- **Fix (`3737353`):** `POST /plans` now defaults to **`"Untitled plan"`** when both are absent. ⚠️ **`name` stays `.optional()` — defaulted, NOT rejected.** The optional-name POST is deliberate per the inline comment. Client half rides the `DisplayTitle` primitive's fallback, which **reuses the same string** already present at `PlanNameEditor.tsx:59` rather than inventing a second one.
- **Cross-ref:** D-WS9-121 (the primitive).

### BUG-067 — A–Z sort would order by `title` while the user sees `displayTitle` 🟡 **PARTIALLY FIXED — P3**

- **Date:** August 8, 2026. **Found by chat-Claude while auditing**, not on device — the defect could only appear *after* a backfill populated `displayTitle`.
- **Symptom (latent):** *"Classic Slow-Simmered Beef Bolognese"* sorts under **C** but would display as *"Beef Bolognese"*. Sorting by a string the user cannot see.
- **Client half ✅ FIXED (`d4c88c7`):** `mealSort.ts`, `plans.tsx` and `groceries.tsx` alpha comparators now route through `resolveDisplayTitle` rather than reading `title` directly.
- ⚠️ **Server half DEFERRED and still open.** `orderBy: { title: "asc" }` (`me.ts:1550`, `me.ts:1666`, `meals.ts:759`) **cannot express `COALESCE(displayTitle, title)` in Prisma.** Three options were reported and none applied: **(a)** a denormalised indexed `sortTitle` column written on every meal/dish write — recommended, keeps keyset pagination; **(b)** `$queryRaw` — breaks keyset pagination unless carefully rebuilt; **(c)** in-memory sort after fetch — breaks cursor pagination.
- ⚠️ **HARMLESS TODAY AND MUST STAY THAT WAY.** `displayTitle` is **null on every row** (D-WS9-125 abandoned the program that would have populated it). **If anything ever populates `displayTitle`, this becomes live immediately and option (a) must ship in the same commit.**
- **Priority:** **P3** while dormant. **P1 the moment a backfill runs.**
- **Cross-ref:** D-WS9-121 · D-WS9-125.

### BUG-068 — ⚠️ `GET /meals` used an inline `select` that silently dropped `description` — and `tsc` could not see it ✅ **FIXED — P2**

- **Date:** August 8, 2026. **Found by CC incidentally**, while doing unrelated work in the same file. **Nobody was looking for it.**
- **Symptom:** catalog list rows returned `description: null` **even though all 1,124 catalog meals have populated descriptions.** The meals tab (`GET /me/meals`) was already correct; only the catalog endpoint was affected.
- **Root cause:** the catalog query used an **inline `select`** that omitted `displayTitle`, `description` and `authoredServingsDefault`, yet still passed its rows to the **shared `toListShape`** mapper. At runtime the missing fields came back `undefined` and coalesced to `null`.
- ⚠️ **THE TRANSFERABLE MECHANISM — THIS IS WHY THE ENTRY IS LONG.** It typechecked **only because `Array.prototype.map`'s callback parameter is checked BIVARIANTLY**, so TypeScript never flagged a mapper receiving less than it declares. **A shared shape function paired with an ad-hoc `select` is invisible to `tsc`.** ⚠️ **Grep for other inline `select` blocks feeding shared mappers — this is unlikely to be the only instance, and the failure is silent in production and green in CI.**
- ⚠️ **It would also have masked a fix.** Part 1c wired `description` into the DTO; without this find, a reseed would have shown **no change on Recipes → Meals** and the wiring would reasonably have been blamed.
- **Fix (`780dd20`):** the catalog query now uses the shared `MEAL_LIST_SELECT`. **One move makes both list endpoints identical.**
- **Cross-ref:** D-WS9-124.

### BUG-069 — `estimatedTimeMinutes` undercounts unattended cook time ✅ **FIXED — P2**

- **Date:** August 8, 2026. **Found by Hans on device** — Swap for Different, Cook Time ascending: a **slow-cooker meal sorted above a sheet-pan meal.**
- **Mechanism:** `Meal.estimatedTimeMinutes` is the **only** persisted meal-level time field (`schema.prisma:522`), and the sort reads it alone (`mealSort.ts:42`). Sort and card cannot disagree — the stored value was simply wrong.
- ⚠️ **THREE HYPOTHESES WERE TESTED, NOT ONE.** **H1 undercounting confirmed. H2 REFUTED** — `parsedMealToDraft.ts:49` sums `estimatedPrepMinutes + estimatedCookMinutes` before save, so nothing is ever stored half. **H3 REFUTED** — the quick meals store plausible small numbers; the long-cook side held the bad value.
- **Evidence:** *Slow Cooker Beef Stew* stored **30** while carrying an honest **480-minute** *"cook on low for 8 hours"* step. ⚠️ **Step durations are HONEST — the undercount lives only in the meal-level field**, because store-fill composition and step generation are **two independent passes** (`storeFill.ts:12`) that diverged.
- ⚠️ **THE DETECTION RULE IS PROVABLE, NOT HEURISTIC:** a meal cannot take less wall-clock than one of its own steps, so `estimatedTimeMinutes < max(step estimatedMinutes)` is **necessarily** wrong. **No keyword matching needed.**
- 🔴 **CORRECTED September 10, 2026 (BUG-245's Phase 0): THE RULE IS PROVABLE AND IT UNDER-DETECTS BY ~35×.** It fires only when a SINGLE step exceeds the meal. **`parallelGroup` is 0 of 25,564 steps, so a dish's own steps are strictly sequential — the tighter honest floor is `est < longest dish's serial sum`, which flags 1,199 of 1,605 meals (74.7%), or 649 (40.4%) even excluding every prep step.** ⚠️ **August's "20 meals" was the tip; the class was always ~35× larger than the instrument could see, and D-WS9-128's backfill drained the visible 20 from a pool of hundreds.** **D-WS9-122's prompt change went into the import and Mode-A prompts only — never the wizard path — so the leak continued: the post-Aug-8 violation rate is HIGHER (5.5% vs 2%).**
- **Scope:** **20 meals** (14 `batch_generated`, 6 `wizard`) at `maxStep ≥ 240`.
- **Fix (`ec56836`):** deterministic backfill, **no AI call**. `et := max(current_et, Σ step durations)`. All 20 strictly increased (30→527, 25→429, 210→675). Idempotent — a re-run flags zero. `title`/`description`/`tags` byte-identical on spot-check. **Sort verified: zero slow-cook meals now appear among the 25 quickest.**
- ⚠️ **One row overcounts by design:** *Kansas City Sweet BBQ Pulled Pork Plate* (210 → 675) is the only flagged meal with **two** long steps — a 480m slow-cook plus an 80m bean bake on a separate dish — which may overlap in a real kitchen. **True wall-clock is likely ~560–600. Accepted by Hans**: still far better than 210, and the never-lower rule makes it safe.
- **Cross-ref:** D-WS9-122 (the generation-side definition, which stops NEW meals reintroducing this) · D-WS9-128 (the formula and the deferred ≥120 band).

### BUG-070 — ⚠️ "Add to Plan" shows FIXTURE plans, not the user's real plans ✅ **FIXED — P2** (`d17a404`, August 10, 2026)

- **Date:** August 8, 2026. **Found by Hans on device.** Recipes → Meals → **Add to Plan** → *"Pick an existing plan"*.
- **Symptom:** the sheet lists **"This Week"**, **"Last Week"**, **"Easter family dinner"**. ⚠️ **None of these are Hans's plans** — his live plans are *"Italian Anchor, Bright Lentils…"*, *"Bold American Summer…"*, *"Add meals test"*. **The names are the tell: these are placeholder fixtures.**
- ⚠️ **ON THE PRIMARY PATH.** Recipes → Meals → Add to Plan is a core add-a-meal flow. **Picking a fixture either fails or writes against a non-existent plan ID.** ⚠️ **Do NOT tap through to test it before the trace — a write against a fixture ID is the bad outcome.**
- ✅ **ROOT CAUSE CONFIRMED, August 9, 2026 (Phase 0 static trace — the flow was NOT executed).** `AddMealToPlanSheet.tsx:72-78` calls `getUserPlans()` imported from `@/lib/stubs` (`:19`), which returns a **hardcoded 3-element literal** (`stubs.ts:1816-1846`): `demo-plan-this-week` "This Week", `demo-plan-last-week` "Last Week", `demo-plan-easter` "Easter Family Dinner". **No hook, no query, no server call.** ⚠️ **There is no fallback path — the placeholders ARE the only data**, which is why the empty branch (`:145-149`) is dead code.
- ✅ **SEVERITY SETTLED: P2, NOT P1.** `POST /plans/:id/items` does a `mealPlanInstance.findUnique` **plus an ownership check** and returns **404** for a fixture id (`plans.ts:1617-1623, 1707-1708`). ⚠️ **No fixture id can mutate a real plan.** The defect is a misleading list and a confusing error, **not** data corruption. **It was P1-shaped only in appearance.**
- ⚠️ **TWO CONSUMERS, IN DIFFERENT STATES.** **Meal detail** (`meal/[id].tsx:504-528`) already calls the real `addMealToPlan` → `postPlanItem`, so it should work once the list is real. **Meals tab** (`meals.tsx:177-187`) is `console.log` + `Alert.alert("Coming in WS7")` and **has never written anything.** **Both need wiring.**
- ⚠️ **REMOVING THE FIXTURES MAKES THE EMPTY STATE REACHABLE FOR THE FIRST TIME** — a user with no plans has literally never hit that branch. **Confirm it renders sensibly; new copy or a new affordance is a product decision.**
- **Fix:** replace `getUserPlans()` with the real plans query (`usePlans(["my_plans"])`, already used on Home and the Plans tab — **verify before adopting**). ⚠️ **`addMealToPlan` (`AppContext.ts:562-576`) is APPEND-ONLY — correct here; do not substitute replace semantics.**
- ⚠️ **`getUserPlans` is ONE of FIVE reachable stub-fed surfaces** — see **BUG-072 / -073 / -074**. `lib/stubs.ts` still carries its original *"will be replaced by API calls in WS7"* header. **WS7 landed long ago.**
- ⚠️ **Why it survived this long:** Phase 0 inventoried this sheet only as a `Modal` needing safe-area flags. **Its data source was never traced.** The sheet has been edited twice recently (nav-bar flag; `DisplayTitle` `slim` conversion) **without anyone asking where its list comes from** — the same shape as BUG-068.
- **Priority:** **P2**, upgradeable to **P1** depending on what the tap handler does with a fixture ID.
- **Target:** **WS9 Part 2 Block 2a** (fixture teardown). **Commissioned August 9, 2026.**

### BUG-071 — `plans.test.ts` rate-limit test is order-dependent and flaky 🔴 **OPEN — P3**

- **Date:** August 8, 2026. **Found while verifying whether an api-server failure was a regression.**
- **Symptom:** *"returns 429 when the mutation rate limit is exhausted"* — got **201**. **Fails and passes on identical code.** Observed failing alongside `ec56836`, then **passing on the same branch on a re-run**, and passing on the parent `17d9062`.
- **Root cause:** a **rate-limiter counter shared across concurrently-running suites.** The bucket drains differently depending on scheduling, so the test occasionally finds capacity left. Passes 155/155 in isolation.
- ⚠️ **WHY IT IS LOGGED RATHER THAN SHRUGGED OFF:** a test that passes or fails on scheduling luck **will eventually mask a real failure**, and "pre-existing flake" was initially an *inference* — it took two runs at two commits to establish. **Fix is an isolated limiter instance per suite, not a retry.**
- **Priority:** **P3.** **Target:** unscheduled.

- **Date:** August 9, 2026. **Found by the WS9 Part 2 Phase 0 fixture sweep** (not on device).
- **Symptom:** both screens call `getCurrentSubscription` from `@/lib/stubs` (`profile.tsx:22,73` · `manage-account.tsx:9,15`), which returns a **hardcoded trial state and "14 days."** ⚠️ **This is invented account status displayed as fact on a real screen.**

- **Priority:** **P2.** **Target:** **Block 2a.**

### BUG-073 — Fake meal/dish catalogs render on two reachable authoring surfaces ◑ **DATA HALF FIXED — P2** (`bc8a1ac`; write path BLOCKED — see below)

- ✅ **DATA HALF SHIPPED (`bc8a1ac`, `d7bb73a`).** `AddDishToMealSheet` now reads real meals via `useInfiniteMeals(["my_meals"])`; the three structurally-empty chips are gone (**D-WS9-137**). `meal-builder`'s stub dish lookup is replaced by a real `useDish` fetch (**BUG-078**).
- ⛔ **WRITE HALF BLOCKED — AND THE REASON IS SEVERE.** There is **no** mutator and **no** endpoint for "add existing dish → existing meal." The only meal-dish mutation is `updateMeal` → `PATCH /me/meals/:id` with `dishes[]`, which is a **full wipe-and-recreate**: `rematerializeMeal` (`mealMaterialize.ts:640-684`) computes `exclusiveDishIds` and **`tx.dish.deleteMany`s them** before recreating. ⚠️ **Wiring the handler through it would have DELETED the user's dish rows and left dangling links — silent corruption, green suites.**
- ⚠️ **The stub was accidentally protective.** The control was designed to do exactly the thing that corrupts, and has never fired because it was never wired. It now shows *"Coming soon — you'll be able to add this dish to an existing meal."*
- **Remaining work is NET-NEW server:** a non-destructive attach path, plus a ruling on exclusive-vs-shared dish semantics. ✅ **Hans ruled (August 10, 2026): its own block after Part 2 closes**, paired with **BUG-080**'s sweep — same code path, and auditing it twice is waste. **Pre-beta, not pre-WS9-close.**
- **Cross-ref:** D-WS9-137 · BUG-078 · BUG-080.

- **Date:** August 9, 2026. **Phase 0 fixture sweep.**
- **Symptom:** `AddDishToMealSheet.tsx:22-27` imports `getSavedMeals` / `getFeaturedMeals` / `getTopRatedMeals` / `getHostingMeals` and renders **fake meals**; `meal-builder.tsx:78-82, 348-350` imports `getSavedDishes` / `getFeaturedDishes` / `getTopRatedDishes` and feeds the **"combine" dish catalog** with fake dishes (Jasmine Rice, Roasted Broccoli…).
- ⚠️ **`AddDishToMealSheet`'s pick handler is ALSO a dead stub** — `Alert.alert("Coming in WS7")` (`meals.tsx:199-202`). **Fake data AND a dead handler on the same control.**
- **Fix:** replace with the real meals/dishes queries; wire the handler if the real mutator exists.

### BUG-076 — ⚠️ `template.imageUrl` dies at **BOTH** hops — server builder never maps it, **then** `PlanDetailSchema` has no slot ✅ **FIXED — P3** (`6b6c3ad`, August 11, 2026)

- ✅ **CLOSED August 11, 2026, Block 2b (`6b6c3ad` + fixture follow-up `a5a9b49`).** **Both hops in one commit** — server now emits `image: instance.template?.imageUrl ?? null`; `PlanDetailSchema` gained `image: z.string().nullable()`. Adapter and types threaded; **6 new tests** (schema parse/reject, adapter threading, round-trip GET).
- ⚠️ **THE FIELD IS WIRED BUT THE PICTURE IS STILL EMPTY.** Only **~4.7%** of plans can resolve an image, so `TreatedImage`'s gradient fallback renders on essentially every plan. **Whether plans should carry imagery at all is UNRESOLVED — see D-WS9-144.** Closing this bug did not answer that.

- ⚠️ **CORRECTION (August 11, 2026, WS9 Part 2 consolidated audit).** This entry originally described a **Zod-only** defect. It is **worse and earlier than that.** The server selects `template.imageUrl` (`plans.ts:345`) but the emitted plan object (`plans.ts:474-513`) **never references it** — it maps `template?.title` and `template?.sourceType` and drops the rest. **So the field dies at the server builder, and the missing Zod slot is the SECOND layer, not the first.** The `select` at `:345` is currently a **dead select**.
- **A fix must change BOTH hops.** Server: add `image: instance.template?.imageUrl ?? null` inside the plan object. Mobile: add `image: z.string().nullable()` to `PlanDetailSchema` (`lib/api/plans.ts:127-159`). **The plumbing already exists** — `PlanListItemSchema:50` and `TemplateDetailSchema:185` both carry `image`.
- ⚠️ **Verified dormant either way:** only **6 of 71** `MealPlanTemplate` rows are public and non-archived — and **all 6 carry an image.** `TreatedImage` already renders a warm gradient fallback with `source` null, so the header can be built now and wired later.
- **Target:** **Block 2b.**

- **Date:** August 9, 2026. **Phase 0.**
- **Symptom:** `GET /plans/:id` **selects** `template.imageUrl` (`plans.ts:345`), but the mobile `PlanDetailSchema` (`lib/api/plans.ts:127-159`) **has no slot for it**, so it is **discarded at parse.** The client can never see a plan's image no matter what the server sends.
- ⚠️ **THE MIRROR IMAGE OF BUG-068, AND WORTH THE SEPARATE ID FOR THAT REASON.** BUG-068 was a server `select` omitting a field a shared mapper read. This is a **client schema omitting a field the server sends.** **Both are silent in production and green in CI.** ⚠️ **Sweep the other mobile Zod schemas for the same shape** — this is unlikely to be the only one.
- **Not a mystery, just unwired:** `PlanListItemSchema` (`:50`) and `TemplateDetailSchema` (`:185`) **do** carry `image`. The plumbing exists; plan detail never got it.
- ⚠️ **Dormant in practice today** — only **6 of 127** plan instances resolve to a non-null template `imageUrl` (D-WS9-134). **Fixing it changes what 4.7% of plans can display.**
- **Priority:** **P3.** **Target:** **Block 2b**, wired alongside the header band. ⚠️ **NOT Block 2a** — 2a is forbidden from touching this surface.
- **Cross-ref:** BUG-068 · D-WS9-134.

- **Date:** August 9, 2026. **Phase 0.** ⚠️ **BUG-064's sibling — previously carried as an unlogged finding; this is its first ID.**
- **Symptom:** the wrapper spreads `...props` straight through without a default (`KeyboardAwareScrollViewCompat.tsx:23-31`), so every consumer inherits the library default of `0`. **BUG-064 was fixed on the wizard alone; the same condition holds app-wide.**

- ⚠️ **Own commit — blast radius is every keyboard-aware screen**, which is also the point of fixing it once.
- **Priority:** **P2.** **Target:** **Block 2a.**

### BUG-078 — `meal-builder` resolved a REAL dish id against the STUB fixture array ✅ **FIXED — P2** (`d7bb73a`, August 10, 2026)

- **Date:** August 10, 2026. **Surface:** Recipes → Dishes → a dish → Add to a meal → **Create a new meal**.
- **Symptom:** the meal builder opened **empty**. The dish the user explicitly chose was silently not injected.
- **Root cause:** `meal-builder.tsx:347-351` resolved `addDishId` by searching the **stub** dish arrays (`getSavedDishes` / `getFeaturedDishes` / `getTopRatedDishes`). `addDishId` is produced by `AddDishToMealSheet.tsx:87` and carries a **real `DishListItem.id`**, which could never match a fixture id → `all.find(...)` returned `undefined` → `console.warn` → nothing injected.
- ⚠️ **THIS WAS SHIPPING, NOT LATENT.** The audit initially framed it as *"latent once real"*; the trace proved the id was already real and the path already broken.
- **Fix:** resolve the dish via `useDish` (`GET /dishes/:id`). ⚠️ **The fix also carries the dish's STEPS into the seeded meal** — the old stub path dropped them, matching `pickSavedDishToBuilderDish`.
- ⚠️ **VERIFICATION WAS BLOCKED TWICE.** The fix could not be device-tested until the *"Create a new meal"* card was moved above the paginating list, because the card had become unreachable. Confirmed passing August 10, 2026.
- **Priority:** **P2.** **Target:** ✅ Block 2a.
- **Cross-ref:** BUG-073 · D-WS9-137.
