### BUG-080 — ⚠️ Dish IDs **CHURN** on every meal save; persisted `dishId` references are not updated 🔴 **OPEN — P2 (LATENT)**

- **Date:** August 11, 2026. **Surface:** any meal edit-save through the builder (`PATCH /me/meals/:id` with `dishes[]`).
- **The mechanism, confirmed:** `rematerializeMeal` (`mealMaterialize.ts:640-684`) reads `currentLinks`, computes **`exclusiveDishIds`** = the meal's own dishes **not shared with another meal**, then deletes their steps, ingredients, link rows **and the `Dish` rows themselves** before recreating with **fresh ids**. ⚠️ **Every builder edit-save rotates the ids of a meal's inline dishes.** Single churn path — `PATCH /me/meals/:id` (`me.ts:1340`); create and fork never delete.
- ⚠️ **VERDICT: LATENT, NOT LIVE — established by tracing every holder, not by a plausible story.**
  - `DishIngredient.dishId` — `onDelete: Cascade`, dies with the dish. **Safe.**
  - `MealDishLink.dishId` — links deleted before dishes; exclusive dishes link only to this meal. **No orphan.**
  - `GroceryListItemSource.dishId` — **persisted and FK-less**, but used only as a Map key for change-signature matching (`groceryReconcile.ts:265,296`). Never dereferenced via `findUnique`. A churned id reads as "source changed" and re-resolves.
  - `MealPlanItem.componentSelections` (`{ [dishId]: … }`) — ⚠️ **structurally cross-boundary**, but **unpopulated and unread**. The schema comment literally says *"Nothing reads it yet — migration only."*
  - `PrepWeek` structure cache, mobile React Query cache — derived/transient, self-healing.
  - `MealPlanTemplateItem` — **no dish reference** (verified negative).
- ⚠️ **TWO TRIPWIRES THAT MAKE THIS LIVE:**
  1. **The moment `componentSelections` READS are wired** (Block 3.7 Phase 2, or any feature doing `dish.findUnique(source.dishId)`), a churned id dangles.
  2. ⚠️ **AN EXCLUSIVE DISH'S `timesCooked` AND `lastUsedAt` RESET TO 0/null ON EVERY PARENT-MEAL EDIT.** Invisible today only because the Dishes "Most used" sort keys off `mealUseCount`, not `Dish.timesCooked`. ⚠️ **THIS MAKES BUG-080 A PREREQUISITE FOR WS7-11**, which wires cook-completion writes — the counters it introduces would silently reset on edit.
- **A user-visible saved dish CAN be exclusive:** a meal built with `kind:"new"` dishes creates user-owned `Dish` rows linked only to that meal. They appear in the Dishes tab and are exactly what gets deleted and recreated.
- **Priority:** **P2** while latent; **P1** the moment either tripwire fires.
- **Target:** the dish→meal endpoint block (paired with BUG-073's write half — same code path), **and gating WS7-11.**
- **Cross-ref:** BUG-073 · D-WS9-137 · WS7-11.

### BUG-083 — `PlanDetailSchema.image` is nullable but **NOT optional** — a white-screen risk the moment client and server can drift 🔴 **OPEN — P3 (LATENT)**

- **Date:** August 11, 2026. **Surface:** Plan Review (`app/plan/[id].tsx`), parse-time.
- **The shape:** BUG-076 added `image: z.string().nullable()` to `PlanDetailSchema`. **Nullable accepts `null`; it does NOT accept the key being absent.** If a server response ever omits `image`, the Zod parse **throws** and Plan Review renders nothing — a white screen, not a degraded image.
- ⚠️ **HARMLESS TODAY, AND THAT IS EXACTLY WHY IT NEEDS RECORDING.** Mobile and api-server ship from one monorepo and deploy together, so the key is always present. **WS9A moves the api-server to Google Cloud** — at which point a mobile build can outlive or outrun a server version and the two can genuinely disagree.
- **The fix, when it matters:** `.nullish()` (or `.optional().nullable()`) plus a default, so an absent key degrades to the gradient fallback instead of killing the screen.
- ⚠️ **THIS IS A CLASS, NOT ONE FIELD.** Every non-optional slot in `lib/api/**` carries the same exposure once deployment splits. ⚠️ **Chat 1's client-side Zod sweep is still PARTIAL** — the plans surface was confirmed end-to-end; **~11 other schemas were never field-diffed against their builders.** That sweep has now run out of budget twice.
- **Priority:** **P3** while monorepo-deployed; **re-rate at WS9A.**
- **Target:** **WS9A** (hosting) — or the deferred client-side Zod sweep, whichever lands first.
- **Cross-ref:** BUG-076 · BUG-068 · WS9A.

### BUG-088 — Plan Review's Breakfast/Lunch defaults sections accept typing and persist NOTHING

- **Date:** August 17, 2026 · **Surface:** `app/plan/[id].tsx` · **Found by:** CC, Block 2e Phase 0 (not sought — found while inventorying the panel).
- **Symptom:** two collapsible sections render `TextInput`s. Type a breakfast default, navigate away, it is gone. **No error, no toast, no indication anything failed.**
- **Root cause, verified:** `breakfastDraft` / `lunchDraft` are `useState("")` at `:286` / `:288` and appear at **exactly two other places — the two `value=` bindings.** Never seeded from `reviewPlan.breakfastOverrides` (which the adapter *does* populate) and never written by any mutator. A second grep found all 10 `breakfastOverrides|lunchOverrides` hits are wire-schema, adapter and type declarations — **zero consumers in `app/` or `components/`.** Data flows server → adapter → `ReviewPlan` and **terminates there.**
- ⚠️ **This is the same class as BUG-085** (profile phone field does not persist). **Two independent instances of "a field that does not persist is not a field that does nothing" — worth treating as a class, not two bugs.** If BUG-085 turns out to be the field-drop class it belongs with D-WS9-152, and this may too.
- ⚠️ **Deliberately NOT fixed in 2e, and fenced in the prompt by name** — the sections sit directly below the panel being rebuilt, so "while we're in there" was a live risk. Block 2e Part 1 hid them on composted plans (a render condition only); **the persistence path is byte-untouched.**
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN** · **Priority:** **P2** · **Target:** correctness block / **3g** · **Cross-ref:** BUG-085 · D-WS9-152 · D-WS9-159.

### BUG-093 — Prep steps drop the COMBINE/ASSEMBLE action; ingredients get prepped but never brought together

- **Date:** August 17, 2026 · **Surface:** prep-week generation → Prep & Cook · **Found by:** Hans, cooking a real plan.
- **Symptom, in Hans's words:** he measured the spices and lime juice for a carne asada marinade **but was never told to assemble them, and never told to add the olive oil.** Same for a coleslaw (cabbage shredded, red onion sliced, never combined) and pico de gallo (components prepped, never combined). **It was also unclear which vegetables belonged in which bowl.**
- ⚠️ **CRITICAL DIAGNOSTIC — THE FULL TEXT SURVIVES DOWNSTREAM, SO THIS IS NOT TRUNCATION OF THE SOURCE.** Hans's screenshot of Cook Mode's "You already prepped this" block contains the **complete** step, including *"…and 2 tablespoons olive oil to form the marinade."* Viewing Recipe Detail shows the same step as recipe step 1. **Prep is a LOSSY TRANSFORMATION of a complete source, not a truncated read of it.**
- ⚠️ **THE SHARPEST CLUE — the two surfaces do not appear to read the same list.** Hans noted cotija cheese appears in the already-prepped block but he *"doesn't remember anything about cotija cheese in the prep."* **If confirmed, this is a data-derivation bug, not a prompt-quality one — and prompt tuning would waste a cycle.**
- **Hans's own control experiment, worth preserving:** he marked all prep complete (⚠️ **a plan cannot be un-prepped once prepped**), then cloned the plan and entered Cook Mode **without** prep — which **did** instruct combining all present ingredients. So the loss is specific to the prep-sequence derivation.
- **Two candidate causes, neither confirmed:** an AI instruction issue in the prep-sequencing prompt, **or** a step-length/character cap where the model never sees the full ingredient list. **Diagnose before prescribing.**
- ⚠️ **Hans wants this settled BEFORE WS9A hosting.**
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN** · **Priority:** **P2** · **Target:** correctness block · **Cross-ref:** BUG-094.

- ⚠️ **BOTH ORIGINAL CANDIDATE CAUSES ARE DEAD.** (1) **Pre-model truncation: NOT FOUND** — the prompt hands the model a full per-step `components[].measures[]` array (`aiPrompts.ts:1369`); the only caps are a 120-step schema ceiling and `MAX_REPORTED_ISSUES = 20` on retry text. (2) ⚠️ **THE COTIJA CLUE DISSOLVED** — prep-week generation is *ingredient-row driven* (`prepWeekAggregation.ts:118`, week-wide, denylist-filtered) while Cook Mode's recap is *that one meal's `RecipeInstructionStep` rows filtered by phase* (`cookSession.ts:226`). Different sources **by design and correctly**. This also **refutes the shared-root-cause hypothesis with BUG-101.**
- ✅ **VERDICT AT PHASE 1: PLANNER-CANNOT-EMIT. VERIFIED.** `buildStepPlan` emits **exactly four step kinds and there is no fifth**: `seasonings_dry#dish#<id>` and `sauces_marinades#dish#<id>` (**dish-grouped**), `produce#<ingredientId>` and `proteins#<ingredientId>` (**one step per ingredient, NO dish grouping at all**). No assembly/combine concept exists, and `assemblePrepWeekResult` iterates `plan.steps` only — **an AI-invented step is silently discarded.** The model provably cannot add one.
- ⚠️ **A PHASE 0 CLAIM WAS REFUTED HERE:** the prep path **does** read `RecipeInstructionStep` (`stepTextByDishId → dishSteps`), and `blendSpiceDish` already ships combine wording — *"Combine these with the &lt;dish&gt; spices from your seasoning blend."* **But it only ever lands on a `sauces_marinades` step.**
- **Traced end-to-end on Hans's real plan** (`bf205267…`), *Shredded Cabbage and Radish Slaw*: recipe text reads *"Whisk together 2 tbsp lime juice, 1 tbsp olive oil, ¼ tsp salt…"*; the planner emitted `produce#20` (lime juice, **alone**), `produce#13` (cabbage, **alone**), `produce#16` (radishes, **alone**), **no `sauces_marinades` step at all** so `blendSpiceDish` never fired — and **olive oil EXCLUDED as Tier-1 denylisted** (`prepCombineEngine.ts:241`, D3 "no oil carve-out"; `isDenied` runs first). **That is exactly "never told to add the olive oil."**
- ⚠️ **COMPOUNDING DATA DEFECT, AND IT IS THE CHEAP HALF.** `assignPhase` routes by `Ingredient.category`, and **`fresh lime juice`, `apple cider vinegar` and `celery seed` are categorised `Produce`.** Were they `Pantry`, `SAUCE_NAME_HINTS` ("juice", "vinegar") would route them to `sauces_marinades`, they would group into the dish's sauce step, **and the combine wording would already fire.** ⚠️ **The one grouping mechanism that exists is being bypassed by a category mis-assignment.**
- **NOT prompt-adherence** — the combine rule is correct and has no step to attach to. ⚠️ **DO NOT COMMISSION PROMPT TUNING FOR THIS BUG.**
- ✅ **FIX RULED BY HANS (option C, August 19): data fix first, measure, then the structural step kind. See D-WS9-172 — which carries his worked example as the acceptance criterion, and it is a HIGHER BAR than one combine step.**
- **Status:** 🟡 **DIAGNOSED — fix not built** · **Priority:** **P2** · **Target:** next block, before WS9A · **Cross-ref:** D-WS9-172 · BUG-094 · BUG-101.
---

### BUG-094 — Cook Mode's "You already prepped this" block replays prep procedures verbatim; it should state OUTCOMES

- **Date:** August 17, 2026 · **Surface:** Cook Mode · **Found by:** Hans, on device (same session as BUG-093, **logged separately at his instruction**).
- **Symptom:** the block restates prep steps verbatim, **unstyled and without annotation** — hard to read, and *"out of context it reads weird."*
- **Ruled direction (Hans):** it should list **outcomes**, not procedures — *carne asada marinade · pico de gallo · street corn sauce* — or failing that, the ingredients. **The user needs to know what they should now HAVE, not to re-read how they made it.**
- **Distinct from BUG-093.** That one is *content going missing*; this is *correct content presented wrongly*. **Fixing either does not fix the other.**
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN** · **Priority:** **P3** · **Target:** correctness block · **Cross-ref:** BUG-093.

### BUG-095 — Recurring grocery items carry wrong units, and the units appear SHIFTED BY ONE relative to the items

- **Date:** August 17, 2026 · **Surface:** grocery list, recurring items · **Found by:** Hans, on device.
- **Symptom, verbatim:** `1 bunch eggs` · `1 dozen milk` · `1 bottle (1 quart) bread`.
- ⚠️ **HYPOTHESIS, UNVERIFIED — read the units against the PREVIOUS item and they line up.** Milk carries eggs' unit (`dozen`); bread carries milk's unit (`bottle (1 quart)`). **This has the signature of an off-by-one zip** — two arrays combined where one has been sorted, filtered or offset relative to the other. **It is a hypothesis, not a finding: audit to confirm before fixing** (Hans: *"agreed. audit to confirm"*).
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN** · **Priority:** **P2** · **Target:** correctness block.

- ✅ **CONFIRMED AT PHASE 0, AND THE MECHANISM IS BROADER THAN OFF-BY-ONE — IT IS AN OFF-BY-K POSITIONAL ZIP.** `generateFinalGroceryList` (`groceryListAI.ts:415-437`) zipped AI output back to source items **by array index**, and `...pack` was spread **last**, so `purchaseUnit`/`purchaseQuantity`/`purchaseDisplay` — **and the row's final `index`** — came from position, not identity. ⚠️ **The prompt EXPLICITLY PERMITS the model to reorder and merge**; the only guard was a **length** comparison. The function's docblock admitted the index reuse — it was written for *ordering*, and the purchase-pack attachment (WS7-8b B2 commit 3) later rode along on it.
- **Proven three ways against stored rows:** `Ingredient("eggs").purchaseDisplay = "1 dozen"` yet the eggs row read `1 bunch`; on list `641eb305` the **bananas** row wore **white onion's** pack; and `"1 bottle (1 quart / 32 oz)"` appears in **exactly one of 1,649** Ingredient rows — whole milk.
- ⚠️ **BLAST RADIUS WAS NEVER RECURRING ITEMS.** `aiSubset` = vague canonicals + **every** extras row + every same-canonical/different-unit pair. **The misordering had never been reported because misordering is invisible.**
- ✅ **FIXED** in `1abdc75`. Matching is now on `canonicalName` through `normalizeIngredientName`, via a **FIFO queue per name consumed in original-index order**; pack **and index** are identity-derived. **Merge case decided and stated:** a merged output pops the first queue entry, taking the **lowest original index** and that source's pack basis; **ambiguous duplicate canonicals use the same FIFO rule** — one rule covers all three cases. **Fails closed:** an unmatched output gets NULL pack fields plus a `grocery_ai_output_unmatched` warn.
- ⚠️ **TWO OF CC'S FOUR TESTS STAYED GREEN UNDER THE DELIBERATE BREAK — because the model output happened to be in input order, so position and identity agreed.** Rewritten to reorder; all four then RED, the last reproducing the bug verbatim. **A fixture that accidentally satisfies both the right and the wrong implementation is the tautology shape wearing different clothes.**
- **Blast radius swept:** `resolvedIngredientIds[idx]` at `groceryLists.ts:280` / `groceryReconcile.ts:371` are safe. `recipes.ts:140` aligns positionally too **but is contractually backed** — its prompt forbids reordering, merging and splitting. Left alone, deliberately.
- **Device-tested August 19: items 19 and 20 PASS.** · **Status:** ✅ **FIXED** (`1abdc75`).

### BUG-096 — The same ingredient appears on two grocery rows with inconsistent purchase sizes

- **Date:** August 17, 2026 · **Surface:** grocery list · **Found by:** Hans, on device.
- **Symptom:** `7 roma tomatoes (6 each)` and `4 roma tomatoes (3 each)` **on adjacent rows**, for different meals across a 3-meal plan.
- **Two defects in one row pair:** (1) the rows **should have merged before pricing** — and ⚠️ `dedupeByTitle` keys off canonical `title` **deliberately**, so a singular/plural or case difference defeats it; (2) **the same ingredient received two different purchase sizes.**
- ⚠️ **(2) IS EXPLAINED BY THE CALL SHAPE, AND THE FIX IS THE SAME AS THE COST FIX.** `grocery.gap_fill_purchase_size` fires as **16 independent single-item Haiku calls, all started in the same millisecond.** **Each call sees one item with no knowledge of the others, so nothing can make them agree.** Batching into one call makes the answers mutually consistent **and** cuts that step from **$0.0214 to ~$0.0046**. ⚠️ **Trade to measure, not assume:** the 16 calls are parallel *for latency*, so batching trades ~1s of parallel wall-clock for one sequential generation of ~613 output tokens. **Correctness argues for it regardless.**
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN** · **Priority:** **P2** · **Target:** correctness block · **Cross-ref:** BUG-095.

- ⚠️ **BOTH PREMISES IN THIS ENTRY WERE WRONG. PHASE 0 CORRECTS THEM.**
- ⚠️ **`dedupeByTitle` DOES NOT EXIST SERVER-SIDE.** Two-shaped, repo-wide: the only hits are **client-side meal-title** dedupe in `SwapMealSheet.tsx` and its test. **The real merge key is `bucketKeyOf` (`groceryList.ts:124`) = `normalizeIngredientName(canonical)|unit`**, and `groceryNormalization.ts:6` states its own limit — it lowercases, trims and strips articles but **does NOT stem, pluralize or handle synonyms.**
- ⚠️ **THE 16 PARALLEL CALLS ARE NOT THE CAUSE. REFUTED.** `fillPurchaseSizesWithWriteBack` treats a fully-populated row as a **cache hit and makes no call**, writing each answer back to the shared `Ingredient` row — so **each canonical is gap-filled once, ever.** `roma tomato` (7 each) and `roma tomatoes` (4 each) were filled at **different times from different generations** and could not have coordinated even in a batched world.
- ✅ **ROOT CAUSE: DUPLICATE SINGULAR/PLURAL `Ingredient` ROWS.** Phase 1 re-measured: **67 collision groups, all exactly 2 rows, 134 rows (8.1261%)**; 18 groups carry a pack on both sides and **10 of those disagree**; 51 have conflicting `nutritionRefPerUnit`. FKs are **exactly two** — `DishIngredient.ingredientId` (4,222 rows in collisions) and `GroceryListItem.ingredientId` (188). **2,378 of 3,556 Dish rows touch a colliding ingredient.**
- ⚠️ **THE `gap_fill` BATCHING ITEM DOES NOT FIX THIS AND WAS DROPPED FROM THE ROSTER (chat-Claude's claim, FALSIFIED).** Batching measures **−92.9147% input tokens** and **−78.7519% cost** ($0.486 lifetime — negligible) against an estimated **~1.5–2× wall clock**. ⚠️ **Prompt caching is unavailable regardless:** the prefix is **~1,140 tokens against Haiku 4.5's 4,096 minimum**, and the 16 calls launch within ~1ms so none could read another's cache. **Token hygiene only → latency program.**
- ⚠️ **THE WRITE-PATH-NORMALIZER FIX DIRECTION WAS REFUTED BY MEASUREMENT — CHAT-CLAUDE'S INSTRUCTION WAS WRONG AND CC PUSHED BACK WITH DATA.** A singularising normalizer would produce **468 collateral RENAMES against 67 real MERGES — 7 renames per merge** — and a hand-rolled rule yields `molasses→molass`, `couscous→couscou`, `asparagus→asparagu`, `cherry tomatoes→cherry tomatoe`, `haricots verts→haricots vert`; **88 of the 468 hit a mass-noun/-ss shape.** Worse, **14 of 17 plural keys in the curated `INGREDIENT_CONVERSIONS` table have no singular twin**, so singularising breaks **13 of 16 live lookups**, and `mealCreate.ts:131` throws rather than auto-creating on a miss.
- **CC's counter-proposal (⛔ NOT YET RULED):** merge the 67 pairs from a **curated fold list** (all 2-row, all enumerable), **survivor = higher `DishIngredient` count** (e.g. `yellow onion` 635 over `yellow onions` 12; `garlic cloves` 598 over `garlic clove` 48), the 10 disagreeing packs resolve to the survivor's, loser's name kept as an **alias**, and the 468 plural-only rows left untouched.
- ⚠️ **CHAT-CLAUDE'S CATCH ON THAT PROPOSAL, AND IT IS LOAD-BEARING: `ingredientResolve.ts:312` UPSERTS ON EXACT `lower+trim` AND IS NOT SHOWN TO CONSULT ALIASES.** Only `ingredientSearch.ts:48` reads aliases. **Without making the RESOLVER alias-aware, the merge is a one-time cleanup that re-accumulates** the next time a dish writes "roma tomatoes".
- ✅ **RULED AND BUILT, August 21, 2026. Hans ruled option (b), full merge, all carriers — *"build it right and get this fixed now so it's done"* — and separately ruled the resolver ALIAS-AWARE in the same block (D-WS9-176), because without it the merge is a one-time cleanup that re-accumulates.**
- ⚠️ **EVERY FIGURE IN THE PROPOSAL ABOVE WAS WRONG, AND PHASE 0 MEASURED THE CORRECTIONS.** **81 groups, not 67** — canon's fold could not see `-es`/`-ies` (`potatoes`, `tomatoes`, `radishes`, `chilies`, `strawberries`), and `bay leaf`/`bay leaves` is caught by no suffix rule at all. **162 rows, not 134. 457 plural-only rows left untouched, not 468. Nine ties, not seven. Twelve pack disagreements, not ten. Fifteen nutrition decisions — canon said 51, corrected to 6, and the real number is 15.**
- ⚠️ **FIVE ID-CARRIERS, NOT TWO OR FOUR**, and the two declared FKs behave differently: `DishIngredient.ingredientId` is `ON DELETE RESTRICT` (blocks) while `GroceryListItem.ingredientId` is `ON DELETE SET NULL` (**silently nulls — same risk class as the FK-less carriers**). The three with no FK at all: `RecipeInstructionStep.amountRefs`, `PrepStepCompletion.stepKey`, and **`PrepWeekStructure.structureJson`, which canon never listed.** Name-carriers are three, not five — `PantryStaple` and `ingredientOverrides` are empty tables — and **`Dish.substitutions` is the largest at 117 dishes and was not in canon either.**
- ⚠️ **"MERGE-FIRST GUARANTEES ONE ROW PER INGREDIENT PER DISH" IS FALSE.** 160 duplicate `(dishId, ingredientId)` groups already exist and the merge adds exactly one. Merge-first is still correct, but the reason is that tagging-first would leave BUG-121 reconciling `componentKey`/`pathKey` across two rows nothing in the carrier set can arbitrate. **The merge therefore does NOT collapse `DishIngredient` rows** — collapsing would destroy the juiced/zested distinction on legitimate duplicates.
- **THE DURABLE RECORD OF THE NUTRITION REVIEW.** The reviewed CSV is gitignored and local-only (D-WS7-219), so the six contested decisions are recorded here. **Three took the loser, three kept the survivor — not the 5/1 split CC's chat summary proposed:**

| ingredient | survivor record | loser record | landed |
|---|---|---|---|
| `apple` | 174988 — *Croissants, apple* — 254 kcal | 171688 — *Apples, raw, with skin* — 52 | **LOSER** |
| `ripe avocado` | 171705 — *Avocados, raw* — 160 | 173573 — *Oil, avocado* — 884 | **SURVIVOR** |
| `sweet potatoes` | 169303 — *Sweet potato leaves, raw* — 42 | 168482 — *Sweet potato, raw* — 86 | **LOSER** |
| `lemon` | 167746 — *Lemons, raw* — 29 | 167802 — *Lemon juice from concentrate* — 17 | **SURVIVOR** |
| `yellow potatoes` | 169764 — *french fried, frozen* — 162 | 170026 — *Potatoes, raw* — 77 | **LOSER** |
| `russet potatoes` | 170027 — *russet, flesh and skin, RAW* — 79 | 170030 — *…BAKED* — 95 | **SURVIVOR** |

- ⚠️ **CC'S CHAT SUMMARY RECOMMENDED "LOSER FOR THE FIRST FIVE" AND WAS INVERTED ON TWO — `ripe avocado` AND `lemon`, WHERE THE SURVIVOR WAS ALREADY CORRECT.** Taking the loser on avocado would have written *Oil, avocado* — BUG-032's headline P1, ~5.5× live on the macro path — straight back into the catalog five weeks after it was fixed. **Its own Phase 0 prose had both right.** The CSV itself correctly pre-filled nothing and left all six as `REVIEW`, so the contract held and the error was caught at audit. Pack decisions: `egg` → *1 dozen*, `garlic cloves` → *1 head of garlic*; the other ten defaulted to the survivor.
- **Applied:** 715 `DishIngredient` · 82 `GroceryListItem.ingredientId` · 678 `amountRefs` in 643 steps · 34 `PrepStepCompletion` (30 rekeyed, 4 deleted as duplicates-of-survivor) · 61 `structureJson` ids in 23 rows · 128 `substitutions` names in 124 dishes · 63 `displayName` · 4 `recurringGroceryItems` · 4 `recipeOverrideJson` = **1,769, matching the projection exactly.** Catalog 1,649 → 1,568 ingredients; 63 → 140 alias rows; `DishIngredient` unchanged at 34,215; grocery null-FK count **unchanged at 80**, which is the evidence the `SET NULL` path never fired.
- ✅ **DURABILITY PROVEN, NOT ASSERTED.** A live `pnpm prisma:seed` after the merge exited 0 with **zero P2002, zero losers re-created, and 81/81 merge-written aliases still resolving.** Pre-block that same command re-created three merged-away rows and then hard-failed. Second run of the merge script: zero live groups, no transaction opened, no write attempted.
- ⚠️ **WHAT THIS DOES NOT FIX, MEASURED AND DEVICE-CONFIRMED.** The 81-pair claim is **bounded to suffix-fold collisions** and is recorded that way in three places in the code. Non-suffix duplicates survive — **BUG-123**. Existing grocery lists are **not** consolidated (1,292 rows before and after); only regeneration consolidates, and until then a stale list shows two rows sharing one name. And the order-quantity line is wrong independently — **BUG-125**.
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED — six local commits, August 21, 2026, device-tested (item 2: `roma tomatoes` consolidated to a single row at 9 each, previously two rows at 6 and 3).** · **Priority:** was **P2** · **Cross-ref:** BUG-032 · BUG-095 · BUG-122 · BUG-123 · BUG-124 · BUG-125 · D-WS9-171 · D-WS9-176 · D-WS9-177 · D-WS9-178 · D-WS7-217 · D-WS7-218 · D-WS7-219.

### BUG-098 — The same meal appeared twice WITHIN a single candidate set

- **Date:** August 17, 2026 · **Surface:** plan candidate generation · **Found by:** Hans, on device.
- ⚠️ **SCOPE NARROWED DELIBERATELY — this bug is the WITHIN-SET duplicate ONLY.** Hans also reported the same meal recurring **across** regenerated sets. ⚠️ **THAT IS EXPECTED BEHAVIOUR AND MUST NOT BE "FIXED":**
  - `buildRecentRotation()` walks the user's **last 3 PLANS**, not candidate sets (D-WS9-073). **Rejected candidates were never saved as plans, so their meals never enter the rotation.** Hans's recollection was *"meals they saw"*; **the shipped rule is meals recently SERVED.**
  - It is an **AI-judged SOFT NUDGE, explicitly NOT hard exclusion** (D-WS9-073), and **D-WS9-039 rules directly against aggressive never-repeat logic**: *"the risk to avoid is not 'user sees a repeat' — it's 'user gets the best burger of their life and then never sees it again.'"*
  - ⚠️ **`wizard.surprise.generate` was DELIBERATELY left at v2 with NO rotation threaded.** Only build-plans and directed received it. **If any regenerate ran the surprise path it had no rotation by construction.**
- **The real defect:** same-dish exclusion **within** a set is a **model judgment via prompt instruction** (D-WS9-059), after the `dishFamilyKey` mechanism was abandoned. **There is no mechanism backstop — a within-set duplicate is a prompt-adherence failure with nothing to catch it.**
- **⚠️ SEPARATELY, THIS IS THE TRIGGER D-WS9-073 ASKED FOR.** That entry states in bold that **efficacy is unverified and cannot be verified yet** — at ship time Hans's account carried 24 lineage-bearing forks with `timesRecentlyServed` mostly 1, *"almost nothing for prefer-unseen to push against"* — and says **revisit with real usage before assuming this works.** Real usage now exists. **D-WS9-073 should be reopened on efficacy; it is not a bug against a mechanism never demonstrated.**
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN** · **Priority:** **P3** · **Target:** correctness block · **Cross-ref:** D-WS9-073 · D-WS9-039 · D-WS9-059.

### BUG-101 — `prep.narrate_steps` fails validation ~1 in 5 calls, each after ~50s

- **Date:** August 18, 2026 · **Surface:** `prep.narrate_steps` prompt / api-server · **Found by:** a per-`promptKey` aggregation run for an unrelated reason.
- **Symptom:** across the whole `LLMCallLog` (7,896 rows), `prep.narrate_steps` shows **18.9% validation-failure (7 of 37 calls) and 29.7% retry**, on the slowest prompt in the fleet — **median 50.7s clean, 104.3s with a retry.** Roughly one in five Prep-narration calls errors after nearly two minutes.
- ⚠️ **CONTEXT THAT MAKES THIS URGENT: Block 2e just promoted `Prep and Cook` to the visual primary on two screens.** The most emphasized action in the app sits in front of the least reliable call in the fleet.
- ⚠️ **FLEET-WIDE RATES ARE FINE — 1.3% retry, 0.1% validation-failure.** This and `parse_intent` are the outliers, not the norm. **The chat-Claude hypothesis that high retry rates were systemic and understating per-plan cost was FALSIFIED by this same query**; the three keys holding $75 of $105 total spend retry at 0.2–1.5%.
- ⚠️ **HYPOTHESIS, NOT A FINDING: BUG-093 (prep steps dropping the COMBINE action) is on this same prompt.** Two defects on one prompt may share a root cause. **Test it; do not assume it.**
- **Next step:** re-measure after BUG-100 ships — better retry feedback may move the recovery rate on its own — then diagnose. **No fix attempted.**
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN** · **Priority:** **P3 — DOWNGRADED FROM P1 at Phase 0 (below); the status field was not written back until September 13, 2026** · **Target:** the latency program (roadmap row 8a, post-launch) — what survives is v8's 67 s median with no prompt caching, not a failure rate · **Cross-ref:** BUG-093 · BUG-100 · `kiwi_latency_program.md`. **Re-open at P2 immediately if a v8 failure appears.**

- ⚠️ **PRIORITY DOWNGRADED P1 → P3 AT PHASE 0. THE HEADLINE FIGURE WAS AN ARTEFACT.** The 18.9%/29.7% rates are **all-time aggregates over a prompt versioned eight times.** By version: v5 (n=3) 66.6667% fail · v7 (n=16) 31.2500% fail · **v8, the ACTIVE version (n=7): 0 retries, 0 failures.** **All 7 failures fall between 2026-07-02 and 2026-07-04; there has been none since.**
- ✅ **THREE OF THE SEVEN ARE A TRUNCATION SIGNATURE ALREADY FIXED.** `runAICall` **accumulates** tokens across attempts, so `outputTokens: 8192` at `retryCount: 1` means **two attempts each capping at exactly 4096** — then the default `maxTokens`. Fixed as **BUG-022** in `15f2396` (2026-07-04, *after* those rows), raising the ceiling to **16,384**; peak single-attempt output since is **7,115 tokens = 43.4241% of budget.** ⚠️ **The other four have NO truncation signature and their verbatim errors are UNRECOVERABLE** — `LLMCallLog` stores only `failureReason`, and the full-path issue list goes to stdout-only `logger.warn`.
- ⚠️ **WHAT SURVIVES IS LATENCY, AND IT IS REAL:** v8 median **67,323ms**, the slowest key in the fleet, one Sonnet call carrying the whole week with **no prompt caching** (all 38 rows null/0 cache tokens; the 12,743-char body is a fixed prefix every call). **Routed to the latency program.**
- ⚠️ **CAVEAT: n=7 ON v8 IS THIN.** The downgrade rests on a clean window plus a traced-and-fixed mechanism. **If a v8 failure appears, re-open at P2 immediately.** Post-BUG-100 n=3 — **too small to attribute anything; do not extrapolate.**
- ⚠️ **THE MEASUREMENT-HYGIENE LESSON, WHICH IS THE TRANSFERABLE PART: AN ALL-TIME AGGREGATE OVER A VERSIONED PROMPT IS NOT A STATEMENT ABOUT CURRENT BEHAVIOUR.** This bug was minted P1 and drove roster order for two days on a number that described July. **Break any prompt-level rate out by `promptVersion` before acting on it.** Encoded as **§27.5**.
- ⚠️ **STILL A TRUE OUTLIER, AND NOT THIS ONE: `wizard.directed.parse_intent` — 26 calls, 14 retries (53.8%), 3 failures**, even after BUG-099/100 shipped. Fleet-wide is 1.3%.

### BUG-102 — tapping a featured plan lands on a retired review screen

- **Date:** August 18, 2026 · **Surface:** Home featured-plans rail → plan review route · **Found by:** Hans, on device.
- **Symptom:** tapping a featured plan card opens *"a weird review screen"* rather than the Plan Review draft state. Hans: *"it should go to a screen like the plan review when you go to 'view and save' and it's not saved (still draft) and the user can save from there. I think this is an old screen we just never retired."*
- ⚠️ **THE DIAGNOSIS IS HANS'S HYPOTHESIS AND IS UNVERIFIED.** A dead-screen-never-retired reading is plausible and matches this arc's pattern, but **nothing has traced the route.** Phase 0 must establish what the tap targets before anything is deleted — this project has already caught one name-matching deletion trap (`changeRecipeForPlanItem`).
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN** · **Priority:** **P2** · **Target:** correctness block.

- ⚠️ **HANS'S DIAGNOSIS AND CHAT-CLAUDE'S BOTH REFUTED AT PHASE 0. THE FEATURED TAP DOES NOT NAVIGATE AT ALL.** `app/(tabs)/index.tsx:436-443` calls `preview.open(item.id)` — a `useState` holding a `templateId` — rendering **`components/PlanPreviewModal.tsx`**, a `<Modal>` bottom sheet. **That file is LIVE:** created `1b4bae8`, last touched `3737353` (WS9 3f-4d Part 1), **two current consumers.** Plan Review is reached **only after commit** — which is what reads as "weird."
- ⚠️ **THE GENUINELY RETIRED SCREEN IS A DIFFERENT FILE AND IS NOT WHAT THE TAP HITS.** `app/wizard-plan-details.tsx` has **zero navigation call sites**; its default export is already a `<Redirect>`. **The single apparent nav hit is a COMMENT** — the comment-is-not-a-call-site trap, caught. **Nothing was deleted.**
- ⚠️ **NEITHER RULING IS CHEAP.** Draft mode carries **the whole payload in the `expanded` route param** (`plan/[id].tsx:169-184`; `usePlan("")` is disabled there). The featured tap holds only a `templateId` and a `TemplateDetail` shape, so routing it into Plan Review draft needs **a third adapter** or a new read-only template state.
- **Open question for Hans:** *"the modal is the wrong destination"* (needs the adapter) or *"the modal is fine but reads as a review screen"* (a presentation fix)?
- ⚠️ **Related, and already fixed:** the route behind this modal (`GET /plans/templates/:id`) was **opening a write transaction on a read path** — see BUG-116.

### BUG-103 — Neon cold start surfaces to the user as a raw 500

- **Date:** August 18, 2026 · **Surface:** `artifacts/api-server/src/lib/planDates.ts` → `routes/home.ts` · **Found by:** Hans, from a pasted server log.
- **Symptom:** after a few idle hours, `GET /api/home` failed with Prisma **`P1001 — can't reach database server`** against the Neon endpoint, 2,284ms, returned as a generic **500**. No warm-up, no retry, no useful message.
- **Related, same family:** a `GET /home` after 20 idle minutes took 2.4s; Hans has separately observed multi-minute grocery latency plausibly explained by a suspended compute stacked on an already-slow path. ⚠️ **Also unresolved from an earlier capture: a 45s `POST /save`, 26.8s of it inside a `$transaction`** — not AI, and **not yet checked for on other write paths.**
- ⚠️ **THIS IS A WS9A GATING ITEM.** A cold-start 500 on the first screen of the app is a first-impression failure, and hosting decisions should account for it rather than inherit it.
- ⚠️ **`/readyz` IS NOT THE INSTRUMENT FOR THIS — recorded September 15, 2026 so the same reading is not taken as reassurance twice.** Hans ran `GET /api/readyz` against Cloud Run after letting Neon sit overnight and got `{"status":"ready","latencyMs":4}`. **4 ms is below the fastest real Neon round-trip this project has measured** (bare `SELECT 1`, 40 iterations, min 22.93 ms from Hans's laptop; co-located was REASONED at 10–20 ms and never measured), **so that call did not make a cold database round-trip — either Neon had not suspended, or `/readyz` does not probe it on that path.** Canon already carried the same caution: *"`/readyz`'s 2 s bound has never met a real cold start."* ✅ **THE HONEST TEST when someone wants one: leave it genuinely idle, then load a REAL authenticated screen (`GET /api/home`) and watch for the multi-second wait or the 500 — this entry's own symptom. A green `/readyz` gives false comfort about exactly this bug.** 🔵 **Hans, September 15: not chasing it for now — *"If I can find a sleeping neon someday I'll find a log and report it. for now, I'm working consistently enough it's not sleeping often."* Unscheduled, still WS9A-gating.**
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN** · **Priority:** **P2** · **Target:** correctness block / WS9A.

- ⚠️ **PHASE 0 WIDENED THIS: THERE WAS NO TERMINAL EXPRESS ERROR-HANDLING MIDDLEWARE ANYWHERE.** Verified two-shaped: **zero 4-arg handlers**; `app.ts`'s chain was `pinoHttp → cors → json → urlencoded → /api router` with **nothing after**, so **any throw outside a route's own try/catch returned an HTML body — with a stack trace in non-production — to a JSON client.** `P1001` appeared **nowhere**; `prisma.ts` was a bare singleton with no boot `$connect()`.
- ✅ **THE 45s `POST /save` IS TRACED AND IS NOT WHAT IT LOOKED LIKE.** The 26.8s inside the transaction is **Pass-2 sequential writes** (every `Meal`, `Dish`, `DishIngredient`, `MealDishLink`, `RecipeInstructionStep` for a full week on one pooled connection). **No AI call is inside it** — `wizardFinalize` runs the Sonnet call *before* `__txStart`, by design, which is the remaining ~18s.
- ✅ **THE FULL 22-SITE `$transaction` SWEEP CAME BACK CLEAN** — no AI call, network call or unbounded loop in any of them. Two exceptions logged as **BUG-116**.
- ✅ **FIXED** in `4bd8a39`. New `errorHandler.ts` mounted last: always JSON, **never a message or stack to the client**, honours a 4xx so body-parser's 400 does not become a 500, delegates when `headersSent`. Cold-DB classification returns **503 + `Retry-After: 2` + `{ code: "db_unavailable", retryable: true }`** for `PrismaClientInitializationError` and `P1001`/`P1002`/`P1017`. ⚠️ **`P2002` deliberately does NOT classify — selling a constraint violation as retryable is worse than a 500.** Boot `$connect()` in `index.ts`, not awaited, not fatal. **No Prisma retry wrapper**, as instructed.
- ⚠️ **THE HAZARD THIS TASK PRODUCED IS WORTH MORE THAN THE FIX.** CC's first deliberate break **silently no-op'd because `app.ts` is CRLF and the edit used `\n` anchors** — the suite stayed green and the obvious reading was *"nothing depends on this."* Caught only by grepping the file back. **A no-op break and a tautological assertion are indistinguishable from test output.** Encoded as **§27.5**.
- ⚠️ **REQUEST LOGGING IS CONSOLE-ONLY AND NOT PERSISTED** — HTTP timings for any past incident are gone. This is why `UserActivity` had to carry the BUG-104 reconstruction.
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED** (`4bd8a39`) · **device item 25 NOT RUN** (cold DB, hard to trigger; rides).

### BUG-104 — plan-item day assignment reverts; stale responses land out of order

- **Date:** August 19, 2026 · **Surface:** `app/plan/[id].tsx` day pills → `PATCH /api/plans/{id}/items/{itemId}` · **Found by:** Hans, on device, with a server log.
- **Symptom:** Hans cleared today's meal, the UI showed it correctly, then Home still displayed the meal as today's; navigating back to the plan showed it re-assigned to today. He moved it to tomorrow, returned to Home, and the same stale meal displayed. **It eventually settled after repeated attempts.**
- ⚠️ **THE LOG SHOWS THE SHAPE CLEARLY: two PATCHes to the SAME plan item 54ms apart** (`19:58:43.271` and `19:58:43.325`), and responses completing badly out of order — one `GET /api/plans/{id}` took **3,037ms and resolved after three later-issued requests had already returned.** Every request returned 200; **nothing failed, which is why no error surfaced.**
- ⚠️ **TWO MECHANISMS FIT AND NEITHER IS VERIFIED:** (1) a slow in-flight GET resolving after a mutation and overwriting fresh state with stale data; (2) a `304` served against a stale ETag on the `/api/home` and `/api/plans` refetches, of which the log shows many. **Do not build on either until one is demonstrated.**
- **Why it matters more than it looks:** this is **data correctness, not polish.** A day assignment that silently reverts undermines the plan the whole product is organised around, and it is invisible — no error, no spinner, no indication anything went wrong.
- *(The original `Status: OPEN · P1` line that sat here was removed September 13, 2026 — the entry carried two Status fields and the stale one was read first by a grep. The sole status claimant is the field at the end of the entry.)*

- ✅ **MECHANISM SETTLED AT PHASE 0 AND FIXED AT PHASE 1. M2 REFUTED, M1 DEMONSTRATED, AND THE DEFECT WAS WORSE THAN "THE UI REVERTS."**
- ⚠️ **M2 (STALE 304) REFUTED AT THE CODE LEVEL.** Express 5.2.1 sets `etag: weak` at init and **no API route set any `Cache-Control`**, but `response.js:191-199` computes the ETag **from the current body** and only then tests `req.fresh` — **so a 304 can only mean the fresh body is byte-identical to what the client holds.** Client-side, two-shaped search returned **zero** conditional-request code (all 6 text hits were variables named `cuisineTag`/`visibleTags`).
- ✅ **M1: THE OPTIMISM WAS NOT IN THE QUERY CACHE, WHICH IS WHY NOTHING GUARDED IT.** React Query guards cache-vs-cache races; **component-local state is unguarded.** `plan/[id].tsx:216-228` re-seeded the whole `reviewPlan` from `planQuery.data` on every identity change, and **no plan mutation called `cancelQueries`** — used **exactly twice app-wide** before this fix.
- ⚠️ **THE PART THAT MADE THIS DATA CORRUPTION, AND IT TOOK BOTH PHASE 0s TO SEE.** The day-pill handler computed its payload **from the clobbered mirror**, so a stale mirror produced a **wrong write.** `UserActivity` shows **four A→B→A oscillations**, each reverting PATCH's `from` matching the previous `to` — **the client sent the old day back as a real, server-honoured write. The server reverted nothing.**
- ✅ **FIXED** across `83fb435` (server: `no-store` on `/api`, SSE verified unaffected) and **`2aae661`** (client). Device-tested: **items 1, 2, 5, 6 PASS.**
- ⚠️ **THE FINDING THAT CHANGED THE DESIGN, AND THE MOST TRANSFERABLE THING IN THIS BLOCK: `cancelQueries` ALONE DOES NOT FIX THIS.** Cancelling at write-start kills only the GET in flight *at that moment* — **each write's own success invalidation then starts a FRESH refetch** that lands after the next optimistic update and clobbers it. **The race survives the obvious fix.** CC found this by testing its own fix, not by reasoning about it.
- **The actual mechanism: a write-depth counter** (`beginPlanWrite`/`endPlanWrite`/`invalidatePlanCaches`), so `handleMutationResult` **defers while depth > 0** and the runner invalidates **once when the burst drains.** ⚠️ Non-runner callers keep immediate behaviour — **two invalidation regimes now coexist in one context.** Not in the prompt; disclosed. ⚠️ **OPEN QUESTION FOR THE NEXT CHAT: is the decrement in a `finally`? A leaked depth counter would defer plan invalidation FOREVER, silently, looking exactly like a caching bug.**
- **Shape (i) chosen — optimism in the query cache**, per "build it right". New `lib/plans/planDetailOptimistic.ts`, `lib/plans/planWriteRunner.ts`, `hooks/usePlanWrite.ts`, mirroring `usePrepStepToggle`'s **pure-runner + thin-wrapper split** rather than copying the hook. Because `planDetailToReviewPlan` derives `dayStrip` from `assignedDayOfWeek`, **a correct cache ⇒ a correct day strip ⇒ a correct next write.**
- **Day-pill guard: a 350ms timestamp lockout, NOT an in-flight latch** — `onAssignDay` returns `void`, so a latch awaiting completion would **wedge the pills permanently on a failed-and-rolled-back write.** Device verdict (item 4): *"seems fine and I barely notice any delay."*
- ⚠️ **THE `reviewPlan` MIRROR STILL EXISTS AND CC DECLINED TO REPORT THIS CLOSED ON IT.** Three sites re-seed or write it: the `:242` wholesale `useEffect` (**now harmless because the cache it reads is correct — but ungated**), the `:268` `?addMealId=` deep-link injection (**still a raw local write**), and the draft-mode initializer. **The mirror is now a pure derivation, which IS the fix; removing it is a separate change.**
- ⚠️ **THE 54ms DOUBLE-PATCH WAS NEVER SETTLED AND THE DEVICE PASS DID NOT SETTLE IT** (item 7). ⚠️ **The server log from the PASSING run shows the race window is still WIDE: `PATCH` 3,780ms, and `GET /api/plans/{id}` 2,475ms resolving LAST behind three later requests.** The guard holds — **BUG-115 is what would make the window narrow enough to stop relying on it.**
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED** (`83fb435` + `2aae661`) · **the mirror and the double-emit cause remain open** · **Cross-ref:** BUG-115 · BUG-119 · BUG-109.

### BUG-105 — `Start Cooking` asks whether the meal was prepped when the plan already knows

- **Date:** August 19, 2026 · **Surface:** Home this-week card → cook flow · **Found by:** Hans, on device.
- **Symptom:** tapping `Start Cooking` on the meal-today card prompts the user to choose whether they prepped, before entering cook mode. Hans: *"I think it has the context to know the meals are already prepped in the plan. and because they're prepped it goes right to cook mode and skips prep."*
- ⚠️ **THE DEFECT CLASS IS THE INTERESTING PART: the app asking for state it already holds.** That is worse than a missing feature — it tells the user their earlier work wasn't recorded. Same family as the silent no-op surfaces this arc has been closing.
- **Unverified:** whether prep state is actually readable at that call site. **Phase 0 establishes it before any change.**
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN** · **Priority:** **P3** · **Target:** correctness block.

- ✅ **VERDICT: READABLE TODAY — not a wire gap, not a missing fetch.** `heroModel.kind === "today"` already carries `planId` and `planItemId` (`lib/home/heroState.ts:58-65`), and **the very next handler in the same file spends both** (`index.tsx:222-232`, under a comment reading *"The ids were already plumbed and simply never spent."*). `isPrepped` is on the wire and typed.
- ✅ **FIXED** in `ffb6603`. ⚠️ **Reuse win:** routed through **`buildCookSessionParams`** — already used by `meal/[id].tsx`, already tested, its *"never fabricate a `planItemId`"* rule pinned in `cookSession.test.ts:49-66` — instead of a hand-rolled literal, **which would have been a fourth copy of the shape.**
- **All 6 cook-session entry points swept; Home was the ONLY defect and the legitimate ones were NOT "fixed"** (`meals.tsx:166,169` and `dish/[id].tsx:96` pass `{mealId}`/`{dishId}` correctly — no plan context exists there).
- **Destination traced, not assumed:** `isPrepped: true` → gate `"prepped"` → no question → `skipPrep` drops prep steps → **opens on the first NON-prep step** with the skip bar and mise-en-place recap. Device-tested: **item 15 PASS.**
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED** (`ffb6603`).

### BUG-110 — `["plans", planId]` matched no query; recalculated macros never refetched

- **Date:** August 19, 2026 · **Surface:** `contexts/AppContext.ts:395`, `:419` · **Found by:** CC, Phase 0 (mobile).
- **Symptom:** the key matched **nothing** — the live keys are `["plans","detail",id]`, `["plans","list",filter]`, `["plans","template",id]`, and a plan id at index 1 matches none. Harmless at `:419` (the next line's `["plans"]` covers it); ⚠️ **NOT harmless at `:395`, where it was `dispatchRecalcMacros`'s ONLY invalidation** — after an AI macro recalc, Plan Review never refetched and stale `macroDailyAverage` rendered until `staleTime` expired.
- ✅ **FIXED** in `4df596d`. **It was the ONLY dead key in the entire client** — every invalidation/`setQueryData`/`removeQueries` key checked against all **17 live `useQuery` keys**. `:395` corrected; **`:419` REMOVED rather than corrected**, since a corrected duplicate is dead weight.
- ⚠️ **THE TEST IS SHAPED TO DISTINGUISH A DEAD KEY FROM A SLOW NETWORK — which is exactly why the defect survived.** Subscribed `QueryObserver` + counting `queryFn`, the recalc POST held open so its invalidation is separable in time, then sample, release, assert one further fetch.
- ⚠️ `AppContext.ts` **is CRLF**; CC used CRLF-aware anchors and **grep-verified the mutation landed** — the §27.5 hazard, caught within a turn of being written down.
- **Device-tested: item 17 PASS.** · **Status:** ✅ **FIXED** (`4df596d`).

### BUG-111 — a successful grocery generate invalidated nothing

- **Date:** August 19, 2026 · **Surface:** `app/(tabs)/index.tsx:256`, `app/plan/[id].tsx:369` · **Found by:** CC, Phase 0 (mobile).
- **Symptom:** both call sites navigated on success **without touching `["groceries"]` or `["home"]`**, and the Groceries tab's focus refetch is gated on `isStale` against a 60s `staleTime` — **so a user who generated a list and switched tabs within 60 seconds did not see it.**
- ✅ **FIXED** in `56c3e37`, **gated on `dispatchGenerateResult` returning `"navigate"`** so an error alert costs no refetches — reusing the existing return value rather than adding a sink parameter to a tested pure module for two callers.
- **Reuse check reported and deliberately NOT acted on:** the deleted `useGroceryGeneration` held exactly this plus the overlay. **Not reinstated — the overlay is D-WS9-170's ruling, and building half of it now would build it twice.**

### BUG-115 — `GET /plans/:id` loads the same plan twice, the second time through a 5-level nested include

- **Date:** August 19, 2026 · **Surface:** `api-server/src/routes/plans.ts:327`, `lib/prepWeekAggregation.ts:118` · **Found by:** CC, Phase 0 (api-server).
- **Symptom:** **9 sequential round trips, no caching layer.** Step 1 loads the plan with template and items; **step 4a re-reads the same plan** through `items → meal → dishLinks → dish → dishIngredients → ingredient` **purely to derive `isPrepped`/`prepStatus`**.
- ⚠️ **THIS IS THE SOURCE OF BUG-104's RACE WINDOW, AND THE WINDOW IS STILL OPEN.** The device pass that certified BUG-104 fixed shows `GET /api/plans/{id}` at **2,475ms resolving LAST behind three later requests**, and a `PATCH` at **3,780ms**. It does **not** require a cold Neon — 9 serialized round trips on one pooled connection, one a five-level include, is sufficient. (Measured: first query 1,188ms, second 31ms — so the 2.4s `/home` after idle is **connection cost**, not query shape.)
- ⚠️ **FIXING THE RACE IS THE CORRECTNESS FIX; SHRINKING THIS WINDOW IS WHAT MAKES IT RARE.** BUG-119 may disappear for free with it.
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN** · **Priority:** **P3** · **Target:** latency program / WS9A · **Cross-ref:** BUG-104 · BUG-119 · BUG-103.

### BUG-116 — a plan-scale write transaction on the default budget, and a write transaction on a read path

- **Date:** August 19, 2026 · **Surface:** `api-server/src/lib/groceryReconcile.ts:413`; `routes/plans.ts:557` · **Found by:** CC, Phase 0, during the 22-site `$transaction` sweep.
- ✅ **FIXED** in `184c79f`. Reconcile budget → `{ timeout: 15000 }`, matching the `me.ts` precedent rather than wizard's 60s (four bulk statements, no AI, no per-row loop — same weight class as `materializeMeal`). Template GET: the read now stands alone and the activity emit is fire-and-forget, **threading the router's injected prisma as `tx`** — `emitActivity` otherwise falls back to the module singleton and would bypass DI to hit the real DB from tests.
- ⚠️ **CORRECTION TO THIS ENTRY'S OWN PREMISE:** `groceryLists.ts:264` was cited as an already-raised precedent. **It still runs on the default budget too.** Out of scope, reported, **still open.**
- ⚠️ **CC HAD TO STRENGTHEN TWO TEST FAKES BEFORE THE GUARDS COULD FAIL AT ALL** — the grocery stub now records `$transaction` options, and the C3 stub now models **Postgres abort semantics** (a statement erroring inside a tx poisons the commit). Without the second, *"the read survives an activity failure"* could not distinguish the two designs, because `emitActivity` swallows the throw itself. ⚠️ **A fake too weak to express the failure makes a guard untestable — a finding, not a licence to skip the guard.**
- **Device-tested: item 24 PASS.** · **Status:** ✅ **FIXED** (`184c79f`) · **Cross-ref:** BUG-102 · BUG-103.

### BUG-117 — grocery-list item editing is hard to reach, sticky once open, and cannot edit the item name

- **Date:** August 19, 2026 · **Surface:** `app/grocery-list/[id].tsx` item row editor · **Found by:** Hans, device test item 18.
- **Three defects, one surface.** (1) ⚠️ **The edit affordance is undiscoverable** — reached by tapping the card, no visible control. Hans's suggestion: **a pencil icon beneath the `×` remove control.** (2) ⚠️ **The editor is STICKY** — on the first attempt the cursor could not be dismissed; on the second, tapping away jumped straight into editing an adjacent row. (3) **The item NAME is not editable at all** — only quantity and unit. Repro: a recurring `1 bottle (1 quart) milk` could not be changed to `1 gallon`, and the unit control offered `each` rather than a real unit set.
- ✅ **SCOPE RULED AT THE SAME MOMENT (D-WS9-171): grocery-list edits are LIST-LOCAL. No sync back to any recipe or `Ingredient` row, and a later reconcile sweep overwriting a user edit is ACCEPTED.** That ruling is what makes this a contained UI fix rather than a data-model question.
- ⚠️ **NOTE THE INTERACTION WITH BUG-096: `1 bottle (1 quart) milk` is a CORRECT pack for milk** — it is the one Ingredient row that string comes from. **This is not the unit-shift bug returning.** The complaint is that the user cannot override it.
- 🟡 **ALL THREE FIXED September 8, 2026 (Lane B, committed locally, DEVICE-UNPROVEN). No server contract was needed.** Pencil affordance beneath the `×` on every editable row (the old route in — tapping the `(need)` parenthetical — still works, and **a row with no need previously had no way in at all**); item NAME now editable and persisted through `displayName`, which the PATCH contract already accepted; one-tap canonical unit chips added, free text retained.
- 🔴 **DEFECT (2) WAS MISDIAGNOSED IN THIS ENTRY FOR THREE WEEKS, AND THE TRUTH IS WORSE THAN WHAT WAS FILED.** It was written up as a sticky cursor that "jumped into an adjacent row." **There was no commit-on-leave at all** — no `onBlur` commit, no Done control, and the affordance called `enterQuantityEdit` directly, bypassing the commit-then-return path. **So tapping away was SILENTLY DISCARDING THE EDIT.** ⚠️ **It was quiet data loss wearing a UI complaint, and the device report that produced it could not have known.** ✅ **Fixed with an explicit Done control plus `enterQuantityEdit` committing any open edit on a different row first.**
- 🔴 **AND IT IS THE SAME MECHANISM AS BUG-236, IN A SECOND FILE: `editingItemId` carried the identical `useState`-closure guard that cannot close a same-tick double-fire.** Backed by `editingItemIdRef`, cleared synchronously, every state write paired to a ref write. **Fixed the same way rather than twice differently.** ⚠️ **TWO INDEPENDENT INSTANCES MAKES THIS A MEASURED CLASS, NOT A ONE-FILE MISTAKE — a sweep for other `useState` guards against same-tick double-fire is owed, and is carried without an ID until the concurrent lane reports.**
- ✅ **ONE SEMANTIC RULED IN PASSING (chat-Claude, September 8): a rename now moves `userResolvedTo` as well as `name`.** The row renders `userResolvedTo ?? name`, so on a row carrying a clarify projection a rename to `name` alone would have been **invisible — the projection keeps winning and the user's typing looks like it did not take.** 🔴 **A user's explicit edit beats an AI projection. This is a consequence of D-WS9-171 (edits are LIST-LOCAL), not a new product decision, so it needed no separate ruling.**
- ⚠️ **UNTESTED AND UNSEEN.** `app/**` is outside the test glob (D-WS9-164) and CC never launched the app. **The pencil's position relative to the `×` is a thumb-safety question only a device can answer — a row that deletes when the user meant to edit is worse than no pencil.**
- ✅ **DEVICE-PASSED, Android, September 8, 2026 — Hans on the affordance, verbatim: *"works great and I like it. I like that you can select the units."*** ✅ **Repeat editing of one row works; rename and unit change both persist across leaving and re-entering the list.**
- 🟠 **THREE THREADS THE PASS LEFT OPEN, NONE OF THEM REGRESSIONS:**
  1. 🔴 **THE UNIT CHIPS ARE STRETCHED TO ~5× THEIR NEEDED HEIGHT, AND THE SYMPTOM NAMES THE CAUSE.** Screenshot, Android: each chip renders as a **tall empty box roughly 180 px high with its label at the top**, where a chip should be a short pill. **That is cross-axis stretch — a row-direction flex container leaving `alignItems` at its default `stretch` inside a tall parent.** ✅ **The fix is `alignItems: 'flex-start'` on the chip row (or an explicit chip height); it is not a padding or font problem.**
     - ✅ **DIAGNOSIS CONFIRMED IN THE CODE, NOT ADOPTED ON MY SAY-SO, AND FIXED IN `ec15ca9`:** `s.unitChipRow` is the `contentContainerStyle` of a `flexDirection: "row"` container and declares no `alignItems`, so it takes the flex default `stretch`; `s.unitChip` declares `paddingVertical` and no height. **`alignItems: "flex-start"` plus `flexGrow: 0`.**
     - 🔴 **THE STRIP ALWAYS SCROLLED — the missing thing was the AFFORDANCE, not the scrolling.** `showsHorizontalScrollIndicator={false}` had been set, so it scrolled silently and read as a list that simply clips `ml`. **Indicator now shown.** ⚠️ **A control that works but shows no sign of working is indistinguishable from one that is broken.**
     - 🔴 **AND LOOKING AT THE `ounce`/`oz` MISMATCH TURNED UP THE REAL DEFECT UNDERNEATH IT: the highlight test is `editUnit.trim().toLowerCase() === u`, so a row whose unit reads `ounce` HIGHLIGHTS NO CHIP AT ALL.** **The strip looks like nothing is selected, on exactly the rows a user most wants to change.**
     - ✅ **RULED (chat-Claude, September 9): normalize the HIGHLIGHT COMPARISON only. Do not rewrite stored data and do not rewrite the box under the user's cursor.** 🔴 **Both alternatives are DECLINED and the reason is durable: the app must not silently rewrite what a user typed, and a field that rewrites itself mid-edit is worse than a spelling mismatch.** ⚠️ **That the row says `ounce` while the chip says `oz` is a DATA-SIDE canonicalisation question belonging to the unit work — do not solve it in the editor.**
     - ✅ **The strip stays SCROLLING rather than wrapping** — a 3–4 row chip block inside a list row is heavier than a scroller with a visible indicator.
     - 🔴 **THE CHIP STRIP IS RETIRED BY RULING (Hans, September 9): *"do we really need those? can we just have it be text with validation that it's an actual unit? I thought that was a nice little UI feature, but it's going to be more work than it's worth. users can edit and type in the unit field."*** ✅ **A free-text unit field replaces it. That deletes the highlight problem, the 20-chip vocabulary, the off-screen-chip question and the horizontal strip in one move.**
     - ⚠️ **AND THE MEASUREMENT THAT JUSTIFIES RETIRING IT: 55 of 110 reachable unit spellings match NO chip** — `packet · carton · loaf · block · wedge · pint · quart · gallon · stick · ear · slice · piece · stalk · sprig · fillet · breast · thigh · bulb · sheet · strip · cube · leaf · wrap · roll · pinch · dash · to taste · large · medium · small` and their plurals. **`COUNT_NOUN_PLURALS` alone holds 32 container nouns the strip never showed.** 🔴 **The chip set was never going to catch up with the live vocabulary.**
     - 🔴 **VALIDATION MUST NOT BECOME A GATE. Accept anything; normalise on read for the arithmetic.** **A unit the system cannot relate already degrades gracefully — BUG-125's path echoes the stored pack rather than guessing — so rejecting a user's word buys nothing and blocks a real one.** ⚠️ **`tablespoon` was the reported symptom and it was NEVER the bug: `normalizeUnitToken("tablespoon")` already folds to `tbsp`. The likeliest explanation for the sighting is that `tbsp` is the 7th chip in a scrolling strip and was simply off-screen — one more argument for the strip going away.**
     - ⚠️ **A SEPARATE FINDING THAT SURVIVES THE RETIREMENT, because the arithmetic still needs it: the alias table SINGULARISES INCONSISTENTLY.** `slices → slice` folds; `pieces`, `stalks`, `sprigs`, `fillets`, `breasts`, `thighs`, `bulbs`, `sheets`, `strips`, `cubes`, `leaves`, `wraps`, `rolls` do not. **A plural row misses where its singular would hit.**
     - 🔴 **THE TAP-OUTSIDE-TO-CLOSE ENHANCEMENT IS CUT (chat-Claude, September 9) AFTER THREE FAILED ATTEMPTS, EACH A DIFFERENT REGRESSION.** `e7dc399` (wrapping `Pressable`) closed the editor and killed the next tap on any lower row; `6518530` (floating sibling) dismissed the keyboard but left the editor open and still killed the next tap; both cleared on a small scroll. **Hans's third report:** *"the row I was editing stays in edit mode. if I click edit or 'x' it no-ops, but if I scroll a little it allows me to edit."* ✅ **The target state is the one that device-passed on `452fa4e`: Done closes, tapping another row commits-then-opens, nothing else.** ⚠️ **Dismissal gets decided ONCE, on BUG-240's redesigned editor — not patched a fourth time on this one.**
     - ✅ **REMOVED as `420f5fa` (LOCAL) — a pure 39-line deletion in `app/grocery-list/[id].tsx`, zero insertions; `SessionGate`, the BUG-238 timing test and `profile.tsx` byte-identical to `6518530`.** ⚠️ **CC corrected the prompt's "exactly two dismissal paths": there are FOUR, all byte-identical to the device-passed `452fa4e` — Done, the keyboard's done key on each input, tapping another row, and opening another row's editor via the pencil. CC refused to trim to two because that would be a behaviour change inside a removal, and it was right.**
     - 🔴 **THE HYPOTHESIS THAT SURVIVES THE REMOVAL, CC's, and it is the thing most likely to surprise the next device pass: in attempt 3 the editor STAYED OPEN when the next tap died — so the tap-catcher was still mounted and cannot have been the cause of the dead tap by unmounting.** **The dead tap may be a consequence of the EDITOR's LAYOUT COLLAPSE ITSELF — rows shift up, hit-rects go stale — and may predate all three attempts.** ⚠️ **If a tap still dies after `420f5fa` when the editor is closed by Done, that is a SEPARATE pre-existing bug and needs its own ID. The `452fa4e` pass never tested "close via Done → tap a lower row" specifically.**
     - ✅ **HIGHLIGHT FIXED September 9, LOCAL: `editUnit.trim().toLowerCase() === u` → `normalizeUnitToken(editUnit) === u`.** **One expression inside a render-time boolean; nothing is read back, rewritten, normalised or persisted, and the text box still shows exactly what the user typed.** ✅ **The right-hand side needs no folding — `GROCERY_UNIT_OPTIONS` entries are already canonical by an existing guard.**
     - ✅ **DEVICE-PASSED September 9, Android, all three checks: the chips read as chips, the scroll is now visible, and the highlight defect REPRODUCED EXACTLY AS PREDICTED — Hans: *"only highlighted on Each."*** 🔴 **`each` is the one unit whose stored spelling matches its chip label character-for-character, which is the cleanest possible confirmation that the comparison is the defect and not the data.**
  2. ✅ **RESOLVED ON RE-TEST, September 8, 2026: the first edit DOES survive when the user taps another row.** **The silent-discard defect is fixed and device-proven** — the question was asked again after the first pass answered a different one. 🟠 **What remains is an ENHANCEMENT, not a defect: Hans wants a tap anywhere outside a text box or editable field to EXIT edit mode.** Today the editor commits but stays open. **Folds naturally into BUG-240's editor work — same surface.**
  3. 🔴 **THE EDITOR EDITS THE *NEED*; THE ROW DISPLAYS THE *PURCHASE*. THEY ARE DIFFERENT QUANTITIES AND ONLY ONE OF THEM IS EDITABLE — SPUN OUT AS BUG-240.** Screenshot: the row header reads `2 containers (5 oz) fresh baby spinach` while the open editor reads `10` / `ounce`. **Hans wanted `1 container` and there is no control that expresses it**, because the pack is DERIVED from the need and the chips offer only measurement units. ⚠️ **On a grocery list the quantity the user wants to control is what they BUY.**
     - ⚠️ **NOTE FOR BUG-147, AND DO NOT MIS-READ IT: this particular row is arithmetically CORRECT — 2 × 5 oz covers a 10 oz need.** CC's census lists a `fresh baby spinach` row as a genuine under-order; **that is a different list instance, not this one, and this screenshot neither confirms nor refutes the census.**

Thread 2's dismissal question is RULED: the keyboard's done key and the back gesture, nothing else built. ✅ **BACK GESTURE LANDED `296e5d3` (mobile lane, September 10, +38/−0) — DEVICE-PROVEN September 10 night on `d002962` (grocery pass item 9); its first version crashed every list open (a hook below the early returns, fixed `e644c6c`): Android back while an editor is open COMMITS (never discards — defect 2 by another door) and returns `true` so the screen does not pop; `BackHandler` subscribed only while editing; iOS untouched (the keyboard's done key remains, the stack's swipe-back is not intercepted).** ⚠️ **`app/**` is outside the test glob and `BackHandler` is native — nothing but the device proves it.** The dead tap that survived `420f5fa` is BUG-247.** · **Priority:** **P2** · **Target:** the BUG-240 client block · **Cross-ref:** D-WS9-171 · BUG-096 · BUG-095 · **BUG-236** · **BUG-147** (thread 3) · BUG-141 · **BUG-240** · **BUG-247**.

### BUG-121 — dual-path recipes render BOTH paths: the catalog tells you to make the sauce and then to open the jar

- **Date:** August 21, 2026 · **Surface:** every step- and ingredient-consuming surface (Cook Mode, meal detail recipe, prep-week assembly, `cookingScheduler`, nutrition, grocery list) · **Found by:** Hans, cooking real meals on device.
- **Symptom, as the user experiences it:** *"Cheesy Taco Bell-Style Ground Beef Tacos"* renders a from-scratch nacho cheese sauce (shred cheddar → roux → milk → melt) **and then** *"Empty the 15-ounce can of nacho cheese sauce."* Second instance: **Caprese Chicken** prep says measure **3 tbsp balsamic glaze** while the recipe simmers **3 tbsp balsamic vinegar** down to a glaze and a following step takes **1 tbsp glaze from a container.** ⚠️ **Hans's framing, and it is the priority test: *"hey, I just made that"* / *"why do I have to buy that."***
- **Root cause — NOT a new defect class. This is D-WS9-066 shipped with only half its wiring.** `RecipeInstructionStep.componentKey` + `pathKey` (`'scratch'`|`'bought'`) landed in `25cbc7a`; **null `componentKey` = base = always shown.** ⚠️ **BOTH COLUMN COMMENTS READ "Nothing reads it yet."** With no reader, no filter runs and **every row of every path renders.** The screenshot is the predicted shape exactly: steps 1–4 scratch, step 5 bought, steps 6–7 base and deliberately path-agnostic (*"the finished cheese sauce"*).
- ⚠️ **THE TWO HALVES ARE IN DIFFERENT STATES, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE TRIAGE.** **Steps ARE tagged** — D-WS9-066 measured 34/34 substitution-carrying dishes with a bought path, 43 components, **zero tag-validation findings.** **Ingredients are NOT** — `DishIngredient.componentKey`/`pathKey` were added in the same commit and **deliberately left null** (finalize authors steps, not ingredients; D-WS9-067). So the step half may be a filter; **the ingredient half is a catalog authoring pass** and cannot be fixed by wiring.
- **Four wrong answers from one missing filter:** the user **buys both** (can *and* cheddar/milk/butter/flour); **macros count both**, since nutrition derives from ingredient rows; **`cookingScheduler` sums both paths** — the screenshot's sauce reads 26 min for work that is either ~14 or ~5; and **prep says measure an ingredient you are about to make from scratch.**
- ⚠️ **PRE-BETA BLOCKER, NOT A FEATURE (D-WS9-174).** The roadmap parks this in WS9B behind WS9A on the theory that toggle UI should build on finished screens. That reasoning holds for **the toggle** and not for **the filter** — a default-to-scratch filter needs no UI. Hans, August 21: features may ship after beta; issues of this class may not.
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN** · **Priority:** **P1** · **Target:** read-only Phase 0 commissioned August 21, 2026, ahead of the BUG-096 merge build · **Cross-ref:** D-WS9-066 · D-WS9-067 · D-WS9-068 · D-WS9-069 · D-WS7-215 · D-WS9-174 · roadmap row 3c (WS9B).
- ✅ **ONE OF THE FOUR WRONG ANSWERS IS FIXED, September 14, 2026 — the SCHEDULER half: BUG-270 (`f654432`) makes `scheduleCookingSequence` walk base + the DEFAULT path only, so the stored time no longer sums both paths.** The other three — the user buys both, macros count both, prep measures what it then makes — are unchanged and still this entry’s. ⚠️ **Side effect recorded on BUG-270: Cook Mode’s wire sequence now omits bought rows and the mobile appends them TRAILING; before, they were interleaved. Both are this entry’s symptom, neither is worse.** 🔵 **Hans, September 15, confirmed the consumer side in his own words — *"the sequencer needs an update to hide the store-bought or the full cook steps based on what the user chooses on the meal. and obviously same with the grocery list"* — and raised two things this entry does NOT cover: whether a bought path is a REAL product, and whether from-scratch is the right DEFAULT for staple components (dough, pasta, ravioli, fries). Those are D-WS9-242 (measurement instructed).**

### BUG-122 — Wrong-food USDA nutrition pointers, again: this is BUG-032 recurring on rows its judge never saw

- **Date:** August 21, 2026 · **Surface:** `Ingredient.nutritionRefPerUnit` catalog-wide; every macro surface downstream · **Found by:** CC's BUG-096 Phase 0, connected to BUG-032 chat-side.
- **Symptom, as the user experiences it:** an apple reads **254 kcal** (it is pointing at *Croissants, apple*). An avocado reads **884 kcal / 100 g fat** (*Oil, avocado*). A sweet potato reads **42 kcal** (*Sweet potato leaves, raw*). A lemon reads 17 kcal (*juice from concentrate*). Meal macro totals are wrong wherever these appear.
- ⚠️ **BUG-032 READS ✅ FIXED AND ITS TWO MARQUEE EXAMPLES ARE `sweet potato` AND `avocado`. BOTH ARE WRONG AGAIN, UNDER THEIR PLURAL NAMES.** The singular rows BUG-032 repaired are still correct; the plural rows created afterwards are not.
- **Root cause, established from the enrichment timeline rather than inferred.** BUG-032's fix wrote **94 re-matched rows** and the `fetchedAt` census shows a `2026-07-13` wave of **matched=94** — that is the fix. A **`2026-07-24` wave then wrote 1,041 refs, 63% of the catalog**, twelve days later, growing it from ~451 rows to 1,649. **The Opus judge never saw those rows.** Same permissive matcher, new rows.
- **Sizing, from a measured base rate rather than a guess:** BUG-032 found **101 of 329 bad — 31%**. Against ~256 unjudged matched refs from the July 24 wave, expect **roughly 75–80 ingredients carrying another food's macros.** CC's independent count of refs bearing the `foodCategory`-non-null shape is 320.
- ⚠️ **UNVERIFIED HYPOTHESIS, and CC labelled it correctly against itself:** that the `foodCategory`-bearing refs contain proportionally more bad matches. Six of six checked here were wrong on the divergent side, **but all six were selected because they diverged — that is not a random sample and does not estimate a base rate.** Measure before acting on any figure.
- **Method is already proven and written down in BUG-032 — do not invent a new one.** An Opus judge with **no `YES` write path**: a `YES` leaves the row alone, only `NO`/`UNSURE` surfaces for review. A string matcher structurally cannot see this class. **SR Legacy records only; Foundation Foods 404 on by-id fetch.**
- ⚠️ **THE CONVENTION MUST BE HANDED TO THE JUDGE, NOT RE-INFERRED — D-WS9-178.** Macros describe the ingredient **as purchased**; quantities are as-purchased and "cooked"/"baked"/"fried" is a preparation note on the step, never a different ingredient. BUG-032's entire wrong-food class came from a matcher inferring a convention instead of being given one.
- **Six of the ~80 are already fixed incidentally**, because the BUG-096 merge forced a pick on them.
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN** · **Priority:** **P2, pre-beta under D-WS9-174** (Hans ruled it the *"that's just wrong"* class on the launch-critical macro path) · **Target:** its own block, sequenced after BUG-121 · **Cross-ref:** BUG-032 · BUG-096 · D-WS9-174 · D-WS9-178.

### BUG-123 — Duplicate ingredient rows that are not singular/plural pairs, so BUG-096 does not touch them

- **Date:** August 21, 2026 · **Surface:** `Ingredient` catalog; visible on the grocery list · **Found by:** CC's BUG-096 Phase 1 §9, confirmed against live data in the review sheets and then on device.
- **Symptom:** the same user-visible complaint as BUG-096 — one ingredient occupying two grocery rows — arriving by a different route. Device-confirmed August 21: **`1 head garlic (6 cloves)` and `garlic cloves (6 each)` on the same list.**
- **The shapes, each confirmed live:** **bare-vs-qualified** (`Garlic` / `garlic cloves`; `jalapeño` / `fresh jalapeño`, both pointing at the identical USDA record 168576; `yukon gold potatoes` / `baby yukon gold potatoes` / `baby potato`, all three resolving to 170026) · **qualifier drift** (`flour tortillas` / `large flour tortillas` / `small flour tortillas` as three rows; `Olive oil` / `extra-virgin olive oil`) · **synonyms** (`green onions` / `scallions`, both with real reference counts) · **spelling and diacritic variants** (`jalapeño` / `jalapeno`).
- ⚠️ **WHY BUG-096 CANNOT REACH THESE, AND WHY THE 81-PAIR CLAIM MUST STAY BOUNDED.** BUG-096's fold list came from a 16-rule morphological sweep. **That is a statement about the rule set, not about the catalog.** It is recorded as bounded to suffix-fold collisions in three places in the code and must stay that way — *"all 81 found"* must never be written down as *"all duplicates found."*
- **Fix direction, not yet designed:** this is a curation problem needing a different instrument — semantic grouping rather than morphology. **The BUG-096 machinery is reusable once a fold list exists**: `ingredientMergeFold.ts` takes a curated pair list, `ingredientMergeCarriers.ts` holds the per-carrier rewriters, and the alias table plus `IngredientAlias` unique index makes the result durable.
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN** · **Priority:** **P2** · **Target:** unscheduled; candidate for the same arc as BUG-124 · **Cross-ref:** BUG-096 · BUG-124 · BUG-125.
