### BUG-124 — The purchase-pack field holds computed needs, not buyable packages

- **Date:** August 21, 2026 · **Surface:** `Ingredient` purchase-pack fields; the grocery list order line · **Found by:** chat-Claude auditing BUG-096's pack review sheet; device-confirmed by Hans.
- **Symptom:** the field that is supposed to say *what you buy* frequently holds *how much this one recipe needed*. **Four of the twelve rows on the review sheet offer two computed quantities with no buyable package on either side:** `roma tomatoes` (4 vs 7), `lime` (2 vs 3), `shallot` (1 vs 2), and the survivor side of `garlic cloves` ("13 cloves (1–2 bulbs)"). Tomatoes and limes are sold loose; there is no 4-pack.
- **Why it matters beyond tidiness:** the pack drives rounding. **Device-confirmed:** `roma tomatoes` shows an order line of **4** against a computed need of **9** — the user is told to buy less than half of what the recipe calls for. See BUG-125 for the display half of this.
- **The counter-example that shows the field working:** `garlic cloves` → *1 head of garlic* against a need of 6 cloves is correct, and is what every row should look like. It became correct only because BUG-096's review picked the loser's value.
- ⚠️ **CATALOG-WIDE, NOT CONFINED TO THE TWELVE.** The twelve were visible only because BUG-096's merge forced a comparison on them. Nothing measured how many of the 1,568 rows carry a computed need in the pack field — **that number sizes the fix and does not exist yet.**
- ⚠️ **UNVERIFIED HYPOTHESIS, carried from CC's BUG-096 report:** the ten pack rows that defaulted to the survivor on *"both are the same kind of unit"* hold the right pack size. **Nothing was verified against real retail packaging.**
- ✅ **THE WRITER IS IDENTIFIED (BUG-125 findings probe, August 24, 2026) — a producer nobody had named.** `fillPurchaseSizesWithWriteBack` → `gapFillPurchaseSize` passes `requestedQuantity` / `requestedUnit` to the AI (`groceryListAI.ts:151-152`) on a catalog miss. **The prompt's own example is `{"canonicalName":"boneless skinless chicken thighs","requestedQuantity":1.5,"requestedUnit":"lb"}` → `"1.5 lb pack"`.** The pack is sized to the need of whichever plan first hit the gap, **and then written back to the `Ingredient` row, catalog-wide, permanently.** Live consequence: `boneless skinless chicken thighs need=3.5 pound | pd="1.5 lb pack"` — correct for the plan that minted it, wrong for every plan since.
- ⚠️ **THIS IS BUG-125's EXACT ERROR ONE LAYER DOWN: a need-derived value cached need-independently.** BUG-125 fixed the render; this fixes the write. **A pack must be a property of the product, never of a plan.**
- **Live instance from Hans's device test of `1c61e8e`:** a new plan ordered **2 lb ground beef against a ¾ lb need**. Correct arithmetic (BUG-131) over a pack the gap-fill had sized to some earlier plan. ⚠️ **Verify that specific row's stored pack before treating it as confirmed** — an epsilon fault in the new scaling is the competing explanation and has not been ruled out.
- ✅ **THE GROUND BEEF INSTANCE IS BENIGN — NO `1c61e8e` REGRESSION (catalog probe, August 24, 2026).** The row: need `0.75 pound`, stored `purchaseQuantity: 2 / lb / "2 lb pack"`, **byte-identical to its `Ingredient` row.** Arithmetic: `nu("pound") === pu("lb")` → `max(1, ceil(0.75/2 − 1e-9))` = `max(1, 1)` = **1 pack = 2 lb**. **The binding constraint is `Math.max(1, …)` — you cannot order less than one pack.** The epsilon never fires and the units match cleanly.
- **Sweep, and CC did not stop at the first benign row.** Order-quantity logic ported verbatim over all 2,067 live rows (793 computable). **13 rows order more than 2× the need — Parmesan at 6×, shaved Parmesan 5×, Kalamata olives 4×, olive oil spray 4×, sweet potatoes 3×, ground beef 2.67× — and every single one is `packs = 1`, the smallest order the code can emit, with a row pack byte-identical to its stored catalog pack. Cause is the stored pack in 13 of 13; not one row over-scaled.** ⚠️ **Under-ordering among the computable set: ZERO** (164 exact, 92 over, 0 under). The `1c61e8e` scaling is arithmetically sound where it applies.
- ⚠️ **WHERE THE REAL RISK SITS: the 1,274 rows the scaling does NOT apply to**, which print the stored pack verbatim with no coverage guarantee. Top shapes: 255 tsp-need vs container-pack · 154 tbsp vs bottle · 68 cup vs bottle · 56 each vs package · 44 cup vs bunch · **34 ounce-need vs lb-pack** · **30 tbsp vs each — the citrus shape (BUG-133).**
- **Two cheap partial wins needing NO new data:** an **oz↔lb factor** in `packsToCoverNeed` (34 live rows, pure arithmetic) and the **clove↔head** case (25 rows), which BUG-137's garlic fold resolves for free.
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN** · **Priority:** **P2** · **Target:** **D-WS9-226 (the intake block) owns the writer fix; the writer fix (stop `gapFillPurchaseSize` persisting a need-derived pack to the shared row) is the scoped part · **Cross-ref:** BUG-096 · BUG-123 · BUG-125 · BUG-131 · BUG-133 · BUG-136 · BUG-137.
- ✅ **MEASURED September 7, 2026 (read-only audit, CC): THE DENOMINATOR THIS ENTRY SAID DID NOT EXIST.** **1,078 of 1,569 catalog rows (68.7%) carry a NULL pack** — each a primed trap: the first plan to want it fires a Haiku gap-fill carrying that plan's need, written back catalog-wide. **The loop was observed in the call log, not inferred: 13 `gap_fill_purchase_size` calls at 01:22, then 8 at 01:27 on the same menu** — run 1's write-back filled the catalog for run 2. The rest: 375 retail-shaped (23.9%) · 65 need-shaped (4.1%) · 51 static-default (3.3%). **Only 441 of 1,569 rows (28.1%) have ever appeared on a live list; the other 1,128 packs have never been shopped by anyone.**
- ⚠️ **THE "13 ROWS ORDER >2× NEED, ALL `packs = 1`" FIGURE ABOVE IS PRE-ROOT-A AND NO LONGER DESCRIBES THE RENDER.** The ordered amount is derived client-side at render (`composePackName` → `packsToCoverNeed`): count-pack against count-need ignores the pack and orders `ceil(need)`; differing units scale the container; only an unrelatable pair prints the stored pack verbatim. **Classifying against the stored pack would have manufactured ~60 phantom under-buys; CC refused that premise and was right.** Of 22 over-buys ≥2× on the four newest lists, 21 are the smallest real retail container (4 tbsp oil → 1 bottle) — BUG-125's accepted trade — and **one is this bug: `shredded cheddar cheese` carries a `2.5 lb bag` (need 0.5 cup, 9.29×) while its sibling `shredded sharp cheddar cheese` carries the correct `1 package (8 oz)`.**
- ⚠️ **TWO DETECTION RULES, MEASURED — use these, not "pack equals a need":** (1) *need-shaped* (pack quantity equals a historical need and ≠ 1) has a **64% false-positive rate** on live rows — 4 roma tomatoes, 2 avocados, 2 cans are real shelf shapes that coincide with a need. (2) **A weight pack at 1.5 / 2.5 / 3.5 lb catches ~22 rows with essentially no false positives** (chicken thighs 1.5 lb, pork shoulder 2.5 lb, ground beef 3.5 lb, the cheddar above); 0.5 lb is a real 8 oz cheese/charcuterie pack and must be excluded. (3) **A container-unit pack on the `to` end of a component edge from a fresh fruit** catches the phantom bottles exactly — `lemon juice → 1 bottle (15 oz)`, `orange juice` and `fresh orange juice → 1 bottle (64 oz)` — 3 rows, zero false positives.
- ⚠️ **`Ingredient` has NO `createdAt` / `updatedAt`** (column list read from `information_schema`), so *"how many rows were created since X, by which path"* is unanswerable and no proxy is honest. **The intake block adds them (D-WS9-226).**

### BUG-129 — No escape hatch: `POST generate_grocery` 409s whenever a non-archived list exists, so a list can never be fully rebuilt

- **Date:** August 21, 2026 · **Surface:** grocery list regeneration route, api-server · **Found by:** BUG-126 probe (the "regenerate" Hans ran never ran — one `generate_grocery` event at 22:42, the route 409'd Case-1, and the success toast was reconcile-on-read).
- **Shape:** when a non-archived list exists the route returns **409 Case-1** (`groceryLists.ts:195-207`; the route header states Case 1 only, and mobile routes to `existingListId` regardless of staleness) and the only path that touches the list is reconcile. Once BUG-126 is fixed the toast becomes accurate, **so this is a resilience gap, not a live defect — but there is no escape hatch when reconcile is wrong, and reconcile has now been wrong** (8 stale buckets across 7 live lists with no user-reachable recovery). A full rebuild — archive-and-regenerate, or an explicit force flag — would be the escape hatch. Worth having before the next reconcile-shaped defect.
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN** · **Priority:** **P3** · **Target:** unscheduled · **Cross-ref:** BUG-126 · D-WS7-124 · D-WS7-137.

### BUG-133 — Derived products of one physical ingredient do not consolidate, and their yields are wrong

- **Date:** August 24, 2026 · **Surface:** consolidation / grocery list · **Found by:** Hans, device test of `1c61e8e`, across two freshly generated plans.
- **Observed, verbatim:** one list carried `3 limes lime juice (14 1/2 tablespoon)`, `4 lime wedges (4 each)`, `1 lime lime zest (1 1/2 teaspoon)`. Another carried `1 lime fresh lime juice (2 tablespoons)` beside `3 limes lime juice (7 1/2 tablespoon)`.
- **Two stacked defects, and they are separable:**
  1. ⚠️ **YIELDS ARE WRONG.** Hans: *"I don't think there is 14.5 tablespoons of juice in 3 limes."* A lime yields roughly 2 tbsp, so 14½ tbsp needs ~7 limes. **This under-orders**, which is the ruled-worst failure.
  2. **DERIVED PRODUCTS DON'T DEDUPE.** `lime juice`, `fresh lime juice`, `lime zest` and `lime wedges` each convert independently back to whole limes and each occupy their own row. Hans: *"the zest can come from a lime that's being juiced"* — and separately, *"the lime wedges should be 1/2 lime in quantity."*
- ⚠️ **HANS FRAMED THE GENERAL CASE AND IT IS THE LOAD-BEARING PART:** *"not sure how to say that without adding a hard rule somewhere. lemon zest, lemon peel, lime zest, lime peel… probably more out there."* **This is a class, not a lime bug** — citrus juice/zest/peel/wedges, and plausibly ginger, garlic and herbs. **The remedy is a derivation model with per-product yield factors, not a hard-coded rule**, and that is why this is not a quick fix.
- **Relationship to BUG-137:** synonym fragmentation (`lime juice` vs `fresh lime juice`) is BUG-137's shape and may be fixed by that fold. **The derivation half is not** — `lime zest` and `lime juice` are genuinely different products of one fruit and must stay distinct rows while sharing a source count.
- ✅ **MEASURED (catalog probe, August 24, 2026). Hans is right on both counts.**
- **Yields: the pack is a frozen scalar and it under-orders.** `lime juice`'s `conversionRef` carries `gramsPerCup: 242` and nothing else — **no `gramsPerEach`, no `subUnit`, no parent pointer. There is no data path from tablespoons-of-juice to limes anywhere in the system.** `purchaseQuantity: 3` is a static value written once by `gapFillPurchaseSize` and persisted to the shared catalog row (BUG-124's writer). At a real yield of ~2 tbsp/lime, 14½ tbsp needs **~8 limes; the list orders 3 — under-ordering by ~2.4×.**
- ⚠️ **THE INCONSISTENCY IS WITHIN ONE ROW ACROSS LISTS, NOT BETWEEN ROWS — which is a sharper finding than the original report.** The same `lime juice` catalog row, three plans generated within twelve minutes: 7⅓ tbsp → 3 limes (2.44/lime ✅) · 8 tbsp → 3 limes (2.67 ✅) · **14½ tbsp → 3 limes (4.83 ❌)**. The pack never moves; only the need does. Same for `fresh lime juice`: 2 tbsp → 1 lime ✅, 4 tbsp → 1 lime ❌.
- ⚠️ **A SECOND, SEPARATE DEFECT — AND IT WAS INTRODUCED BY BUG-125's RULE 1 (see BUG-138).** `lime wedges` has need-unit and pack-unit both `each`, so the same-unit rule fires and **discards the pack entirely**, rendering `4 lime wedges` — an order for a quantity of a thing no store sells. **The stored `1 lime` was the right answer and was thrown away.**
- **The derived class, enumerated: 72 catalog rows are a derived product of another catalog row** (2,411 dish refs, 141 live grocery rows). Largest families: lime (7 derived rows, 38 live) · garlic (6, 28) · herbs (11 rows, 26 live) · orange (4, 14) · lemon (5, 12) · celery stalks · corn kernels. **Mechanical false positives explicitly flagged not to fold:** `celery seed`←celery, `mustard greens`←mustard, `walnut halves`←walnuts (a grade), `frozen corn kernels`←fresh corn (a different product), `kimchi juice`←kimchi.
- ⚠️ **The catalog already encodes three incompatible theories of one thing:** `lime juice` packs as `3 limes` (treated as derived), `lemon juice` as `1 bottle (15 oz)`, `orange juice` as `1 bottle (64 oz)` (treated as bottled goods). **Three sibling rows, three models.**
- **DERIVATION vs SYNONYMY — the distinction that decides the remedy.** `fresh lime juice` vs `lime juice` is a **SYNONYM** (BUG-137's fold handles it). `lime zest` vs `lime juice` is a **DERIVATION** — two legitimate rows that must stay separate while sharing one source fruit. `lime wedges` is a derivation (a cut of the parent). ⚠️ **`garlic cloves` vs `garlic` is a HYBRID: mechanically a derivation, but the catalog already models it inside one row via `conversionRef.subUnit`, so the correct remedy there is the synonym fold, not a derivation link.**
- **What a derivation model needs (design only, nothing built — `grep` for `parentIngredient|derivedFrom|yieldFactor|sourceIngredient` returns ZERO hits):** a real FK parent pointer, not a name convention, *"or it re-accrues exactly like the 81 singular/plural pairs did"* · a **`yieldPerParent` in the derived row's own unit** (lime juice → 2 tbsp, lime zest → 2 tsp, lime wedges → 8 each, celery stalks → 9 each) — **this is the number that does not exist anywhere today and it is what turns 14½ tbsp into 8 limes** · a **share-vs-add rule** (→ D-WS9-182) · a rollup stage running after bucketing and before `fillPurchaseSizesWithWriteBack`, for which `mergeConvertibleGroups` is the existing precedent · ⚠️ **and it must NOT write back to `Ingredient.purchaseQuantity` — that is the BUG-124 wound and the reason `lime juice` says "3 limes" forever.**
- ⚠️ **ENTANGLEMENT WITH BUG-137: do not run the CITRUS synonym fold before the derivation model is ruled**, or the fold will eat rows the model needed. **Garlic has no such entanglement and can go first.**
- **Status:** 🔵 **READY TO BUILD — the gate this line claimed is GONE.** 🔴 **CORRECTED September 9, 2026: this status read *"needs D-WS9-182 ruled before build"* for two weeks after D-WS9-182 was RULED (Hans, August 24, 2026).** ✅ **Nothing is owed: the ruling carries the component-pool model, web-sourced yield constants for all four citrus, the whole-source-unit fallback, the 8-wedge convention, and the build constraints (extend `conversionRef`, do NOT add a column, do NOT write back to `Ingredient.purchaseQuantity`).** ✅ **Its one sequencing gate — BUG-137's citrus fold must not run before BUG-134's duplicate-bucket guard — is ALSO cleared: BUG-134 is FIXED and device-verified.** ⚠️ **What remains is an AUTHORING task (~40 derived rows of `yieldPerParent`), not a decision** · **Priority:** **P2** (the yield half under-orders, which is the ruled-worst failure) · **Cross-ref:** BUG-124 · BUG-131 · BUG-134 · BUG-137 · BUG-138 · D-WS9-179 · D-WS9-182.

### BUG-135 — 16 alias rows written by the BUG-096 merge were dead on arrival

- **Date:** August 24, 2026 · **Surface:** ingredient catalog · **Found by:** the BUG-125 findings probe — **and only because CC tested the alias table empirically rather than trusting the code read.** All five name→id write paths verified correctly alias-aware by two differently-shaped searches; **both searches were right about the code and wrong about the outcome.**
- **Shape:** for 16 of the 140 alias rows, a canonical `Ingredient` row of the same text already exists under a different id. `lookupIngredientByName` matches canonical first, so **those aliases can never fire.** Examples: `egg` (shadow row 24 live / 34 dish refs, against a survivor with 7) · `boneless skinless chicken thighs` (14 / 200) · `salt` (10 / 53) · `freshly ground black pepper` · `sharp cheddar cheese` · `sugar`.
- ✅ **NOT A LIVE LEAK — this was checked and downgraded.** All 16 predate the merge (earliest dish reference between 2026-06-26 and 2026-07-26; merge stamp `20260821-1408`). **CC corrected its own Phase 0 wording: "re-minted" was wrong — these names were never merged away.** The defect is in the merge script's alias-writing, which should have detected the collision, not in any live write path.
- ⚠️ **CAVEAT TO RE-CHECK WHEN THE FOLD RUNS:** the dating proxy was earliest-dish-reference, and the merge itself rewrote 1,769 references across five id-carriers. A row that was a **survivor** in the 81-pair fold could therefore show borrowed early dates. Confirm none of the 16 were survivors before relying on the pre-merge conclusion.
- ⚠️ **AND IT QUALIFIES BUG-096's DURABILITY CLAIM.** The `prisma:seed` round-trip proved seeds do not re-create losers. It did not prove the alias table functions — **11% of it does not.**
- ✅ **THE CAVEAT IS CLOSED, AND IT CLOSED HARDER THAN THE ORIGINAL ARGUMENT (catalog probe, August 24, 2026).** ⚠️ **The hole was REAL: 2 of the 16 shadowed rows WERE fold survivors** — `egg` (pair `egg`/`eggs`) and `navel oranges` (pair `navel oranges`/`navel orange`) — so their earliest-dish dates were indeed potentially borrowed from a merged-away loser.
- **But re-dating them proved unnecessary, by a better argument: being a fold survivor is itself proof of pre-merge existence.** `selectLiveGroups` only merges a pair when **both** survivor and loser are present (`ingredientMergeFold.ts:137-143`); a loser without a survivor is refuse and is never merged. **And independently, all 16 alias strings are hand-authored in `HOUSEHOLD_BASIC_INGREDIENTS` (`prisma/seed.ts:451-500`), verified 16/16** — not one matches a fold loser's name, and the merge writes aliases only from `loser.displayName`. **Both halves of every shadowing pair predate 2026-08-21, on source-authorship and fold-survivorship rather than on the dish-date proxy.**
- **Minor canon drift worth a note:** the doc comment at `ingredientLookup.ts:86` and `seed.ts:470` both say **"20 such pairs"**; the live count is **16**.
- **Disposition — four options, deliberately not picked:** (1) **delete the alias** — blast radius nil, but the next seed re-creates it, so it is not durable without a seed edit · (2) **repoint the alias** to the shadowing row — cosmetic today, but makes the row self-consistent and survives a later fold · (3) **fold the two rows** — the only option that fixes anything, **and the one that mints duplicates via BUG-134's mechanism** (`egg` alone has 29 live rows) · (4) **leave them and add a guard test** asserting no `aliasKey` equals any `canonicalName`, so the next one is caught at authoring time — **zero blast radius, free, and does nothing for the existing 16.**
- ⚠️ **THE PROPOSED GUARD ASSERTION IS THE INVERSE OF A STANDING RULING AND WOULD BE RED ON DAY ONE (Root-B Phase 0, August 25, 2026).** Chat-Claude scoped a test asserting *"no `aliasKey` may equal any `canonicalName`."* **Two code sites do not merely count these pairs — they declare them LEGAL AND DELIBERATE.** `ingredientLookup.ts:86`: canonical beats alias unconditionally, *without which each pair would be an ambiguity error on a pair that is not ambiguous.* `schema.prisma:442-445` says the same. **The assertion as stated must not be written.**
- **Three replacements, and only one is a unit test:** **(a) a DRIFT guard** — *this set is exactly these 16; a 17th means someone added a shadowing alias without ruling.* **(b) a RESOLVER guard** — *for every such pair, `lookupIngredientByName` returns the canonical row.* ✅ **This is the invariant that actually protects anything, and it is the one worth building.** **(c) a write-time guard** on the merge/seed paths.
- ⚠️ **(a) CANNOT BE A UNIT TEST, AND THE REASON GENERALISES: A STATIC FIXTURE HOLDS ONLY ONE HALF OF EVERY PAIR.** **Zero of the 16 shadowed canonical rows exist in `seed.ts`** — the aliases come from the seed; the canonical rows they shadow were created by **AI dish generation.** Two further blockers: `HOUSEHOLD_BASIC_INGREDIENTS` is module-local, not exported (`seed.ts:443`); and the seed's alias write is an **upsert that re-points on conflict** (`seed.ts:565-569`), so a collision is silently resolved rather than raised. **The drift guard must be a DB-reading script.**
- ⚠️ **A THIRD STALE DOC SITE, AND ITS EXAMPLE IS ALSO WRONG.** `schema.prisma:443-444` carries the same "20 such pairs" **and cites `apple`/`apples`, which is not among the live 16.** All three sites must move together.
- ✅ **THE RESOLVER GUARD IS BUILT (local, unpushed).** Five guards pin that the canonical row wins **and the alias table is never consulted.** ⚠️ **THE QUERY-SHAPE HALF IS LOAD-BEARING AND BREAK I PROVES IT: a mutation returned the CORRECT id while reading the alias table anyway — an id-only assertion passes it.** Only the *which queries ran* assertion catches it.
- ✅ **All three prose doc sites corrected: 20 → 16**, and `schema.prisma`'s worked example replaced — `apple`/`apples` does not exist; **`apples` was folded away by BUG-096.**
- ⚠️ **A FOURTH SITE EXISTS, FOUND ONLY BY THE SECOND SEARCH SHAPE — §27.3 EARNING ITS KEEP AGAIN.** `migrations/20260821120000_ws9_bug096_ingredient_alias/migration.sql`. CC's `find | xargs grep` globbed `*.ts|*.prisma|*.md` and **skipped `.sql`**; `rg --no-ignore` caught it. **Deliberately NOT edited — the migration is applied with checksum `c27e86dd…`, so editing it trips Prisma's modified-migration check.**
- **Status:** 🟡 **RESOLVER GUARD ✅ SHIPPED; dispositions 1–3 for the 16 existing rows still unruled** · **Priority:** **P3** · **Target:** with BUG-137's fold; the guard test is free and can land any time · **Cross-ref:** BUG-096 · BUG-128 · BUG-134 · BUG-137 · D-WS9-176.

### BUG-136 — 204 legacy rows carry the purchase pack baked into `displayName`

- **Date:** August 24, 2026 · **Surface:** grocery list · **Found by:** the BUG-125 Phase 0 audit, while sizing the no-pack rule.
- **Shape:** of 274 live rows with no pack, **204 (74%) have a name that already begins with a quantity** — `"1 bottle (17 oz) Olive oil"`, `"1 head Garlic"`. These are pre-`b0cd677` rows created before the pack became structured data. **No new list can produce this shape.**
- **Why it is logged rather than fixed:** BUG-125's no-pack rule would have rendered `"30 1 head Garlic"` on every one of them. A leading-number guard was ruled in and shipped in `083d935`, so **the display is safe today** — but the underlying rows still carry unstructured pack text that nothing can scale, sort or reason about.
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN** · **Priority:** **P3** · **Target:** unscheduled data cleanup · **Cross-ref:** BUG-124 · BUG-125.

### BUG-137 — Catalog synonym fragmentation: nine garlic rows mean one food, and only one carries the conversion

- **Date:** August 24, 2026 · **Surface:** ingredient catalog · **Found by:** the BUG-125 findings probe (Root C1), from Hans's device observation that one plan rendered `20 garlic cloves (20 each)` while another rendered `2 heads garlic (13 cloves)`.
- ⚠️ **CHAT-CLAUDE'S HYPOTHESIS WAS REFUTED.** `garlic cloves` is not a merge escapee — **it is a BUG-096 survivor.** BUG-096 folded singular/plural pairs (`clove`/`cloves`); `garlic` vs `garlic cloves` is a **SYNONYM** pair, and synonyms were never in that merge's scope.
- **Measured:** the catalog holds **15 garlic-ish rows, nine of which mean the same food, and only `garlic` (e6753984) carries the `head↔clove` `subUnit`** that makes scaling work. `garlic cloves` (7835b896) has 21 live rows and 651 dish rows and no conversion at all — which is why it renders as a bare count.
- **A second fragmentation axis: prep-note suffixes.** `garlic, minced` · `garlic cloves, minced` · `garlic cloves, peeled` · `whole garlic head` · `garlic head` · `fresh garlic`. **Cilantro has it too** — `fresh cilantro leaves`, `fresh cilantro, roughly chopped`, both with live rows. **A prep note is a preparation instruction on the step, not a different ingredient** (D-WS9-178's principle, applied to naming rather than macros).
- **A fold dissolves part of BUG-133 and all of the garlic symptom for free** — merging `garlic cloves` into `garlic` makes `20 garlic cloves (20 each)` render `2 heads garlic (20 cloves)` with **no code change.**
- ⚠️ **DO NOT RUN THE FOLD BEFORE PROBING BUG-134** — merging ids blurs the duplicate-row evidence.
- ✅ **MEASURED (catalog probe, August 24, 2026).** Garlic is **15 rows, 10 of them the same bulb** (CC corrected its own earlier "9" — it had left out `head of garlic`). Survivor is `garlic` e6753984 on BUG-096's own ladder: 1,022 dish refs, 32 live rows, **and the only row in the catalog carrying `subUnit`.** `garlic cloves` 7835b896 has 665 dish refs and 26 live rows and no `subUnit` at all. **Not the same food, leave alone:** garlic powder, chili garlic sauce, garlic chives, ginger-garlic paste.
- ⚠️ **THE PAYOFF CLAIM WAS WRONG AND CC REFUTED IT: ZERO currently-broken renders fix themselves.** Chat-Claude wrote that a fold would make `20 garlic cloves (20 each)` render correctly *"with no code change."* **It will not.** The merge machinery repoints `ingredientId` and rewrites `displayName`; **it does not recompute `purchaseUnit`/`purchaseQuantity`/`purchaseDisplay` on existing grocery rows**, which are persisted per-row and rendered as stored. A repointed row keeps `purchaseUnit: "each"` and still mis-renders. **The fix arrives only on the next re-resolution** (new list, or reconcile after a plan edit). **So the 26 existing rows need a grocery-row pack backfill — and that is the piece BUG-096's machinery does not have.**
- **Scale of the bare-count-of-a-sub-unit population: 45 live rows** — `garlic cloves` ×26, `ears of corn`/`ear of corn` ×12, `lime wedges` ×5, plus two singletons.
- **Other candidate groups, flagged not decided** (CC is explicitly not authoritative about food identity; **Hans rules these**): citrus juice families (27 live lime, 8 lemon, 14 orange — ⚠️ **ambiguous on a real axis: is `lime juice` the bottle or the squeezed fruit?**) · black pepper ×40 · parmesan/parmigiano-reggiano ×20 (arguably a distinct purchase) · **ground beef / ground beef (80/20) / lean ground beef — packs of 1 lb, 2 lb and 3.5 lb for one food, and directly causing BUG-124's over-orders; but fat ratio is a real shopping distinction** · corn (mixed derivation + synonym + frozen-vs-fresh) · the sixteen from BUG-135.
- **Reuses from BUG-096 directly:** the survivor/loser ladder, the five-carrier repoint, the two-CSV dry-run/review/apply protocol, the P2002-enforcing alias table, `selectLiveGroups` idempotence, the seed-durability guard. **Needs that the singular/plural fold did not:** a **curated predicate no morphological rule can generate** (`ingredientMergeFold.ts:21-27` says so and scopes this case out explicitly) · **unit reconciliation as load-bearing rather than cosmetic** — `garlic`(cloves, subUnit), `garlic cloves`(each), `head of garlic`(head) are three granularities, so the merge must also decide the survivor's default unit and re-key the losers', **which the pack CSV has no column for** · the grocery-row pack backfill above · ⚠️ **and the duplicate-bucket guard, or the fold reproduces BUG-134 at larger scale.**
- ⚠️ **PREP-NOTE SUFFIX STRIPPING IS NOT SAFE — and the DB probe refuted the code read.** 100 catalog rows carry a `, <suffix>` whose bare base exists (283 dish refs, 22 live rows). Two sweeps confirmed nothing parses the suffix out of `canonicalName`, and the client appends `preparationNote` separately — **so the code says it is safe. The data says otherwise: of 349 dish-ingredient references to a comma-suffixed row, 210 have `preparationNote` NULL — the suffix is the ONLY carrier of the instruction.** Stripping those deletes the recipe step's instruction. Worst offenders: `black pepper, freshly ground` (35), `lime juice, fresh` (9), `garlic cloves, minced` (8), `beef chuck, cut into 1½-inch cubes` (7).
- **Three further hazards, so treat suffixes as THREE TIERS, not one rule:** ⚠️ **not every suffix is prep** — `pinto beans, canned` · `chicken broth, low-sodium` · `salmon fillets, skin-on` · `large shrimp, peeled and deveined` are product/form/grade, i.e. real buying distinctions (16 of 20 counted) · **the comma is not always a suffix boundary** — `bone-in, skin-on chicken thighs` splits to `bone-in` · **usage clauses are the safe subset** (`for serving`, `for frying`, `for garnish`). **Verdict: safe (usage clauses) · safe-after-migration (true prep, 210 refs need `preparationNote` backfilled first) · do not touch (product/form/grade).**
- ⚠️ **THE FOLD AS SCOPED WILL NOT WORK — IT LEAVES 22 LISTS HOLDING TWO GARLIC ROWS (SIMULATED over all 35 affected lists, August 25, 2026).** `planGroceryBucketMerges` keys on `(groceryListId, ingredientId, unit)`. **Loser rows carry `unit = "each"`; pre-existing survivor-side `Garlic` rows carry `"clove"`/`"cloves"`; head rows carry `"head"`. Different unit → different bucket → NO MERGE.** Simulation: 35 lists touched · **2 merge** · **22 left with >1 garlic bucket** · 13 clean. `c6ff50af` would show **`1 head Garlic (6 each)` beside `1 head Garlic (6 cloves)`** — ⚠️ **two identical-looking lines is WORSE than one wrong-noun line.** ⚠️ **`"clove"` and `"cloves"` are themselves distinct bucket keys, so this split already exists inside the survivor's own rows.** **A UNIT-NORMALISATION step is a hard prerequisite** — normalise before bucketing, or extend the key through `UNIT_ALIASES`. **Per D-WS9-185 the merge script cannot currently show that step in dry-run.**
- ⚠️ **THE PACK BACKFILL IS RESTATED: IT IS A `purchaseUnit` RE-RESOLUTION, NOT A `purchaseDisplay` BACKFILL.** Probe 1 proved the mobile render **never reads `purchaseQuantity`** (zero call sites) and on the count path **discards the pack count entirely**, printing the row's own `quantity`. Rewriting `"1 head of garlic"` → `"2 heads of garlic"` while `purchaseUnit` stays `"each"` is **INVISIBLE — verified by running the real composer.** What changes the line is `purchaseUnit` moving off `each`, **achieved BY THE FOLD, not by writing strings. The display string is a passenger.**
- **Live `garlic cloves` rows corrected: 23 (24 with `, peeled`), not 26** — 17 active, 6 archived, all on `7835b896`, all `unit = "each"`, all generated. Two pack variants: `"13 cloves (1–2 bulbs)"` pq 13 ×7 and `"1 head of garlic"` pq 1 ×16.
⚠️ **AMENDED August 27, 2026 — TWO NEW INSTANCES FROM HANS'S DEVICE PASS, AND HE RULED BOTH.** Found on one 69-item list:
  - **Sugar:** `1 bag (4 lb) Granulated sugar` (½ tsp) **and** `1 bag (4 lb) sugar` (1 tsp). ⚠️ **Hans-ruled: `sugar` / `white sugar` / `granulated sugar` are ONE thing; `brown sugar` is a DIFFERENT thing.**
  - **Oil:** `1 bottle (51 oz) neutral oil` **and** `1 bottle (51 oz) vegetable oil` — same pack, same list. ⚠️ **Hans-ruled: `neutral oil` / `vegetable oil` / `cooking oil` are one thing; `olive oil` / `extra virgin` / `extra-virgin olive oil` are one OTHER thing.**
- ⚠️ **THESE ARE NOT NEW BUGS AND WERE DELIBERATELY NOT MINTED AS SUCH — they are further instances of THIS class, and minting per-instance is the whack-a-mole Hans called out.** **The general fix is D-WS9-189, which supersedes this entry's per-food approach.** BUG-137's garlic fold remains the near-term tactical item; the citrus half stays blocked on unit normalisation.
⚠️ **LIVE EVIDENCE, August 28, 2026 — 3-list CSV dump, exact ids. THE GARLIC PAIR IS ON 3 OF 3 LISTS**, reported by Hans unprompted:
  - `Garlic` (`e6753984`, unit **clove**) — 10 / 13 / 4 · `garlic cloves` (`7835b896`, unit **each**) — 7 / 4 / 9. **Same food, two ids, two units.** (`garlic powder` is legitimately separate.)
  - **Eleven distinct duplicate/component instances across the three lists (~4 per list):** the garlic pair (3/3) · the lime family (3/3: `Lime` + `lime juice` + `lime zest` + `lime wedges`) · `black beans` (2 can) + `canned black beans` (30 oz) — ⚠️ **that pair alone orders ~4 cans** · `Granulated sugar` + `sugar` · `neutral oil` + `vegetable oil` · `Olive oil` + `extra-virgin olive oil` · `Fresh parsley` + `fresh flat-leaf parsley` · `eggs` + `Large eggs` (2/3) · `jalapeño` + `fresh jalapeño` · `bread` + `italian bread loaf` · `pepperoncini brine` + `pepperoncini peppers` (BUG-170).
- ⚠️ **THIS IS THE CONCRETE CASE FOR D-WS9-189 AND IT IS BETTER EVIDENCE THAN ANY CATALOG STATISTIC** — ~4 visible defects per generated list, on the surface a user actually shops from.
- **Status:** 🟡 **DIAGNOSED — superseded in general by D-WS9-189; garlic fold still the near-term tactical item, citrus still blocked on unit normalisation** · **Priority:** **P2** · **Cross-ref:** BUG-096 · BUG-133 · BUG-134 · BUG-135 · BUG-138 · BUG-161 · BUG-164 · BUG-170 · D-WS9-176 · D-WS9-178 · D-WS9-182 · **D-WS9-189**.


⚠️ **AMENDED August 29, 2026 — FIVE MORE LIVE INSTANCES, FROM ONE PLAN EXPORT (`d0d1bea8`), COUNTED FROM DATA RATHER THAN FROM A SCREENSHOT.**

| Rows shipped | Should be | Class |
|---|---|---|
| `olive oil` 3 tbsp · `extra virgin olive oil` 5 tbsp · `extra-virgin olive oil` 5 tbsp | **one row, 13 tbsp** | **BUG-181** — three bottles ordered |
| `Red pepper flakes` 0.5 tsp · `crushed red pepper flakes` 1.5 tsp | one row | SYNONYM |
| `eggs` 1 dozen · `Large eggs` 1 each | one row | SYNONYM **+ a unit-dimension split** |
| `Lemon` 2 each · `lemon juice` 3 tbsp · `lemon zest` 1 tsp | **one purchase** | **COMPONENT — D-WS9-182's pool, live** |
| `Whole milk` 3 tbsp · `milk` 1 each | see D-WS9-188 | recurring-vs-need |

✅ **AND THE ONE THAT WORKED, WHICH IS THE BEST EVIDENCE IN THE EXPORT: `Garlic` — 22 cloves from SIX dishes on ONE row, packed as `3 heads`.** **That is the fold doing exactly what the rest of these need.**

⚠️ **THE LEMON TRIO IS HANS'S OWN WORKED EXAMPLE, APPEARING UNPROMPTED IN HIS OWN PLAN.** The packs read `2 lemons`, `1 bottle (15 oz)` and `1 lemon` — **so the list asks him to buy lemons twice and a bottle of juice besides.** **This is D-WS9-182 + D-WS9-188 in one row-group, and it is not hypothetical.**

⚠️ **A MEASUREMENT CAVEAT ON THIS EXPORT, AND IT MATTERS FOR D-WS9-188: the CSV shows POST-PERSIST canonical names.** `whole milk` appears as the canonical on BOTH milk rows here, while CC's code read found the recurring synthetic bucketing under `milk`. **Both are consistent — persist re-resolves the name after bucketing — so this export CANNOT tell you what the bucket key was.** **Do not read it as refuting BUG-177; read it as silent on the question.**

✅ **GROUND TRUTH CONFIRMED BY HANS, August 29, 2026: `red pepper flakes` and `crushed red pepper flakes` ARE THE SAME PRODUCT — *"one shaker for both."*** ⚠️ **Worth keeping as a labelled pair for D-WS9-189: a human-ruled SYNONYM, usable as rubric calibration rather than a model guess.**

### BUG-139 — The grocery-list index is SILENTLY truncated at the default page size; older lists simply are not there

- **Date:** August 24, 2026 · **Surface:** `app/(tabs)/groceries.tsx` and the `getGroceryLists` client · **Found by:** Hans, while trying to open older lists for BUG-134's device pass.
- **Symptom, plainly:** lists past the first page **do not appear on the Groceries tab at all**, with no indication that anything was omitted. Search and A–Z sort cannot find them either, so the list looks genuinely absent rather than paged away.
- ⚠️ **HANS'S COMPOST / SOFT-DELETE HYPOTHESIS WAS REFUTED, AND THAT IS WORTH KEEPING.** All three missing lists are `status: active`, on `draft` plans with `isArchived: false`. **Nothing had been deleted or composted — the rows were simply past the page boundary.** A plausible destructive explanation for a missing row was wrong; the boring pagination one was right.
- **Stopgap shipped `cc90e95`:** `getGroceryLists` now sends `?limit=100`, the server's own clamp ceiling. ⚠️ **The test asserts the literal `100` rather than reading `GROCERY_LIST_PAGE_LIMIT` — deliberately, because deriving the expectation from the same constant the code uses moves both sides at once and pins nothing (§27.4).** Deliberate break (removing the `limit` line, proven applied at `groceries.ts:110`) turned all three tests red; restore `cmp`-identical, hash back to `7063f5d3…`, zero `BREAK-` markers left. Client typecheck clean, 1169 tests / 0 fail.
- ⚠️ **STILL OPEN, AND THE STOPGAP CANNOT REACH IT: THE CAP IS SILENT.** At 101 lists the truncation recurs with no warning at all. CC declined to add a dev warning rather than invent a logging convention in that file — correct call, but it leaves the failure mode intact.
- ⚠️ **INFINITE SCROLL WAS CONSIDERED AND CORRECTLY DECLINED — keep this reasoning, it will come up again.** Two blockers: **(1)** the screen cannot drive it — `groceries.tsx` is `<Screen>` → `ScrollView` → `{sorted.map(...)}` with no `FlatList` and no `onEndReached`, so paging means restructuring the screen (header, search box and sort chips into a `ListHeaderComponent`). **(2)** it would not fix what Hans actually hit: **sort and search are PAGE-LOCAL** (`lists.filter(...)` then `[...filtered].sort(...)`), so with infinite scroll a search would still miss any list the user had not manually scrolled past. **The real fix needs SERVER-SIDE sort and search, not just paging.**
- **Status:** 🟡 **STOPGAP SHIPPED `cc90e95` (local, unpushed at time of writing) — root fix not commissioned** · **Priority:** **P2** · **Target:** its own block — server-side list sort, search and paging · **Cross-ref:** BUG-134 · BUG-140 · BUG-141.

### BUG-141 — Add-item search offers one catalog row at a fixed pack unit, so "1 roma tomato" can only be added as "1 lb roma tomatoes"

- **Date:** August 24, 2026 · **Surface:** grocery list add-item predictive search, mobile · **Found by:** Hans, BUG-134 device pass, item 6/7.
- **Symptom, plainly:** Hans wanted to add **one roma tomato**. The predictive search returned **exactly one selectable candidate — `1 lb roma tomatoes`** — and adding it wrote a new produce row in pounds. **There was no way to choose the count unit, no way to adjust the quantity at add time, and no way to add an item that is not already in the catalog.**
- **Three distinct defects wearing one symptom, and they should be separated at Phase 0:**
  1. **Only one candidate is returned** where the catalog demonstrably holds several roma-tomato rows (the duplicate groups in BUG-134 prove it). Either the search caps results or it filters harder than intended.
  2. **The unit comes fixed to the catalog row's pack** (`lb`), with no count-unit alternative offered — even though the very list being edited already carried `roma tomatoes` in **`each`**. ⚠️ **The two adds therefore land in DIFFERENT buckets, which is why item 7's same-bucket duplicate-add test could not actually be performed.**
  3. **No off-catalog add.** A user cannot type a free-text item the catalog does not know. ⚠️ **This is a real product gap for a grocery list — Extras are explicitly the user's own lane (D-WS9-183), and a lane that only accepts catalog rows is not that lane.**
- ⚠️ **Do NOT "fix" this by making the add path merge into an existing bucket.** That was ruled against in **D-WS9-183**: a user-added row legitimately sits beside a generated one, and the add route's lack of a bucket check is deliberate, not a defect.
- 🟡 **PARTIALLY CLOSED September 8, 2026 (Lane B, committed locally, DEVICE-UNPROVEN) — and the three defects came apart exactly as this entry said they would.**
  - ✅ **Defect 3 (off-catalog add) FIXED, CLIENT-ONLY.** 🔴 **THE LANE ALREADY EXISTED AND WAS UNREACHABLE:** `performAdd(null, text)` was there all along, but `handleAddItem` auto-picks `candidates[0]` whenever one is present — **so Hans typing "roma tomato" and getting exactly one row back had no route to it.** An always-present `Add "…" as typed` row now sits at the foot of the suggestions. ⚠️ **`emptyMessage` on the shared `TypeaheadList` is consequently dead for this consumer (`items.length` is never 0). Intended — a visible control beats a hint — and it changes no other consumer.**
  - ⚠️ **Defect 2 (unit fixed to the pack) MITIGATED, NOT CLOSED.** The row can be edited to `each` after adding, so the two adds can be merged by hand, **but the add path still takes the catalog row's pack.** Choosing it properly means knowing which alternatives the catalog row supports — **which is defect 1's question, so this closes when defect 1 does.**
  - 🔴 **Defect 1 (only one candidate) NOT FIXED AND NOT EXAMINED — CROSS-LANE, CORRECTLY REFUSED.** The client sends no limit; `GET /grocery-items/lookup?q=` alone decides how many candidates return, and that route lives in `artifacts/api-server/`, which the concurrent lane was actively editing. **CC stopped rather than absorbing it, and reserved no ID.** ⚠️ **"The catalog holds several roma-tomato rows" is still Hans's observation carried forward, NOT something anyone has confirmed in the route.**
- 🔴 **A NEW RISK THIS FIX CREATES, GATED ON THE DEVICE PASS AND NOT YET ANSWERED: making the free-text lane reachable may make CATALOG POLLUTION reachable with it.** **BUG-231's mechanism is the intake minting a typed name as its own catalog row.** If `performAdd(null, text)` writes to the shared `Ingredient` catalog rather than creating a personal extra, **every user typo becomes permanent product data every future user inherits** — which D-WS9-230's carve-out puts squarely in scope. ⚠️ **The device discriminator needs no DB access: add a nonsense name on one list, then search for it on a DIFFERENT list. Found = it minted a catalog row and this is a push blocker; not found = it correctly stayed an extra.**
- ✅ **DEFECT 3 DEVICE-PASSED, Android, September 8, 2026 — the `Add "…" as typed` row works.** 🔴 **AND THE CATALOG-POLLUTION RISK IS MEASURED AND CLEARED: a deliberate nonsense name (`zzqx test item`) added on one list did NOT come back when searched from a DIFFERENT list.** ✅ **So `performAdd(null, text)` creates a PERSONAL EXTRA, not a shared `Ingredient` row — making the lane reachable did not widen BUG-231. That was this block's only push-blocker candidate and it is closed.**
- **Status:** 🟢 **DEFECT 3 FIXED AND DEVICE-PROVEN · DEFECT 2 MITIGATED · DEFECT 1 OPEN, BLOCKED CROSS-LANE** · **Priority:** **P2** · **Target:** defect 1 rides with the next server grocery block · **Cross-ref:** BUG-124 · BUG-134 · BUG-140 · BUG-117 · **BUG-231** (the pollution mechanism) · D-WS9-183 · D-WS9-230.

### BUG-143 — `packsToCoverNeed` could not relate an oz need to an lb pack

- **Date:** August 25, 2026 · **Surface:** client render, `grocery.ts:329-372` · **Status:** ✅ **FIXED (local, unpushed) — device-verified item 8** · **Priority:** **P2** · **Cross-ref:** BUG-125 · BUG-146 · BUG-147 · D-WS9-186.
- **Symptom:** a weight need and a weight pack in *different tokens* could not be related — `packsToCoverNeed` returned `null` and fell through. 35 live rows, all `oz` → `lb`.
- ⚠️ **THE FACTOR WAS NEVER MISSING — IT WAS LEFT BEHIND.** `WEIGHT_UNIT_TO_GRAMS` (`ingredientConversions.ts:88-105`) has carried the constants server-side all along; **the render moved client-side in BUG-125/Root A and did not bring them.** That reframed the work from *author data* to *share a table*.
- **The fix:** four canonical tokens mirrored client-side (not the server's fifteen — `normalizeUnitToken` has already folded the rest), with `ingredientConversions.ts` named as source of truth per the hand-synced-mirror precedent in `lib/api/*`. The weight rule sits **ahead of** `packSizeHint`, because a direct weight relation is exact while the hint is parsed from authored prose.
- ⚠️ **IT CHANGES ZERO LIVE ROWS TODAY, AND CC MEASURED THAT RATHER THAN ASSERTING IT.** Pre-change module extracted from HEAD, both composers run over all **2,061** live rows: **10 order lines change, all 10 from BUG-144, 0 from this.** Every one of the 35 rows needs ≤1 pack today. **Purely forward-looking — the D-WS9-186 shape exactly.** Device item 8 confirmed no visible change.
- **Pinned by three breaks**, of which ⚠️ **the good one is the DRIFT break: `lb` 453.59237 → 453.0, a 0.13% change, goes red at the 16 oz boundary — proving the constant's VALUE is pinned, not merely its presence.**
- **Two deliberate limits, now logged: BUG-146** (`fl oz` would misroute volume into the weight table) and **BUG-147** (weight need vs *container* pack still under-orders; pinned as guard W7).

- **Date:** August 25, 2026 · **Surface:** `UNIT_ALIASES`, `grocery.ts:282-295` · **Found by:** CC, **flagged against its own change rather than left silent.**

### BUG-147 — A weight need against a CONTAINER pack still under-orders

- **Date:** August 25, 2026 · **Surface:** `packsToCoverNeed` · **Found by:** CC, **declined deliberately and pinned as guard W7 so the choice stays visible.**
- **Symptom:** BUG-143 relates weight↔weight but not a weight need to a pack expressed as a container carrying a weight — `"1 package (12 oz)"` against a **2 lb** need. ⚠️ **A real UNDER-ORDER, the ruled-worst direction.**
- **Why declined:** widening moves rows outside the measured 35-row population, and the scope guard's whole value was that 0 rows outside it changed. Needs its own before/after.
- **Likely shape:** `packSizeHint` already parses `"(12 oz)"`; the gap is relating that parsed weight through the gram table rather than only by token equality.
- 🔴 **IT IS A CLIENT BUG, AND CHAT-CLAUDE SCOPED IT INTO THE SERVER LANE BY MISTAKE (September 8, 2026).** The only fix site is `packsToCoverNeed` at **`artifacts/kiwi/lib/format/grocery.ts:466`** — the MOBILE tree. ✅ **A subagent verified independently that no server-side equivalent exists: `scalePurchaseForSubUnit` is ladder-only with no weight branch, and `totalBought` is weight-aware but is a RANKING criterion that never becomes an order quantity. The server passes the stored pack through unscaled.**
- 🔴 **THE 125-ROW / 14-UNDER-ORDER MEASUREMENT WAS MEASURING THE WRONG LAYER, AND CC CAUGHT IT WHILE FIXING THE TICKET (September 9).** **The server passes the stored pack through UNSCALED; the CLIENT scales it at render.** ✅ **So a population counted server-side counts under-orders THE CLIENT HAD ALREADY FIXED ON SCREEN.** **Both headline rows already scaled correctly before anything was touched: `crushed tomatoes` 28 oz vs `1 can (14.5 oz)` → 2 cans; `fresh baby spinach` 10 oz vs `1 container (5 oz)` → 2 containers. Guard W8 has pinned that path since BUG-143.**
- 🔴 **AND CHAT-CLAUDE HAD THE REFUTING EVIDENCE IN HAND AND EXPLAINED IT AWAY.** Hans's own screenshot shows that spinach row rendering as **`2 containers (5 oz)`**, and this entry recorded it as *"a different list instance, not this one."* **It was not a different instance — it was the client doing the arithmetic the server-side census could not see. §27.6 item 5 again, and this time the tell was a contradiction that was noticed and rationalised rather than followed.**
- ⚠️ **THE ORIGINAL FIGURE, KEPT SO NOBODY RE-DERIVES IT AS NEW: 125 rows / 14 "under-orders"** — `crushed tomatoes` 28 oz vs `1 can (14.5 oz)`, `crushed san marzano tomatoes` 28 oz vs `1 can (14.5 oz)`, `fresh baby spinach` 10 oz vs `1 container (5 oz)`, among others. ⚠️ **The other 111 are covered by a single pack — RIGHT BY LUCK, NOT BY ARITHMETIC, so the population is the exposure and 14 is only today's realisation of it.**
- ✅ **FIXED September 9 (mobile lane, LOCAL) — THREE NARROWER THINGS WERE GENUINELY BROKEN, none of them the headline:**
  1. **Same system, different spelling** — a need in `lb` against a hint in `oz` required an exact token match. 🔴 **This was old guard W7, which ASSERTED THE UNDER-ORDER AND SAID SO IN ITS OWN TEXT:** *"a real under-order… deliberately NOT fixed here… Pinned so the next person sees the choice was made."* ✅ **A guard that pins a DEFERRAL rather than a behaviour, and it worked exactly as intended — the assertion was updated, not worked around.**
  2. **Two-word units** — `(16 fl oz)` parsed the unit as `fl`.
  3. **Volume, entirely** — a weight table existed and no volume table did.
  Plus a measured unit leading the display (`1 lb block`, `purchaseUnit: "block"`), taking the precedence lesson from the server's `packMagnitude`, read as a reference only.
- **Rows that move:** same-system-different-spelling pairs · packs sized `N fl oz` · volumetric packs · packs whose measurement leads the display. **Rows that do NOT:** no stated size, and **any weight-vs-volume pair — that needs a per-ingredient density the client is never sent, and guard V6 pins the refusal with an absurd need so it cannot pass by luck.**
- ⚠️ **`quart` and `gallon` were added to `COUNT_NOUN_PLURALS` (`pint` was already there) because the volume work made those packs REACHABLE for the first time — without it, `2 quart` would have shipped as a NEW visible defect.** **A fix that makes a dormant path live owes a sweep of what that path now renders.**
- ⚠️ **POPULATION NOT RE-MEASURED.** The mechanisms were verified on constructed rows; **the 125 live rows were never enumerated, so how many of the 14 actually move is unknown.** **8 new guards V1–V8 plus the updated W7.**
- **Status:** 🟡 **FIXED IN THE WORKING TREE, DEVICE-UNPROVEN (mobile lane, September 9, LOCAL)** · **Priority:** **P2** · **Target:** done, pending a device pass · **Cross-ref:** BUG-143 · BUG-155 · BUG-138 (same misrouting) · BUG-117.

### BUG-150 — A recurring item and a meal need for the same ingredient collapse into ONE row, losing the meal's quantity

- **Date:** August 25, 2026 · **Surface:** grocery generation / consolidation · **Found by:** Hans, Root-B device pass item 1, **reproduced twice.**
- **Symptom, plainly:** ⚠️ **the shopper is told to buy less than the plan needs.** With `bananas` set as a recurring preference and **1 banana in a meal**, using *Use Again* on the plan and regenerating produced a list showing **only `1 banana (recurring)`** — the meal's need was absorbed into the recurring row rather than added to it.
- ⚠️ **IT IS INCONSISTENT, WHICH IS THE STRONGEST CLUE.** Hans added a **custom `lime`** to recurring preferences and got the correct behaviour — **two separate rows, `2 limes` for the meal and `1 lime` recurring.** ⚠️ **Bananas collapse; limes do not. Find what differs — seeded-vs-custom recurring entry, or an ingredient-id match that succeeds for one and not the other.**
- ⚠️ **PLAUSIBLY THE SAME MISSING INVARIANT AS BUG-142, IN THE OPPOSITE DIRECTION.** BUG-142 is a merge that **gains** quantity; this is a merge that **loses** it. **BUG-142 established there is NO conservation guard anywhere — the only no-add invariant guards `items.length`, a COUNT, and its own test title carves quantity out by name.** A merge with no quantity invariant can drift either way. **Probe them together.**
- **Hans's own read:** *"I honestly think this is a really narrow almost invalid use case."* ⚠️ **Recorded, and respectfully weighted against the fact that it under-orders — the ruled-worst direction on this surface — and that it reproduced twice.**
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN** · **Priority:** **P2** · **Cross-ref:** BUG-142 · BUG-148 · BUG-151 · D-WS9-188.

### BUG-155 — `"1 lime fresh lime juice (3 tablespoon)"` — a derived product orders its parent without a yield conversion

- **Date:** August 25, 2026 · **Surface:** grocery list detail · **Found by:** Hans, device pass item 5.
- **Symptom:** a need of **3 tbsp** of `fresh lime juice` renders as **`1 lime`**. Hans flagged it as *"small and low priority unless it's a symptom of something bigger."*
- ⚠️ **IT IS A SYMPTOM OF SOMETHING BIGGER — IT IS BUG-133's DERIVATION MODEL, ALREADY RULED IN D-WS9-182.** Under that ruling's sourced table a lime yields **2 tbsp**, so 3 tbsp needs **1.5 limes → 2**. **`1 lime` UNDER-ORDERS**, the ruled-worst direction.
- ✅ **NO NEW RULING NEEDED — D-WS9-182's fractional component-pool model already covers it exactly.** This is a live instance confirming the model is needed, not a new question. **Hans's "1 bottle fresh lime juice" alternative is a different product (bottled juice is its own catalog item) and should not be conflated with juicing a lime.**
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN — no new ruling required** · **Priority:** **P2** · **Target:** BUG-133 · **Cross-ref:** BUG-133 · BUG-147 · D-WS9-182.

### BUG-157 — `neutral[600]` is used as body text in places where it fails AA contrast

- **Date:** August 25, 2026 · **Surface:** design tokens, app-wide · **Found by:** CC, while fixing BUG-154 — **surfaced as a scope question rather than silently widened.**
- **Symptom:** `neutral[600]` `#8A8474` measures **3.7278:1** on the white card. That **clears 3:1 for non-text UI** but **fails the 4.5:1 AA threshold for normal text.** It is the **LOCKED muted-text token**, read by roughly **20 placeholders** and — ⚠️ **critically — by real text, including meal descriptions at `MealRow.tsx:81-85`.**
- ⚠️ **TWO DISTINCT QUESTIONS WEARING ONE TOKEN, AND CONFLATING THEM IS THE TRAP.** (1) **Placeholders** — arguably non-text UI, so 3:1 may be defensible; the counter-evidence is that **an outside tester missed a field entirely** (BUG-154). (2) **Body text at `neutral[600]`** — that is straightforwardly below AA and is not a matter of taste.
- ⚠️ **AND BUG-153's REUSE TARGET PROPAGATES IT.** `MealRow.tsx:81-85` is the pattern CC proposes reusing verbatim for the new subtext on two more screens (correct per §27.2). **Reusing it as-is ships below-AA body text on two additional surfaces.**
- **Three candidate dispositions, unruled:** change `neutral[600]`'s value (widest blast radius, and it is a locked token) · add a distinct **placeholder** semantic token at `neutral[700]` and repoint placeholders only (mechanical, leaves body-text uses untouched) · audit text-vs-non-text uses and move only the text ones.
⚠️ **MEASURED AGAINST THE LOCKED TOKEN FILE (chat-Claude, August 31 — computed from `design-tokens_4.ts`, full precision, both backgrounds):**

| foreground | on paper `#FBF7EF` | on white card | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| `neutral[600]` `#8A8474` (LOCKED muted) | **3.4886** | **3.7278** | non-text only; **fails AA for text on both** |
| `neutral[700]` `#6B5E4D` | 5.8956 | 6.2999 | passes AA |
| `neutral[800]` `#4A3F30` | 9.6107 | 10.2697 | passes; rejected at BUG-154 as too loud |
| `Palette.text.placeholder` `#A89A7A` | **2.5967** | **2.7748** | **fails even the 3:1 non-text floor** |

✅ **THE TWO FIGURES IN CANON WERE BOTH RIGHT AND ARE NOT A CONTRADICTION** — BUG-106's 3.49 is on **paper**, BUG-154/157's 3.7278 is on the **white card**. Recorded so nobody re-litigates it.

🔴 **A SEMANTIC PLACEHOLDER TOKEN ALREADY EXISTS AND IS WORSE THAN THE THING IT WOULD REPLACE.** `Palette.text.placeholder = '#A89A7A'` is defined in the locked file at **2.60:1 / 2.77:1** — below even the 3:1 non-text floor — while the ~20 live placeholders read `neutral[600]` instead. ⚠️ **THE TRAP THIS SETS: a sweep told to "repoint placeholders to the placeholder token" would make every one of them WORSE while appearing to fix them.** Any build here must set that token's VALUE, not merely point at it.
⚠️ **AND THE LOCKED FILE CONTRADICTS ITSELF ON THIS EXACT QUESTION:** `Palette.text.placeholder` says `#A89A7A` while `Components.tellKiwi.inputPlaceholder` says `Colors.neutral[600]`. **Two placeholder answers in one file** — the same one-mechanism-two-questions shape as `STAPLE_VARIANT_TO_BASE` (D-WS9-189).

⚠️ **THE COST ARGUMENT THAT SHOULD DRIVE THE DISPOSITION: the expensive half is IDENTICAL in all three options.** Every option requires enumerating and classifying each `neutral[600]` read as text vs non-text; only the trivial half (which colour each class lands on) differs. **Ruling for only placeholders (2) or only body text (3) pays that enumeration once and banks half the value, then pays it again later.**
⚠️ **WHAT OPTION 1 ACTUALLY COSTS, and it is a DESIGN consequence not a technical one:** `neutral[600]` is the quiet tier of a deliberate three-tier hierarchy (ink `neutral[900]` → secondary `neutral[700]` → muted `neutral[600]`). Raising it to pass AA lands it beside `neutral[700]`, **collapsing three tiers to two** — and it also darkens things that are meant to recede: the italic-dash section labels (`Components.sectionLabel.color`) and Cook Mode's dimmed next-step preview (`Palette.cookMode.nextPreview`). **A1 locked this value June 12; changing it is Hans's design call, not a technical one.**
- **Adjacent, same class, natural fold-in:** BUG-106's other half — `Palette.button.primary.text` on `terracotta[400]` measures **4.4273**, **0.07 under AA**, on the shared primary button.
✅ **RULED BY HANS, August 31, 2026 — THE HYBRID (options 2 + 3 together).** `neutral[600]` **KEEPS its value and its quiet-tier role** (section labels, Cook Mode's dimmed next-step preview, genuinely decorative muted UI stay exactly as designed — the A1 three-tier hierarchy is preserved). **Placeholders get a dedicated semantic token with a CORRECTED VALUE** — ⚠️ **not merely a repoint at the existing `text.placeholder`, whose `#A89A7A` is WORSE than what it replaces.** **Genuine body text moves to `neutral[700]`** (5.90 / 6.30 — the value BUG-154 already shipped for the one field Hans ruled on, chosen there over `neutral[800]` as *"just a bit darker, not super standout"*).
**Why the hybrid rather than 2 or 3 alone (the argument Hans ruled on):** the expensive half — enumerating every `neutral[600]` read and classifying it text vs non-text — is **identical in all three options**; only the trivial half differs. Ruling for one half banks half the value and pays that enumeration twice. **Option 1 was declined on its design consequence:** raising the locked token to pass AA lands it beside `neutral[700]` and collapses three text tiers to two.
**Build notes:** known text sites to move — `MealRow.tsx:81-85` (meal descriptions) plus BUG-153's two recorded sweep sites `PlanReviewMealRow.tsx:358` and `AddMealsSheet.tsx:341`. Resolve the locked file's self-contradiction in the same pass (`Palette.text.placeholder` vs `Components.tellKiwi.inputPlaceholder`). **Fold in BUG-106's other half** (primary-button text at 4.4273, 0.07 under AA). ⚠️ **The enumeration is the deliverable — every `neutral[600]` read classified, not just the ones already named here.**
- **Status:** 🟡 **RULED, unbuilt** · **Priority:** **P2** · **Target:** rides the mobile fix pass (the ruling lands now because BUG-153's reuse pattern is actively propagating below-AA body text to new surfaces) · **Cross-ref:** BUG-152 · BUG-153 · BUG-154 · BUG-156 · **BUG-106** (folded in).

### BUG-160 — `"1 pepper orange bell pepper (1 pepper)"` — the pack residue is prepended AND repeated

### BUG-161 — `1 lemon` and `2 fresh lemons` sit as separate rows

- **Date:** August 26, 2026 · **Surface:** grocery list · **Found by:** Hans, Block D device pass item 10.
- ⚠️ **HANS READ THIS AS A PLURALISATION EXCEPTION AND IT IS NOT — IT IS CATALOG SYNONYM FRAGMENTATION, THE BUG-137 CLASS.** His words: *"just another exception to map, right?"* **No: `lemon` and `fresh lemons` are two DIFFERENT catalog rows for one food**, exactly like `garlic` / `garlic cloves`. No pluralisation map can merge them, because the singulariser is not the thing keeping them apart — **their `ingredientId`s differ, so they bucket separately by construction.**
- **The fix is a synonym fold**, and it inherits BUG-137's blocker: ⚠️ **the bucket key includes `unit`, so a fold alone can leave two rows on the same list unless unit-normalisation lands first.**
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN** · **Priority:** **P2** · **Target:** with BUG-137's fold · **Cross-ref:** BUG-133 · BUG-137 · D-WS9-182.

### BUG-162 — Duplicate meals in My Meals

- **Date:** August 26, 2026 · **Surface:** Recipes → My Meals · **Found by:** chat-Claude, reading Hans's screenshot 2 — **not reported by Hans.**
- **Symptom:** *"Air Fryer Crispy Chicken Tenders with Honey Mustard and a Simple Green Salad"* appears **twice**, both `28 min · serves 4 · American`. A near-twin (*"…with Honey Mustard Dipping Sauce"*, 35 min) sits above them, so the catalog is also accumulating near-duplicates.
- ⚠️ **UNVERIFIED AS TO CAUSE — this is an observation from a screenshot, not a probe.** Two genuinely distinct meal rows, or one row rendered twice, are different bugs. **Establish which before designing anything.**
- ⚠️ **If it is two rows, it is the meal-level analogue of the ingredient-level fragmentation this whole arc has been unwinding**, and it matters more once wizard meals enter the shared catalog (see BUG-156).
- ✅ **CAUSE ESTABLISHED (Probe 1, August 26): distinct rows, no existing-copy guard on the clone path — and NOT historical.** Three distinct ids behind the screenshot (Hans's pair was created 3 minutes apart on July 12 — he was right about that pair, wrong about the general claim: **3 excess rows were created August 26 itself**, and the by-date breakout runs July 1 → August 26 with no stopping point). Population: **34 duplicate groups · 76 rows · 42 excess of 311 user meals.**
- **Mechanism:** `forkMealForUser → cloneMealInto` (`mealFork.ts:116-123`) deep-copies Meal + Dish + links + ingredients + steps and stamps `sourceStoreMealId: sourceMealId` — **and nothing anywhere reads it as a dedupe key** (two search shapes, one ignore-blind; the only near-hit is a comment). Proof row: pool meal `9e894ce4` forked twice, 30 days apart, the second on August 26. `dishFamilyKey` is no help — only `batch_generated` meals carry it.
- **Group anatomy: 16 groups are pure re-forks sharing one `sourceStoreMealId` (dedupable today, no schema change) · 18 involve a lineage-less wizard row (ties to BUG-156's catalog question) · 0 independent-generation collisions.**
- ✅ **FIX RULED (chat-Claude technical call, August 27): existence guard in `forkMealForUser` on `(userId, sourceStoreMealId)` — reuse the existing fork, including a previously customized copy** (matches what re-adding a meal should mean; Hans may override). The 42 historical excess rows stay per D-WS9-186. **Build follows Probe Block 2**, because the wizard-lineage half of the population depends on BUG-156's A4 answer.
⚠️ **SCOPE CORRECTION (Probe 2 audit, August 27) — THE RULED GUARD COVERS 16 OF 34 GROUPS, NOT ALL OF THEM.** Probe 2 established why the other 18 carry no lineage: they involve **live-generated** wizard meals, which by construction have no source store meal — and ⚠️ **the pool copy the publish path creates for them carries NULL `sourceStoreMealId` and NULL `dishFamilyKey` BY DESIGN** (`mealFork.test.ts:464`; see BUG-156). **A `(userId, sourceStoreMealId)` guard cannot see a NULL on either side.**
- ⚠️ **CANDIDATE MECHANISM FOR THE OTHER 18 — chat-Claude's audit, UNVERIFIED, and Phase 0 Q2 tests it:** a user's live-generated meal is published to the pool as an unlinked copy → that copy is later served back to **the same user** via store-fill → they fork it → **nothing can tell it is the meal they already have.** The test is whether each group's NEWER row carries a `sourceStoreMealId` pointing at a `live_writeback` meal matching its OLDER lineage-less twin. **If it holds, the fix needs a second identity signal, not a wider guard.**
- ⚠️ **THE CENSUS ALSO SIZES THE FORK POPULATION THE GUARD MUST COVER, AND CC LEFT IT UNREMARKED:** `batch_generated` 1,235 total / 1,124 public+catalog → **111 user-owned**; `live_writeback` 62 / 51 → **11 user-owned**. Those 122 rows can only be forks. Phase 0 Q3a confirms.
⚠️ **PHASE 0 SHARPENED THIS AGAIN, AND THE RULED GUARD IS NARROWER THAN EITHER PRIOR ESTIMATE: IT FIXES 16 OF 42 GROUPS AND *ZERO* OF THE 9 BOOMERANGS.**

**The census moved in ONE DAY** — user meals 311 → **359**, duplicate groups 34 → **42**, rows/excess 76/42 → **97/55**. Two of the five stamped boomerangs were created during the August 26 test run. ⚠️ **Any before/after measurement must pin a timestamp; this population is not static.**

**Three distinct mechanisms, now separated:**
1. **16 groups — re-forks** sharing one `sourceStoreMealId`. **The ruled `(userId, sourceStoreMealId)` guard fixes exactly these.**
2. **9 groups — the BOOMERANG, hypothesis CONFIRMED from both directions.** A user's live-generated meal is published to the pool 1–3s later by `publishMealToStore`, then **re-acquired by the same user** days later via store-fill. Direction A found 5 stamped instances with ids and timestamps; direction B enumerated all 51 pool meals independently and agreed exactly — **51/51 have a user-owned same-title parent 1–5s earlier, 0 orphans**, 5 stamped + 4 unstamped boomerangs (the 4 predate the `sourceStoreMealId` migration `20260724213633`, so they are provably forks with the column simply not yet written). **True boomerang population: 9 of 51 pool meals (17.6%).**
   - ⚠️ **WHY THE GUARD IS BLIND TO ALL NINE: in every boomerang the user's ORIGINAL carries `sourceStoreMealId` NULL** — it was live-generated, a fork of nothing. A guard asking *"do I already have a fork of this pool meal?"* correctly answers **no**. It cannot see the original.
3. **20 groups — a THIRD mechanism, and the largest bucket. See BUG-166** — it includes the very pair from Hans's founding screenshot (3 minutes apart, 2026-07-12).

**What identity signal exists today to link a live-generated meal to its own pool copy: none.** `sourceStoreMealId` is NULL on the pool copy **by design** (`mealFork.ts:212`; asserted `mealFork.test.ts:477`). `dishFamilyKey` is never written on any clone (0 hits in `mealFork.ts`; sole writers `mealMaterialize.ts:286` and `storeFill.ts:474`) and is NULL on all 97 duplicate rows. Title + `createdAt` proximity is the **only** thing that works — it matched 51/51 with zero orphans — but it is a heuristic, not an identity, and nothing in the product uses it. ⚠️ **The link exists in memory at `wizardActivation.ts:382`, where `publishMealToStore(tx, mealId)` holds the source id and receives the pool id — and persists neither direction.**

✅ **Negative claim re-verified two shapes at Phase 0:** nothing reads `sourceStoreMealId` as a dedupe key — query-shaped probe (`grep -B4 -A1 … | grep -ci 'where|findFirst|findUnique'`) returns **RAW COUNT: 0**, every one of 16 file hits classified.

- **Fix ruled (chat-Claude, August 27):** keep the `(userId, sourceStoreMealId)` guard for bucket 1, and for bucket 2 persist the discarded link as a **NEW column on the pool copy (`originUserMealId`)** — ⚠️ **NOT by overloading `sourceStoreMealId`**, which already means *"I was forked from this,"* is read by the rotation hop at `planningContext.ts:434`, and would be the line-824 two-sources-of-truth pattern (§10). Additive, so `mealFork.test.ts:477` stays true.
- **Status:** 🟡 **IN-PROGRESS — three mechanisms separated; ruled guard covers bucket 1 only; build deferred to the duplicate-meal block so `mealFork.ts` is opened ONCE with all three known** · **Priority:** **P2** · **Cross-ref:** BUG-156 · BUG-161 · BUG-163 · **BUG-166** · D-WS9-186.

### BUG-164 — The synthetic recurring-item bucket collides with the plan-derived row for the same ingredient

- **Date:** August 27, 2026 · **Surface:** grocery generation, `groceryList.ts:498` · **Found by:** CC, Phase 0, **while running down BUG-142's mechanism — surfaced against a chat-Claude hypothesis that was otherwise refuted.**
- **Symptom:** `whole milk` persists as **`1 each` AND `3 tablespoon`** — two rows, same ingredient — on **all seven** lists generated August 26. `bananas` and `lime` split the same way on three.
- ⚠️ **THE STATED MECHANISM IS REFUTED (build block, August 27). The `"each"` collision is UNREACHABLE BY CONSTRUCTION** — `buckets.has(norm|"each")` is only reached *after* the unit-agnostic name loop found no match, and any bucket at `norm|<anything>` necessarily has a `canonicalName` normalizing to `norm`, so that loop would have matched and continued. **The hard-keyed unit was never the cause.**
- ✅ **THE REAL CAUSE IS A CANONICAL-*NAME* MISMATCH**, verified against live prefs `["egg","milk","bread","coffee","Pet treats","Paper towels","Toilet paper","Bananas"]`: `milk` (pref) vs `whole milk` (plan) → synthetic `milk 1 each` on **7 of 7** · `egg` vs `large eggs` → synthetic `egg 1 dozen` · `Bananas` matches correctly on 5 lists and is **correct behaviour** on the 2 where the plan has no bananas.
- ⚠️ **AND `bread` vs `plain breadcrumbs` IS THE PROOF THAT NO MECHANICAL NAME RULE IS SAFE HERE** — absorbing that pair would be flatly wrong. **Fixing the name match requires ruling whether a recurring item ABSORBS a meal's need, which is D-WS9-188 and is UNRULED. CC stopped, correctly, per instruction.**
- ⚠️ **CHAT-CLAUDE'S CITED EVIDENCE WAS CONTAMINATED AND IS RETRACTED.** The `limes` and duplicate-`bananas` rows quoted when this entry was minted **do not reproduce from the current consolidator** — no lime pref exists for any list owner. They are **stale persisted rows from an earlier prefs state: the same contamination class as BUG-148.** All conclusions above are drawn from the live consolidator, not from persisted rows.
- **What shipped `dbd389e`:** the key/unit fix only, and it is **behaviour-neutral — differentially proven** by reverting it and re-running the consolidator: all **482 rows across 7 plans byte-identical**. ⚠️ **Break 6 stayed GREEN and the assertion was the exact tautology the brief warned against** (it recomputed the key from the row's own fields, so the break could not move it). **CC DELETED the test rather than leave something that looked like coverage** — the correct call, and it means this change is unpinned by design, evidenced instead by the 482-row differential.
- ⚠️ **THIS IS WHERE CHAT-CLAUDE'S REFUTED RECURRING-ITEM LEAD ACTUALLY LANDED.** The hypothesis was wrong about BUG-142 — it was not a recurring quantity — but a real recurring-item collision exists, one bug over. **Worth remembering as a shape: a wrong hypothesis can still be pointing at something.**
- **Relationship to D-WS9-188:** that entry owns the *design* question — how a recurring item SHOULD relate to a meal's need. **This is the mechanical defect underneath it.** Fix the collision; do not settle the design here.
⚠️ **RE-ATTRIBUTED AGAIN, August 28, 2026 — THE CSV DUMP DISPROVES THE NAME-MISMATCH THEORY FOR MILK. THERE ARE TWO MECHANISMS, NOT ONE.**
  1. ⚠️ **SAME ingredient, split by UNIT.** On list `6900ccc0`: `Whole milk` (0.5 **cup**) and `milk` (1 **each**, recurring) carry the **IDENTICAL `ingredientId` `d10747ca`.** **The name resolution WORKED — the ids match.** The rows never merge because the bucket key includes `unit`, and `each` cannot convert to `cup`. ⚠️ **This is the SAME shape as the `hot sauce` pair the conservation guard leaves standing — not a lookup failure at all.**
  2. **Genuinely different ids.** `eggs` (`ff985025`) vs `Large eggs` (`179e8716`); `bread` (`8e7dc0de`) vs `italian bread loaf` (`616b839f`). **These are the canonical-name mismatch CC diagnosed — and they are relationship 1 (synonym) or relationship 3 (distinct), which is D-WS9-189's job to tell apart.**
- ⚠️ **CONSEQUENCE FOR THE FIX: a name-matching fix would not touch the milk case at all.** The synthetic recurring row is emitted at unit `each` under the preference's raw text, and buckets away from a resolved row of the same ingredient. **Any fix must reconcile the synthetic row's IDENTITY AND UNIT with the resolved ingredient, not just its name.**
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN — TWO mechanisms separated (unit-split on a shared id; and true id mismatch). Still blocked on D-WS9-188 for the design half.** · **Priority:** **P2** · **Cross-ref:** BUG-142 · BUG-148 · BUG-150 · BUG-165 · **D-WS9-188** · **D-WS9-189**.

### BUG-166 — Duplicate meals from a plan expanded twice — the largest duplicate bucket, and the one behind the original screenshot

- **Date:** August 27, 2026 · **Surface:** wizard expansion / activation · **Found by:** chat-Claude, auditing Phase 0 — **CC characterised this bucket in a single parenthetical and did not investigate it.**
- **Symptom:** **20 of BUG-162's 42 duplicate groups** carry `sourceStoreMealId` NULL on *every* row, so they are neither re-forks nor boomerangs. CC's one-line description: *"manual rows seconds apart (dev seed re-runs, 07-14/07-15) and wizard pairs ~3 min apart (same plan expanded twice)."*
- ⚠️ **THIS IS THE MECHANISM BEHIND HANS'S FOUNDING REPORT, AND NOBODY HAS LOOKED AT IT.** Probe 1 established that the pair in his screenshot was created **3 minutes apart** (00:17:40 / 00:20:30, 2026-07-12) — the exact signature of the "expanded twice" sub-bucket. **BUG-162's ruled guard and the boomerang fix both leave it untouched.**
- ⚠️ **IT IS ALSO THE BIGGEST BUCKET** — 20 groups against 16 re-forks and 9 boomerangs. **Scoping the duplicate work without characterising it first would fix the two smaller causes and leave the largest one shipping.**
- **What is not yet known:** whether the dev-seed sub-bucket and the double-expansion sub-bucket are one mechanism or two; whether expansion is genuinely non-idempotent or a draft is being activated twice; and whether **BUG-030**'s content-hash idempotency work already covers part of it (⚠️ **check before building — §27.2**).
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN — uncharacterised** · **Priority:** **P2** · **Target:** read-only characterisation opening the duplicate-meal block · **Cross-ref:** BUG-030 · BUG-162 · D-WS9-072 · D-WS9-186.

### BUG-167 — `partitionForAI` and `mergeConvertibleGroups` now disagree about ingredient identity

- **Date:** August 27, 2026 · **Surface:** `groceryListAI.ts` · `groceryMerge.ts` · **Found by:** CC, self-reported against its own change in the grocery-correctness block.
- **Symptom:** none today. `partitionForAI` rule 3 still keys on raw **`canonicalName`**; `mergeConvertibleGroups` now keys on **`baseStapleName`** after BUG-142's fix. **Two functions that must agree about what counts as "the same ingredient" no longer do.**
- ⚠️ **BENIGN ONLY BECAUSE OF EXECUTION ORDER — the merge runs first and collapses the variants, so the partition never sees the disagreement. NOTHING PINS THAT ORDER.** Reorder them, or add a third caller, and the divergence becomes live.
- ⚠️ **THIS IS THE §10 TWO-SOURCES-OF-TRUTH SHAPE, and it was created by the fix rather than found in old code** — which is exactly when it is cheapest to close. **Replace, don't layer: one identity function, both callers.**
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN — latent** · **Priority:** **P3** · **Target:** next time either file is opened · **Cross-ref:** BUG-142 · BUG-168.

### BUG-173 — `composeStoreMealDetails` also drops `displayTitle`

- **Date:** August 27, 2026 · **Surface:** `src/lib/store/storeMealDetails.ts` · **Found by:** CC, small-block build — **self-reported while fixing the same function for BUG-163.**
- **Symptom:** the same field-by-field composition that dropped `description` also omits `displayTitle`. **Same function, same gap, same class.** Not fixed — out of that block's scope.
- ⚠️ **DORMANT, NOT HARMLESS — AND BUG-067 IS THE STANDING GUARD THAT SAYS WHY.** `displayTitle` is null on 100% of records today, so nothing renders wrong. **If it is ever populated, store-composed meals will silently not carry it** — and the field would appear to work everywhere except that path.
- ⚠️ **THE PATTERN IS WORTH MORE THAN THE FIELD: a hand-written field-by-field composer silently drops every field nobody remembered.** `description` was found only because a user noticed blank subtext. **Neither the Prisma `select` nor the return object is derived from the type, so the compiler cannot help.** A third field will be next.
- **Also unchecked:** non-wizard callers of `composeStoreMealDetails` (swap, add-meal) for the same class of drop.
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN — dormant** · **Priority:** **P3** · **Cross-ref:** BUG-067 · BUG-163 · D-WS9-121.

### BUG-175 — Sixteen `IngredientAlias` rows are permanently unreachable, and one contradicts a Hans ruling

- **Date:** August 28, 2026 · **Surface:** `IngredientAlias` / `ingredientLookup.ts:104-118` · **Found by:** CC, D-WS9-189 Phase 0.
- **Symptom:** of 140 alias rows across 106 ingredients, **16 have an `aliasKey` that is ALSO some other row's `canonicalName`.** `lookupIngredientByName`'s ruled precedence is **canonical beats alias, unconditionally** — so step 2 is never reached and those 16 aliases can never resolve.
- **The inert set is almost exactly Hans's own cases:** `salt → kosher salt` · `sugar → granulated sugar` · `white sugar → granulated sugar` · `ground black pepper → black pepper` · `freshly ground black pepper → black pepper` · `cheddar cheese → sharp cheddar` · `egg → large eggs` · and 9 more.
- ⚠️ **THE FINDING THAT MATTERS FOR D-WS9-189: RELATIONSHIP 1 HAS BEEN ATTEMPTED BEFORE, BY SOMEONE, AND SILENTLY FAILED.** These rows are authored synonym assertions that do nothing. **A program that "just populates the alias table" would be repeating a move the catalog has already made and quietly lost.**
- ⚠️ **AND ONE OF THEM IS NOW WRONG BY RULING: `salt → kosher salt` contradicts Hans's August 27 call** that different salts are different products. **It is inert, so it causes no live defect — but it would become one the moment aliases were made reachable.**
- ⚠️ **STRUCTURAL CONSEQUENCE (D-WS9-189's Q2 answer): `IngredientAlias` maps a STRING to a ROW. Relationship 1 holds between TWO ROWS that both exist and are both referenced by dishes.** An alias can only express that by **deleting the loser row** — destructive, irreversible, and the BUG-096 pattern. **Right substrate for name→row; wrong substrate for row→row.**
- **Collision policy, verified:** silent **last-write-wins** at both writers — `prisma/seed.ts:566` upserts on `aliasKey` with `update: { ingredientId }`, and the BUG-096 merge script deliberately re-points. ⚠️ **No P2002, no skip — an authoring pass could silently steal an alias from a prior assertion with no error.**
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN — inert today** · **Priority:** **P3** · **Cross-ref:** BUG-096 · D-WS9-176 · D-WS9-177 · **D-WS9-189**.
