### BUG-177 — Recurring "Milk" never matches the plan's `whole milk` bucket and appends a synthetic `1 each` row — ⚠️ **the mechanism behind 12 of the 31 duplicate groups**

- **Date:** August 28, 2026 · **Surface:** `groceryList.ts:500-502` (bucket match) → `:541-558` (synthetic append) · **Found by:** CC, D-WS9-188/189 program Phase 0. ⚠️ **FLAGGED, NOT FILED, BY CC — deliberately, because `groceryList.ts` and `ingredientConversions.ts` were reported mid-edit. Chat-Claude is filing it so it is not lost; the RE-VERIFY GATE below is mandatory.**
- **The chain, as read:** `normalizeIngredientName("Milk")` → `"milk"`. ⚠️ **There is no `milk` catalog row** — `"milk": catalogRow=NONE, alias→whole milk`. The bucket-match loop at `:500-502` compares `normalizeIngredientName(entry.canonicalName)`, which for the plan's row is `"whole milk"`, **and it does NOT consult `ingredient_aliases`.** So recurring `Milk` never matches, falls through to the synthetic append at `:541-558`, where `lookupPurchaseDefault("milk")` returns **null** (the code-table `milk` entry carries `gramsPerCup` only, no purchase pack) and the row is created as **`quantity: 1, unit: "each"`**.
- 🔴 **THIS IS THE COMMON PATH, NOT A CORNER: 6 of 13 users have `Milk` recurring, and `whole milk` has 211 dish references.**
- ✅ **IT EXPLAINS THE `milk 1 each` ‖ `Whole milk 3 tablespoon` SHAPE THAT IS 12 OF THE 31 DUPLICATE GROUPS** — and it explains the `each` unit, which had no other explanation. ⚠️ **The two rows share an `ingredientId` only because persist re-resolves it from the final name; the name split happens here, upstream.**
- ⚠️ **RELATIONSHIP TO D-WS9-188, AND THEY ARE NOT THE SAME THING.** D-WS9-188 is the ruled **product** question — how a recurring quantity and a meal need should be DISPLAYED together. **This is the mechanical reason they never met in the first place.** **Hans's ruling can be implemented correctly and these rows would still not pair up until this is fixed.**
- 🔴 **RE-VERIFY GATE — DO NOT BUILD FROM THIS ENTRY UNTIL IT PASSES.** Both implicated files were mid-edit by the BUG-174 + BUG-176 block when this was read, **and the `milk` code-table entry is exactly the kind of thing that block may touch.** ⚠️ **Re-read `groceryList.ts:500-502` and `:541-558` and re-run the `lookupPurchaseDefault("milk")` probe AFTER BUG-174/176 pushes, and quote both.** **Until then this entry is an UNVERIFIED HYPOTHESIS with strong evidence, not a finding.**
- ✅ **CORROBORATED August 28, 2026, INDEPENDENTLY AND BY ACCIDENT.** The fold-proof run measured `grocery.gap_fill_purchase_size` misses on a live consolidation: **6 misses, and ALL SIX are recurring synthetics with `ingredientId` NULL — `milk` · `bread` · `coffee` · `pet treats` · `paper towels` · `toilet paper`.** ⚠️ **`milk` having no `Ingredient` row at gap-fill time is exactly the mechanism this entry describes, observed from a different direction by a script that was not looking for it.** **The re-verify gate below still stands — this corroborates the mechanism, it does not re-read the two cited line ranges.**
- **Status:** 🟡 **OPEN — UNVERIFIED, re-verify gated on BUG-174/176 push** · **Priority:** **P2** · **Cross-ref:** **D-WS9-188** · **BUG-164** · BUG-174 · BUG-175 · D-WS9-193.

### BUG-183 — Re-entering a saved plan from the options screen appears to write a second copy

- **Date:** August 29, 2026 · **Surface:** plan-options → saved plan back-navigation · **Found by:** Hans, device pass.
- **His account, verbatim:** *"I backed out of the plan I saved for later to double check the results. I got back to the plan options (results) screen, went to the same meal I had saved, and it saved for a while. this makes me think it was writing a duplicate copy. which makes sense because of how I got to the screen."*
- ⚠️ **THE LONG SAVE IS THE EVIDENCE — a re-entry that merely READS should not take as long as the original write.** **UNVERIFIED: no duplicate row was confirmed; the symptom is latency plus the shape of the flow.**

Check his account against the 9 before designing anything.**

Carry the egg pair into the D-WS9-188 display build as its second worked example.** Two new items minted from the pass: **BUG-192** (duplicate chipotle instruction) · **D-WS9-198** (narration flow preference). *Build record:* 3 commits `a7bdb8e` · `fb001d4` · `364fc0b`: 21 category rows applied (dry-run == apply == 21), the 22-row `PREP_CATEGORY_OVERRIDE` loader-seam table shipped with its rulings as comments + a key-integrity test, **golden diff GREEN across 487 dishes (337 affected + 150 control): ONLY the two ruled deltas — `large eggs` out of proteins (185 dishes; 186 refs, one dish lists it twice) and `hard-boiled eggs` into proteins (3) — cheese/tofu/pasta byte-identical, control 0.** Guard-break went RED (16 disallowed deltas on a scoped tofu mutation), restore hash-verified. ### BUG-187 — Two rows on one list can share a reconcile `matchKey`, and only a flag filter keeps it from mattering

- **Date:** August 29, 2026 · **Surface:** `groceryReconcile.ts:111-119` `matchKey` · **Found by:** CC, reconcile safety audit — **as a latent hazard, not a live symptom.**
- **Observed:** list `93a03e23` carries **three** `Lime` rows, all `ingredientId=9741d311`, all unit `each`:

```
"Lime"   2 each  plan-derived   srcs=1   pack "2 limes"
"limes"  1 each  RECURRING      srcs=0   pack "1 lime"
"Lime"   1 each  USER-ADDED     srcs=0   pack null
```

- ⚠️ **`matchKey` returns `` `id:${ingredientId}|${unit}` `` — so all three produce the IDENTICAL key `id:9741d311|each`.** 🔴 **The key is not unique within a list, and the matcher assumes it is.**
- ✅ **HARMLESS TODAY ONLY BECAUSE RECONCILE SKIPS BOTH THE RECURRING AND THE USER-ADDED ROW**, so exactly one candidate ever reaches the matcher. ⚠️ **That is a filter, not an invariant. Change either flag's handling — which D-WS9-188's build will do — and two rows compete for one key with no defined winner.**
- 🔴 **D-WS9-188 IS THE TRIGGER, AND IT IS THE NEXT THING IN THE QUEUE THAT TOUCHES THIS.** Its whole job is to make the recurring row and the plan row relate to each other. **Read this entry before that build starts.**
- ✅ **CORROBORATED August 29, 2026, BY ACCIDENT AND FROM A DIFFERENT DIRECTION.** CC's BUG-181 regression harness initially reported `lime` and `lemon` collapsing — names that are in no fold map. ⚠️ **Cause: the harness fed PERSISTED rows straight to the merge, and a recurring row plus a user-added Extra CAN share a canonical name and unit in the table though they never do through the consolidator.** **Re-run with production bucketing as a pre-pass, the artifact vanished.** 🔴 **That is this entry's hazard reproduced in a harness — the collision is real in the DATA and is masked only by the code path, exactly as stated.**
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN — latent** · **Priority:** **P2, upgraded from P3 on the trigger** · **Cross-ref:** **D-WS9-188** · BUG-177 · BUG-164 · BUG-126.

### BUG-188 — A measured need for a bunch-sold herb should buy a bunch, not sit in its own row — 🔵 **RULED, NOT BUILT — and the ruling RECLASSIFIES what chat-Claude called correct**

- **Date:** August 29, 2026 · **Surface:** `groceryMerge` conservation guard / purchase resolution · **Found by:** Hans, reviewing the triage reference — **by disagreeing with a verdict chat-Claude had written as EXPECTED.**
- **What chat-Claude had ruled:** `cilantro 1 bunch` beside `cilantro 0.5 cup` is **correct as two rows**, because a bunch and a cup genuinely do not convert without per-ingredient data.
- 🔴 **HANS, VERBATIM, AND HE REFRAMED THE QUESTION:** *"1/2 cup cilantro doesn't really convert to a bunch. BUT I would argue that if you need a 1/2 cup or a cup of chopped cilantro or whatever cilantro, you should buy a bunch. so, in the example you provided, I would expect 2 bunches cilantro (1 bunch + 1/2 cup)."*
- ⚠️ **THE REFRAME IS THE FINDING: THE QUESTION IS NOT "HOW DO I CONVERT A CUP TO A BUNCH." IT IS "WHAT DO YOU BUY."** **A measured need for a herb sold in bunches resolves to a whole bunch — the conversion never has to be exact, because you cannot buy half a bunch.** ✅ **Same shopper-convenience principle the D-WS9-189 rubric now encodes: the test is what leaves the store, not what the arithmetic says.**
- 🔴 **BUT IT DOES NOT GENERALISE UNIFORMLY, AND A CRUDE VERSION OVER-BUYS.** Applying *"any measured need adds a pack"* across the board:

| Case | Crude rule gives | Right? |
|---|---|---|
| `cilantro 1 bunch` + `0.5 cup` | 2 bunches | ✅ Hans's ruling |
| `parsley 1 bunch` + `3 tbsp` | 2 bunches | ✅ same shape |
| `green cabbage 1 head` + `2 cup` | 2 heads | ❌ **one head is far more than 2 cups** |
| `white onion 1 each` + `1 cup` | 2 onions | ❌ probably over-buys |

- ⚠️ **THE DISCRIMINATOR IS YIELD PER PACK, WHICH IS EXACTLY D-WS9-189's `yieldQuantity` (D-WS9-194).** **A bunch of cilantro yields about a cup, so a cup demands a second bunch; a head of cabbage yields many cups, so it does not.** **Without that number the rule either under-buys herbs or over-buys produce.**
- ✅ **AND HANS GAVE THE TIE-BREAKER PRINCIPLE IN THE SAME MESSAGE, RULING ON `white onion 1 large`:** *"get a white onion and we're probably fine. if the user gets a small one and it's not enough they'll blame the grocery shopping more than the list."* ⚠️ **SO: a small under-buy is forgivable — the user blames the store. A gross over-buy is not, because the list is visibly wrong.** **Bias toward buying enough on small cheap items; do not round up large or expensive ones.**
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN — RULED, gated on D-WS9-189's yield data** · **Priority:** **P2** · **Cross-ref:** **D-WS9-194** · **D-WS9-182** · **D-WS9-189** · BUG-142 · BUG-138 · BUG-251 (the duplicate-row list; cilantro is this entry, not that one).
- 🔵 **SEEN AGAIN September 11 (Hans, a new list): `1 bunch fresh cilantro` printed on TWO rows, from needs of 1/4 cup and 1/2 bunch.** Hans, verbatim: *"it should be one bunch one row"* — and *"I would overlook if it said 2 bunches."* **Consistent with the ruling: a bunch yields about a cup, so 1/4 cup + 1/2 bunch buys one bunch.** ✅ **TRACED (read-only lane, September 11 night):** one catalog row (`fresh cilantro`, default unit bunch, pack `1 bunch`); the two needs bucket as `cup` and `bunch`; the merge refuses because **`bunch` has no unit dimension** (neither volume, weight nor a count unit) and there is no ladder; the Sonnet pass then cannot accept a merge either (no common basis) — **so bunch + cup for one herb ships two rows on every path today.** Both rows wear the catalog's one-bunch pack, and the client composes the count per row. **A cup-per-bunch yield exists nowhere the runtime reads** — only in the reviewed but unloaded `pack_yields_REVIEWED.csv` (leaves 2 cup/bunch · chopped 1 cup/bunch · stems ½ cup), and keyed to the leaves/stems rows, not this one. **Two shapes sized, neither built:** (i) *same pack → one row, counts summed* needs no data but buys 2 bunches tonight (Hans wants one), 2 cabbages and 3 onions — exactly where his tie-breaker says no; (ii) *yield-based, rounded up* gives 1 bunch tonight and 2 for the ruled case, but a plain round-up still buys 2 cabbages for `1 head + 2 cup` and 3 onions — **so it needs the yield data AND a rounding tolerance, which is a ruling.** Since September 1: cilantro ×3, white onion ×2, yukon gold ×1, green cabbage ×1 (7 pairs, 14 rows). **Nothing ships before D-WS9-226's CSV load; the tolerance question goes to Hans with that block.**

### BUG-193 — An ingredient added by a device-side meal edit reached the grocery list UNLINKED (`ingredientId: null`) despite an exact-name catalog row — so no catalog fix can ever reach it

- **Date:** August 31, 2026 · **Surface:** the meal-edit → reconcile → grocery path (`ingredientId` resolution) · **Found by:** CC, BUG-186 follow-up probe — reported as *"an AI-invented row"*; **reframed at chat audit with context CC could not have had.**
- ⚠️ **THE REFRAME, AND THE PROCESS LESSON IT CARRIES:** the probe flagged two "findings" — `firm tofu 8 oz` on the list *"sourced to the Ribs dish, which carries no tofu"*, and `egg noodles 12 oz` *"substituted"* for penne rigate. **Both are almost certainly Hans's OWN device-test edits** (device items 3 and 4: *"i edited penne to egg noodles in a meal and it worked"* · *"no tofu present in the plan, I edited a meal and it worked"*) — plan-scoped edits leave `DishIngredient` untouched by design, which is exactly the shape CC observed. ⚠️ **A probe commissioned after device testing MUST be told what the tester changed, or it will mint the tester's own edits as defects** — carry this into every post-device probe prompt.
- **What SURVIVES the reframe, and it is real:** the edit-added `firm tofu` row carries **`ingredientId: null` + `wasAiInferred: true` and sits in `pantry`** — while `firm tofu` exists in the catalog as an exact canonical name (one of BUG-186's own 21 rows, now Dairy). **Because the row never linked, `sectionForCategory` never ran, the AI final pass guessed `pantry`, and the BUG-186 fix structurally cannot reach it — nor can any future catalog correction.** Meanwhile the same session's `egg noodles` edit DID link (correct catalog section applied), so resolution on this path works sometimes — the discriminator is uncharacterised (casing? add-path vs reconcile-path? typed name vs picker?).
- ⚠️ **Beta-gate class (D-WS9-174):** an unlinked row with a guessed section is a *"why is that there / why is it in the wrong aisle"* item that no data fix can correct.
- **Open question attached (timing, unasserted):** the plan's `PrepWeekStructure` (generated 18:23Z) still narrates *"12 oz penne rigate"* — if Hans's penne→egg-noodles edit preceded that generation, prep is not reading plan-scoped overrides; if it followed, this is ordinary cache staleness (revision bump regenerates on next open). **Establish edit timestamps before reading anything into it.**
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN — needs characterisation** (edit timing · which path created each row · why tofu missed the link while egg noodles hit) · **Priority:** **P2 — pre-beta under D-WS9-174** · **Target:** a read-only characterisation rider on the next grocery-touching block · **Cross-ref:** BUG-186 · D-WS9-174 · BUG-124 (the write-back writer) · D-WS9-189 (the resolution program).

### BUG-195 — The plan generator invents tags (`game day`, `season opener`) into an open, unfilterable field that feeds TWO AI inputs and one rendered card label

- **Date:** September 1, 2026 · **Surface:** wizard candidate tags → `Meal.tags` / plan-template tags · **Found by:** Hans, BUG-190 device pass item 1.
- **Symptom, verbatim:** *"it added 'game day' as a tag and 'seaon opener' or something else like that on my first try. we don't have a 'game day' tag... I want to be sure AI isn't generating things users can't filter on."*
- ⚠️ **HIS CONCERN IS RIGHT AND UNDERSTATED — THERE IS NO TAG VOCABULARY AND NO TAG FILTER AT ALL.** Verified in the August 4, 2026 Phase 0 (retained in D-WS9-100 as the standing reference on `tags`): `Meal.tags` (`schema.prisma:530`) and `Dish.tags` (`:436`) are open `String[]`, validated only at `max(10)` items × `max(40)` chars — **no enum, no closed set**. 🔴 **AND THE "SEARCH MATCHES TAGS" BELIEF IS FALSIFIED — no text search on meals or dishes exists.** PRD §9.6 (LOCKED) and §9.3.3 describe a tags-inclusive search that was never built. **So today NO tag is filterable, not just the invented ones.**
- 🔴 **THE HARM IS NOT DISPLAY — IT IS FEEDBACK. THREE LIVE NON-SEARCH CONSUMERS READ `tags`:**
  1. **Find-Similar's AI input** (`findSimilar.ts:25`, `SwapMealSheet.tsx:98,243`) — ⚠️ **and BUG-058 is open on that surface, so a novelty tag changes an unfixed bug's inputs.**
  2. **The plan-generation shortlist** (`storeShortlist.ts:316`) — so an invented tag re-enters generation.
  3. **The Featured/Tried-&-True rail label** (`triedTrue.ts:61`) — ⚠️ **which renders `tags[0]` directly. A user can see a home-screen card labelled `game day`.**
- ⚠️ **THE LOOP IS SELF-REINFORCING: AI emits a novelty tag → write-back persists it → the shortlist surfaces it → the next generation copies it.** **This is exactly the "divergence generator" D-WS9-109 named — arriving from the AI side. The ruling that killed user-typed tags (D-WS9-100/-101) never covered AI-emitted ones, so nothing guards this direction.**
- ✅ **AND THE UNDERLYING BEHAVIOUR IS A KEEPER — HANS REVERSED HIS OWN SKEPTICISM ON DEVICE ITEM 10: *"the NFL opener comment came up again, and honestly I think it's a good feature even if I was skeptical at first."*** 🔴 **SO THE DEFECT IS THE CHANNEL, NOT THE CONTENT. Seasonal and timely awareness belongs in PROSE — `whyBullets`, the meal description — where it is read once and consumed by nothing. It does not belong in `tags`, a field with three machine consumers and no vocabulary.** ⚠️ **Any fix that suppresses the timeliness rather than redirecting it is the wrong fix and Hans will reject it.**
- 🔴 **UNVERIFIED — CHARACTERISE BEFORE DESIGNING ANYTHING.** Hans saw these on a plan-candidate card. **Whether candidate/plan-level tags PERSIST (to `MealPlanTemplate`, or to `Meal.tags` via the live write-back path) or are ephemeral to the card is unestablished, and it decides both the blast radius and the fix.** Establish it first; the three-consumer harm above only fires if they persist.
- ⚠️ **§27.2 REUSE CHECK BEFORE ANY BUILD: D-WS9-109 already owns "typed attributes that generation READS, instead of user tags nothing consumes."** This is plausibly a rider on that, not its own feature. **And D-WS9-109's own sequencing rule applies directly here: ask what READS a field before building what writes it.**
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN — needs characterisation** · **Priority:** **P2 — pre-beta under D-WS9-174** (a mislabelled home-screen card is a *"why does it say that"* item; the Find-Similar contamination is silent and worse) · **Target:** a read-only characterisation rider on the next plan-generation or catalog block · **Cross-ref:** **D-WS9-109** · **D-WS9-100 / D-WS9-101** (the tags Phase 0 and the decline) · **BUG-058** (open, on consumer 1) · BUG-190 · D-WS9-200.

### BUG-197 — Plan-review meal descriptions rendered two lines where the card had room for three

- **Date:** September 3, 2026 · **Surface:** `PlanReviewMealRow.tsx` · **Status:** ✅ **FIXED** — `1a6ce0b`, device-confirmed, pushed.
- `numberOfLines` 2 → 3. Catalog descriptions run a **median 149 characters** ≈ 3.7 lines at that width, so two lines showed about half of one.
- 🔴 **THERE WAS NEVER A CLIP, AND THE PROMPT THAT SAID SO WAS WRONG.** Chat-Claude misread Hans's *"the cards should get bigger to accommodate a third line"* as a truncation report, wrote the prompt as a clipping hunt, **and explicitly forbade the third line he was asking for.** CC searched the row and its only caller, found no `overflow: hidden`, no `height`, no `maxHeight`, and reported the absence as the finding. **The row always grew; the text was simply told to render fewer lines than it had.**
- ⚠️ **The transferable part: a search that finds nothing is a finding, not a failed search** — and a prompt built on a misread premise sends it hunting for something that was never there.
- **Per-surface and deliberate, not a pattern:** plan review **3** · `AddMealsSheet` **2** · `MealRow` **2** (Hans named three and declined it in the same sentence). All three pinned by their own tests; a harmonising sweep in either direction goes red.
- **Cross-ref:** BUG-158 · BUG-153 · D-WS9-127.

### BUG-198 — After the contrast sweep, the active tab was distinguished by hue alone and failed at arm's length

- **Date:** September 3, 2026 · **Surface:** `app/(tabs)/_layout.tsx` · **Status:** ✅ **FIXED** — `deb1d73`, device-confirmed, pushed.
- BUG-157 moved `tabBarInactiveTintColor` from `neutral[600]` (3.11:1) to `neutral[700]` (5.26:1) — a real legibility gain on every screen. **But the only thing separating active from inactive was hue** (sage green vs warm brown), and darkening inactive narrowed the gap. Hans: *"at arm's length it's not clear which one you're on."*
- **Fixed with TWO non-colour signals** — an indicator bar above the active icon (always laid out, transparent when inactive, so the icon never shifts) and a weight step on the active label. **The contrast gain was kept.**
- ⚠️ **COLOUR ALONE IS WHAT FAILED, SO A COLOUR-ONLY FIX WOULD HAVE BEEN THE SAME BUG AGAIN** — and reverting the contrast to "restore the gap" would have traded legibility on every screen for an affordance. A comment at the site says so.
- ⚠️ **Navigator constraint worth keeping:** `tabBarLabelStyle` is static and cannot vary on focus; only `tabBarIcon` and `tabBarLabel` receive `focused`. Supplying `tabBarLabel` opts that tab out of `tabBarLabelStyle` entirely, so the label style must carry the full type, not just the weight.
- **Cross-ref:** BUG-157 · BUG-106.

- **Date:** September 3, 2026 · **Surface:** Prep & Cook hub, Cook Mode, Prep the Week · **Status:** ✅ **FIXED** — `deb1d73` + `1a6ce0b`, device-confirmed, pushed.
- ✅ **AMENDS THE AUGUST 31 QUIET-TIER RULING, on device evidence.** BUG-157 protected `neutral[600]` as a deliberate quiet tier and named Cook Mode's dimmed next-step preview as staying. **Hans reversed that after using it:** the dimmed preview, the "N steps remaining" beside it, and the Cook Mode dish tag all moved to `neutral[700]`. ⚠️ **The quiet tier SURVIVES for section labels and small-caps eyebrows — he confirmed those read fine.** **The durable form of his reasoning: a quiet tier is a reading-room decision, and Cook Mode is not a reading room.**
- **Hub neutral chips** separated by luminance (fill `neutral[200]`→`[300]`, text `[700]`→`[800]`). ⚠️ **The sage and gold tones sit no further from the page than the neutral chip did (1.16 and 1.14 vs 1.12) and read fine anyway, because they separate by HUE. The neutral chip is the page's own neutral, so it has nothing to separate with and needs the luminance step the hued tones get for free.** That reasoning is in the code so a later "consistency" pass does not darken all three.
- **Prep the Week step text up one size step** (instructions 15→17, meta/where/storage 12→14, optional tag 11→12). ⚠️ **A DELIBERATE DEVIATION FROM `kiwi_prep_cook_design_spec.md` [LOCKED], Hans-ruled** — a locked spec that was never device-tested at counter distance loses to hardware. Cook Mode sizing was left alone; he confirmed it reads fine. Redline owed against that spec.

The ring separates at **5.2197** on luminance alone.

### BUG-205 — Store-bought products live in `Dish.substitutions` JSON and NO allergen mechanism reads them — ✅ RULED by Hans, September 14, 2026 (option C, scoped down); UNBUILT

- **Date:** September 5, 2026 · **Surface:** `Dish.substitutions` (JSON) → every allergen-filtered surface · **Found by:** CC, ingredient-rollup Phase 0, **while measuring something else.**
- **Status:** ✅ **RULED September 14, 2026 — UNBUILT.** · **Priority:** 🟡 **P2, DOWNGRADED FROM P1 BY THE RULING** — the filter Hans named runs BEFORE substitutions exist, so the exposure is far narrower than the raw numbers below suggest · **Target:** the WS9B store-bought toggle lane, which is where Kiwi itself starts offering swaps · **Cross-ref:** **BUG-121** · D-WS9-211 · D-WS9-174 · D-WS9-175.
- **Measured:** **1,517 dishes** carry substitutions · **660 distinct product names** · **614 have no matching `Ingredient` row at all** · **147 would derive ≥1 allergen token today** if anything looked at them (*sour cream, Italian-style breadcrumbs, pre-shredded mozzarella, store-bought pizza dough*).
- 🔴 **THE DERIVATION WALKS `meal → dishLinks → dishIngredients → ingredient`. SUBSTITUTIONS ARE NOT ON THAT PATH.** Neither today's stamping nor the proposed ingredient rollup sees a single one of them. **This is not a gap the rollup introduces or closes — it is orthogonal to that decision, and it is larger than the AI tail the rollup was proposed to eliminate (147 vs 110).**
- ⚠️ **IT IS BUG-121's BLAST RADIUS, ARRIVING FROM THE OTHER SIDE.** BUG-121 says dual-path dishes render both paths because `pathKey` is unread. **This says the store-bought path is also unstamped.** A user on the bought path can be served a dish whose allergens were computed entirely from the scratch path's ingredients.
- ⚠️ **UNVERIFIED, AND IT DECIDES THE PRIORITY: whether those 147 product names reach a user-visible surface at all.** CC measured the JSON, **not its consumers.** `DishIngredient.pathKey` is null on all 36,293 rows, so the bought path may be inert today. **If it is inert this is P3 latent; if any surface renders it this is a live P1 of exactly BUG-201's class. Measure the consumers before scoping anything.**

---
- ✅ **RULED BY HANS, September 14, 2026 — option (c), and his reasoning RESHAPES the problem rather than just picking a branch. Verbatim:** *"the allergen filter is when meals are suggested to users. so the filter runs before they get a meal they can adjust to store-bought ingredients/mixes/elements. once the user goes to the store and actually orders the food it’s out of our hands, we just don’t want to look stupid telling someone with a shellfish allergy to make baja shrimp tacos. and ‘not checked’ could be implied or included in the T’s&C’s unless it makes the feature feel more powerful and reliable to have it note that we didn’t evaluate what’s in a package."*
- 🔴 **WHAT THIS SETTLES: THE ALLERGEN MECHANISM DOES NOT NEED TO READ `Dish.substitutions` AT ALL.** The filter’s job is the MEAL, at suggestion time, before any substitution exists. **A user adapting a already-safe meal to a store-bought element is downstream of the promise Kiwi makes** — and the promise is "we will not suggest you a dish you are allergic to", not "we have audited every package in your grocery store". ✅ **So the 614-products-with-no-Ingredient-row number below is NOT a 614-item backlog; it is context for a feature that does not exist yet.**
- ⚠️ **CHAT-CLAUDE’S CALL ON THE ONE SEAM HANS’S FRAMING DOES NOT COVER, made under standing delegation — HANS OBJECTS IF HE DISAGREES: the distinction is WHO OFFERS THE SWAP.** A user reaching for a taco kit on their own is out of our hands, exactly as ruled. **But WS9B’s store-bought toggle is KIWI offering the swap** — and an app that surfaces "use a store-bought pizza crust" to a coeliac user is making a suggestion, not observing one. ✅ **RULED: where KIWI offers the swap, the offer carries a short, plain line at the point of offer that the package’s contents are not allergen-checked — NOT buried in the T&Cs.** Hans left this door open himself (*"unless it makes the feature feel more powerful and reliable"*) and **it does: for an allergy audience, saying plainly what you have and have not checked reads as careful, not as weak.** One line, at the offer, is the whole treatment.
- ✅ **HANS REFINED IT THE SAME DAY, and his version is better than chat-Claude’s — THIS IS THE RULING: the line is a SUBTLE footnote, *"* Kiwi does not evaluate store-bought items for allergens"*, and it renders ONLY IF THE USER HAS INDICATED AN ALLERGY OR AVOIDANCE** (the allergy + avoidance chips or the free-text field). 🔴 **The conditional is the whole point: a blanket disclaimer on every store-bought swap trains everyone to ignore it, and it makes the app look nervous to the ~most users who have no allergen set. Shown only to the people it is for, it reads as precision rather than as a hedge.** ⚠️ **So the treatment has a PRECONDITION: it needs the user’s allergen/avoidance state at the point the swap is offered. Confirm that state is available on that surface before building — if it is not, the footnote is not buildable as ruled and that is a finding, not a licence to show it unconditionally.** *(Chat-Claude’s earlier version — an unconditional line at the point of offer — is superseded; the seam it identified stands.)*
- ✅ **HANS ANSWERED THE PRECONDITION THE SAME EVENING, AND RE-PRIORITISED: the allergen indication comes from the WIZARD. A standing allergy lives in preferences and hydrates from the profile; the user can ADD or OVERRIDE per plan** — *"if, for example, this week they have a guest who has an allergy."* **So the state the footnote keys on is the plan’s effective allergy/avoidance set (profile-hydrated + this-plan overrides), not the profile alone.** 🔵 **And his priority call, verbatim: *"I honestly view this as P2 and it could go after launch."* P2 confirmed; NOT on the launch path.**
- 🔵 **THEREFORE THE BUILD IS SMALL AND IT BELONGS TO WS9B, NOT TO THE CORRECTNESS BLOCK:** no allergen derivation over 660 product names · no `Ingredient` rows for the 614 · no name-derived stamping (⚠️ **D-WS9-211: allergen stamps are name-derived and the false-positive class RECURS; `Ingredient.category` is not a usable cross-check — `vegan butter` is filed as Dairy. Do not propose it again**) — just the one honest line where Kiwi offers a swap.
- ✅ **AND THIS CLOSES THE CORRECTNESS BLOCK’S SECOND OPEN RULING.** Only D-WS9-238 remained beside it, and that was ruled the same day.

### BUG-206 — The public retrieval pool holds the same dish twice, and 3 of 9 pairs carry DIFFERENT allergen stamps

- **Date:** September 5, 2026 · **Surface:** the `isPublic` dinner retrieval pool; `sourceType` `batch_generated` vs `live_writeback` · **Found by:** CC, ingredient-rollup Phase 0 §3.
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN** · **Priority:** **P2** · **Target:** unassigned · **Cross-ref:** BUG-201 · **BUG-166 (DIFFERENT POPULATION — see below)** · BUG-162 · D-WS9-214.
- **Measured:** pool = **1,192 rows**; **9 groups / 18 rows** share a normalised title (1.5%). **Every group is one `batch_generated` copy + one `live_writeback` copy** — no group is same-source. Batch copies all dated 2026-07-24; write-back copies 2026-07-28 to 07-31. Both sides `userId = null`. Jaccard ingredient overlap **0.45–0.70** — genuinely the same dish written twice, not a title collision.
- 🔴 **THE FINDING IS NOT THE DUPLICATION, IT IS THE DIVERGENCE. 3 of the 9 pairs carry different allergen stamps for the same dish.** Carolina vinegar pulled pork is `[egg]` in one copy and `[dairy, egg, wheat]` in the other; buffalo wings `[dairy, fish]` vs `[dairy]`. **Each stamp is CORRECT for its own row's ingredient list** — the two generations diverged.
- ⚠️ **CONSEQUENCE, AND IT IS A SAFETY CONSEQUENCE, NOT A VARIETY ONE: a dairy-free user is shown one copy of a dish and filtered away from the other.** Which copy they get is arbitrary. **The allergen work now shipped does not touch this and cannot — both stamps are right. Only dedupe fixes it.**
- ⚠️ **THIS IS NOT BUG-166, AND CHAT-CLAUDE PROPOSED THAT IT MIGHT BE. IT WAS WRONG.** BUG-166 counts **user-owned** duplicates (`sourceStoreMealId` NULL, 20 of BUG-162's 42 groups, one plan expanded twice). **This counts PUBLIC catalog rows with `userId = null`.** Different tables, different populations, different mechanisms; the scales (20 vs 9) never matched either. ⚠️ **CC refused the connection when told not to conclude it, and was right to. Do not re-merge these two.**
- **UNVERIFIED (CC's own framing, kept):** that the mechanism is a **missing dedupe on the live write-back publish path** rather than a retrieval defect. All nine write-back copies land in a 4-day window immediately after the July batch run, which fits — but it was not confirmed.

- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN** · **Priority:** **P2** · **Target:** unassigned · **Cross-ref:** **BUG-209** · **D-WS9-219** · D-WS9-189 · BUG-150 · BUG-164 · BUG-177 · BUG-187.
- **Measured on list `bd29f91a`:** `2 limes Lime (5 each)` (plan-derived) and `1 lime limes (1 each)` (`isRecurringItem=true`, `wasAiInferred=true`) **carry the SAME `ingredientId` — `9741d311` — and do not merge**, because `groupKey("lime") = "lime"` ≠ `groupKey("limes") = "limes"`, at both the merge stage and the partition.

### BUG-213 — Cilantro cannot pool: no bunch↔cup figure exists anywhere

- **Date:** September 6, 2026 · **Surface:** the component pool's `toYieldUnit`; catalog row `fresh cilantro` · **Found by:** CC, D-WS9-189 A3, while the pool was being opened.
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN** · **Priority:** **P2** · **Target:** **BUG-188's unbuilt ruling — this is an instance of it, not a separate fix** · **Cross-ref:** 🔴 **D-WS9-189** · **D-WS9-222** · BUG-208 · D-WS9-218.
- 🔴 **THIS IS THE ROW HANS STOPPED THE SEPTEMBER 6 DEVICE PASS ON, AND A3 DID NOT CLOSE IT.** List `bd29f91a` still ships three lines: `1 bunch fresh cilantro (1⅓ cup)` · `1 bunch fresh cilantro stems (¼ cup)` · `1 bunch fresh cilantro leaves (½ cup)`.
- **Why:** `fresh cilantro` is authored in **cups**; the component basis is **bunch**; cilantro's `conversionRef` carries `gramsPerCup: 16` but **no grams-per-bunch**. Converting cups → bunches is therefore undefined.
- ✅ **THE POOL DECLINES, AND DECLINING IS CORRECT.** Emitting the twin — two rows both named `fresh cilantro` — is worse than leaving three honest rows. **This is BUG-208's guard working as designed, not a new failure.**
- ⚠️ **CC did NOT invent a grams-per-bunch figure, which is exactly what A1 did and is why 95 edges shipped unverified.**
- 🔴 **CORRECTED September 6, 2026, HOURS AFTER THIS ENTRY WAS WRITTEN — AND THE CORRECTION IS THE POINT. THE CONVERSION IS NOT NEEDED AND HANS ALREADY RULED SO, IN BUG-188 ON AUGUST 29.** His words there, verbatim: *"1/2 cup cilantro doesn't really convert to a bunch. BUT I would argue that if you need a 1/2 cup or a cup of chopped cilantro or whatever cilantro, you should buy a bunch. so, in the example you provided, I would expect 2 bunches cilantro (1 bunch + 1/2 cup)."*
- ⚠️ **BUG-188's own headline says THE QUESTION IS NOT "HOW DO I CONVERT A CUP TO A BUNCH" — IT IS "WHAT DO YOU BUY." A measured need for a herb sold in bunches resolves to a WHOLE BUNCH, and the conversion never has to be exact because you cannot buy half a bunch.** **So the fix is `ceil` to whole bunches and sum, NOT a grams-per-bunch figure. BUG-188 is RULED AND UNBUILT; this entry is a live instance of it, not a new problem.**
- 🔴 **AND THE PROCESS FAILURE IS AN EXACT REPEAT, ONE WEEK APART.** BUG-188 exists because chat-Claude ruled `cilantro 1 bunch` beside `cilantro 0.5 cup` **"correct as two rows"** and Hans overturned it. **On September 6 chat-Claude wrote that the pool's decline was "correct" and that the fix was a catalog figure — the same wrong verdict, on the same food, for the same reason: it treated an unconvertible unit as the problem instead of asking what the shopper buys.** ⚠️ **Cause both times: scoping from what the code/report said rather than searching canon for an existing ruling. `project_search` finds BUG-188 in one call.**

### BUG-214 — `grocery.generate_list` runs at temperature 0.7, so the same menu produces different grocery lists

- **Date:** September 7, 2026 · **Surface:** `groceryListAI.ts` — the `grocery.generate_list` call omits `temperature`; `runAICall` defaults to 0.7 · **Found by:** CC, read-only 4-list audit (post-A3), on two lists generated five minutes apart from the same four meals.
- **Status:** 🔴 **REOPEN, P2 (CC's call, September 9, and it has better evidence than chat-Claude did) — the pipeline is genuinely not reproducible AT THE MODEL LAYER, so any claim otherwise is false; but NO USER-VISIBLE DEFECT EXISTS AT HEAD (6/6 row-identical), which is why it is not P1.** *(Temperature pin landed and was correct; what was refuted is that it was sufficient.)* *(Original fix, unchanged:)* **`a9fb3b9`, September 7, 2026.** Pinned to `temperature: 0`; the guard reads the params the SDK was actually called with, and a second guard pins `gap_fill_purchase_size` the same way. ⚠️ **`grocery.recurring_item_categorize` was deliberately LEFT at 0.7 and CC gave the reason: its result becomes a typeahead suggestion with `ingredientId: null` and is never persisted. D-WS9-053's rule is about persisted output, so it does not reach that call.** · **Priority:** **P1** · **Target:** the September 7 grocery P1 block · **Cross-ref:** **BUG-215** (the under-buy it exposed) · D-WS9-053 (§2.0 pinned `gap_fill_purchase_size` to 0 for exactly this reason) · D-WS9-189.
- **Symptom, measured:** lists `3346e106` (65 rows) and `cb5c8f6e` (63 rows) — **same 4 meals, same 91 mentions, same commit `beba57d`, five minutes apart.** The deterministic consolidator returns 65 rows for both plans; the divergence is the Sonnet pass. Call log: two `grocery.generate_list v2` calls, input 4,162 vs 4,163 tokens (the list title differs by one character), output 1,543 vs 1,304.
- 🔴 **AND THE MERGED VERSION WAS THE WORSE ONE** — its garlic line landed on a catalog row without the head↔clove ladder (BUG-215). **This output is persisted; D-WS9-053 already ruled that a persisted AI output runs at temperature 0.** Same rule, one more call site.
- 🔴 **REOPENED September 9, 2026. THE TEMPERATURE PIN DID NOT MAKE GENERATION REPRODUCIBLE, AND THE EVIDENCE THAT SAID IT DID WAS AN ARTEFACT OF THE INSTRUMENT.**
- 🔴 **THE CORRECTION, AND CC CAUGHT IT AGAINST ITSELF: the "three runs, 65/65/65, row-identical" result reported September 8 was measuring BUG-217's REFUSAL PATH, not generation.** **A refusal is a DETERMINISTIC SINK — whatever the model returns, the rebuild emits the same rows.** ⚠️ **Once the guard sometimes ACCEPTS, model variance becomes visible again.** 🔴 **Chat-Claude relayed that figure into the position block as evidence for BUG-214 without asking what it measured — §27.6 item 5, and the first instance where the bad relay was of a CORRECT measurement OF THE WRONG THING.**
- 🔴 **THE ACTUAL MEASUREMENT, September 9, Neon branch, ONE plan, THREE runs, THREE DIFFERENT BEHAVIOURS, all at temperature 0:**

| run | rows | garlic |
|---|---|---|
| warm-up | 65 | 3 rows, **no refusal** — the model echoed all three, so the passthrough shortcut ran |
| A | 65 | 3 rows, **refused** (`neededGrams: 16`, `returnedGrams: 17`) |
| B | **63** | **merged** to 16 clove / 2 heads, all three `sourceKeys` |

- ⚠️ **A SECOND, INDEPENDENT DIVERGENCE IN THE SAME SET, AND IT IS NOT GARLIC: `paper towels` came back `1 package (6 rolls)` on the warm-up and `1 package (6-pack)` on run A.** **Gap-fill divergence at temperature 0 — or a write-back that had not committed before the next read. NOT DIAGNOSED.**
- 🔴 **SETTLED September 9, 2026, AND CHAT-CLAUDE'S HYPOTHESIS IS REFUTED. 12 runs, two independent replications (3 write-back ON + 3 OFF, twice).**
  - **`promptHash` IDENTICAL in all 12** — the full request hashed: system, messages, model, `temperature: 0`, `max_tokens`. **The input never moved, in either phase.**
  - 🔴 **SIX DISTINCT `outputHash` VALUES FROM TWELVE IDENTICAL PROMPTS.** Modal 6/12, another 2/12, four singletons; raw length 4089–4490. **The model is not deterministic at temperature 0.**
  - ✅ **The catalog fingerprint was UNCHANGED across all 12 (the catalog is warm, so the write-back had nothing to move) and suppressing the write-back entirely changed nothing.** **The write-back is not the variance source; it cannot be.**
- ✅ **AND THE VARIANCE DOES NOT REACH THE USER AT HEAD: `rowsHash` IDENTICAL across all 6 runs of the second experiment, despite 4 distinct raw outputs. The model varies PROSE AND FORMATTING, not the substantive numbers.**
- 🔴 **THE REFRAME, AND IT IS THE MOST USEFUL SENTENCE THIS ARC HAS PRODUCED — CC's, replacing its own earlier "a refusal is a deterministic sink": MODEL OUTPUT VARIES ALWAYS; IT REACHES THE USER EXACTLY WHEN THE VARIATION CROSSES A GUARD'S DECISION BOUNDARY.**
  - **At HEAD the garlic group refuses UNCONDITIONALLY — no boundary, so no visible variance.** **Last block's 65/65/63 happened because the basis change put that group onto a CONSERVATION BOUNDARY where 16 conserves and 17 does not, and the model's arithmetic noise straddles it.**
  - 🔴 **SO BUG-214 IS NOT "MAKE GENERATION DETERMINISTIC" — THAT IS NOT ACHIEVABLE AND IS EXPECTED INFERENCE BEHAVIOUR, NOT OUR DEFECT. IT IS "A GUARD BOUNDARY MUST NOT SIT WHERE THE MODEL'S NOISE LIVES."**
  - ⚠️ **THAT IS A LIVE DESIGN CONSTRAINT ON BUG-241's FIX AND ON EVERY FUTURE GUARD: any change that converts an UNCONDITIONAL refusal into a CONDITIONAL one makes model noise user-visible.**
- 🔵 **`paper towels` IS ACCOUNTED FOR AND IT IS A DIFFERENT MECHANISM.** **Zero catalog rows match it; all 6 live grocery rows carry `ingredientId: null` and `isRecurringItem: true`.** **It is synthetic, skips the write-back by design, and is gap-filled fresh on EVERY generation forever — it can never be cached.** **Its variance is Haiku's `gap_fill_purchase_size`, not `generate_list`: 5/5 stable inside one burst, but THREE distinct answers across a day, two of them already persisted onto live rows.** ⚠️ **This is BUG-124 / D-WS9-226's null-`ingredientId` population, now with a measured consequence.**
- 🔵 **SUPERSEDED — chat-Claude's write-back hypothesis, kept because the refutation is the value:** `fillPurchaseSizesWithWriteBack` mutates the catalog DURING generation, so run N+1 reads a different input than run N and the prompt is not actually identical. **The paper-towels difference is the shape that fits it.** ⚠️ **TESTABLE IN ONE PROBE: repeat the same plan with the write-back disabled, or against a freshly branched catalog each time. Do not adopt it because I proposed it.**
- ⚠️ **THE ORIGINAL FIX STANDS — pinning to 0 was correct and necessary. What is refuted is the claim that it was SUFFICIENT.**

### BUG-217 — A cross-name AI merge drops a source row entirely — quantity and provenance — and the conservation guard is structurally blind to it

- **Date:** September 7, 2026 · **Surface:** `groceryListAI.ts` — `entriesByName` / `queueByName` / `absorbedKeys` all key on the RAW, unfolded name · **Found by:** CC, unprompted, while fixing BUG-215 in the September 7 P1 block.
- **Status:** 🟡 **FIXED — `5cecc7c`, LOCAL, NOT PUSHED. Acceptance measured on a Neon branch; not device-tested.** · **Priority:** **P1** · **Cross-ref:** 🔴 **BUG-214** (evidence, not closure) · **BUG-215** · **BUG-142** · **BUG-138** (🔴 **sequencing constraint — see the trap below**) · BUG-095 · D-WS9-189.
- 🔴 **SCOPE WAS WIDER THAN THE ENTRY IMPLIED: `"garlic cloves": "garlic"` sits in `MERGE_GROUP_VARIANT_TO_BASE`, which applies under `EMPTY_RELATION_INDEX` — so this reached EVERY user, including one with no relation data loaded.** It was never conditional on the relationship program having run.
- ✅ **REPRODUCED BEFORE ANY EDIT, and the numbers are the entry's own mechanism made concrete:** need `6 clove + 10 each = 16`, **AI returned 10, one output row, `sourceKeys` naming only one of the two sources, and NO warn of any kind.**
- 🔴 **CHAT-CLAUDE'S PRESCRIBED FIX WAS WRONG AND WOULD HAVE MADE THINGS WORSE.** The prompt said re-key conservation to the folded group name. **Grams is the guard's basis, a subUnit child is deliberately NOT gram-convertible (`clove` → null), and `head` is not a count unit — so re-keying alone turns EVERY garlic group into `not_convertible` and refuses it wholesale, including the correct merges BUG-215 ships.** ✅ **What landed instead: a SECOND basis that sums in child units exactly as `mergeGroup`'s own subUnit branch does, with both sides always using one basis or neither.**
- ✅ **Four changes: group-keyed conservation · the sub-unit basis · exact-name-then-fold matching (row-for-row echoes stay byte-identical) · and a structural backstop — every `aiSubset` entry is popped, absorbed, refused or rebuilt, with no fifth path.** ⚠️ **`leftoverIndices` is GONE: its only remaining job was handing a dropped row's slot to a row the model invented, which is how the drop stayed invisible.**
- 🔴 **THE "THREE RUNS, 65/65/65 IDENTICAL" MEASUREMENT IS WITHDRAWN AS EVIDENCE OF DETERMINISM — CC RETRACTED IT AGAINST ITSELF on September 9.** **A refusal is a deterministic SINK: whatever the model returns, the rebuild emits the same rows. The identity was the GUARD's, not generation's.** ✅ **It stays valid for what it actually shows — BUG-217's fix produces a STABLE OUTPUT — and that is worth keeping.** 🔴 **It is NOT evidence for BUG-214, and the position block carried it as such until corrected.**
- ✅ **AND A TRUE A/B AGAINST THE PRE-FIX SOURCE on the same branch, same plan, warm catalog — the strongest single result in the block: 64 rows → 65.** **Pre-fix shipped `garlic cloves 15 clove` against pack `1 head of garlic` — ordering 10 cloves for a 15-clove need, a 33% UNDER-ORDER, with two source buckets silently dropped.** Post-fix refuses the merge and ships the full need in two rows, and **logs the refusal** (`grocery_ai_merge_refused`, `canonicalName: "garlic"` — the FOLDED key, `inputRows: 3`, `basis: "sub_unit_children"`).
- ⚠️ **THIS IS EVIDENCE FOR BUG-214, NOT CLOSURE OF IT.** A ≡ B says BUG-217 rather than sampling was the divergence; **but the stored September 7 list's 63 rows came from OLDER code that was never re-run, so the 65-vs-63 pair is not proven to be one mechanism.** One plan, one branch, one evening, temperature 0.
- 🔴 **AND THE GUARD CAUGHT THE MODEL DOING ARITHMETIC ACROSS INCOMPATIBLE UNITS — a second defect class the drop was hiding.** On list `cb5c8f6e` the consolidated inputs are **6 each + 9 clove + 1 head**, and the model returned **16**: `6 + 9 + 1`. ⚠️ **It summed a HEAD as one CLOVE.** True need is `6 + 9 + 10 = 25` cloves. ✅ **Post-fix the guard refuses that merge, the three parts ship separately each carrying a head pack, and the shopper buys 3 heads for a 25-clove need — OVER, not under.** ⚠️ **CC flags "the model summed a head as one clove" as an INFERENCE: `6+9+1 = 16` fits exactly and no other decomposition does, but the model never said so.**
- 🟠 **THE USER-VISIBLE CONSEQUENCE, AND IT NEEDS A DEVICE LOOK: a refusal ships THREE garlic rows on one list** (`garlic cloves`, `garlic`, `garlic head`, plus `garlic powder` separately). **Safe, and it looks broken.** ✅ **The cure is the basis change described below, which BUG-138 has now unblocked.**
- 🔴 **THE TRAP FOR THE NEXT BLOCK, AND IT IS WORTH MORE THAN THE FIX. The garlic group is now REFUSED rather than merged, because `conversionForGroup` picks whichever member resolves first — here the ladder-less `garlic cloves` row.** **Making the basis see the ladder would let it merge to 15 cloves — and the pack would STILL read `1 head of garlic`, because the pack ladder is BUG-138's separate fix. That converts a SAFE REFUSAL into a SILENT UNDER-ORDER.** 🔴 **The basis change and BUG-138's ladder resolution must land TOGETHER OR NOT AT ALL.** ✅ **CLEARED September 8, 2026: BUG-138 LANDED as `bd2590e`, so the basis change is now SAFE and is the next block's obvious work — it is what collapses the three garlic rows into one correct row. CC declined to take it in the same commit and was right to.**
- 🔴 **THE MECHANISM, IN CC'S WORDS AND WORTH KEEPING:** the AI echoes a merged line under one name; `entriesByName` keys on the raw name, so **a two-row cross-name fold gives each entry a length < 2 and BUG-142's conservation guard skips both.** The unechoed source is never popped from the queue, then finds no host in the `absorbedKeys` loop **because that loop also matches on the unfolded name** — so its quantity and its provenance vanish from the list with nothing raising a flag.
- ⚠️ **THIS IS A BETTER EXPLANATION FOR BUG-214's 65-vs-63 THAN SAMPLING IS, AND IT WOULD SURVIVE THE TEMPERATURE PIN.** `a9fb3b9` removed the sampling source; it did not prove sampling was the cause. **Do not close BUG-214's reproducibility question until this is fixed and a repeated run is measured on a Neon branch.**
- ⚠️ **THE GUARD'S BLINDNESS IS THE TRANSFERABLE PART: BUG-142 conserves quantity WITHIN a name and cannot observe a fold ACROSS names.** Every fold mechanism shipped since — the synonym reader, the component pool, the subsumes work — moves rows across names. **A guard scoped to the pre-fold key cannot police post-fold behaviour, and this is the second time that shape has bitten (BUG-215 was the first, on survivor identity rather than membership).**

### BUG-218 — The ladder asks for a conversion the row already states: 39 live rows carry the mass in `purchaseDisplay` and are still reported as gaps

- **Date:** September 7, 2026 · **Surface:** the subUnit/ladder gap detection — it fires whenever need-dimension ≠ pack-dimension without first reading `purchaseDisplay` · **Found by:** CC, `unit_conversions.csv` pre-fill pass.
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN** · **Priority:** **P3** · **Target:** rides with the intake block (D-WS9-226) · **Cross-ref:** **D-WS9-222** · **D-WS9-225** · BUG-124.
- **Symptom:** 39 of the 260 rows on the conversion-gap list need no conversion at all — the need unit is a mass and the pack display already states a mass: `bacon` need lb against `1 package (12 oz)`, `crushed tomatoes` need ounce against `1 can (14.5 oz)`, five pasta rows against `1 box (1 lb)`, three cheeses against `1 block (8 oz)`. **`1 oz = 28.3495 g` is arithmetic, not a figure anyone has to supply.**
- ✅ **INDEPENDENTLY CORROBORATED AT EXACTLY 39, September 8, 2026** — the pre-fill lane's own `_uc_askwrong.txt` sidecar lists **39 rows, every one of the form *"asks grams per oz but oz IS a mass unit."*** **Same count, same rows, reached from the other direction (the audit flagging its own asks as suspect rather than the review flagging the data).** ⚠️ **Two methods agreeing on a count is worth more than either number alone — and this project has been bitten twice by figures that measured something other than their caption.**
- 🔴 **THE OPEN QUESTION IS WHETHER THIS IS A SCRIPT ARTIFACT OR A LIVE RESOLVER BLIND SPOT, AND IT IS UNMEASURED.** The gap detector that produced the asks is audit-side. **If the PRODUCTION resolver shares the blind spot, then 39 live rows fail a conversion that is pure arithmetic; if it does not, the only cost was 39 wasted review rows.** ⚠️ **Measure that before scoping the fix — the two answers are different bugs with different priorities.**
- ✅ **THE FIX IS TO READ THE DISPLAY BEFORE DECLARING A GAP**, which shrinks the human-review surface by 15% at zero judgement cost. ⚠️ **The complement is the real hole and belongs to D-WS9-222: 22 rows carry a pack unit with NO size stated anywhere** — `1 packet` (fajita/taco seasoning: 1 oz or 1.25 oz by brand), `1 container` (salt), **17 herb rows reading `1 bunch`**, `1 head`, and `4 lb pack` for chicken halves with no count. ⚠️ **Every low-confidence figure in `unit_conversions.csv` traces back to that one missing datum.**

### BUG-222 — Free unverified signup + the one AI route with no limiter + no spend ceiling = unbounded Anthropic cost per attacker

- **Date:** September 7, 2026 · **Surface:** `groceryLists.ts` (`POST /api/plans/:id/generate-grocery-list`) · **Found by:** CC, WS9A Phase 0.
- **Status:** 🟡 **CHEAP HALF FIXED — `fa1859c`, local; the PRODUCT half is OPEN** · **Priority:** **P1 — before a public URL** · **Target:** WS9A · **Cross-ref:** BUG-223 · **D-WS9-229** (the budget ruling) · D-WS9-226.
- ✅ **FIRST, THE GOOD NEWS, AND IT IS THE MOST IMPORTANT SENTENCE HERE: NO AI ROUTE IS REACHABLE WITHOUT AUTH.** All nine anonymous routes were checked against the AI import graph and none touches it.
- 🔴 **SO THE GATE IS NOT AUTH — IT IS SIGNUP, AND SIGNUP IS FREE, INSTANT AND UNVERIFIED**, handing back a working JWT with no email verification and no CAPTCHA. The cost model is *cost per account × accounts an attacker can mint*.
- 🔴 **AND WITHIN ONE ACCOUNT, `generate-grocery-list` IS THE ONLY AI ROUTE WITH NO LIMITER AT ALL** — `groceryLists.ts` is the only route file in the server that never imports `rateLimit`. It is also the most expensive request the server serves (multi-call, Sonnet-inclusive). The 409 guard on an existing list is not a brake: `PATCH /api/grocery-lists/:id` sets status freely, so **generate → archive → generate loops unmetered.**
- ⚠️ **There is no per-user AI quota anywhere in the server** — `GET /api/wizard/limits` reports a cap and enforces nothing. **A limiter on this route is the cheap half; signup hardening (email verification or a CAPTCHA) is the product decision.**
- ✅ **FIXED September 7, 2026 — commit `fa1859c` (LOCAL, NOT PUSHED at the time of writing), WS9A Phase 1.** **CHEAP HALF ONLY.** `groceryLists.ts` now imports `rateLimit` (it was the only route file that never did), gets a `generateLimiterOpts` DI seam matching `wizard.ts`/`cooking.ts`, and mounts the limiter **after `requireAuth`**.
- ✅ **KEYED ON `userId`, NOT `ip`, AND THE REASON REACHES BEYOND TIDINESS.** Under BUG-223 the default IP key collapses behind Cloud Run's front end, **which on this route would mean one user's regenerate exhausting everyone's.** A per-user key is immune whatever `TRUST_PROXY_HOPS` turns out to be.
- 🔵 **THE BUDGET — capacity 4, refill 4 per 300 s — IS REASONED FROM THE LADDER, NOT PRICED, AND IT IS NOW D-WS9-229.** Half the wizard's burst (8) and refilling 5× slower, because canon calls this the most expensive request the server serves. ⚠️ **CC could not price a single generate — that needs a live AI call or an `LLMCallLog` read, both barred in the block — so the number is calibrated by RELATIVE POSITION, not by dollars.**
- 🔴 **AND IT WILL BITE HANS FIRST. A device pass that generates repeatedly hits 429 after four, then one per 75 seconds** — which will read as a bug on his screen. **See D-WS9-229; do not device-test grocery generation without reading it.**
- ✅ **THE EXISTING SUITE CAUGHT THE LIMITER BEFORE THE GUARD DID, AND THAT IS THE ENCOURAGING PART.** The first after-run failed **17 existing cases** with `actual: 429, expected: 502` — the new limiter draining across `groceryLists.test.ts`'s many sequential generates for one user. ✅ **Fixed by handing those 3 harnesses a non-firing bucket through the DI seam, so a limiter doing its job cannot masquerade as a broken assertion.** ⚠️ **The guard's own second case first failed for the WRONG reason** — the module-global store keys on `method:path:userId`, identical across cases, so case 2 inherited case 1's drained bucket; fixed with `__clearRateLimitStoreForTests()` in `beforeEach`.
- 🔴 **STILL OPEN, UNCHANGED: signup hardening (email verification / CAPTCHA) and a per-user AI SPEND CEILING.** Those are product decisions and were deliberately not attempted.

### BUG-223 — The rate limiter is keyed on the TCP peer address and collapses to one global bucket behind a proxy

- **Date:** September 7, 2026 · **Surface:** `rateLimit.ts` + `app.ts` (no `trust proxy`) · **Found by:** CC, WS9A Phase 0.
- **Status:** 🟡 **CODE FIXED — `fa1859c`, local; the VALUE is unverified and gates the deploy** · **Priority:** **P1 — this one breaks the beta for legitimate users on day one** · **Target:** WS9A.
- 🔴 **REFUTES A CHAT-CLAUDE CLAIM, AND THE CORRECTION MATTERS FOR BUDGETING: THERE IS NO "MISSING RATE LIMITER."** There is a hand-rolled token bucket wired into **17 limiter instances across 8 route files**. Chat-Claude's acceleration review said the server has none. **It is misconfigured for a proxy, which is a far cheaper fix than building one.**
- **The break:** the key is `req.socket.remoteAddress`, and the code documents its own failure — *"on a proxied host this collapses to a global limit."* On Cloud Run every request arrives from the Google front end, so that is **one value for the entire internet**. `authLimiter` is 10 per minute: **the first ten people to open the beta lock everyone else out for six minutes.**
- ⚠️ **AND THE OPPOSITE ERROR AT THE SAME TIME:** the bucket store is module-level, so N instances = N× every published limit. **Simultaneously too tight (shared key) and too loose (per-instance buckets).**
- ⚠️ **Getting the fix wrong re-opens the spoofing hole the current code deliberately closed** — `trust proxy` must name the hop count, not `true`. ✅ **Cheapest measurement: deploy any revision with one line logging `remoteAddress` and `x-forwarded-for`, then curl from two networks.**
- ✅ **EXPRESS'S NUMERIC SEMANTICS WERE VERIFIED EMPIRICALLY RATHER THAN FROM MEMORY, and this is the item where that mattered most.** Against `x-forwarded-for: 9.9.9.9, 8.8.8.8, 7.7.7.7`: `0` → `127.0.0.1` (XFF ignored) · `1` → `7.7.7.7` (the LAST hop) · `2` → `8.8.8.8` · unset → `127.0.0.1`. 🔴 **`0` IS BYTE-IDENTICAL TO UNSET, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE REASON THIS COULD LAND AHEAD OF THE INFRASTRUCTURE.**
- ✅ **FIXED September 7, 2026 — commit `fa1859c` (LOCAL, NOT PUSHED at the time of writing), WS9A Phase 1.** `app.ts` gains an exported `parseTrustProxyHops()` and `app.set("trust proxy", …)` from `TRUST_PROXY_HOPS`, **defaulting to 0**, warning on garbage. `rateLimit.ts` key becomes `req.ip || req.socket.remoteAddress || "unknown"`. ✅ **The original "never trust x-forwarded-for" reasoning is PRESERVED in the comment rather than deleted — it still holds for every hop the deploy has not named.**
- ✅ **GUARDED IN BOTH DIRECTIONS, BEHAVIOURALLY (capacity 1, no refill — a second request 429s iff it shared a bucket):** at `hops=0` a different spoofed XFF must still 429 (**the rotation bypass stays closed**) · at `hops=1` the same client shares a bucket and a different client gets its own (**the beta-lockout half**) · at `hops=1` a caller-controlled prefix left of the trusted hop must not move the key · parser: `"true"`/`"abc"`/`"-1"`/`"1.5"` → 0, `"1"` → 1. **Three breaks, all red — including "trust XFF unconditionally", which is the WRONG FIX a future chat is most likely to propose.**
- 🔴 **THE VALUE IS STILL UNVERIFIED AND IT GATES THE DEPLOY. `TRUST_PROXY_HOPS=1` IS REASONED FROM THE ARCHITECTURE, NOT MEASURED — nobody has a Cloud Run instance yet.** ⚠️ **The code is safe to ship at the default (0 ≡ today's behaviour); THE VARIABLE MUST NOT BE SET UNTIL the two-network curl in this entry has been run against a real deployment.**
- ✅ **The per-instance bucket store is deliberately UNCHANGED — it only matters above one instance and it is on the deferred list.** Said out loud rather than quietly fixed.
- ⚠️ **Minor, unverified, harmless:** at `hops=0` `req.ip` returned `127.0.0.1` where the socket may report `::ffff:127.0.0.1` on a dual-stack listener. **It is only a bucket key; existing buckets reset once on deploy.**

### BUG-224 — There is no email provider, so password reset does not work at all

- **Date:** September 7, 2026 · **Surface:** repo-wide — no `resend` / `sendMail` / `smtp` / `sendgrid` / `postmark` / `mailgun` anywhere in `src` or `prisma` · **Found by:** CC, WS9A Phase 0.
- 🔴 **THE ACTUAL CAUSE OF THE 403, FOUND September 14 — and it was not the key and not the code: Cloud Run’s `EMAIL_FROM` was `noreply@kitchenwizard.ai`, ON THE APEX. Resend verified the SUBDOMAIN `notifications.kitchenwizard.ai`. Sending from an unverified domain is a 403 by design.** Read from the live service with `gcloud run services describe kiwi-api --region us-east4 | Select-String EMAIL_FROM` (the `Select-String` form sidesteps the Windows `.cmd`-shim quoting hazard). ✅ **Fix given to Hans: `gcloud run services update kiwi-api --region us-east4 "--update-env-vars=EMAIL_FROM=noreply@notifications.kitchenwizard.ai"`** — same local part, moved onto the verified domain; a new revision of the SAME image, which is correct here. **The final from-name is still PRD OQ-16.3’s decision; this only makes the address deliverable.** 🔵 **The test it unblocks, no app needed: POST `/api/auth/password-reset/request` at the deployed API for the `+8` test account, watch the inbox, complete the reset on the Cloud Run page, sign in on the phone (same Neon). That is BUG-224 + D-WS9-231 + BUG-235’s server half, end to end, for the first time.** ⚠️ **If a 403 SURVIVES the domain fix, the next suspect is the key’s scope — canon’s standing caveat.**
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED — VERIFIED END TO END September 14, 2026.** Cloud Run revision `kiwi-api-00006-97c` carries `EMAIL_FROM=noreply@notifications.kitchenwizard.ai` (read back with `describe | Select-String`); `POST /api/auth/password-reset/request` for the `+8` account returned `success: true`; **the email ARRIVED, the reset completed on the D-WS9-231 web page, and the new password signed in on the phone.** First real delivery in the project’s history. **Two observations left behind; neither reopens this.** (1) 🟡 **The mail landed in Gmail’s SPAM folder. Cause UNVERIFIED** — a first send from a domain with no history is the ordinary explanation; an authentication failure is the one that would matter. ✅ **The 30-second check that separates them: open the message in Gmail → ⋮ → *Show original* → read the three verdict lines (`SPF: PASS` / `DKIM: PASS` / `DMARC: PASS`).** ✅ **READ September 14, late (Hans): SPF PASS · DKIM PASS · DMARC PASS.** So it is new-domain reputation, not authentication — **NO BUG; nothing to chase.** Time and *Not spam* clicks fix it; Resend’s dashboard shows per-message delivery. **Do not re-open this as a defect.** (2) **The reset page is unstyled — BUG-271.** · **Priority:** was P1 · **Target:** WS9A / roadmap row 3a · **Cross-ref:** **BUG-219** · D-WS9-226 · **BUG-235** (the app’s forgot-password screen is STILL un-exercised — tonight’s request went in by `Invoke-RestMethod`) · **BUG-271**.
- **The reset flow generates a token, stores it, and logs it.** There is nothing to deliver it. **In a beta where people will forget passwords and Hans is not going to read logs to rescue them, the account is simply lost.**
- ⚠️ **THIS RECLASSIFIES THE RESEND ROW.** It was scoped as a domain-verification errand for *"email me my list."* **It is net-new work and it gates account recovery**, which is a harder requirement than a convenience feature.
- 🟡 **UNBLOCKED September 7, 2026 — HANS CREATED THE RESEND ACCOUNT.** ⚠️ **That removes the PREREQUISITE, not the work.** Still to do, and none of it is done: the **API key** into `.env` and `.env.example` (see **BUG-228**) · **domain verification** (the part with a real-world wait — DNS records must propagate before anything sends) · the provider module and its DI seam · wiring the reset and email-change flows to it · **and deleting the void-mint comments `fa1859c` left behind, which name this entry as the reason they exist.**

- 🔵 **THESE TWO VALUES ARE NOW FIXED AND FEED THE CODE: `EMAIL_FROM` sends from `notifications.kitchenwizard.ai`, and `PUBLIC_APP_URL` is built on `kitchenwizard.ai`** — the same host that will later serve `/.well-known/apple-app-site-association` for D-WS9-231's universal-link upgrade.
