- ✅ **AND `rua` IS OPTIONAL — RULED September 8 after Hans raised scraping. `v=DMARC1; p=none;` ALONE IS A COMPLETE, VALID RECORD.** **Delete GoDaddy's, keep his, add NO reporting address.** ⚠️ **No mailbox needs buying for this, and none should be.**
- 🔵 **THE REASONING, so nobody "fixes" it back later: at pre-launch volume Resend's own dashboard reports delivery, bounce and complaint PER MESSAGE, which is strictly more actionable than aggregate DMARC XML from mailbox providers.** **The reports become worth having at the moment the policy tightens to `quarantine` — that is when you need alignment data before turning the screw, and NOT before.**
- ✅ **DNS RE-VERIFIED FROM OUTSIDE HANS'S NETWORK 2026-09-08 20:22Z — GODADDY DID NOT RE-ADD ITS DEFAULT.** `_dmarc.kitchenwizard.ai` returns **exactly one** record, `v=DMARC1; p=none;`. MX, SPF (chain resolving) and DKIM all single and correct. `_dmarc.notifications` NXDOMAIN, which is correct inheritance. ✅ **Hans reports the Resend domain is VERIFIED.**
- 🔴 **THE ENV VAR NAME IS `RESEND_SENDONLY_API_KEY`, NOT `RESEND_API_KEY` — Hans named it for the key's scope, and CANON AND THE OVERNIGHT PROMPT BOTH SAID `RESEND_API_KEY`.** ⚠️ **THIS IS THE DANGEROUS KIND OF MISMATCH BECAUSE THE SEAM IS DESIGNED TO NO-OP ON AN ABSENT KEY: code reading the wrong name finds nothing, returns `no_api_key`, logs, and returns success — INDISTINGUISHABLE FROM WORKING.** ✅ **Whichever name wins, `.env`, `.env.example` and the provider module must agree, and the acceptance test must prove a send was ATTEMPTED, not merely that the route returned 200.**
- ⚠️ **WHEN THAT DAY COMES, POINT `rua` AT A DMARC MONITORING SERVICE'S ADDRESS, NEVER AT A PERSONAL INBOX.** **That dissolves both objections at once: nothing of Hans's is published in DNS, and the daily XML attachments from every large provider land somewhere that parses them instead of somewhere he reads.**
- ⚠️ **`_dmarc.notifications` is NXDOMAIN, which is CORRECT — a subdomain inherits the organizational domain's policy.** **But that inheritance is only worth having once the duplicate above is resolved.**
- ✅ **IT UNBLOCKS A SECOND THING THAT IS EASY TO FORGET: D-WS9-226's `ai_gapfill` REVIEW EMAIL** — Hans's ruled mechanism for learning that unreviewed AI-invented packs exist. **Same provider, same domain verification. Scope them together.**
- ⚠️ **DO NOT LET THE RESET FLOW SHIP UNVERIFIED.** A provider that sends from an unverified domain lands in spam, **and a password-reset email in spam is indistinguishable from no email at all** — which is the exact failure this entry describes.
- 🔴 **THE ENTRY MAY HAVE UNDERSTATED THE GAP. Hans, device pass September 7, verbatim: *"there is no external reset password link or option on the auth page."*** ⚠️ **If that holds, the flow is unreachable AND undeliverable** — this entry has been treating it as *built but unwired to email*, and it may be *not entered from anywhere either*.
- ✅ **MEASURED September 7, 2026 (auth+email Phase 0, read-only): HANS IS RIGHT, AND THE WORK IS LARGER THAN THIS ENTRY ASSUMED. VERDICT (b) — BUILT SERVER-SIDE, WITH NO CLIENT AT ALL.** **The gap is now its own entry, BUG-235.** 🔴 **So "wire up Resend" was never the whole job: without a client, a working provider makes reset no more reachable than it is today.**
- 🔴 **AND THE DEFERRAL REASON IS THE OPPOSITE OF WHAT WAS REMEMBERED. `D-WS7-022` (May 19, 2026) says email is sequenced BEFORE Stripe so that subscription receipts inherit a working send path — "Owner: WS9A — sequenced before WS8 Stripe".** ⚠️ **Hans recalled the work as being HELD UNTIL Stripe. Nothing was ever waiting on Stripe. There is no blocker to clear — only work that was never done.** ⚠️ **FOURTH remembered-as-something-else item in two days (after BUG-171's status, D-WS9-224's `(at least one red)`, and the ToS draft).**
- ⚠️ **`D-WS7-022`'s OWN "current state" IS HALF WRONG and should be read with this correction: it calls the log-only behaviour *"fine for Hans's dev-account testing."* **True for email-change, FALSE for password-reset, which he cannot reach at all.**
- 🔵 **COMPANION, ALREADY IN CANON: `D-WS7-024`** — tighten both token expiries from 1 h to 15–30 min pre-launch, deliberately deferred until provider latency is known. ✅ **§3.5 step 7's real-delivery test is what supplies that number.**
- ✅ **SERVER HALF BUILT AND PUSHED — `c38a596`, September 8, 2026 (`fa1859c..c38a596`).** One provider seam injected through the existing auth and `me` router factories so tests record sends and can never reach the provider by accident; both send sites wired — **reset mail to the account address, email-change verification to the NEW address, because possession of that inbox is what proves the change**; `fa1859c`'s void mints and their BUG-224 markers gone; bodies plain text per D-WS7-022; links `https://<PUBLIC_APP_URL>/reset-password?token=…` and `/verify-email?token=…` per D-WS9-231. Suites 1925 / 1923 pass / 0 / 2 skip.
- ⚠️ **THREE CC CALLS, ALL DISCLOSED, ALL ACCEPTED AT AUDIT:** ⓐ **`fetch`, not the Resend SDK — chosen on merit, not after a failed install** (`pnpm-workspace.yaml`'s `minimumReleaseAge: 1440` exists to make a new dependency expensive; the SDK buys one POST; the repo already uses this `fetch` + `AbortController` shape; no lockfile churn unattended). ⓑ **The boot check lives in `index.ts`, not at module import** — the module-load version took down all ~1,900 tests, measured. ⓒ **The gate is KEY PRESENCE, not `NODE_ENV`** — `bug219LogFixture` runs `NODE_ENV=production` deliberately. **Belt and braces: the sender independently refuses a half-set config (`missing_config`).**
- ✅ **DEVICE-TESTED September 8 (Hans, 8 of 9 script items).** Boot check fires as designed — `pnpm dev` refused to start on the key alone and booted once `EMAIL_FROM` and `PUBLIC_APP_URL` were added. **Suite 1923/0/2 with those two variables PRESENT in `.env`, which answers the script's open question: the tests are NOT coupled to `.env`.** Tree clean; `resend api.txt` deleted; `git log --all` for it empty.
- 🔴 **FIRST REAL CONTACT WITH THE RESEND API, AND IT PARTLY SETTLES CC'S UNVERIFIED HYPOTHESIS.** The send reached the provider and returned **`403`**, surfaced as `!res.ok` (not a throw), logged `WARN … event: "email_send_failed" · status: 403`, **and the route still returned 200** — the anti-enumeration contract and the whole error path confirmed on real data. ⚠️ **CC assumed a 401; it is a 403. Mechanically irrelevant (`!res.ok` is status-agnostic) but recorded so nobody re-derives the assumption.**
- ⚠️ **THE 403 DOES NOT VALIDATE THE REQUEST BODY.** A provider that rejects on auth or sending-domain **before parsing** returns exactly this, so the body shape (`from`, `to[]`, `subject`, `text` → `{id}`) is **still unproven. The first SUCCESSFUL send is the real test.**
- 🔴 **TWO THINGS WILL EACH BREAK THE FIRST REAL SEND, AND THEY FAIL DIFFERENTLY — FIX BOTH BEFORE THE ACCEPTANCE TEST.** ⓐ **The env-var name mismatch above** (`RESEND_SENDONLY_API_KEY` in `.env` vs `RESEND_API_KEY` in code) — **fails SILENTLY: the seam no-ops on an absent key, logs `no_api_key`, and returns success, which is indistinguishable from working.** ⓑ **`EMAIL_FROM` must be on `notifications.kitchenwizard.ai`, the VERIFIED sending subdomain — not the root domain.** 🔴 **CHAT-CLAUDE'S SEPTEMBER 8 DEVICE SCRIPT TOLD HANS TO USE `noreply@kitchenwizard.ai` AND REASONED IT FROM "DKIM is at the zone root" — THAT WAS A GUESS ABOUT A RESEND CONFIG THAT DID NOT EXIST YET, AND IT WAS WRONG.** **Sending from an unverified domain 403s forever, so if a 403 survives verification this is the first thing to check — before the key scope and before the code.**
- ✅ **THE NAME RECONCILIATION, RULED: `RESEND_API_KEY` WINS.** The code, the boot check and `.env.example` (BUG-228) all already say it; renaming one line in `.env` is a one-line change on Hans's side against a three-place change in the repo, and the key's scope is a property of the key in Resend, not of its variable name. ⚠️ **The acceptance test must prove a send was ATTEMPTED — a `sent: true` or a provider status in the log — never merely that the route returned 200.**
- ✅ **HANS'S KEY PLAN, September 8, 2026 — ONE KEY, ONE VARIABLE NAME, AND IT IS THE SEND-ONLY ONE.** He created a **full-access** key on September 7 (the one that briefly sat in `prisma/seeds/resend api.txt`) and a **sending-scope-only** key on September 8. **Plan: put the send-only key's value into `RESEND_API_KEY` and DELETE the `RESEND_SENDONLY_API_KEY` line entirely, so exactly one variable claims the key** — §A's decoy-name hazard at env-var scale, and the seam's silent no-op is what makes a second claimant dangerous here rather than merely untidy.
- 🔵 **CHAT-CLAUDE'S ADDITION, NOT AN INCIDENT RESPONSE: REVOKE THE FULL-ACCESS KEY IN RESEND ONCE THE SEND-ONLY KEY IS IN PLACE.** ⚠️ **This does NOT contradict §3.1 r13's recovery rule** — that key was verified never-committed (`git log --all` empty) and moving it was genuinely sufficient for the git-history risk. **The argument is least-privilege, not leakage:** Kiwi only ever sends (message 3's digest included), domain verification happens in the dashboard rather than the API, so **nothing the product does needs full access.** ✅ **The payoff is blast radius: a leaked send-only key lets someone send mail as Kiwi; a leaked full-access key lets them read and change the account's domains and keys.**

### BUG-225 — A recurring row does not say it is recurring, so it reads as a duplicate

- **Date:** September 7, 2026 · **Surface:** the grocery list row provenance label · **Found by:** Hans, device pass on `a9fb3b9`.
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN** · **Priority:** **P2** · **Target:** the grocery display block · **Cross-ref:** **BUG-210** (the fold that would remove the second row entirely) · D-WS9-189.
- **Hans, verbatim:** *"1 dozen eggs is on the list twice. that's ok, but I thought it was supposed to say 1 dozen (recurring) (1 each)."*
- ⚠️ **TWO SEPARATE THINGS AND THEY MUST NOT BE CONFLATED.** That there are two rows is **BUG-210** — a recurring row and its plan twin share an `ingredientId` and still do not fold. **That the surviving row does not announce itself as recurring is this bug**, and it is the cheaper half: with the label, two rows are legible; without it, they read as a defect even to the product owner. ⚠️ **§27's standing lesson applies — if Hans misreads it, a user will.**
- 🔴 **PREMISE REFUTED September 7, 2026 (WS9A lane, code read only) — THE LABEL EXISTS AND IS ALREADY RENDERED.** Two search shapes, gitignore-blind, `dist/` excluded: `isRecurringItem` is a column with its own migration, is projected by the server, is in the wire schema, is mapped and typed on the client, and **the grocery screen draws `{item.isRecurringItem && <Tag label="Recurring" tone="sage" />}`** as a sibling to *"Pantry Staple"* and *"Optional"*. **So this is neither a schema gap nor a missing render, and it must not be commissioned as either.**
- **It narrows to two, with different owners.** ⓐ **The flag is `false` on those rows** — the tag is correctly not drawn, and the real question is why generation or the fold left it false on a recurring row. **That is a grocery-lane data question and it bears directly on BUG-210's twin-row hypothesis.** ⓑ **The flag is `true`, the chip rendered, and Hans was reading the quantity line** — he expected `1 dozen (recurring) (1 each)` *inline*, and the app draws a separate chip below the pack name. **Then it is a presentation-expectation mismatch, not a defect** — and still worth a ruling, because if the product owner looked past the chip a user will too.
- ✅ **SETTLED September 7, 2026 — IT IS ⓑ, AND THE ROW HANS SAW TWICE IS NOT THE SAME FOOD.** Measured on both new lists: the recurring row `eggs` carries **`isRecurringItem = true`** and its chip renders correctly. **The second line is `Large eggs` — a different catalog row (`179e8716` vs `ff985025`), plan-derived, one source, correctly carrying no chip.**
- 🔴 **AND IT IS EXPLICITLY NOT A BUG-210 TWIN.** The two `ingredientId`s differ **and the catalog holds a deliberate high-confidence `distinct` edge saying `egg` is not `large eggs`.** They are judged-different rows and are **supposed** to be two lines.
- ⚠️ **SO THE REAL DEFECT IS LEGIBILITY, AND IT IS NARROWER AND CHEAPER THAN THE ENTRY ASSUMED.** Both lines open with the same three characters for different reasons: the recurring row's pack **is** `1 dozen`; the `Large eggs` row has need `1 each` against a pack unit of `dozen`, which `packsToCoverNeed` cannot relate with no size hint, **so Rule 2 prints the stored pack verbatim** — `1 dozen Large eggs (1 each)`. **Two adjacent lines reading "1 dozen", one chipped and one not.** ⚠️ **Fix the unrelatable-pack render, not the recurring flag.**
- ⚠️ **One oddity, documented not defective:** the alias `egg` is owned by `large eggs`, yet the recurring preference string `egg` lands on the `egg` row — **a canonical name always beats an alias.** Worth knowing before anyone edits alias precedence.
- ⚠️ **Incidental, checked and cleared:** the grocery screen hard-codes `isRecurringItem: false` on the optimistic local row for a user-added extra, before the server round-trip replaces it. **Correct as written; not a source of a false negative on a generated row.**

- ✅ **HANS'S RULING, September 7, 2026 — THE RECURRING EGG DEFAULT IS `1 dozen LARGE eggs`, AND IT IS RULED ON RECIPE CONVENTION, NOT ON SALES DATA.** Hans, verbatim: *"I always buy (at least almost always) and mostly only see Grade A Large eggs... I think that means the recurring eggs should default to 1 dozen large eggs."* ⚠️ **CHAT-CLAUDE SEARCHED FOR THE SALES SHARE HE ASKED FOR AND DID NOT FIND ONE.** USDA's shell-egg reports price and move volume by size band without publishing a retail share; the consumer sources are explainer articles. **No share figure is recorded here, and none may be cited later as though it were.** ✅ **The ruling stands on the stronger fact anyway: the American recipe convention IS Large — a recipe that says "2 eggs" means 2 large eggs unless it says otherwise.** So the default is not a guess about what shoppers buy; it is the size the app's own recipe math already assumes. **A shopper who buys Large is never wrong against a Kiwi recipe.**
- 🔵 **RECORDED AS D-WS9-228** (the generalisable half: a recurring staple's default names the GRADE the recipes assume, not the ungraded generic). **This bullet holds the eggs instance; the rule lives there.**
- ⚠️ **CONSEQUENCE FOR THE `egg` / `large eggs` PAIR, AND IT IS NOT A FOLD.** The `distinct` edge between `egg` (`179e8716`) and `Large eggs` (`ff985025`) **STANDS — do not delete it and do not commission a subsumes fold off this ruling.** What changes is only which row the RECURRING PREFERENCE STRING lands on: the string `egg` today beats the alias and lands on the generic `egg` row; the ruling says the recurring staple should resolve to the **Large** row. ⚠️ **That is an alias-precedence question (the oddity two bullets up), and it is the smallest possible change that satisfies the ruling.** **Do NOT let it become a catalog merge.**
- ⚠️ **AND IT DOES NOT RETIRE THE LEGIBILITY FIX.** Even resolved to Large, two lines can still read *"1 dozen"* for different reasons. **The unrelatable-pack render (Rule 2 printing the stored pack verbatim against a `1 each` need) is still the defect of record here.**

### BUG-226 — A recurring staple carries a pack nobody buys: milk at `1 quart`

- **Date:** September 7, 2026 · **Surface:** the `milk` catalog row's purchase pack · **Found by:** Hans, device pass on `a9fb3b9`.
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN** · **Priority:** **P3** · **Target:** the CSV pass (D-WS9-226) · **Cross-ref:** **BUG-124** · D-WS9-222.
- **Hans, verbatim:** *"I see 1 quart of milk, which is a weird recurring milk to buy. I would assume 1 gallon or 1/2 gallon."*
- ⚠️ **A RECURRING STAPLE'S PACK IS SEEN EVERY SINGLE WEEK, so a wrong one costs more attention than a wrong pack on a food that appears twice a year.** **Sweep the recurring-item catalog rows as their own group in the CSV pass** rather than letting them sit in the 1,569-row tail.
- 🔴 **AND THIS — NOT THE EGGS — IS THE BUG-210 TWIN, MEASURED September 7.** `Whole milk` and `milk` **share `ingredientId d10747ca`** and still ship as two rows, because **the recurring match-or-append tests NAME equality** (`groupKey` `milk` vs `whole milk`), which **D-WS9-188 explicitly left unruled.** ⚠️ **Cross-ref BUG-210 to this row, not to the eggs.**
- ✅ **PROVENANCE SETTLED: THE `1 quart` IS A HAIKU GAP-FILL, NOT THE STATIC TABLE.** Three independent proofs: `lookupPurchaseDefault("milk")` and `lookupConversion("milk")` both return **null**; **no catalog row is named `milk` at all**, so the synthetic recurring entry was built with a null pack — a write-back cache miss by construction; and decisively, **the catalog row the line resolves to holds a DIFFERENT string** (`"1 bottle (1 quart / 32 oz)"`), so the rendered pack cannot have come from the catalog. **Both lists got byte-identical text because `gap_fill_purchase_size` is pinned to temperature 0.**
- ⚠️ **The milk line therefore has NO `pack_class` — it is not one of the 1,569.** Its resolved row `whole milk` classifies retail-shaped. **A catalog-row-keyed CSV pass cannot see this line at all.**
- 🔴 **AND THERE IS NO IN-APP ESCAPE HATCH, BY DESIGN — measured September 7 (WS9A lane, code read).** `purchaseUnit` / `purchaseQuantity` / `purchaseDisplay` appear in **no input schema on any route.** The schema's own comment states the rule: **the pack is DERIVED (reconcile regenerates it from fresh conversion data); the need is AUTHORED and edited via `quantity`.** So the pack is not merely unexposed — **it is defined as non-user-authored, and a reconcile pass would overwrite an edit even if one were possible.** ⚠️ **That is why a wrong recurring pack cannot be worked around by the user and has to be fixed in the catalog (D-WS9-226's CSV load) — and it is the argument for shipping the `ai_gapfill` review email rather than trusting anyone to notice.**

### BUG-227 — `POST /api/me/email/verify-change` is unauthenticated and unlimited — ⚠️ **BUT ITS COST FRAMING WAS WRONG AND IS CORRECTED HERE**

- **Date:** September 7, 2026 · **Surface:** `me.ts:766` · **Found by:** CC, WS9A Phase 0 §1d, **re-surfaced as a candidate at Phase 1 close because it was NOT one of the five.**
- **Status:** 🟡 **OPEN, DOWNGRADED** · **Priority:** **P3 on its own — the P1 that lives here is now BUG-233 (replay), which this entry's probe discovered** · **Target:** the auth+email block · **Cross-ref:** **BUG-233** (the real defect on this route) · BUG-234 · BUG-220 · BUG-222.
- 🔴 **THAT HEADLINE WAS WRONG ON TWO COUNTS AND CC REFUTED BOTH BY MEASUREMENT, September 7, 2026 — with a probe it wrote against the real handler and a stubbed Prisma, no database.**
  - **It is TWO ops, not three, and only ONE is a write.** `prisma.user.findUnique` (conflict check) then `prisma.user.update` (`me.ts:791-806`). ⚠️ **The "three" traces to CC's own Phase 0 phrasing — *"Token check + two DB queries and a user.update"* — which reads as three if the `findUnique` is counted separately from "the queries". Chat-Claude then wrote it into this entry as fact.**
  - 🔴 **AND IT DOES NO WORK FOR AN ANONYMOUS CALLER AT ALL: `INVALID token -> 400, reads=0, writes=0`.** `verifyToken` rejects before the try block. **Reaching the database requires a validly signed `email_change` JWT, which requires `JWT_SECRET`.**
- 🔴 **SO IT IS NOT THE THIRD MEMBER OF THE BUG-220 / BUG-222 CLASS. Those two let ANYONE impose cost; this one requires a credential first.** ⚠️ **The class framing was chat-Claude's, it was tidy, and it was wrong — a pattern is not evidence.**
- ✅ **AND IT CANNOT REQUIRE AUTH — that is by design, not an oversight.** The token IS the auth: the link goes to the NEW address, which may be opened on another device or while signed out. **`requireAuth` would break the feature.** ✅ **Purpose-scoping already works — a session token gets `400` with zero DB ops (WS7-2 Block A doing its job).**
- 🔵 **WHAT REMAINS HERE IS GENUINELY SMALL: no limiter.** Fix shape — key on `payload.userId` AFTER verification (**never on IP; BUG-223 shows why**), plus a small IP-keyed bucket in front to cap signature-check work, roughly capacity 10 / refill 10 per 300 s.
- 🔴 **THE PROBE THAT CORRECTED THIS ENTRY IS ALSO WHAT FOUND BUG-233. Read that one before scheduling this one** — they are one commit's worth of work and only one of them is P1.
- ⚠️ **IT WAS VISIBLE IN PHASE 0 AND WAS NOT COMMISSIONED — that is chat-Claude's scoping call, not a CC miss.** The Phase 1 prompt named five items and CC correctly refused to widen. **Recorded so the next block does not have to rediscover it.**
- **Fix shape (untested — the code has not been read for this):** a limiter at minimum; **the harder question is whether the route can require auth at all**, since it is reached from an email link. **Read the flow before assuming it can.**

### BUG-230 — WS9A minor sweep: four small findings from the Phase 1 close, none individually worth a block

- **Date:** September 7, 2026 · **Surface:** various, api-server · **Found by:** CC, WS9A Phase 1 close (candidates c, d, e, f, g).
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN** · **Priority:** **P4 — fix on next touch of each file** · **Target:** WS9A cleanup · **Cross-ref:** BUG-228.
- **The four:** ⓐ `POST /api/auth/logout` has **no limiter** (`auth.ts:237`) — the only auth route without one. ⓑ `cookie-parser` is a **dependency with zero call sites** in `src/`. ⓒ **dead env key `KiwiInstacartDev`** in `.env` with no consumer. ⓓ **mixed CRLF/LF inside `artifacts/api-server/src/`** — git emits a normalization warning on 4 files every time they are staged.
- ⚠️ **ⓓ IS THE ONE WITH TEETH, AND IT ALREADY COST REAL CARE THIS BLOCK.** `app.ts`, `logger.ts`, `health.ts` and `rateLimit.ts` are CRLF while `auth.ts`, `me.ts`, `recipes.ts` and `groceryLists.ts` are LF. **CC had to write every CRLF edit through a script emitting `\r\n` and verify `bare LF: 0` afterwards, then prove no whole-file churn with `git diff --numstat`.** ✅ **It worked — 43/0, 11/0, 16/5, 94/5.** ⚠️ **A future edit that skips that step reformats the whole file and buries the real diff.** **Normalizing the tree is a one-commit fix that should NOT ride along with behaviour changes.**
- 🔵 **NOT INCLUDED HERE — `x-powered-by: Express` is still advertised and there are no helmet headers.** That is **Phase 0 item N7**, already scoped as hardening; it is named here only so the Phase 1 candidate list resolves to something.

### BUG-231 — The intake path mints prep modifiers as catalog rows: `onion` and `diced onion` ship as separate buy lines

- **Date:** September 7, 2026 · **Surface:** `ingredientResolve` (the one runtime creation point), reached from the mode-A meal parser and recipe import · **Found by:** CC during the two-list verification; ⚠️ **RE-OBSERVED BY HANS ON DEVICE TWICE, September 7, on two different plans.**
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN** · **Priority:** **P2 — it is visible on every list that contains an affected meal** · **Target:** **D-WS9-226's intake block** · **Cross-ref:** **D-WS9-226** (the design that owns the fix) · D-WS9-189 · BUG-210 · D-WS9-223.
- **Hans, device pass, verbatim:** *"I have 1 onion for Corn Tortilla Tacos… I see 1 diced onion for corn tortilla tacos, and other grocery rows for 2 yellow onions for the other meals and 1 white onion for other meals."*
- 🔴 **`diced onion` IS A PREP MODIFIER THAT BECAME A CATALOG ROW.** It gets its own `ingredientId`, its own fold key, and its own grocery line. **No amount of relationship data fixes this, because the two rows are not two foods — they are one food and an instruction.**
- 🔴 **THIS IS NOT A REGRESSION FROM `fa1859c` AND MUST NOT BE READ AS ONE.** It was measured during the September 7 verification pass, before any Phase 1 code existed: one meal through the mode-A parser produced **four onion rows and three ground-beef rows.**
- ⚠️ **SEPARATE THE TWO THINGS ON THE LIST, BECAUSE THEY HAVE DIFFERENT OWNERS AND ONLY ONE IS A DEFECT.** ⓐ **`onion` vs `diced onion` is THIS BUG** — unambiguous, no ruling needed. ⓑ **`yellow onion` vs `white onion` vs `onion` is D-WS9-223's discriminator** (*does the distinction change the dish?*) and is an UNRULED per-item question, not a defect. **Do not fix ⓑ while fixing ⓐ.**
- ⚠️ **THE INTAKE FIX IS THE ONLY REAL FIX.** A fold that strips `diced` after the fact is the whack-a-mole D-WS9-189 exists to end (Hans's own framing). **Normalise prep modifiers out of the ingredient NAME at creation, or keep feeding the fold work it should never have been handed.**
- 🔵 **A SECOND INTAKE-NAMING SHAPE, MEASURED September 8, 2026 in the `unit_conversions` review — recipe-text "X or Y" constructs became single catalog rows: 6 of 260 (2.3%).** `chicken or vegetable broth` · `fresh or thawed frozen corn kernels` · `green or french lentils` · `lard or neutral oil` · `toothpicks or wooden skewers` · `wooden or metal skewers`. ⚠️ **Same root as this bug — a recipe-text construct became a catalog identity — but a DIFFERENT construct, so a `diced`-stripping fix does nothing for it.** 🔵 **One row cannot be two foods: `lard or neutral oil` has two densities (12.8 vs 13.6 g/tbsp).**
- 🔵 **HANS OFFERED TO RULE THE SIX ON September 8 AND WAS DECLINED — HIS OWN REASONING IS WHY, AND IT IS THE DURABLE PART.** He proposed *"chicken broth, neutral/vegetable oil, green lentils"* and then undercut it: *"the point can be made if you need lard you don't want vegetable oil. lard would be more for pastries/baked stuff, so most of our use cases would be the oil."* ✅ **THAT IS THE RULE, AND IT IS NOT A PER-ROW VERDICT: the head term is decided by WHICH SIDE THE RECIPE CORPUS ACTUALLY USES, measured — not by which is listed first.**
- 🔴 **AND ONE OF THE SIX PROVES A PICK IS NOT ALWAYS LEGAL: `chicken or vegetable broth` IS A DIETARY FORK, NOT A SUBSTITUTION HINT.** Collapsing it to `chicken` would silently break every vegetarian user, and dietary restriction is a hard constraint here, not a preference. **Any normalisation rule must carve out the case where the two sides differ by DIET rather than by technique.**
- ⚠️ **WHY IT WAS NOT RULED NOW: nothing is blocked on it, and a ruling made against an unbuilt loader goes stale** (precedent **D-WS9-005** — by build time both its file citation and its prescribed fix were wrong). ✅ **And 6 of 260 is a SAMPLE, not a census — the CSV is a subset of ~1,569 and nobody has counted the shape across the full catalog.**

### BUG-232 — `Celery Seed` is filed under Produce on the grocery list

- **Date:** September 7, 2026 · **Surface:** grocery store-section assignment · **Found by:** Hans, device pass on `fa1859c`.
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN** · **Priority:** **P3 — cosmetic on one row, but it is a shopper sending someone to the wrong aisle** · **Target:** the grocery display block · **Cross-ref:** **BUG-212** (`coffee`, `egg` and `bread` have been sold in `head`s — the same family: a keyword matching the wrong food) · D-WS9-211.
- **Hans, verbatim:** *"I saw Celery Seed in the produce at the top, but otherwise it looked normal."*
- ⚠️ **ALMOST CERTAINLY THE SAME MECHANISM AS BUG-212 AND D-WS9-211's ALLERGEN CLASS: A KEYWORD MATCH OVER AN OPEN VOCABULARY.** `celery seed` contains `celery`; celery is produce; **the modifier that changes the aisle is the word the matcher ignores.** ⚠️ **`Ingredient.category` is NOT a usable cross-check here — D-WS9-211 established that (`vegan butter` is filed as Dairy).**
- 🔵 **UNMEASURED, AND THE MEASUREMENT IS THE POINT: nobody knows how many rows this affects.** ✅ **Cheap: list every live ingredient whose name contains a produce keyword AND a spice/dry word (`seed`, `powder`, `dried`, `ground`, `flakes`, `extract`) and read the assigned section.** **Do not fix one row by hand — that is the whack-a-mole pattern.**
- 🔴 **SECOND CONFIRMED INSTANCE, AND IT IS WORSE THAN A WRONG AISLE — September 8, 2026: `applewood chips` and `applewood smoking chips` are filed under `Snacks`.** **Hardwood smoking chips, in the snack aisle.** ✅ **The mechanism is this entry's exactly: `chips` is the keyword, potato chips are Snacks, and the modifier that changes everything (`applewood`) is the word the matcher ignores.**
- ✅ **SO THIS IS NO LONGER "one cosmetic row, unmeasured" — IT IS A CLASS WITH TWO CONFIRMED MEMBERS AND A NON-FOOD CASE.** ⚠️ **Widen the sweep beyond produce-vs-spice: it must also catch NON-FOODS sitting in food sections.** 🔵 **PRD §12.4's `Household` section already exists and is where wood chips, toothpicks and skewers belong — no new section needed, and Hans ruled these rows out of mass consolidation entirely (D-WS9-226).**

### BUG-233 — 🔴 Every auth token is REPLAYABLE for its full one-hour life — measured, 200/200/200

- **Date:** September 7, 2026 · **Surface:** `lib/auth.ts` — the stateless JWT model · **Found by:** CC, auth+email Phase 0, **measured with an op-counting Prisma stub (`bug227-ops-probe.ts`), no database.**
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED — `bd6baff` + `4721d58`, PUSHED September 8, 2026; migration APPLIED to Neon.** ⚠️ **One narrow verification remains and it is NOT the security property — see the atomicity bullet** · **Priority:** **P1 — account-takeover class, and the largest security finding of the arc** · **Target:** the auth+email block · **Cross-ref:** **BUG-234** (its sibling) · **BUG-227** · BUG-224 · BUG-219 · **D-WS7-024** (expiry, a different lever).
- 🔴 **THE MEASUREMENT, AND IT IS UNAMBIGUOUS: the SAME email-change token returned `200` THREE TIMES IN A ROW.** `INVALID -> 400, reads=0 writes=0` · `VALID x1 -> 200, reads=1 writes=1` · `SAME TOKEN x2 more -> 200, 200, reads=2 writes=2`.
- **The mechanism:** `signToken` embeds `{userId, purpose, …}`; `verifyToken` checks **signature, expiry and purpose — and nothing else.** **There is no `jti`, no `usedAt`, no database row, and no revocation list.** ⚠️ **Nothing marks a token spent, so "spent" is not a state the system can represent.**
- 🔴 **THE PASSWORD-RESET TOKEN IS REPLAYABLE BY THE IDENTICAL MECHANISM, AND THAT IS THE ONE THAT MATTERS: anyone who sees a reset link ONCE can re-take the account REPEATEDLY for the next hour** — and a completed reset does **not** invalidate other outstanding reset tokens.
- ⚠️ **`D-WS7-024` ADDRESSES EXPIRY AND SAYS NOTHING ABOUT SINGLE-USE. NEITHER DOES ANY OTHER ENTRY. THIS IS NEW.** ⚠️ **And shortening the expiry does not fix it — it shrinks the replay window from 60 minutes to 15. A shorter window is not single-use.**
- **Fix shape (untested):** mint a `jti`; reject on replay via a nullable `usedAt` on the relevant row, or a small `UsedToken(jti, expiresAt)` table swept on expiry. ✅ **Forward-only, no backfill — consistent with D-WS9-230.**
- ⚠️ **DO NOT SHIP THE RESET FLOW BEFORE THIS.** Today the flow is unreachable, which is the only reason the replay hole is not live. **Building BUG-235's client without this turns a dormant defect into a shipped one.**
- 🔴 **PHASE 0, September 8, 2026 — THE CHEAP FIX IS NOT AVAILABLE, AND THIS ENTRY OFFERED IT.** The entry's fix shape said *"a nullable `usedAt` on the relevant row, OR a small `UsedToken(jti, expiresAt)` table."* **There is no relevant row: measured, the reset-request handler does exactly `signToken` → `buildAppLink` → `sendEmail`, with no `create` and no `update`, and email-change (`routes/me.ts:750`) is the same.** ✅ **So the `UsedToken` table is the only option, not a preference.**
- 🔴 **AND IT REFUTES BUG-224's PHASE 0, WHICH SAID THE RESET FLOW *"generates a token, stores it, and logs it."* THERE IS NO STORE.** ⚠️ **CC's explanation — that "stores it" described the BUG-219 log line since removed — is an INFERENCE it flagged as unverified. The refutation itself is verified; only the explanation is a guess.**
- ✅ **THE GAP WAS KNOWN AND SHELVED, NOT MISSED: `routes/auth.ts:245` already carries `// TODO(future): add jti blocklist for real server-side revocation.`** It is a comment, not a mechanism — but the design was considered once and deferred without an entry.
- ✅ **Q4 CONFIRMED CLEAN with two differently-shaped searches (one not respecting `.gitignore`): no `jti`, `tokenVersion`, `sessionsValidFrom`, `usedAt` or equivalent anywhere. `model User` has 30 scalar fields and none is a token version, epoch, or password-changed stamp.** The only raw hit was the TODO above; every other match was `Dish.lastUsedAt` / `Template.lastUsedAt` (unrelated, D-WS7-111) or `jsonwebtoken`'s own bundled `jti` support — **the library supports it, we never pass one.**
- ✅ **BUILT September 8, 2026 — `bd6baff` (local).** A `UsedToken` ledger keyed on `jti`, minted on both purpose tokens; a completed reset also invalidates every OTHER outstanding reset token. Suites **1937 / 1935 pass / 0 fail / 2 skip**, up 12 from `c38a596`'s 1925/1923.
- 🔴 **THE BREAK THAT STAYED GREEN IS THE MOST INSTRUCTIVE RESULT IN THE BLOCK, AND IT REVEALS AN ASYMMETRY WORTH KNOWING.** Removing the spent-token ledger did NOT make a replayed RESET token succeed — **the epoch refused it anyway, because completing a reset bumps `tokensValidFrom` past the token's `iat`.** Only the ledger-row-count assertion went red. ✅ **CC treated a green break as a finding about the test (§27.4) and added a CONCURRENCY test — two simultaneous redemptions of one reset token, asserting exactly one 200. The epoch cannot satisfy that (both requests read NULL before either writes); only the atomic primary-key insert can.**
- 🔴 **SO THE TWO MECHANISMS ARE NOT REDUNDANT, AND NOBODY SHOULD LATER "SIMPLIFY" ONE AWAY: the RESET path is covered by epoch AND ledger, but EMAIL-CHANGE bumps no epoch, so the LEDGER IS ITS ONLY PROTECTION.** ✅ **Break 2 proves it — with the ledger removed the email-change token returned `[200, 200, 200]`, reproducing the original probe measurement verbatim.**
- ⚠️ **A TOKEN WITH NO `jti` IS REFUSED OUTRIGHT.** Tokens minted before `bd6baff` are still inside their one-hour window and cannot be proven unused. **Cost: one extra "forgot password" click for anyone mid-flight at deploy — which today is nobody but Hans.** Accepting them would reopen the hole.
- 🔵 **CARRIED, NO ID: `sweepExpiredUsedTokens` EXISTS AND NOTHING CALLS IT.** The prompt forbade a scheduler, so this is deliberate — **but `used_tokens` grows unbounded until something invokes it.** Rows are tiny and dead after an hour, so it is not a beta problem; **it belongs on the go-live list, not in a fast-follow.**
- ⚠️ **UNVERIFIED: `markTokenSpent`'s atomicity is proven against a STUB that models `P2002`, not against Postgres** — the migration is not applied, so two concurrent redemptions have never run against a real unique constraint. **The device pass cannot cover this either; it needs the migration plus a deliberate concurrency probe.**
- 🔵 **THAT PROBE IS NOW CHEAP AND NOBODY HAS RUN IT — the migration is APPLIED as of September 8, so `used_tokens` exists in Neon with `jti` as a real primary key.** ✅ **Two concurrent redemptions of one token against the live database would close the last unverified claim in this entry.** ⚠️ **Until then the atomicity guarantee rests on a stub that models `P2002` faithfully — which is good evidence and is not the same as a measurement.**
- ⚠️ **THE `4721d58` SECOND-FLOORING WIDENS THIS ENTRY'S "kill every OTHER outstanding reset link" CLAUSE BY ONE SECOND, AND THE CLAUSE MUST NOT BE READ AS ABSOLUTE.** **Two distinct reset links minted in the SAME second, one redeemed: the other now survives the epoch check, and the ledger cannot catch it because the `jti` differs.** ✅ **Judged inside the trade already ruled and NOT filed as its own bug — it is a consequence of a deliberate decision, not a new defect.** 🔵 **Reachability: it needs sub-second control over the victim's reset timing, and a real attacker would have to hold the victim's inbox to see either token at all — at which point they have the account regardless.** ✅ **CC surfaced this rather than filing it silently, which is the behaviour the licence-to-refuse clause exists to produce.**
- ✅ **THE ATOMICITY UNKNOWN IS NARROWER THAN IT LOOKED, AND THE SECURITY PROPERTY DOES NOT DEPEND ON IT.** `jti` is the PRIMARY KEY of `used_tokens` (confirmed in the applied DDL), **so Postgres itself guarantees that two concurrent redemptions cannot both insert — one raises a unique violation. Double-redemption is impossible at the database level regardless of application code.** ⚠️ **What has never run against a real violation is the `P2002` CATCH PATH — so the open question is whether a losing concurrent request presents a clean refusal or an unhandled 500.** 🔵 **That is a presentation defect at worst, not a replay hole. The probe (two simultaneous redemptions against live Neon) is still worth running, and is now cheap; it is no longer urgent.**

### BUG-234 — A password reset does not log anyone out: 30-day session tokens survive it

- **Date:** September 7, 2026 · **Surface:** `lib/auth.ts` — `DEFAULT_SESSION_EXPIRY` 30 days, no token-version column · **Found by:** CC, auth+email Phase 0.
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED — `bd6baff` + `4721d58` (`bd6baff..4721d58`), PUSHED September 8, 2026. DEVICE-PROVEN IN BOTH DIRECTIONS against real Postgres: the pre-change token is refused (401) and a re-login inside the bump's own second is accepted (200)** · **Priority:** **P1 — it defeats the purpose of the feature it belongs to** · **Target:** the auth+email block · **Cross-ref:** **BUG-233** (same root: stateless JWTs with no revocation) · BUG-235 · BUG-224.
- 🔴 **THE WHOLE POINT OF A PASSWORD RESET, FOR A USER WHOSE ACCOUNT IS COMPROMISED, IS TO EVICT THE OTHER PERSON. THIS ONE DOES NOT.** The attacker's existing session JWT stays valid for up to **30 days** after the password changes.
- ⚠️ **IT IS SEPARATE FROM BUG-233 AND NEEDS A DIFFERENT MECHANISM — do not let one fix be mistaken for both.** BUG-233 needs a spent-token marker; **this needs a token-VERSION (or `sessionsValidFrom`) on the user, bumped on reset and checked at verify.**
- ⚠️ **The same gap applies to a signed-in password CHANGE (`PATCH /me/password`), which is the path Hans actually exercised on device.** **Check both when fixing.**
- ✅ **Forward-only per D-WS9-230 — a new column defaults harmlessly for existing rows; nothing needs migrating.**
- 🔴 **PHASE 0 MEASURED THE COST, September 8, 2026: THE AUTHENTICATED PATH TOUCHES THE DATABASE ZERO TIMES TODAY.** `requireAuth` (`middleware/auth.ts:14`) does a string op then `verifyToken` (`lib/auth.ts:60`), which is `jwt.verify` plus two field checks — **crypto only, no I/O, and neither file imports prisma.** **So this fix is a genuine hot-path change, not a free correctness fix.**
- ⚠️ **AND `POST /auth/logout` (`routes/auth.ts:246`) IS `return res.json({ success: true })` AND NOTHING ELSE — there is no server-side session state at all.** 🔵 **That is NOT a third instance of this bug and should not be minted as one: a user who logs out is not an attacker holding their own token, and client-side discard is the normal, defensible stateless pattern. Recorded so nobody re-discovers it and mis-files it.**
- ✅ **RULED September 8, 2026 (chat-Claude's call, delegated technical decision): OPTION (a) — read the epoch in `requireAuth` on every authenticated request, narrow `select`, no cache.** **A TTL cache buys the attacker a bounded window after a reset, and eviction-with-a-window is the exact thing this entry says is unacceptable; trading correctness for an unmeasured latency saving is the wrong direction for a P1 takeover path.**
- 🔴 **BUT THE COST FIGURE CC QUOTED MEASURES THE WRONG THING, AND THE CORRECTION MATTERS (§27.6 item 5).** CC counted **"≈54 of ~66 authenticated handlers never load the User row"** — from `grep prisma.user.findUnique`. ⚠️ **The relevant question is not "which handlers load the USER row" but "which handlers make NO DB CALL AT ALL"** — a handler already querying `plans` or `groceryLists` adds one round-trip on an open pooled connection, which is incremental; only a handler doing zero DB work today goes from 0 round-trips to 1, which is the step change. **Phase 1 measures that number instead of inheriting 54.** ✅ **Both errors run in the safe direction: the true count of newly-DB-touching handlers is ≤ 54, so option (a) costs no more than stated and probably much less.**
- ⚠️ **AND THE DATABASE IS NEON, NOT CLOUD SQL** (§2 locked decision) — CC's report says Cloud SQL twice. **It matters: Neon is serverless with cold-start behaviour this project has already been bitten by (`/readyz`'s 2 s bound has never met a real cold start), so the measurement must be taken against Neon, not reasoned from a managed-Postgres model.**
- 🔵 **OPEN, PRODUCT-FACING, FOR HANS: bumping the epoch on `PATCH /me/password` logs the user out of the session they are sitting in**, because the client cannot be handed a fresh token (BUG-235's client is fenced out of this block). **The safe reading is to accept it — change password, get bounced to login, sign in with the new one — but it is user-visible and it is on the path Hans actually exercised on device.**
- ✅ **HANS RULED September 8, 2026: *"bump = auto-log out and require reauth. that's fine by me."*** **Both paths bump.** 🔵 **And the tempting middle option is unsafe and must not be revived: setting the epoch to the CALLER's own token timestamp would leave any attacker session created AFTER the victim's still valid — it breaks the exact thing the fix exists for.**
- 🔴 **THE COST FIGURE COLLAPSED WHEN THE QUESTION WAS FIXED, AND THIS IS THE §27.6-item-5 LESSON IN ITS PUREST FORM.** Phase 0 reported **"≈54 of ~66 handlers pay a new read,"** from `grep "prisma\."` — **which counts MEMBER ACCESS and therefore missed every handler that passes `prisma` INTO a helper** (`resolveRailPlans(prisma)`, `readWizardLastBatch({prisma})`). ✅ **Re-measured against the right question — "which handlers make NO DB call at all" — the answer is: 69 authenticated handlers, 68 already touch the database (N → N+1), and exactly ONE does not.** **That one is `GET /wizard/limits`, and it reads settings through a TTL cache, so it is 0 → 1 only on a cache hit.**
- ✅ **MEASURED against Neon us-east-1, 40 iterations, pool warmed:** bare `SELECT 1` **min 22.93 / p50 29.16 / p95 39.69 ms**; the new narrow PK read on `users` **min 27.12 / p50 30.50 / p95 132.87 ms**. ⚠️ **DO NOT QUOTE "~1.3 ms" AS THE MARGINAL COST — it is a p50-minus-p50 of two unpaired runs, and the min-to-min delta is 4.19 ms over the same pair. The honest statement is that the added statement is at or below measurement noise on this link, not that it costs a specific 1.3 ms.** ⚠️ **The ~29 ms floor is laptop→us-east-1 RTT, which a co-located deployment does not pay — but that is REASONED, not measured from Cloud Run.**
- 🔵 **ONE TAIL NOBODY CHASED: the new read's p95 is 132.87 ms against the bare round-trip's 39.69 ms.** CC called it a cold-connection artefact and did not pursue it. **p95 is what users feel, so if latency work ever revisits this, that is the number to re-take — from production, not a laptop.**
- 🔴 **AN EDGE CASE THAT NEEDS ONE ANSWER BEFORE THE DEVICE PASS: `isIssuedBeforeEpoch` FAILS CLOSED ON THE ONE-SECOND `iat` GRANULARITY, so a SAME-SECOND token is refused.** ⚠️ **If a session minted in the same second as an epoch bump is treated as pre-epoch, then a user who signs in IMMEDIATELY after completing a reset could have their brand-new token refused — a baffling failure that would look like "the new password does not work."** **In practice seconds pass while a password is typed, so this is unlikely rather than impossible; the trade CC took (fail closed) protects a sub-second window that requires an attacker to act within the same second as the victim's reset.** ✅ **Settle it by naming the comparison operator and testing an immediate post-reset login. If the fresh token IS refused, flip to accepting the same second — the usability failure is real and the window it protects is not.**
- 🔴 **CONFIRMED ON DEVICE September 8, 2026, AND THE MECHANISM IS EXACT.** Measured through the API at machine speed: log in → `PATCH /me/password` (200) → **the OLD token is correctly refused (401 — BUG-234's eviction PROVEN)** → **a re-login in the same wall-clock second returns a valid 279-char token that is ALSO refused (401)** → **the same login 5 s later returns 200.** ✅ **`tokensValidFrom` is `TIMESTAMP(3)` (milliseconds) per the migration DDL and JWT `iat` is whole SECONDS, so a token minted at `…07.900` floors to `…07.000`, lands below an epoch of `…07.431`, and fails closed.**
- ✅ **THE SEVERE ALTERNATIVE WAS RULED OUT BY THE SAME TEST, WHICH IS WHY IT WAS RUN THAT WAY: this is NOT a units bug.** Had `iat` (seconds, ~1.78e9) been compared against a millisecond epoch (~1.788e12) without conversion, **every token would be refused forever and a user who changed their password could never authenticate again.** **The 5-second retry returning 200 disproves it.**
- 🔵 **RULED (chat-Claude, delegated technical call): COMPARE AT SECOND GRANULARITY — floor the epoch for the comparison so a token minted in the same second as the bump is ACCEPTED.** ⚠️ **This deliberately accepts a sub-second hole: a token minted a fraction of a second BEFORE the bump, in the same second, survives.** ✅ **The trade is not close — for that hole to matter an attacker's session must be minted in the same wall-clock second as the victim's password change, which is not a reachable attack; meanwhile the current behaviour breaks legitimate logins.**
- 🔴 **AND IT GETS WORSE WITH TIME, WHICH IS WHY IT SHOULD NOT RIDE: today the dead window only catches someone logging in within a second of their own password change — but BUG-235's reset client is exactly where an AUTO-LOGIN-AFTER-RESET flow would be built, and there it would fire on EVERY reset.** **Fix before that client, not after.**
- ⚠️ **NOT A REGRESSION AND NOT A PUSH BLOCKER: before `bd6baff` there was no epoch at all and no token was ever evicted.** The current state is strictly better — correct eviction plus a ≤1 s dead window on a self-inflicted re-login. **Recorded so the trade is legible, not so it is forgiven.**
- ✅ **FIXED — `4721d58`, September 8, 2026, a FOLLOW-ON commit on top of `bd6baff`, deliberately not an amend** (CC's reasoning, and it is right: `bd6baff` shipped a reachable defect and its message documents break results that were true of that tree — **the record should show device acceptance caught it**). The comparison now floors both sides to the second: `Math.floor(epoch.getTime() / 1000)` against `iatSeconds`, so same-second is equal and accepted, strictly-earlier is still refused. **The missing-`iat` branch stays fail-closed — a different question, untouched.** Suite **1940 / 1938 / 0 / 2**, up 3 for the three new guards.
- 🔴 **AND THE SECOND GREEN BREAK OF THE BLOCK IS THE MOST VALUABLE THING IN THIS FOLLOW-ON.** Break 8 (over-floor to the MINUTE) **stayed green**, and the mutation was grep-confirmed real rather than a no-op. **The fixture was the defect: the epoch constants were `12:00:00.000Z` / `12:00:00.500Z` — on a minute boundary, where flooring to the second and flooring to the minute produce the IDENTICAL value.** ✅ **The guard was structurally incapable of detecting a too-wide floor.** **Fixed the fixture, never the claim** — constants moved to `12:00:37`, break 8 went RED, **and it took out a PRE-EXISTING assertion that had been sitting on the same degenerate constant since `bd6baff`, which was therefore partly vacuous too and is now real.**
- 🔵 **CC'S OWN POST-MORTEM, WORTH KEEPING BECAUSE IT NAMES A REUSABLE FAILURE SHAPE:** it justified fail-closed on the grounds that a same-second re-login was unreachable *"because our reset doesn't sign them in"* — **true of the reset route and irrelevant to the route that matters.** `PATCH /me/password` forces a re-login microseconds later, inside the bump's own second, essentially every time. ⚠️ **It had flagged that very path as device-visible in the same report without connecting the two.** **Reasoning about the path you have in mind rather than the path the user actually exercises.**
- ⚠️ **A PASSWORD RESET ALSO KILLS OUTSTANDING EMAIL-CHANGE LINKS — one epoch gates all token classes.** **This is a widening beyond the literal entries and it is the right direction** (a reset means assume-compromise, and an attacker-minted email-change link is exactly what you want dead). **Cost: a legitimate user who reset for unrelated reasons must re-request an email change. Recorded so it is not read later as a bug.**
- ✅ **SCOPE HELD WHERE IT MATTERED: a missing user row is NOT treated as revocation** — `requireAuth` answers *"is this revoked"*, not *"does this user exist"*, so a deleted user's token behaves exactly as at `c38a596`. **Widening that would have changed behaviour well outside this entry.**

### BUG-235 — Password reset has NO CLIENT AT ALL: two server routes with nothing in the app that can reach them

- **Date:** September 7, 2026 · **Surface:** `artifacts/kiwi/` — the entire mobile client · **Found by:** Hans on device (*"there is no external reset password link or option on the auth page"*), **confirmed by CC's code read, two search shapes.**
- **Status:** 🟡 **BUILT AND PUSHED `9ef29e2` (September 13, 2026) — NOT DEVICE-VERIFIED, so NOT yet ✅.** RULED D-WS9-241 (C); the client lane landed all four pieces: `lib/api/passwordReset.ts` (both wrappers, `auth: false`, `z.literal(true)` success schema) · `app/(auth)/forgot-password.tsx` (form → sent; the PRD §3.7 anti-enumeration copy verbatim; 400 → "Enter a valid email address.", other `ApiError` → server copy, network → "Can't reach Kiwi…") · `app/(auth)/reset-password.tsx` (the `kiwi://reset-password?token=…` target; array-safe token; missing token → invalid with NO request; the web page's three client checks verbatim; **an `inFlightRef` double-submit guard**; opaque 400 → one invalid state + "Request a new link"; success → toast then `replace` to sign-in) · the `Forgot your password?` link on sign-in. Four wrapper tests, each asserting a live value against a literal. ⚠️ **AUDITED FROM THE COMMITTED CODE by chat-Claude, not from the lane's report — Hans pushed the branch before pasting it. Everything readable on disk matches the ruling. STILL UNVERIFIED: the suite count, the typecheck, the break-and-restore hashes, and the lane's answer to the `verify-email` / `SessionGate` question.** 🔴 **THE DEVICE PASS IS BLOCKED ON BUG-224's Resend DNS verification** — without a real reset email the deep link can only be exercised from a hand-minted token. **The `/confirm` server test is still owed and rides with the auth-core lane.** *(was 🔴 OPEN → 🟡 IN-PROGRESS earlier the same day)* · **Priority:** **P1 — friends-and-family beta blocker; it is the actual content of BUG-224** · **Target:** the BUG-235 client lane (D-WS9-241) · **Cross-ref:** **D-WS9-241** · **BUG-224** (which framed this as merely unwired) · BUG-233 · BUG-234 · **D-WS9-231** (the deep-link mechanism) · D-WS7-022.

It never specs the CONFIRM screen where the user types the new password — so that half needs a product pass before it needs a build.**

### BUG-238 — A `useState` guard against a same-tick double-fire cannot work, and the pattern has TWO confirmed instances in two files

- **Date:** September 8, 2026 · **Surface:** `app/(tabs)/profile.tsx` (`editingField`) and `app/grocery-list/[id].tsx` (`editingItemId`) — **both fixed; this entry exists for the ONES NOBODY HAS LOOKED FOR** · **Found by:** CC, Lane B, while fixing BUG-236 and BUG-117.
- **Status:** 🟡 **SWEEP RUN September 9, 2026 — ONE NEW INSTANCE FOUND, FIX AUTHORISED** · **Priority:** **P2** · **Target:** the next mobile block · **Cross-ref:** **BUG-236** (instance 1) · **BUG-117** (instance 2) · D-WS9-164.
- 🔴 **THE MECHANISM, ONCE, SO NOBODY RE-DERIVES IT:** a handler wired to fire twice in one tick (classically `onSubmitEditing` + `onBlur` with `blurOnSubmit` at its default) calls `setX(null)` and then guards on `if (!x) return`. **`x` is a closure binding from the current render. A state setter does not mutate the binding the already-scheduled second invocation is holding, so both calls read the stale value and both pass.** ✅ **The fix is a ref that mirrors the state, written synchronously, with EVERY state write paired to a ref write.**
- ⚠️ **AND THE ORDERING RULE THAT CAME WITH IT: clear the guard BEFORE any call that can synchronously re-enter the guarded function.** `Keyboard.dismiss()` blurs the focused input, which fires `onBlur` → the same handler; clearing afterwards reproduces the bug through a different door.
- 🔴 **WHY THIS IS AN ENTRY AND NOT A CLOSED LESSON: two independent authors wrote the same broken guard in two different files, and in both cases the guard LOOKS correct on reading.** **Two instances found without looking makes it a class. Nobody has swept for a third.**
- ✅ **SWEEP RUN, §27.3 SATISFIED: `git grep` over `*.tsx` (39 hits) and a `find | xargs grep` that does not consult `.gitignore` (50 hits); the 11-hit gap is B also sweeping `.ts` test files the `*.tsx` glob excluded — the shapes genuinely differ rather than agreeing by construction.** ✅ **A third command closed the `onBlur` side exhaustively: 5 hits, 2 of them comments, so THE ENTIRE CLIENT HAS EXACTLY THREE LIVE `onBlur=` WIRINGS.** ⚠️ **`node_modules` was pruned for tractability and CC stated that as a limit.**
- 🔴 **INSTANCE 3: `components/PlanNameEditor.tsx:40-41` — `onBlur={commit}` + `onSubmitEditing={commit}` with `blurOnSubmit`.** ⚠️ **IT IS A VARIANT, NOT A COPY: `commit` has NO GUARD AT ALL rather than a broken one.** Both invocations read the same closure, both find `trimmed !== currentName`, and both call `onSave(trimmed)` — **two PATCHes per plan rename.** **Less damaging than BUG-236 because both writes carry identical data, so the second is idempotent if the server is.**
- ✅ **AND UNLIKE THE OTHER TWO, THIS ONE IS TEST-COVERABLE — `components/` is INSIDE the test glob and `components/__tests__/PlanNameEditor.test.ts` already exists.** 🔴 **ITS EXISTING TESTS ARE BLIND BY CONSTRUCTION: they invoke `onSubmitEditing` OR `onBlur`, never both in sequence, and assert `deepEqual(saved, ["Weeknight Speed"])` — which becomes `["Weeknight Speed", "Weeknight Speed"]` on a double-fire.** ⚠️ **A test that was written to pin this behaviour never exercised the one sequence that breaks it.**
- ✅ **FIXED September 9, LOCAL. The failing test was written FIRST and went RED against unmodified code exactly as predicted — `actual: ['Weeknight Speed', 'Weeknight Speed']`. Phase 1's INFERENCE IS NOW A MEASUREMENT.** **9 pass / 0 fail after.**
- ✅ **SHAPE: A REF LATCH — AND THE ALTERNATIVE PROVABLY CANNOT WORK, WHICH SETTLES THE OPEN CALL RATHER THAN CHOOSING BETWEEN TASTES.** *"Make `commit` idempotent on an unchanged name"* would test `trimmed !== currentName`, and **`currentName` is a PROP OWNED UPSTREAM: it has not updated when the second dispatch runs in the same tick, so the comparison is still true and it saves again.** 🔴 **The value that test compares against is exactly the one that has not caught up — the same closure-staleness that causes the bug, one level out.**
- ✅ **A second test pins the RE-ARM, which is the failure mode a latch itself introduces.**
- 🔴 **DEVICE-DISPROVEN September 9: two renames still produced FOUR `PATCH /api/plans/:id` calls, 207 ms and 227 ms apart.** ⚠️ **AND CHAT-CLAUDE'S ASYNC-LATCH HYPOTHESIS IS REFUTED — `commit` clears the ref SYNCHRONOUSLY on its second line, the same ordering as `profile.tsx`. No `finally`, no settle callback, no `await`.**
- ✅ **A NEW TEST REPRODUCING THE DEVICE TIMING (separate `act()`s, a real 220 ms gap, the stale pre-close handler, and an `onSave` that changes `currentName` as the optimistic cache write does) PASSES.** **Also ruled out with evidence: `runPlanWrite` (writes once), a second call site (exactly one reference), and `PlanDateRangeEditor` (no `onBlur`/`onSubmitEditing` at all).**
- 🔴 **THE MEASUREMENT THAT WOULD SETTLE IT AND HAS NOT BEEN TAKEN — AND IT INVALIDATES HOW THIS WAS DIAGNOSED: `patchPlan` serves BOTH the name and the date range on the SAME `PATCH /api/plans/:id`, so two DIFFERENT writers look identical in a URL-only log.** ✅ **The discriminator is the PATCH BODIES: both carrying `{name}` means a genuine double-commit; one carrying `{startDate,endDate}` means the "double rename" was never a rename at all.** ⚠️ **Every diagnosis so far rested on reading URLs.**
- 🟡 **MEASURED WITH THE DIAGNOSTIC, September 9 evening: ONE rename → ONE `PATCH` carrying `bodyKeys: ["name"]`. The earlier four-PATCH result was NOT reproduced.** ⚠️ **One clean sample after two dirty ones is not a fix and not a refutation. It is intermittent or path-dependent, and the impact is two IDEMPOTENT writes.** ✅ **PARKED (chat-Claude, September 9): diagnostic removed, no further cycles. If it recurs, the body-keys log line goes back for one reproduction using the exact earlier procedure — two back-to-back renames.**
- ⚠️ **PROVEN vs MERELY TESTED, as of September 9:** `profile.tsx` **device-proven, not test-proven at device timing** · `PlanNameEditor` **test-proven at device timing AND device-DISPROVEN** · `grocery-list/[id].tsx` **neither — assume nothing.** *(Original ruling:)* **FIX IT, AND WRITE THE FAILING TEST FIRST.** **The defect is already present, so a test that goes RED against today's code and green against tomorrow's is worth more than a break-and-restore.** ⚠️ **Do NOT paste BUG-236's fix — that repaired a guard that existed; this one has none, so the shape is an open call.**
- ⚠️ **THE SWEEP'S SCOPE, STATED HONESTLY BY CC: it is exhaustive for the `onBlur` + `onSubmitEditing` shape ONLY.** **A handler that double-fires by another route — a `Pressable` double-tap, an effect, a gesture responder — is outside what those commands could see, and that class is NOT claimed clean.**
- ⚠️ **NOTHING HERE IS TEST-COVERABLE TODAY.** `app/**` is outside the test glob (D-WS9-164), so both fixes shipped unguarded and any third instance will too.

### BUG-241 — A bare count cannot say WHICH RUNG of the ladder it sits on, so a parent-named row is summed as one child

- **Date:** September 9, 2026 · **Surface:** `conversionForGroup`'s sub-unit basis, and `groceryMerge.groupConversion` (:114-126) / `mergeGroup`'s subUnit branch · **Found by:** CC, while building — and then REFUSING — the BUG-217 basis follow-on. 🔴 **The block was reverted; nothing shipped.**
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN — AND IT IS THE ROOT, NOT A LEAF** · **Priority:** **P2, but it GATES work already queued** · **Target:** the next server grocery block, BEFORE any key-lookup change · **Cross-ref:** 🔴 **BUG-217** · 🔴 **BUG-138** · **BUG-211** (the rule it breaks) · BUG-215 · BUG-214 · D-WS9-189.
- 🔴 **THE MECHANISM, AND IT IS NOT THE LADDER — IT IS THE RUNG.** `garlic head`'s catalog row carries `defaultUnit: "each"`, so its consolidated unit is `each`. **BUG-211's rule reads a bare count as the CHILD — "each means a clove" — which is right for `garlic cloves` and WRONG for `garlic head`.**
- **Measured deterministically, no AI and no DB:** `garlic cloves 6 each` → +6 · `garlic 9 clove` → +9 · **`garlic head 1 each` → +1, when one head IS TEN CLOVES.** **Basis says 16. Truth is 25. The merge buys 2 heads = 20 cloves against a 25-clove need.**
- 🔴 **THE DISTINCTION IS IN THE NAME, NOT THE UNIT — which is BUG-138's own thesis, unapplied here.**
- 🔴 **AND THIS IS WHY THE QUEUED BASIS CHANGE WAS REFUSED.** Before it: the group refuses, ships 3 rows, buys 3 heads = 30 cloves — **OVER, safe, ugly.** After it: when the model returns 16 it merges and buys 2 heads = 20 cloves — **UNDER, silent, the ruled-worst direction.** ⚠️ **Chat-Claude wrote that BUG-138 landing made the basis change safe. IT DID NOT, and CC refused with a deterministic probe rather than an argument.**
- 🔴 **IT IS ALREADY LIVE IN THE DETERMINISTIC MERGE, NOT ONLY THE AI PATH.** `groceryMerge.groupConversion` is the same first-member-wins shape, and `mergeGroup`'s subUnit branch uses the identical `u === parent ? qty * perParent : qty` sum. ✅ **It stays LATENT there only because the group conversion is taken from a MEMBER rather than from the KEY.**
- 🔴 **THE SEQUENCING CONSTRAINT, AND IT IS THE MOST IMPORTANT LINE HERE: BUG-241 AND THE KEY-LOOKUP CHANGE ARE LOAD-BEARING AGAINST EACH OTHER. Fixing the key lookup on EITHER path without fixing the rung FIRST produces the under-order.** **Fix the rung before touching either.**
- ✅ **POPULATION MEASURED September 9, 2026, TWO INDEPENDENT PASSES THAT AGREED EXACTLY (§27.3): Prisma + runtime predicates → 48; raw SQL with its own regex and literal unit list, sharing no code → 48; cross-check `only-A = 0`, `only-B = 0`.** ⚠️ **The vocabulary itself was built two ways and unioned — a 25-term hand list plus the 17 distinct non-count `purchaseUnit` values the catalog actually uses — so the word list is not the limiting factor.**

| measure | count |
|---|---|
| parent-named rows carrying a bare-count `defaultUnit` | **48 of 1,573** |
| …whose fold group carries a ladder | **2** |
| …and the group also holds a child-unit row — **mis-sums today** | **2** |
| live grocery rows on any of the 48 | 61 |
| **live rows on a mis-summing one** | 🔴 **2** |
| fold groups carrying a ladder at all | **1 of 1,212** |

- ✅ **THE TWO ARE `garlic head` AND `whole garlic head`, both `groupKey = "garlic"`.** ⚠️ **AND THE THIRD SPELLING IS CORRECT: `head of garlic` declares `defaultUnit: "head"` and is unaffected — the same concept written three ways, two broken and one right, which is the fragmentation program's whole thesis in one row.**
- 🔴 **THE FUTURE-SCALE ARGUMENT HOLDS BUT CHAT-CLAUDE'S FRAMING WAS WRONG, AND CC CORRECTED THE QUESTION RATHER THAN ANSWERING IT.** **The 48-row scan has a false-negative class: a group's parent is usually the BARE FOOD NAME (`lime`, `celery`, `garlic`) with `defaultUnit: "each"` and NO container noun, so it never matches a name-based scan at all.**
- ✅ **THE RIGHT QUESTION IS HOW MANY FOLD GROUPS HOLD 2+ BARE-COUNT MEMBERS, because authoring a ladder on any of them can mis-sum immediately: 55 of 1,212.** ⚠️ **But most are SAME-RUNG synonym pairs (`lemon`/`fresh lemon`, `bay leaves`/`dried bay leaves`) where summing both as one rung is CORRECT. The dangerous subset is MIXED-RUNG.**
- 🔴 **A SECOND MIXED-RUNG INSTANCE IS ALREADY VISIBLE: `iceberg lettuce leaves` | `iceberg lettuce` — a leaf and a head, exactly garlic's shape.** `corn ears` / `fresh corn` are candidates. ⚠️ **Inferred from names; not verified that the `leaves` row means a leaf.**
- ✅ **VERDICT, CC's, and it is calibrated rather than alarming: D-WS9-226 WOULD install this more widely, but the realistic multiplier is SINGLE-DIGIT (2–5×), NOT 25×.** **That still argues for fixing the rung rule BEFORE the CSV work — retro-fixing authored data costs more than fixing the rule now — but it is NOT an emergency.** ⚠️ **The 2–5× is an estimate from eyeballing 15 of the 55, not a count.**
- 🔴 **AND BUG-214's REFRAME IS A HARD CONSTRAINT ON WHATEVER FIXES THIS: today the garlic group refuses UNCONDITIONALLY, so model noise is invisible. Any fix that makes the refusal CONDITIONAL puts a guard boundary where the model's arithmetic noise lives, and the noise becomes user-visible. Read BUG-214 before designing the fix.**
- ⚠️ **A NARROWER VERSION IS PROBABLY SAFE AND WAS DELIBERATELY NOT BUILT: a garlic group with no parent-named member sums correctly to 15 and would merge to 2 heads.** 🔴 **But gating on "no parent-named member" is the same name-reading inference this entry needs, and CC refused to smuggle it in without its own measurement. That refusal is correct — do not re-propose it as a shortcut.**
- 🔵 **A LATENT TRAP FOUND EN ROUTE AND CORRECTLY LEFT ALONE: `gramsPerEach: 45` on `garlic` is grams-per-HEAD and would misread a clove row by 10×.** ✅ **Structurally unreachable today — `head` is absent from `COUNT_UNITS`, so any group holding a head row fails the grams pass.** ⚠️ **It becomes reachable the moment `head` enters `COUNT_UNITS`.**

### BUG-242 — `fl oz` folds to `oz`, so a fluid-ounce pack is compared against a weight need as if the units were the same

- **Date:** September 9, 2026 · **Surface:** the client unit alias table feeding `packsToCoverNeed` (`artifacts/kiwi/lib/format/grocery.ts`) · **Found by:** CC, self-reported in the BUG-147 fix that made it reachable.
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN — NEWLY REACHABLE, WHICH IS THE REASON IT IS AN ENTRY** · **Priority:** **P2** · **Target:** the next mobile block, or the data-side unit canonicalisation work · **Cross-ref:** **BUG-147** (the fix that exposed it) · BUG-216 · D-WS9-222 · BUG-241.
- **The conflation predates this block and was harmless while it was unreachable:** `(16 fl oz)` used to parse its unit as `fl`, so the row simply refused to scale. ✅ **BUG-147 taught the parser two-word units — correctly — and in doing so made the alias fold LIVE.**
- 🔴 **SO A PACK STATED IN FLUID OUNCES IS NOW SCALED AGAINST A NEED IN WEIGHT OUNCES AS THOUGH THEY WERE THE SAME UNIT.** **For water they are close; for oil, honey, syrup or anything dense they are not, and the error runs in both directions.**
- ✅ **THE SHAPE OF THE RIGHT FIX IS ALREADY IN THE SAME FILE: guard V6 refuses weight-vs-volume outright, because relating them needs a per-ingredient density the client is never sent.** **`fl oz` should be a VOLUME token that hits that same refusal — not an alias of a weight token.**
- ⚠️ **THE TRANSFERABLE RULE: A FIX THAT MAKES A DORMANT PATH LIVE OWES A SWEEP OF WHAT THAT PATH NOW RENDERS.** ✅ **CC caught one instance of this itself in the same block — adding `quart`/`gallon` plurals because the volume work would otherwise have shipped `2 quart` as a new visible defect — and flagged this one rather than letting it ride.**

### BUG-244 — `purchaseQuantity` holds TWO INCOMPATIBLE MEANINGS, so a one-can need ships two cans

- **Date:** September 9, 2026 · **Surface:** the `Ingredient` catalog's `purchaseQuantity` column, written by `fillPurchaseSizesWithWriteBack`'s gap-fill · **Found by:** CC, server lane, running the query that refuted both standing explanations of BUG-243.
- **Status:** 🟡 **DATA FIX DONE AND DEVICE-PROVEN (September 11): all 15 rows read `1` in the column and `1 can (…)` / `1 jar (…)` in the text, and a NEW list showed the right can counts. Existing lists keep their copied text (no repair, D-WS9-230). 🔴 STILL OPEN: the code half — stop the gap-fill writing Class-B packs — and it may already be recurring (below)** · **Priority:** **P2** · **Target:** the code half, with D-WS9-226's intake block; **must not be "fixed" in generation** · **Cross-ref:** 🔴 **BUG-243** (the symptom) · 🔴 **BUG-124** (the machine that wrote it) · **BUG-125** (the ruling that stands) · **BUG-137** (the fragmentation shape) · D-WS9-226 · D-WS9-230.
- 🔴 **THE COLUMN MEANS TWO DIFFERENT THINGS AND NOTHING DISTINGUISHES THEM IN THE SCHEMA. 107 of 1,573 rows carry `purchaseQuantity > 1`:**

| class | meaning | examples | count |
|---|---|---|---|
| **A — pack SIZE** (correct, must NOT scale) | one purchase that IS 1.5 lb / 3 peppers | `80/20 ground beef` 1.5 → *"1.5 lb pack"* · `bell peppers` 3 → *"3 peppers"* · `avocado` 2 | **93** |
| **B — pack COUNT** (should derive from the need) | N separate identical packs | every can row | **14** |

- **ALL 14 CLASS-B ROWS, so nobody has to re-derive the list:** `beef broth` 2 · `canned black beans` 2 · `canned cannellini beans` 2 · `canned mild red enchilada sauce` 3 · `canned navy beans` 2 · `canned pinto beans` 2 · `canned refried beans` 2 · `canned white cannellini beans` 2 · `chicken broth` 2 · `dark red kidney beans` 2 · `low-sodium chicken broth` 2 · `mild red enchilada sauce` 2 · `navy beans` 2 · `whole san marzano tomatoes` 2.
- 🔴 **WHY A CODE FIX IS ACTIVELY DANGEROUS AND MUST NOT BE ATTEMPTED: making generation scale `purchaseQuantity` down to the need would UNDER-ORDER ALL 93 CLASS-A ROWS to fix 14.** ✅ **That is precisely the manufactured under-order the BUG-243 block was stopped to avoid.**
- ✅ **THE DISCRIMINATOR IS IN THE DISPLAY'S OWN WORDING: `each`.** *"2 cans (15 oz each)"* is Class B; *"1.5 lb pack"* is Class A. **Useful for detection; too fragile to build arithmetic on.**
- 🔴 **IT HAS TWO HALVES AND THE SECOND IS THE ONE THAT KEEPS IT FIXED (D-WS9-226's "fix the stock AND close the intake"): correct the 14 rows, AND stop the gap-fill writing Class-B packs, or another one appears next week.** ⚠️ **This is BUG-124's machine writing a wrong pack into the SHARED catalog that every future user inherits — squarely inside D-WS9-230's carve-out.**
- ⚠️ **PROVENANCE IS BY ELIMINATION, NOT EVIDENCE: not seeded (grep returns 0), not curated (every curated can is quantity 1), and `LLMCallLog` STORES NO PAYLOAD — only `promptKey`, model, tokens, latency. A removed seed variant or a manual write would fit the same facts.**
- ✅ **RULED, HANS, September 9: *"They're all individually purchaseable. no packs."*** **All 14 rows → `purchaseQuantity = 1`.** ⚠️ **San marzano tomatoes included — CC's maybe is overruled by the person who shops.**
- 🔵 **AND HANS SPOTTED DUPLICATES IN THE LIST THAT CC HAD NOT CALLED OUT:** `canned mild red enchilada sauce` + `mild red enchilada sauce`, `canned navy beans` + `navy beans` — **plus CC's own `black beans` + `canned black beans`. Three pairs, one shape: a `canned` prefix minting a second catalog row for the same food.** ⚠️ **That is D-WS9-189's fragmentation program, not this entry's data fix — record it there; do not fold rows inside a `purchaseQuantity` correction.**
- ✅ **THE DATA FIX IS NOW FULLY SPECIFIED AND TINY: 14 named rows, one column, one value. It queues behind the server lane's current block.** **The code half — stopping the gap-fill writing Class-B packs — is separate and unscoped.**
- 🔵 **AND A SECOND CLASS RIDES ALONG, UNSIZED: `black beans` vs `canned black beans` is BUG-137-shaped fragmentation IN THE PURCHASE DIMENSION — two rows, one food, disagreeing packs.**
- ✅ **BUG-142 DOES NOT OWN THIS.** BUG-142 is the need exceeding the sum of its sources via the AI merge's arithmetic; here the need is exact and the inflation is in a different field, copied verbatim with no arithmetic. **Its conservation guard reads `quantity`/`unit` and would never see this.** ⚠️ **Chat-Claude proposed BUG-142 as the likely owner and was wrong.**
- ✅ **THE SCRIPT, `312994b` (server lane, September 10): `scripts/bug244-fix-purchase-quantity.ts`, the 14 IDs FROZEN as a literal, the census re-asserted per row and the whole run REFUSED on any drift (proven by drifting one name in a throwaway copy), DRY-RUN by default, `--apply` in one transaction, idempotent, and the 93 Class-A rows hashed before and after** (`sha256 55a653ce…`). **Dry-run against main: all 14 still match, 14 writes needed, `2 → 1` on twelve rows and `3 → 1` on `canned mild red enchilada sauce`; nothing written.** ⚠️ **`--apply` has never executed anywhere, not even on a branch — the device pass runs it on the Neon branch first, then main, and the acceptance is the after-hash of the 93 equal to the before-hash plus 14 × `1`.**
- 🔴 **A 15TH ROW, OUTSIDE THE CENSUS HANS RULED ON: `chunky salsa` — `3 jars (16 oz each)`, `purchaseUnit = jar`.** The `can` discriminator finds 14; the `each` discriminator canon names finds 15. **Class B by identical logic; NOT in the script at `312994b`.** ✅ **RULED, Hans, September 10: *"chunky salsa is purchased 1 jar at a time."* → row 15, `purchaseQuantity = 1`, added to the frozen list as item 0 of the D-WS9-235 Phase 1 prompt; the untouched Class-A set becomes 92.**
- ✅ **APPLIED TO MAIN, September 11 (Hans; same-window verification per the new rule).** `rows needing a write: 15 of 15` → `wrote 15 row(s) in one transaction` → AFTER all 15 read 1, Class-A n=92 hash unchanged → re-run `0 of 15`. **No Neon branch: the dry-run already named the exact rows, and swapping `DATABASE_URL` was the bigger risk.**

BUG-125's no-scale-down ruling is untouched: data only.** Hans paused the device round to ask for this review before continuing.
- ✅ **CONFIRMED IN CODE, September 11 (server lane, read-only Phase 0) — and one step worse than canon said.** The copy is verbatim end to end (catalog → consolidated bucket → gap-fill cache hit → pass-through → row write; the AI merge's pack is overwritten from the source). **`packLeadingQuantity` reads the pack COUNT off the front of `purchaseDisplay`, so the column the script fixed is consulted nowhere on the render path.** The real composer: `2 cans (15 oz each)` against a 15 oz need → `2 cans (15 oz each)`; against 45 oz → **`4 cans`** (an over-order); `1 can (15 oz)` → 1 / 2 / 3 cans at 15 / 30 / 45 oz. **A census of every reader of both columns (two search shapes): no reader of `purchaseQuantity` affects what a user sees; `groceryMerge`'s magnitude parser reads 15 oz either way; `1 can (15 oz)` is the form the gap-fill prompt itself teaches.** ⚠️ **§27.3 earned its keep again: `rg` silently skipped `groceryMerge.ts` and `ingredientRelations.ts` as "binary"; the gitignore-free `grep` found them.**

Dry-run by default, census accepts either form (a drifted string still refuses the whole run; proven), Class-A quantity hash unchanged, a post-fix re-run finds 0 writes (proven by simulation). **Hans's device item 8, taken before the text fix, matches exactly:** *"I saw a 2 cans of beef broth 1 4.5oz can (1 cup) and I found 2 cans (14.5 oz each) low sodium chicken broth (1/2 cup). but 1 can (7 oz) chiptle chile in adobo (3 each) didn't have an issue"* — the chipotle row's text already said `1 can`.
