### BUG-245 — A TellKiwi-generated "30-minute" meal carries a 50–55-minute oven step: BUG-069's class recurring on a NEW meal, possibly constraint-driven

- **Date:** September 9, 2026 · **Surface:** plan generation via TellKiwi — `Meal.estimatedTimeMinutes` versus the meal's own steps · **Found by:** Hans, cooking dinner.
- **Status:** 🟡 **PHASE 1 LANDED September 10 (`09adb58` → `9c79e54`; suite 1960 = 1958 + 0 + 2) · MIGRATION APPLIED + v6 SEEDED (midday) · ✅ BACKFILL APPLIED TO MAIN AND VERIFIED (evening: 1,458 / 20 / 77; two back-to-back dry-runs read the apply's own after-hash `1db94feb…` with zero move). ✅ SHELF PREDICATE `76719f7` + CANONICAL DISH ORDER `bc05d40` (dry-run zero) LANDED, device-unproven. ✅ GAPS CLOSED `ebfec1c` (+ `ed25359`): wizard activation's live slots derive, a scalar time PATCH clears the marker, save-time order reads the stored `positionIndex`; dry-run still zero. ✅ DEVICE ACCEPTANCE PASSED September 11: saved meals measure (28 · 29 · 44 · 56 on a 30-minute plan; `DERIVABLE` 1571, zero move). ✅ CAP-HONESTY BLOCK LANDED September 11 (`3904710` · `9556180` · `ad38241` · `1f0f92b`; suite 2001 = 1999 + 0 + 2), DEVICE-UNPROVEN, reseed pending: the `'most'` ceiling, the Dish Builder re-stamp (the third gap, real), and a selection rule for fresh dinners under a cap. ✅ O1 LANDED September 11 (`f6e6571` · `6ed3b02`; suite 2016 = 2014 + 0 + 2), DEVICE-UNPROVEN, reseed pending (4 bumps with `ad38241`'s three): expand v7 writes a timed outline per dish and the draft's time is derived from it; the authored number is kept as telemetry. ✅ DEVICE-PROVEN September 11 (midday): the drafts read honest numbers (43 · 35 · 23 / 38 · 35 · 55 under a 30-minute cap), every fresh meal outlined, the AI's scalar still clamped while its outline did not. 🔴 STILL OPEN: the SAVED numbers run 11–22 minutes above the draft (38→49 · 35→57 · 55→76) — MEASURED September 11: the outline is the thin side. ✅ BUILT and DEVICE-TESTED September 11 (v8 `d5cd6b3` · `~`/hands-on `ddc1773` · wire `4222db4` · phases `6ad0554`; reseed 1 bump): **the DRAFT is right and Hans accepts it; the SAVE still inflates live meals (+4 to +31 against a ±9 acceptance), and the new suspect is finalize's ATTENDANCE marking.** 🔴 OPEN: that measurement, and the cap-as-ceiling prompt fix Hans ruled. Phase 1's device sequence is further down this entry** · **Priority:** **P1 for beta** · **Target:** its own server block, now in flight · **Cross-ref:** 🔴 **BUG-069** (the class) · 🔴 **D-WS9-122** (the definition that was supposed to stop recurrence) · D-WS9-128 · **D-WS9-230** (no backfills — so the fix is the prompt, forward-only).
- **Hans, verbatim:** *"I generated this plan on Sunday when I was preparing for the week using TellKiwi, and the plan looked great. However, one of my preferences was 30 minute meals every day. And the meatloaf meal says medium difficulty and 30 minutes. The meatloaf is supposed to go into the oven for 50-55 minutes."* — *Classic Beef Meatloaf with Roasted Green Beans and Garlic Mashed Potatoes*, plan *Tacos, Meatloaf, and Two Veggie-Packed Weeknights*.
- 🔴 **THIS SHOULD NOT BE POSSIBLE ON A MEAL GENERATED THIS WEEK.** BUG-069 was fixed August 8 with a provable detection rule — **a meal cannot take less wall-clock than one of its own steps** — and D-WS9-122 put the wall-clock definition into the generation prompts so new meals would not reintroduce it. ⚠️ **But D-WS9-122's entry names only the import-reformat and Mode-A parse prompts. It does not name the TellKiwi / plan-build path.** **Either the definition never reached that path, or it did and something else overrides it.**
- 🔴 **THE VARIANT BUG-069 DID NOT HAVE: A CONSTRAINT. Hans asked for 30-minute meals.** 🔵 **Chat-Claude's hypothesis, flagged as mine and unverified: the generator wrote `30` to SATISFY the constraint while writing honest 55-minute steps.** **If so, this is not undercounting — it is the constraint driving a false claim, and every "30-minute" plan may contain meals that are nothing of the kind.** ⚠️ **The alternative is plain BUG-069 leakage on an unguarded path. Phase 0 distinguishes them: is the 30-minute preference a hard filter, a prompt target, a per-meal instruction, or unpassed?**
- ✅ **BUG-069's RULE IS THE INSTRUMENT: run `estimatedTimeMinutes < max(step.estimatedMinutes)` across all meals, split by source and by before/after August 8.** **Non-zero after August 8 is the leak measured. But if the oven step's own `estimatedMinutes` is ALSO small while its text says 50–55, the leak is a layer deeper than BUG-069 ever reached and the provable rule cannot see it.**
- 🔵 **HANS, September 9, AND IT SOFTENS D-WS9-230 FOR THIS CASE SPECIFICALLY:** *"the time to cook should be estimated or calculated at the meal level, but this MIGHT be one of those data backfill things we would need to do to evaluate the expected cook time. we have meals, dishes, ingredients, and STEPS in the catalog, so the data should be there."* ✅ **So a CALCULATED backfill from step data is on the table here — it is deterministic (BUG-069's `max(current, Σ steps)` shape, D-WS9-128's gating), it needs no AI, and meals are shared catalog, which D-WS9-230's carve-out already covers.** ⚠️ **Evaluate AFTER Phase 0 says which path leaked; a backfill that drains the pool without closing the leak is D-WS9-122's lesson repeated.**
- ✅ **Q1, THE MEAL:** `estimatedTimeMinutes=30`, `difficulty=medium`, `sourceType=live_writeback`, created 2026-09-07T15:58. **The oven step, verbatim: `#5 58min cook — "Bake at 375°F for 55–60 minutes, until an instant-read thermometer inserted in the center reads 160°F."`** **BUG-069's rule: `30 < 58` → VIOLATES. The step is honest; the meal-level field is the lie.** All three dish-level scalars are 30; the meatloaf dish's own steps sum to 95.
- 🔴 **Q2, THE PROMPT — D-WS9-122's DEFINITION NEVER REACHED THE WIZARD PATH, CONFIRMED.** `wizard.candidate.expand v5` authors the field; probed live from the DB (not the seed file, which can drift) for *fridge* · *wall-clock* · *unattended* · *start to finish* — **none present.** Its only definition: *"integer total time in minutes including prep + cook."* **"Prep + cook" is precisely what a model reads as HANDS-ON time.** The prompt itself states the two-pass split — steps come from a separate save-time call, `finalize_steps v7` — **and nothing reconciles them.**
- 🔴 **Q3, THE CONSTRAINT — CHAT-CLAUDE'S HYPOTHESIS IS CONFIRMED BY THE PROMPT'S OWN WORDS, NOT INFERRED.** The cap threads `wizardPreferences → wizardExpansion → candidateContext` as a prompt target. **Verbatim from the live v5 body:** *"treat it as a REAL ceiling on the estimatedTimeMinutes you author … the cap applies to EVERY meal, including the fanciest night. No exceptions. … a dish can be easy-but-slow (a hands-off braise that simmers an hour) … Author estimatedTimeMinutes from the actual recipe, then keep it under the cap."* 🔴 **So the model was told the field means prep + cook, that a dish simmering an hour is compatible with the cap, and to keep the number under the cap with no exceptions. WRITING 30 FOR A 58-MINUTE BAKE IS THE COMPLIANT ANSWER TO THAT PROMPT.** Hans's stored preference is `null`; the cap came per-run from TellKiwi (`maxCookTimeMinutes: 30, maxCookTimeCoverage: "all"`), and the batch's own rationale reads *"All four meals are aimed at your 30-minute cap."*
- 🔴 **Q4, THE SIZE — AND BUG-069's PROVABLE RULE UNDER-DETECTS BY ~35×.** The rule fires only when ONE step exceeds the meal. **`parallelGroup` is 0 of 25,564 steps, so a dish's own steps are strictly sequential, and the longest dish's serial sum is a tighter honest floor:**

| bound | all 1,605 meals | after Aug 8 (146) |
|---|---|---|
| BUG-069's rule (`est < max step`) | 34 (2.1%) | 8 (5.5%) |
| `est < longest dish's serial sum` | **1,199 (74.7%)** | **114 (78.1%)** |
| same, EXCLUDING all prep (most generous) | **649 (40.4%)** | **68 (46.6%)** |

  🔴 **Even granting every prep step as make-ahead and free, 40% of all meals and 47% of recent ones claim a time shorter than their longest dish's day-of work alone.** ⚠️ **The post-Aug-8 rate is HIGHER than before the D-WS9-122 change — the definition went into the wrong prompts.**
- ✅ **THE 8 POST-CUTOFF VIOLATIONS SPLIT CLEANLY, AND THE DISCRIMINATOR IS MECHANICAL:** **constraint class** — 4 rows, all this meatloaf (2 `wizard` + 2 `live_writeback`, 350°F/375°F), `est = 30 = THE CAP EXACTLY`, overshoot +28 (93%) · **plain BUG-069 class** — 4 `batch_generated` rows with no user cap (Carne Asada 55→70, Braised Beef Ragu 135→150 ×2, Al Pastor 55→75), overshoot 11–36%, an authored realistic number. **`est` landing exactly on the cap with ~90% overshoot is the constraint's fingerprint.**
- 🔴 **VERDICT (CC, and it is right): BOTH MECHANISMS, THE CONSTRAINT DOMINATES.** BUG-069's two-pass split is the NECESSARY condition; the cap instruction is the SUFFICIENT one that makes it systematic. **Hans's reading was right and understated: *the "30-minute meals" promise is being satisfied by writing 30, not by choosing 30-minute meals.***
- ✅ **THE FIX, THREE PARTS, DIFFERENT IN KIND:** (1) **PROMPT, forward-only:** `wizard.candidate.expand` gets D-WS9-122's definition and worked example; the *"simmers an hour"* example is deleted or inverted; *"keep it under the cap"* becomes *"if the recipe cannot fit the cap, pick a different recipe"* — **the cap constrains SELECTION, not the number written down.** (2) **STRUCTURE — D-WS9-235:** derive the scalar from steps so no prompt can lie into it; **rule that first, it decides the shape of (1).** (3) **DATA:** a derived formula run once over the 649–1,199 rows; Hans is open and the carve-out covers it.
- 🔵 **RODE ALONG, NOT INVESTIGATED: the same meal exists as FOUR rows** — 2 `wizard`, 2 `live_writeback`, two oven temperatures. **BUG-246.**
- ✅ **HANS RULED September 10, 2026 — AND EVERY CLAUSE IS SCOPE, SO IT IS QUOTED WHOLE:** *"on the structural piece, yes, but to refine that a bit more: the steps need the parallelism baked in to them. bake one thing for 20 minutes and sautee another for 10 minutes can be done in about 20 minutes. we did a lot of work on this and what counts as parallel or not, so the rules are there. I would also say if this requires a backfill or process to get at the data we should do the cached prompt thing and have it run overnight like when the catalog was created. almost more importantly, is if we're going to all this trouble - and we are - we should get active time and cook time. filtering the wizard on active time and whatnot is NOT MVP and can be deferred to the roadmap."*
  1. 🔴 **DERIVE WITH THE SCHEDULER, NOT WITH `Σ steps` AND NOT WITH A SECOND PARALLELISM MODEL.** The rules Hans means are `cookingScheduler.ts` (BUG-018's B2, `141c9b9`): a pure function of each step's `phaseType`, `isTimingSensitive` and `estimatedMinutes`; **unattended/overlappable = `{preheat, rest, hold}` OR `cook && !isTimingSensitive`**, with a tamper-proof comment saying why. **The derived TOTAL is the scheduler's timeline end for the meal's dishes; the derived ACTIVE is the attended minutes on that timeline.** ⚠️ **Whoever builds this reuses that predicate by import, never by copy.**
  2. ✅ **TWO NUMBERS, NOT ONE: `estimatedTimeMinutes` keeps D-WS9-122's meaning (total elapsed wall-clock) and a NEW active-time field is captured beside it.** Filtering or steering the wizard on active time is **NOT MVP — D-WS9-236.** ✅ **DISPLAY RULED September 10 (Hans): *"capture for sure. displayed would be great if it's not much more work."* — so Phase 1 sizes the display (meal card + meal detail) from the design pass's consumer map and includes it if it is small; capture is unconditional.**
  3. ✅ **THE DATA GATE, AND HANS PRE-AUTHORISED THE ANSWER:** if the step metadata the derivation needs is missing or untrustworthy at catalog scale (`isTimingSensitive` was `true` on 0 of 1,959 rows when BUG-018 restored it; today's rate is UNMEASURED), the fix is **an overnight cached-prompt tagging pass over the catalog — the Block 3.8 pattern (`25db784`, 85.7% cache hit), run once** — not a forward-only prompt fix that leaves 1,600 meals lying. D-WS9-230's carve-out covers it (shared catalog).
- ✅ **THE CAP, RULED THE SAME NIGHT — verbatim:** *"on the cap question - if we can't meet their needs, we should be honest about it. the wizard generation falls back to AI, so I would imagine it would figure something out to suggest (right or wrong), so I don't know if we really need an error page or anything."* 🔴 **So: the cap constrains SELECTION (catalog filter on the derived total; the AI fallback gets the cap as a selection instruction and may miss); the NUMBER SHOWN is the derived, honest one whatever was selected; NO error page, no refusal state, no "relax the cap" dialog.** **A 30-minute cap that surfaces a 58-minute meal shows 58 — that is the product telling the truth, which is the whole point.**
- 🔵 **AND HANS'S OWN MITIGATION FOR THE POOL, ON THE SCHEDULE NOW:** *"I will probably do another catalog supplement targeted at 30 minute meals before the big launch and during UAT."* **Recorded in `kiwi_go_live_todos.md`** — it is the answer to "a 30-minute cap against a catalog where 40–75% overshoot will refuse a lot": grow the honest 30-minute pool, do not soften the number.
- ✅ **DESIGN PASS COMMISSIONED AND DONE September 10 (server lane, READ-ONLY; prompt `kiwi_cc_prompt_LANE_A_time_from_steps.md`; answers below). It asked five questions before any schema or backfill was written:** **Q1** what `cookingScheduler.ts` already returns for a meal — does a timeline end exist, and can attended minutes be read off it without changing the scheduler? **Q2** the state of the inputs — `isTimingSensitive` / `phaseType` / `estimatedMinutes` populated on what fraction of the 25,564 steps, by `sourceType`, and how far the derived total would move from the authored one across the 1,605 meals (a distribution, not a count). **Q3** every write path that persists a meal's scalar (wizard expand, `live_writeback`, `finalize_steps`, import, Mode A, batch) — which have steps AT write time, and what the wizard CARD shows before steps exist. **Q4** the prompt fix's remaining shape once the number is derived (the cap becomes a selection instruction; the *"simmers an hour"* example goes; does anything still consume the authored number?). **Q5** the overnight tagging pass — needed or not on Q2's evidence, and its cost from the Block 3.8 datum. **Plus BUG-246's one query.** ⚠️ **No code, no migration, no backfill in this pass. Phase 1 is written from its report.**
- ✅ **Q1 — THE SCHEDULER ALREADY RETURNS THE TOTAL.** `scheduleCookingSequence(dishes) → { steps, totalEstimatedMinutes }`, documented *"wall-clock minutes from cook-start (t=0) to the last finish (serve)"* — a pure call on persisted columns (`stepIndex`, `estimatedMinutes`, `phaseType`, `isTimingSensitive` per dish). **Its anchor is the longest dish, so Phase 0's serial-sum bound was the scheduler's own floor.** 🔴 **ACTIVE cannot be read off the output — `isUnattended` is module-private and `ScheduledStep` carries no flag. The minimal change is `activeEstimatedMinutes` on `ScheduleResult`, computed INSIDE the module that owns the predicate.** CC did not compute active times in the pass because doing so meant reimplementing the predicate — the one thing the prompt forbade. Right call.
- ✅ **Q2 — THE INPUTS ARE FINE, AND THE PROMPT'S WORRY IS REFUTED.** 25,564 steps: `phaseType` fully populated (cook 36.5% · prep 33.9% · assemble 16.3% · rest 6.4% · preheat 6.2% · hold 0.8%); `estimatedMinutes > 0` on 100%; **`isTimingSensitive = true` on 4,191 (16.4%) — not near zero. BUG-018's restoration held.** ⚠️ **16.4% is non-degenerate, not verified correct: CC checked that the flag discriminates, not that any given step is tagged rightly. A wrongly-tagged sear inflates a derived total.**
- 🔴 **Q2 — THE DISTRIBUTION, AND IT OVERTURNS THE PHASE-0 VERDICT.** Derived total vs authored, 1,555 of 1,605 meals (50 excluded — steps missing or total 0, uninvestigated):

| cohort | n | p10 | p50 | p90 | under-claimed |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ALL | 1,555 | +3 | **+20** | +48 | **1,458 (93.8%)** |
| `batch_generated` (no cap exists) | 1,270 | +4 | +20 | +49 | 1,204 |
| `wizard` | 206 | −1 | +18 | +47 | 180 |
| `live_writeback` | 75 | +2 | +16 | +37 | 72 |
| authored value IS a round cap | 475 | +3 | +17 | +42 | 445 |
| authored value is NOT round | 1,080 | +3 | +21 | +51 | 1,013 |

  🔴 **CC, correcting itself, and it is right: "the constraint dominates" is FALSE. Round-capped meals under-claim no worse (p50 +17) than uncapped ones (p50 +21), and 1,204 of 1,270 `batch_generated` meals — which never saw a cap — under-claim.** **The under-claim is systemic across every generation path; the cap did not cause it, it made it visible on a meal the user had been promised would be short.** The constraint-vs-BUG-069 split rested on BUG-069's max-step rule, which under-detects ~35×; against the real derivation the two classes are indistinguishable by magnitude. ⚠️ **Chat-Claude relayed "the constraint dominates" into the position block and D-WS9-235 on the strength of eight rows. Corrected here; the verbatim ruling above is unaffected because Hans ruled the STRUCTURE, not the cause.**
- 🔴 **Q2 — THE POOL A CAP HAS LEFT, WHICH IS THE NUMBER HANS'S UAT SUPPLEMENT EXISTS FOR:**

| cap | over by AUTHORED | over by DERIVED | honestly eligible |
|---|---|---|---|
| 30 min | 1,475 (94.9%) | 1,536 (98.8%) | **19** |
| 45 min | 760 (48.9%) | 1,392 (89.5%) | **163** |
| 60 min | 280 (18.0%) | 988 (63.5%) | **567** |

  **Nineteen meals in a 1,555-meal catalog honestly derive to ≤30 minutes — before dietary fit, discovery flags or archival, which CC did not apply, so 19 is an UPPER bound.** "30-minute meals every day" is near-unsatisfiable by selection; that is exactly why the honest-number ruling matters and why the 30-minute catalog supplement is on the go-live list. Sanity rows: **the meatloaf 30 → 98 · Braised Beef Ragu 135 → 225 · Al Pastor 55 → 132.**
- ✅ **Q3 — EVERY PERSISTENCE PATH HAS STEPS AT WRITE TIME; ONLY THE CARD DOES NOT.** `mealMaterialize.ts:291` and `:522` write `estimatedTimeMinutes: payload.estimatedTimeMinutes ?? 30` — 🔴 **the AI's authored number, DEFAULTING TO 30 when absent — a second, silent source of the cap's own value** — with steps created at `:404` / `:801` in the same transaction, after the meal row but before commit. `mealCreate:83/109`, `mealFork:205/246`, `wizardActivation:227/276` copy a stored value; dish scalars at `:340/:761`. The `LLMCallLog` order settles the wizard path: expand 15:57:26 → `finalize_steps` 15:58:00 → meal row 15:58:12. **Derive-at-save covers every persistence path.** 🔴 **The candidate CARD renders between expand and save, when no steps exist, from `payload.estimatedTimeMinutes` (`wizard.ts:269/388`) — it needs a labelled pre-finalize estimate, and it is the one place the generator's number survives.**
- ✅ **Q4 — RESIDUAL CONSUMERS AND THE PROMPT'S SHAPE.** After derivation the stored scalar is read by `mealCreate` / `mealFork` / `wizardActivation` / `storeShortlist:315` / `storeMealDetails:167` / `storeFill:453` — all inherit the derived value automatically. **Only the card consumes the authored number.** The prompt: the cap becomes a SELECTION instruction; *"then keep it under the cap"* and the *"hands-off braise that simmers an hour"* example both go; the authored number survives only as the card's labelled estimate; **and given nineteen eligible meals, the prompt must carry Hans's honest-number rule, because a 30-minute cap will usually be unmeetable.** 🔴 **CORRECTING D-WS9-235's "the definition half becomes moot": it is moot for the PERSISTED value and NOT for the card — the expand prompt still gets D-WS9-122's wall-clock definition so the card's estimate is at least the right KIND of number.** ✅ **Step minutes need nothing: `finalize_steps v7` already says *"a realistic positive integer for that single step (1–600). Use sensible real-world durations"* with a *"cook on low 8 hours"* example, and the meatloaf's 58-minute step matches its own text. The steps are honest; only the meal scalar lies.**
- ✅ **Q5 — THE OVERNIGHT PASS IS NOT NEEDED.** `phaseType` full, `estimatedMinutes` 100%, `isTimingSensitive` discriminating at 16.4%: the derivation runs today. Priced as contingency only, from `25db784` (1,124 meals · $59.45 · 13h41m): ≈$85 / ~19.5 h on the Sonnet-heavy mix, roughly $8–12 on Haiku with a cached prefix — **and not spent, because there is no measured deficiency to repair. catalog n=1,124: 7.0 · 4 · 24%). **The outlines are the outlier: 2.3–3.0 steps per dish, one prep block per dish, no plating on half the dishes, a preheat on 1 of 8, no rest after a cook.** Four mechanisms, sized against the three deltas: (1) **phases the outline never enumerates but finalize is told to write** — an assemble per dish, a rest after cooked meat, a per-side preheat (≈5 of +11 salmon · ≈7 of +22 al pastor · ≈6 of +21 burgers); (2) **granularity** — finalize writes prep as 3–7 steps at the catalog's grain where the outline writes one block (≈10 of al pastor's +22); (3) **unattended windows the scheduler leaves unfilled** because steps within a dish are serial and nothing is placed into another dish's rest — al pastor's 10-minute marinade sat empty with 11 minutes of sides' prep queued (≈12 of +22; a D-WS9-128-class behaviour of the ruled scheduler, recorded on D-WS9-235, not a prompt error); (4) **one finalize mis-phase** — *"slice 2 onions … soak in buttermilk for at least 15 minutes"* written as a 17-minute attended `prep` step (≈15 of the burgers' +21, and the whole of its active-time jump 48 → 70), under an ambiguity finalize's body already carries (*marinating* listed under both `prep` and `rest`). **No step-minute floor on either side; attended cooks are not over-marked on the saved side** (the outlines mark MORE attended time, not less). `finalize_steps` v7 never reads the meal-level number — it is neither a budget nor a hint; it sets a granularity target (*"4–10 steps per dish … begin with prep, end with serving/plating"*) and duration examples.
- ✅ **HANS, September 11 afternoon, on which side to favour — verbatim, and it backs the honest-number ruling over the cap:** *"I think it's best to be accurate, but if a user asks for 30 minutes or less always and they get a meal that takes an HONEST 38 minutes (vs. blogs saying they have 3 weeks of meals that finish in 30 minutes or less) that's ok in my book. it's a problem when a recipe says 45 minutes and 1.25 hrs later you're eating."* **So the acceptance for a time shown anywhere is that dinner is on the table by then; a cap miss by a few honest minutes is not a defect.**
- ✅ **DECIDED (chat-Claude, delegated, September 11):** **(b) with a prompt correction on the OUTLINE side — `wizard.candidate.expand` v7 → v8: the outline is written at finalize's shape** (prep at finalize's grain; a rest after cooked meat; a plating/assemble step per dish; a preheat wherever a pan or oven must heat; any soak, marinade or chill that just sits as `rest`) so the draft's number is derived from the same amount of work the saved steps will describe; **and the pre-save number is marked as an estimate on the card** (mobile, with the active-time display it already owes). **The skeleton (a) is rejected:** forcing finalize to the outline's 2–3 steps per dish would halve the granularity Cook Mode, the prep gate and per-step timers run on, and would drop the phases from the RECIPE instead of from the number. **`finalize_steps` stays untouched** — the one lie found on its side (the soak) is a single word's ambiguity; **owed, on a second instance:** *"a step that is mostly waiting is its own `rest` step, never folded into the prep before it."* ⚠️ **Small deltas remain expected after v8** (two AI calls, two enumerations); the acceptance is single digits, not zero.
- 🔴 **TWO HYPOTHESES FROM THE SAME LANE, code-read only, carried into the next block as Phase 0 checks:** (i) **both builders drop `phaseType` on save** — the meal-builder and the Dish Builder send `{ text, estimatedMinutes, isTimingSensitive }` and `rematerializeDish` / `rematerializeMeal` write `phaseType` only when supplied, so every step re-created by an "Apply always" or Dish Builder save would fall to the column default `cook`, and the re-stamp would then derive from all-`cook` steps — a fourth D-WS9-235 write-path gap if true, and it would change a meal's time for a reason unrelated to the edit; (ii) **`activeTimeMinutes` is selected but not emitted** — `toListShape` sends only `minutes`, and the client reads `activeTimeMinutes` nowhere — so the hands-on figure is not on the wire yet; the mobile half needs the server to emit it first.
- ✅ **BOTH BUILT (server `d5cd6b3` · `6ad0554` · `4222db4`, suite 2029 = 2027 + 0 + 2; mobile `ddc1773`, suite 1376 / 0; September 11 evening).** **(i) CONFIRMED in data and closed:** dishes re-created by a builder save carried `cook` on 50 of 50 steps (catalog: 35.9%) — minutes survived, phases did not; now an omitted `phaseType` / `isTimingSensitive` inherits the existing step at the same index (sent value wins; a new step defaults). ⚠️ Index-matched: inserting a step mid-dish shifts later phases by one — accepted; a text-similarity match is the fix if it shows on device. Hans's own re-created dishes ("AHans burgers" and eight others) stay all-`cook` — his test residue, D-WS9-230. **(ii) closed:** `activeTimeMinutes` (`number | null`, never absent) beside `minutes` on every payload `toListShape` builds; the `/me/meals` stub now projects through the route's `select`, and dropping the column from `MEAL_LIST_SELECT` turns the list red — the wire-by-projection hazard, demonstrated. **v8's outline rule, verbatim:** *"Write the outline at the grain the written recipe will have — the same steps a home cook follows, in order, one entry per step: usually 4–10 per dish (1–3 for a genuinely simple side; do not pad). Begin with the prep steps as separate entries … include a `preheat` wherever an oven, grill or pan must come to heat, a `rest` for anything that waits with no work … and end each dish with an `assemble` step for plating. A soak or marinade that sits is a `rest` step of its own, never folded into the prep before it."* Headroom v7 p95 1,601 of 8,192; no raise. **Mobile:** one helper formats every time line — `38 min` · `~38 min` (draft) · `38 min · 10 min hands-on` · `~38 min · ~10 min hands-on` — on the draft adapter, the saved-plan adapter and the meal detail header; the list card is a one-line follow-up. CC's expected delta after v8: 0–6 minutes short, only a device round measures it.
- 🔴 **THE v8 DEVICE ROUND, September 11 night (Hans, Android; reseed 1 bump, `promptVersion: 8` in the log). THE DRAFT HALF PASSED.** A 30-minute `all` plan drafted **29 · 44 · 29 · 31** (two shelf, two live); both live meals `timeSource: "outline"`, `outlinedDishes` = `totalDishes` on both, no malformed warning. Hans, verbatim: *"~reads as an estimate … totally acceptable, in my opinion."* **v8's cost, measured: expand input 5,753–5,757 tokens (v7 ≈ 5,380), output 1,253–1,329, 22 s per call** — the ~370 input tokens the outline section adds, as predicted.
- 🔴 **THE SAVE HALF DID NOT MEET ITS ACCEPTANCE (±9 minutes), AND THE SHAPE OF THE MISS CHANGED.** Plan 1: shelf meals 29 → 29 and 29 → 29 (**stable, as designed**); tostadas 31 → 35 (**+4**); chicken thighs 44 → 60 (**+16**, active 38 → 50). Plan 2 (item 5, cap not recorded in Hans's paste): drafts 48 · 47 · 43 · 49 → saved 54 · 56 · 47 · **80** (+6 · +9 · +4 · **+31**). **Three of six live meals missed ±9.** 🔴 **HANS'S OBSERVATION IS THE LEAD:** *"chicken says 60 minutes, but each dish doesn't exceed 37 in the total of the steps. the 60 is WRONG, but INFLATED."* **Read against the scheduler, 60 is arithmetically right and the inflation is upstream of it:** 50 of those 60 minutes are marked ATTENDED, and attended work cannot overlap attended work (one cook), so a 3-dish meal whose steps carry 50 attended minutes cannot plate in 37 whatever the longest dish sums to. **The draft's outline for the same meal carried 38 active; finalize wrote 12 more attended minutes than the outline did.** So the remaining gap is no longer step COUNT (v8 fixed that) — **the suspect is finalize marking cook steps `isTimingSensitive: true` that are genuinely hands-off (a rice simmer, a bake), which inflates ACTIVE and, through the scheduler, TOTAL.** ⚠️ **A hypothesis with one supporting meal, not a finding — measurement commissioned read-only** (`kiwi_cc_prompt_SERVER_attendance_and_ceiling.md`): the attended share of saved wizard steps against the catalog's, step by step on the three worst meals, and whether the 80-minute one is the same mechanism or another.
- ⚠️ **AND A SECOND READING THE MEASUREMENT MUST SEPARATE:** the outline is written per dish, and `deriveMealTiming` schedules the dishes against each other — so a thin outline and an over-attended step set produce the same symptom on a multi-dish meal and different symptoms on a one-dish meal. **The 3-dish chicken (+16) and the 1-dish-ish tostadas (+4) already hint that the miss scales with dish count; confirm before choosing a fix.**
- 🔴 **MEASURED September 12 — CHAT-CLAUDE'S ATTENDANCE HYPOTHESIS IS REFUTED AND WITHDRAWN.** Every `isTimingSensitive: true` cook step in the 17 new wizard meals was read against `finalize_steps` v7's own clause: **43 of 74 are true, and all but four are searing / sautéing / stir-frying / pan-frying / deep-frying — correctly marked.** The four exceptions are grill-with-one-turn steps (**44 minutes in total across four meals**), which the rule leaves genuinely ambiguous (grilling is on neither list; *"default to false when unsure"*). **Over-marked minutes on the two worst meals: chicken 12, burgers 0.** ⚠️ **And the counterfactual settles it: rerun through the real scheduler with the grill hands-off, the chicken plates at 48 (not 44); with EVERY cook step hands-off — which the rule would never endorse — it plates at 45. It cannot reach Hans's 37, because 30 of its 50 attended minutes are PREP and ASSEMBLE, not cooking.** **On the burgers, attendance cannot be the mechanism at all: 35 of its 80 minutes are idle.** ⚠️ **The population gap is real but not new and mostly selection:** new wizard meals mark 61% of cook minutes attended against the catalog's 29% — but the pre-v8 wizard baseline is 56%, and like-for-like against catalog meals of the same length (≤60 min) it is 40.0% vs 37.7%. **A cap selects stovetop meals; stovetop meals are attended. `finalize_steps` v7 has been active since July 21 and nothing about it changed on September 11.**
- 🔴 **WHAT ACTUALLY TRACKS THE MISS: IDLE TIME — unattended stretches the scheduler could not fill.** Burgers +31 with **35 idle**; sausage and peppers +7…+13 with **35 idle**; chicken +16 with 10 idle; tostadas +4 with 3 idle. **Attended share runs the WRONG way** (the biggest miss has the lowest attended share, 13%; the smallest miss the highest, 68%), and dish count is 3 on four of five meals. **So the draft-vs-saved delta is exactly outline-shape versus written-step-shape — the thin-outline reading survives — and the SIZE of the saved number is now suspect in its own right (BUG-256).**
- ✅ **A CORRECTION TO HANS'S READING, WORTH KEEPING:** his *"no dish exceeds 37"* is right about the dishes and does not bound the meal — 50 of the chicken's 60 minutes are attended, and attended work never overlaps attended work. **The chicken dish ALONE derives to 37/24; the meal is 60 because three dishes of hands-on work cannot be done at once.** That part is the scheduler working as ruled.
- 🔵 **HANS, same message, product input toward D-WS9-236 — recorded there, verbatim:** *"if we display the prep time, and especially if we give a prep time max with a flexible total time that's perfect."*
- 🔵 **RODE ALONG, NOT INVESTIGATED: re-opening a saved plan option re-runs expand and pays for it again** (Hans's candidate was expanded three times in five minutes). **BUG-250.**
- 🔵 **SHELF-PREDICATE PHASE 0 (read-only, done September 10):** a threading change, 2 files (`storeShortlist.ts` options + the predicate; `wizard.ts` `retrieveShelf` opts + its three call sites) — the D-WS7-198 resolved bag is already in scope at all three, nothing new is computed. Empty or thin shelf → fully live, per slot, no error, and the cap reaches v6 on live slots. **`'most'` = a shelf-side quota** (CC's counter-proposal, accepted over chat-Claude's flagged-rows-plus-prompt-rule version, which had no pacing vocabulary to anchor to): shelf `≤ cap`, plus ONE over-cap row appended when coverage is `most` — "all but one" by construction, no prompt change. Recorded on D-WS7-166; BUG-248 minted from the same read.

### BUG-246 — One generated meal exists as FOUR catalog rows: two `wizard`, two `live_writeback`, two oven temperatures

- **Date:** September 10, 2026 · **Surface:** meal persistence on the TellKiwi / wizard path — `sourceType` `wizard` versus `live_writeback` · **Found by:** CC, BUG-245's Phase 0, incidentally — it was counting violations and found the meatloaf four times.
- **Status:** 🟡 **REFRAMED September 10 (server lane, Phase 1 item 8) — THE `wizard` / `live_writeback` PAIR IS BY DESIGN, NOT A DUPLICATE: `wizardActivation.ts:402 → publishMealToStore` → `mealFork.ts:146` deep-clones the just-materialised meal into the shared pool (`userId: null`, `isPublic: true`, `sourceType: live_writeback`) per D-WS7-201 / D-WS9-038 — one row is the user's meal, one is the pool copy for reuse, and that is why the pool rows carried `user = -`. CC withdrew its own "genuine duplicate" verdict. WHAT REMAINS: two RUNS (14:38, 15:58) each published a pool copy of the same candidate (350°F / 375°F), so the POOL holds two near-identical meatloafs — the BUG-162 / BUG-166 duplicate-meal class, and evidence the write-back's dedup is keyed too tightly to catch a one-digit variation. FOLDED into that block; nothing to fix here** · **Priority:** **P3 here, P2 in the duplicate-meal block** · **Target:** the duplicate-meal block · **Cross-ref:** **BUG-245** · BUG-137 · BUG-162 / BUG-166 (the duplicate-meal block already queued) · D-WS9-230.
- **The observation:** *Classic Beef Meatloaf with Roasted Green Beans and Garlic Mashed Potatoes*, generated Sunday September 7 from one TellKiwi batch, is **four rows**: 2 `sourceType=wizard`, 2 `sourceType=live_writeback`, split 350°F / 375°F on the bake step. **All four carry `estimatedTimeMinutes = 30`.**
- ⚠️ **NOT DIAGNOSED.** CC did not trace what `live_writeback` is, whether it re-authors `estimatedTimeMinutes` independently of `wizard.candidate.expand`, or why one batch produced two wizard rows with different oven temperatures. ⚠️ **The 350/375 split suggests two independent expansions of the same candidate, not a copy.**
- 🔴 **WHY IT MATTERS BEYOND TIDINESS: every row is a separate carrier of BUG-245's false number, and a fix or backfill that reaches the row Hans is looking at may miss its three siblings.** **And this may be the mechanism behind the queued duplicate-meal block (BUG-162's guard covers 16 of 42 groups; BUG-166 is uncharacterised) — check before treating it as new.**
- ✅ **HANS, September 10, 2026 — A CONDITIONAL RULING THAT TURNS THIS INTO ONE QUERY:** *"on the 4 meatloafs, that might be intentional. we have 6+ burger meals in there for a reason. just mentioning it in case, but if it's THE SAME MEAL that's wrong."* 🔴 **So the question is not "why four rows" but "are the four rows one meal": same name AND same ingredient set AND same step text (modulo the 350/375 digit) → a duplicate and a defect; materially different ingredients or steps → variants, intentional, close it.** **Riding in the BUG-245 design pass as a single bounded query; not its own block.**
- 🔴 **ANSWERED September 10 (server lane): IT IS THE SAME MEAL, TWICE, TWICE.** `5dcee3f0` (`wizard`) vs `6801713c` (`live_writeback`): ingredients SAME, steps SAME. `a2bd1342` (`wizard`) vs `2a3dbaee` (`live_writeback`): ingredients SAME, steps SAME. **The two PAIRS differ from each other (14:38 vs 15:58 — two separate generation runs, 350°F vs 375°F). So each run wrote the meal TWICE, once under each `sourceType`, byte-identical in ingredients and steps.** Per Hans's rule that is a defect. **The seven July `batch_generated` meatloaves are intentional variants** (distinct `dishFamilyKey`: classic-glazed · bbq · italian · bacon-wrapped · mushroom-and-swiss · tex-mex · classic-turkey) — the burger precedent, exactly as Hans said.
- ⚠️ **THE RATE IS UNMEASURED: 206 `wizard` rows vs 75 `live_writeback` overall is not 1:1, so the pairing is proven for these four rows only.** And the mechanism — why a wizard save also produces a `live_writeback` row — is untraced; `mealMaterialize.ts` has two write sites (`:291`, `:522`) and the design pass did not ask which fires twice.
- 🔴 **A MEASUREMENT ARTIFACT CC CAUGHT IN ITSELF, WORTH KEEPING: its first probe reported the steps DIFFER — it had concatenated steps in `dishLinks` order, which varies between rows. Sorting before comparing flipped it to SAME.** **An order-dependent comparison produces a confident false finding; sort, then compare.**
- 🔵 **HANS, September 10 (device): the same pulled-pork meal appeared in TWO of the three plan options of ONE 30-minute generation.** The stored batch (only the latest run per user survives) shows pulled pork once and a live pork-chops slot once — but **the SAME catalog row (`3573a55d`, Ground Beef Tacos) in candidates 1 and 3.** So cross-candidate reuse of one catalog row is REAL in the run that survives, whichever meal Hans saw. ⚠️ **A different mechanism from this entry's residual** (two POOL rows for one meal): this is ONE row offered twice by title selection. **Hans ruled it a defect the same evening — moved to its own entry, BUG-249.**

### BUG-248 — The shelf's difficulty ceiling bypasses the D-WS7-198 resolver: three call sites, three different sources, and the `?? "easy"` shape BUG-201 was about

- **Date:** September 10, 2026 · **Surface:** `storeShortlist` / `retrieveShelf` — the difficulty ceiling on the catalog shelf · **Found by:** CC (Lane A), the shelf-cap Phase 0, incidentally.
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN — NOT fixed by the shelf block, despite `76719f7`'s subject naming it: the D-WS7-198 resolved bag carries four fields and not difficulty, and the block was told not to extend it. Fixing this means adding difficulty to the resolver (presence check preserved) — its own small block** · **Priority:** P3 · **Target:** unplaced · **Cross-ref:** D-WS7-198 (the resolver: `client per-run ?? stored`, presence-check) · BUG-201 (the `?? []` that silently disabled the allergen hard filter) · D-WS7-166 · BUG-245.
- **The observation:** `allowedLevels = allowedDifficultyLevels(opts.difficulty)`, and `opts.difficulty` comes from a different place at each `retrieveShelf` call — build-plans: the client's `aiInput.difficulty`; directed (TellKiwi): `tkPrefs?.difficultyDefault ?? "easy"`; surprise: `storedPrefs?.difficultyDefault ?? "easy"`. **None uses the D-WS7-198 resolved bag, which is computed in the same handler scope a few lines earlier.** Allergens at the same site DO use the resolved value (with a BUG-201 comment on the `?? []` hazard).
- ⚠️ **What it costs today:** on the directed and surprise paths a per-run difficulty override never reaches the shelf, and a user with no stored default is shelved at `easy`; build-plans trusts whatever the client sent. Small, the same class as BUG-201, and one edit away.
- ✅ **The fix, when the shelf block threads the cap through these three call sites: take `difficulty` from the same resolved bag in the same edit** — if the bag carries it. If it does not, do not extend the bag inside the shelf block; report and leave this open.

### BUG-249 — One generation offers the SAME meal in more than one of its suggested plans

- **Date:** September 10, 2026 · **Surface:** wizard title selection — `storeSlots` / `mealTitles` across the three candidates of one generation · **Found by:** Hans, device (a 30-minute TellKiwi run); the class confirmed in the stored batch by Lane A.
- **Status:** 🟡 **BUILT, SEEDED (3 bumps, September 11) AND DEVICE-PASSED ON ONE RUN EACH: no meal repeated across the three plans of a 30-minute generation, and TellKiwi's named meals stayed in all three. The rate is now measured by `wizard_candidate_repeat_check` as testing continues (September 11 midday: another run, `repeatCount: 0`); a deterministic rebind follows only if it stays above about 1 in 10** · **Priority:** P2 · **Target:** that block · **Cross-ref:** BUG-246 (a different mechanism — two POOL rows for one meal) · D-WS7-166 (same stage, same block) · **PRD §5.1** · **PRD §6.5 Scenario C** · D-WS9-191 · D-WS9-237 · D-WS9-073.
- **The observation:** Hans saw the same pulled-pork meal in two of the three plans of one generation. The stored batch (only the latest run per user survives) holds the same catalog row — `3573a55d`, *Cheesy Taco Bell-Style Ground Beef Tacos* — in candidates 1 and 3.
- ✅ **RULED, Hans, September 10, verbatim:** *"When Kiwi generates plans, it shouldn't repeat the same meal across the plans it suggests. So, the tacos example shouldn't be happening."* **It is what the PRD already says — §5.1 [LOCKED]: *"Generate 3 plan candidates that are genuinely different from each other (not three variations of the same plan)."* The code drifted; fix the code (§8).**
- 🔴 **THE EXCEPTION THE FIX MUST CARRY — PRD §6.5 Scenario C: meals the USER NAMED (*"Burgers, grilled chicken caesar, other family friendly meals"*) appear in all three candidates by design; only Kiwi's fillers must differ.** A uniqueness check that ignores this breaks TellKiwi's partial-spec path.
- ⚠️ **"The same meal" means the same catalog id OR the same normalised title** — a live-generated slot carrying a shelf meal's title is still a visible repeat.
- 🔵 **Hans, same message, on why it is worth fixing now:** *"there is an upcoming redesign of the meal plan generation coming where users will select meals for their plan vs. having suggested plans. The suggested plans option will still be available, so that's valuable work to fix."* → **D-WS9-237.**
- 🔴 **NOT THE FATIGUE RULE — KEEP THEM APART (Hans raised it the same evening).** **D-WS9-073** (shipped July 27, under D-WS9-039's *let loved meals recur*) nudges away from meals served in the user's last ~3 plans — ACROSS generations. **This bug is WITHIN one generation, across its three options.** The fix must not phrase or implement itself as general repeat-avoidance, and the server block was sent an addendum: leave the rotation text byte-identical, guard *"never abandoning the shelf"*, scope the new sentence to the three plans in the response.
- ✅ **PHASE 0 (server lane, September 10 night) — WHY THERE IS NO SERVER CHECK YET:** `runAICall`'s one retry ends in a 502 when exhausted, so a Zod refinement cannot "accept after one retry"; **build-plans STREAMS each candidate the moment it validates**, so a whole-set retry would wipe cards already on screen and double the cost; and **the output marks no user-named meal** — `parsedIntent.explicitMeals` is free text, named meals appear *"in spirit"*, and `WizardLastBatch` does not keep it, so the Scenario C exemption is possible only by fuzzy containment. **The prompts demand distinctness (set_preferences, directed) but none says a meal may not appear in two plans; the shared shelf section says only *"don't let the shelf make multiple candidates feel alike."*** All three bodies also still tell the model it *"cannot verify exact cook time"* — false for shelf meals since D-WS9-235 — and the surprise body carries a stale three-candidate distinctness paragraph though it generates ONE.
- 🔴 **THE RATE: a repeat in 4 of 5 stored multi-candidate batches** (n = 5 — `LLMCallLog` keeps no bodies), **every repeat a shelf meal, every one in candidates 1 and 3.** ⚠️ **Hans's own taco example may be Scenario C** — his request text contained *"tacos"*; not traceable, because the parsed intent is not persisted.
- ✅ **DECIDED (chat-Claude, a delegated technical call), from the lane's four options:** **a prompt rule** scoped to the plans in one response, rewriting the existing distinctness text (with the cook-time and surprise corrections in the same edit) **+ a detection log** (`wizard_candidate_repeat`, streamed and buffered, explicit meals exempted by containment) so the rate is measured, not sampled from five. **Check-and-retry REJECTED** — unusable on streaming, and at 4 in 5 it would double most generations. **Rebind-to-an-unused-shelf-meal is the follow-up if the logged rate stays above about 1 in 10** — deterministic and stream-compatible, but it can leave a *why this works* bullet describing the meal it replaced.
- ⚠️ **CHAT-CLAUDE'S ADDENDUM MISQUOTED THE GUARD IT ASKED FOR:** *"never abandoning the shelf"* is set_preferences' wording; the directed body says *"not abandoning the shelf."* The lane caught it before editing anything. **Each body's guard is asserted on its own phrase** (D-WS9-073 corrected).
- ✅ **BUILT (server lane, September 10 late night).** **`b209658` — the rule lives in each multi-candidate body's own distinctness paragraph, not the shared shelf section** (it covers fresh meals too, and the shared section would have pushed a multi-candidate rule into the one-candidate surprise body). set_preferences, verbatim: *"…And no meal may appear in more than one candidate of THIS response — whether it is a shelf meal (same `storeMealId`) or one you compose fresh (same dinner under any title). Each candidate is a different set of dinners, not a reshuffle of the same ones. This is about the candidates you return together; meals from the user's earlier plans are the rotation section's concern, below."* Directed: the same rule for *"no meal YOU choose"*, with the explicit meals *"LOCKED in every candidate — they are the one exception to the rule above."* **The three cook-time sentences now say a CAPPED shelf is filtered on a time measured from steps — and only capped, because an uncapped shelf still carries step-less rows with claimed times** (the lane's precision over chat-Claude's prompt); fresh titles keep a soft bias; directed adds *"An explicitly named meal is honored regardless."* Surprise's stale distinctness section removed (it generates one candidate). **D-WS9-073's rotation sections hash-identical before and after.** **`d3aab11` — one `wizard_candidate_repeat_check` line per multi-candidate generation** (buffered, streamed at completion or failure, TellKiwi with its explicit meals exempted), identity = `storeMealId` for shelf slots, normalised title otherwise; `info` when clean, `warn` when a repeat gets through; never alters the response.
- 🔵 **WHEN THE PLAYLIST LANDS (D-WS9-234):** playlist meals the USER chose to include are user-requested and exempt, like TellKiwi-named meals; playlist meals Kiwi picks on its own are fillers and follow this rule. **Chat-Claude's reading of Hans's *"I don't want Kiwi to get in the way of that"* — confirm at the playlist spec.**
- ⚠️ **The enforcement shape is Phase 0's question, not assumed:** a prompt rule alone depends on the model obeying (the `'most'` design already chose server-side correctness over a count the model must honour); a server check needs somewhere to go on a violation — a retry costs latency, a rebind changes a title the model wrote, and a streamed candidate may already be on screen. **Whatever lands, a violation that survives its retry is accepted and logged, never an error — the honest-number ruling's no-error-page principle.**

### BUG-250 — Re-opening an already-saved plan option re-runs `wizard.candidate.expand`, paying for the AI call again

- **Date:** September 11, 2026 · **Surface:** wizard plan details — the expansion reuse check for a candidate that has already been saved · **Found by:** CC (server lane), incidentally, tracing BUG-245's pre-save number.
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN — UNVERIFIED beyond one session; not measured further, not fixed** · **Priority:** P3 · **Target:** unplaced · **Cross-ref:** BUG-245 (found in its trace) · D-WS9-191 (three cards multiply it) · D-WS9-237 (the multi-select list would too).
- **The observation:** Hans's 30-minute candidate was expanded three times in five minutes (six expand calls for its two live meals; three plan rows with one content hash, two of them archived). The check that reuses an existing expansion matches only rows still flagged `isWizardDraft`; saving flips that flag, so tapping the same option again after saving it expands from scratch.
- ⚠️ **What it costs, from that session's logs:** about $0.05 and 14 seconds per re-open of a candidate with two live meals.
- ⚠️ **CC's hypothesis, not a finding (§27):** how often a user re-opens a saved option, and what re-opening it should do (reuse the saved plan, or expand fresh), are both unmeasured and unruled. **Verify the path and settle the intended behaviour before building anything.**

- **Date:** September 11, 2026 · **Surface:** grocery list rows — two catalog names for one purchase that do not fold · **Found by:** Hans, device (a new list, the BUG-244 check).

### BUG-253 — The Ask-Kiwi import builds every step into ONE dish, so nothing can overlap and the meal's time comes out inflated

- **Date:** September 11, 2026 · **Surface:** Ask Kiwi / TellKiwi meal import → the imported meal's dish structure and its derived time · **Found by:** Hans, device, while setting up the re-stamp test.
- **Status:** 🔵 **ASSIGNED — D-WS9-237** (the meal-selection / import redesign; Hans: *"we'll likely fix that piece as part of the redesign effort"*). **Not a regression — the import path has always done this; D-WS9-235 made its cost visible** · **Priority:** **P2** · **Cross-ref:** D-WS9-235 (the derivation that exposes it) · BUG-245 · D-WS9-226 (the intake program) · D-WS9-237.
- **Hans, verbatim:** *"I created 'Grilled BBQ Chicken Breast with Rice Pilaf and Green Salad' via the ask kiwi import. it worked great, but the steps are built like it's one dish. not multiple dishes… it says 70 minutes of cook time, and I know this is an under 30 minute meal (or at least close to it)."*
- 🔴 **THE TWO HALVES ARE ONE DEFECT.** Steps within a dish are SERIAL by construction (`parallelGroup` is unused); the scheduler only overlaps work ACROSS dishes. **So an import that flattens a three-component dinner into one dish makes every step serial and the derived total is the sum — 70 minutes for a meal whose components would schedule to ~30.** The time is not wrong given the structure; **the structure is wrong.**
- ✅ **MEASURED AND RE-DIAGNOSED (redesign-arc Phase 0, September 16): the prompt DOES ask for dishes (1–5 sub-dishes, per-dish steps), and every `directed` meal in the DB is multi-dish (0 one-dish · 2 two-dish · 3 three-plus). What Hans saw was the CLIENT ADAPTER collapsing every step onto dish[0] — BUG-273's root cause. This entry folds into BUG-273; one fix closes both. Not a separate build.**

### BUG-255 — A meal offered in generations the user never saves can be offered again forever — the rotation only walks SAVED plans

- **Date:** September 11, 2026 · **Surface:** wizard generation — the D-WS9-073 recent-rotation nudge · **Found by:** Hans, device, across several generations.
- **Status:** ✅ **RULED AND CLOSED AS (b), September 12 — measured: the repeat is concentrated where the shelf is thin (six eligible dinners at a 30-minute cap, all six seen), and the nudge fired and lost. The fix is the catalog supplement, not a rule change. The unsaved-generation gap stays recorded on D-WS9-073 as a known, secondary limitation** · **Priority:** **P2 — it is the "kiwi really loves suggesting those dang fajitas" failure D-WS9-073 exists to prevent** · **Cross-ref:** 🔴 **D-WS9-073** (the rule) · D-WS9-039 · BUG-249 (repeats WITHIN one generation — a different rule) · D-WS9-045 (`dishFamily`).
- **Hans, verbatim:** *"I have seen Grilled Peach and Arugula Flatbread with Prosciutto and Balsamic Glaze a handful of times recently when generating plans. We talked about the meal presentation fatigue rule, and I think it's based on plans created vs. a time filter."*
- ✅ **HIS READING IS RIGHT, AND IT IS IN THE SHIPPED DESIGN:** the rotation builder **walks the user's last 3 PLANS** and resolves each served meal's lineage. **A generation that is never saved creates no plan, so nothing it offered is ever "recently served."** A tester — or a user who regenerates until they like a plan — sees the same shelf meal offered indefinitely. **D-WS9-073 already flags a sibling gap (cooked-but-never-planned) and says its fix is likely a union, not a replacement; this is the same shape one step earlier.**
- 🔵 **THE RULING NEEDED (Hans's, not chat-Claude's): does being OFFERED count as "seen"?** Offering is not serving — a user who regenerates twice has not eaten anything — but it is exactly what produces the *"kiwi keeps suggesting that flatbread"* feeling. **Candidate shapes, none built:** (a) union the rotation with meals offered in the last N generations (archived wizard drafts carry them), weighted below actually-served; (b) leave the rule alone and treat it as the catalog being thin at the top (6 dinners fit a 30-minute cap, so the same few are all there is); (c) both. ⚠️ **(b) is not a dodge — with a six-meal shelf, a stronger nudge has nowhere to go, which is why the measurement below comes before the ruling.**
- ✅ **MEASURED September 12, AND IT SETTLES THE RULING AS (b) — THE SHELF IS TOO THIN FOR ANY NUDGE TO WORK.** Under the live shelf predicate at a 30-minute cap there are **six** eligible dinners — `BLT` · `peach flatbread` · `Reuben` · `shrimp scampi` · `Thai basil pork` · `shrimp fried rice` — and **every one of them appears in this user's surviving generations** (uncapped the shelf is 1,214; at 45 it is 116; at 60, 443). Repeats across surviving generations: peach flatbread 2, Baja tacos 2, BLT 2, carne asada 2. 🔴 **AND THE NUDGE DEMONSTRABLY FIRED AND LOST: the peach flatbread was offered at 21:47 while the rotation walk already held the plan saved at 21:42 with that flatbread in slot 0.** **So the unsaved-generation gap is real but secondary — with six candidates, prefer-unseen has nowhere to go.** ✅ **RULING: (b). No change to D-WS9-073's rule and no offered-counts-as-seen union now — the fix is the 30-minute catalog supplement** (`kiwi_go_live_todos.md`; its target list is written). **Revisit only if repeats persist once the shelf is deep.** ⚠️ **Side finding, unrelated and unowned: `excludeMealIds` is fed from `cook_meal` activity rows, and this user has zero — so the HARD exclude has never fired for him. Nothing in the rotation nudge depends on it; it matters when Cook Mode's completion writer lands (D-WS7-204).**

---

- 🔴 **HANS CHALLENGED THE RULING, September 12, AND THE CHALLENGE IS BETTER THAN THE RULING — it goes to the ECONOMICS, not the rule.** Verbatim: *"if it can't find a catalog option for a slot it should make up a recipe and meal or meals as needed. I caught this because you created a list of 70 meals in under 30 minutes no problem."*
- ✅ **THE MECHANISM HE IS ASKING FOR ALREADY EXISTS AND ALREADY RAN.** Per `kiwi_plan_generation_arc_scope.md` §3 and D-WS9-036/-037: **the store is a shelf the AI composes from, and live generation is the gap-fill** — it degrades on *actual coverage*, not on a flag, so a thin shelf simply leans harder on invention. In the September 11 round **two of four dinners were live-composed** and two were shelf. **Kiwi can invent 30-minute dinners; nothing blocks it.** ⚠️ **So "the catalog is thin" was never a capability limit — chat-Claude's (b) ruling stated it as though it were, and that framing is withdrawn.**
- 🔴 **WHAT ACTUALLY CONCENTRATES THE REPEAT IS THE SHELF GUARD, AND IT IS LOAD-BEARING FOR A DIFFERENT REASON.** Both generate bodies close the rotation section with *"prefer-unseen means reaching for a different shelf meal or a different dish family, **never abandoning the shelf**"* (D-WS9-073, set_preferences' wording; directed says *"not abandoning the shelf"*). That sentence exists to protect Block 4b-1's entire reach gain — without it prefer-unseen reads as licence to invent over catalog meals. ⚠️ **But it was written against a shelf of 1,214 dinners, not six.** At a 30-minute cap it tells the model to keep reaching into a six-meal pool that prefer-unseen has already exhausted. **The guard and the nudge are in direct conflict exactly when the cap is tight, and the guard wins.**
- 💰 **THE TRADE IS PRICED, AND IT IS THE REASON THIS IS A DECISION AND NOT A FIX.** Measured at Block 4b-3: a **store-backed** generation is **$0.0458** for three plans; a **live** one is **~$0.27** for one plan — **~18× per plan**. A 30-minute plan built entirely by invention therefore costs roughly what six store-backed plans cost, on **every** generation, **forever**, against a **one-time** authoring cost for a catalog supplement. ⚠️ **Neither number is a device measurement of a thin-shelf 30-minute generation specifically — they are the arc's general figures and should be re-measured before the money argument is leaned on.**
- 🔵 **MINTED FROM THIS: D-WS9-238** — does the shelf guard relax when the eligible shelf is below a threshold, and at what cost? **Not ruled. The BUG-255 (b) ruling stands only as "no change to the rotation rule"; whether the 30-minute supplement is the right fix is now D-WS9-238's question, not this entry's.**

### BUG-256 — The cooking scheduler starts long UNATTENDED steps too late, so a meal's honest time comes out ~40% high

- **Date:** September 12, 2026 · **Surface:** `cookingScheduler.ts` — the single greedy pass that turns a meal's dishes into a timeline (BUG-018's deterministic scheduler, the source of every derived `estimatedTimeMinutes`) · **Found by:** CC (server lane), measuring BUG-245's draft-vs-saved gap.
- **Status:** 🔵 **ASSIGNED — D-WS9-239** (measured September 12, twice; the ordering defect is real and ~1.7%; the lever it pointed at is the generator contract, ruled under D-WS9-239) · **Priority:** **P3 (was P2 — downgraded September 12 on measurement)** · **Target:** its own block, after the measurement · **Cross-ref:** 🔴 **BUG-245** (the deltas that surfaced it) · **D-WS9-235** (the ruling the scheduler implements) · **BUG-018** (the scheduler's origin) · D-WS9-128 · BUG-253 (one-dish imports, the same arithmetic from the other side).
- **The measured case.** A saved burger dinner derives to **80 minutes / 45 attended — 35 of them idle.** Its three dishes: smash burgers (serial 51, including a **28-minute unattended onion caramelisation**), comeback sauce (5), oven fries (serial 58, including a **15-minute attended potato cut** and a **28-minute unattended bake**). **The greedy pass takes the attended fries cut first (it is available at t=12) and only then starts the caramelisation — so the onions begin at 27 instead of 12, and the burger dish's serial chain drags the meal to 80.** Hand-scheduled under the SAME rules (attended work serialises; steps within a dish stay in order), it plates at **~56–58**. ⚠️ **That is a ~24-minute error from ordering alone, with no AI involved.**
- ⚠️ **AND THE MODEL IS ALREADY WRITING THE FIX IT CANNOT EXPRESS:** the step text reads *"While the onions caramelize, divide the beef into 4 balls"* — intra-dish overlap the scheduler has no way to represent, because `parallelGroup` is set on **zero** steps.
- **The shape of a fix, unbuilt and unruled:** prefer starting a long UNATTENDED step over an attended step whenever both are eligible (a critical-path / longest-unattended-first heuristic instead of first-available). 🔴 **DO NOT BUILD IT BEFORE THE MEASUREMENT:** every meal's stored time is derived by this function, so a change re-derives the whole catalog — **measure how many of the 1,124 catalog dinners move and by how much, in both directions, before touching it.** A change that shortens times is a change to the promise the product makes; Hans rules the size of it.
- ✅ **Why it matters commercially, stated plainly:** this pushes meals ABOVE the user's cap that do not belong there. If the measurement holds at catalog scale, it is worth more to the 30-minute promise than any prompt change made this week.
- 🔴 **MEASURED September 12, 2026 (CC, read-only, 2,344 meal-schedules; scheduler NOT edited) — THE HEADLINE WAS WRONG AND THE ERROR WAS CHAT-CLAUDE'S.** The *"~56–58 by hand"* schedule had DROPPED the finish-alignment constraint (BUG-018's invariant: every dish plates at the anchor). CC's ALT-C — the scheduler with alignment removed — reproduces that number exactly (58). **Under the rules as ruled, the ordering effect is small: mean 1.35 min over 1,124 catalog dinners (438 shorter, 37 LONGER, 649 unchanged; p90 8, max 17); the burger meal goes 80 → 65, not 56; the 30-minute shelf moves 6 → 7 (45: 116 → 128; 60: 443 → 480).** Derived vs stored: 0 of 2,344 mismatches — the stored numbers are honest about the schedule computed. **"~40% high" describes ALT-C, which destroys BUG-018's invariant (571 meals plate a dish ≥10 min early vs 39 today) and is NOT an ordering fix — it is a scheduler rewrite (a backward as-late-as-possible pass), and it is the only version that moves the shelf materially (60-cap 401 → 505 vs 401 → 435).**
- ✅ **THE CLAIM ITSELF REPRODUCES AND IS LITERALLY THE CODE:** the comparator sorts `(idealStart, dishIdx, stepIndex)` once and never consults the unattended flag; the idealStart-first walk is deliberate and documented (it is a critical-path order — most remaining work first). What is EMERGENT: no preference for a ready unattended step over a ready attended one, and a dish-blocked step holding the clock for steps listed after it (the sauce started at 70 that could have run 30–55). **Verified by chat-Claude against `cookingScheduler.ts` at `aa13fc6`, September 12.**
- ⚠️ **THE CHEAP FIX (ALT-B, ~30 lines: at each cook-free instant kick off every ready unattended step longest-first, then pick the attended step by the existing key) makes 37 meals LONGER (worst −11: a cheesesteak 93 → 104 — greedy without lookahead takes a 12-minute onion step at t=44 and a 33-minute roast then waits). A critical-path tie-break does not fix that (ALT-D ≡ ALT-B). Oven contention is unmodelled everywhere: CURRENT already double-books two unattended bake steps on 97 catalog meals; ALT-B adds 2.** Every stored `estimatedTimeMinutes` needs a re-derivation after any scheduler change.
- 🔵 **UNVERIFIED HYPOTHESIS (CC's, measured for nothing yet, and it is the one that would matter): the lever is INTRA-DISH serialisation, not ordering.** The burger dish's own text says *"While the onions caramelize, divide the beef…"*, and its griddle preheat could run inside that window too — but D-WS9-235's derivation keeps a dish's steps serial, `parallelGroup` is set on **0 of 25,564 steps**, and the burger dish's 51-minute serial chain sets the anchor. **If a dish's own prep may overlap its own unattended cook, CC expects the catalog effect to be several times ALT-B's.** ⚠️ **That touches a Hans-ruled derivation (D-WS9-235 — the scheduler's parallelism) — measure it read-only before anyone rules on it; do not build it on this entry.**
- **Status of the fix shape after the measurement: STILL UNRULED. Three options for Hans, none built:** (a) leave the scheduler — the number is honest and the ordering defect is ~1.7%; (b) ALT-B, small win, 37 regressions to accept; (c) measure the intra-dish lever first and rule on that. Priority stays P2.

- 🔴 **INTRA-DISH LEVER MEASURED September 12, 2026 (CC, read-only, same 2,344 schedules; scheduler untouched; finish-alignment and attended-serial kept in every variant, 0 invariant violations) — THE HYPOTHESIS IS VERIFIED FOR THE BOUND AND REFUTED FOR WHAT EXISTING TEXT CAN CAPTURE.** Structural bound (UPPER: every attended step waits only for the dish's previous ATTENDED step; UPPER-G adds rest- and preheat-gates): **mean 12.0–12.4 min shorter over 1,124 catalog dinners, 870–930 shorter, 26–31 longer; shelf eligible at 30 / 45 / 60: 6 → 17–20 · 116 → 240–250 · 443 → 650–680.** ⚠️ **Neither bound is a livable schedule** (810 attended-after-unattended-cook breaks — the ramen ladles noodles before the broth exists; UPPER sears on a griddle still heating). **What the recipes' own text signals ("While the potatoes bake…", 195 of 19,928 steps, 0.98%; 20/20 hand-check clean each way): mean 0.5–1.9 min; shelf 30-cap 6 → 6–7; 45-cap 116 → 124–128.** The text marks where an overlap STARTS and nothing marks where it ENDS — as spec'd it drains the meatloaf's potatoes 16 min before the boil finishes; closing the window at the next unsignalled step over-corrects the burger's griddle preheat (79 vs 68). **The realistic lever lies between those two, and the data cannot say where because no step records which later step consumes an unattended step's output.** Burger meal: CUR 80 · TEXT 68 · UPPER 58 (= ALT-C's number, reached WITH alignment kept). Active time: 0 of 2,344 changed under every variant (D-WS9-236's labels are safe). Oven proxy: CUR 97 double-books → TEXT 98 → UPPER 105. `parallelGroup`: 0 of 26,409 steps (the table grew from 25,564). **One-dish dinners are 235 of 1,124 — the population no ordering rule helps and the one the intra-dish rule helps MOST (UPPER-G mean 17 there vs 12 overall); BUG-253's imports sit in it.**
- 🔴 **VERDICT: the lever is real (~12 min, 3× the 30-minute shelf) and it is NOT a scheduler-ordering fix — it is a GENERATOR-CONTRACT fix: the step generator must record where an intra-dish overlap starts AND ends (`parallelGroup`, a field that exists and is set on zero rows), the scheduler honours it, and existing meals get a tagging backfill. Minted as D-WS9-239; the ruling lives there.** ALT-B (ordering) stays unbuilt — its 1.35 min is inside the noise of this, and the burger's cold-patty wait under UPPER-G shows the two interact, so ordering is revisited only alongside D-WS9-239's build, never alone. **BUG-256 itself is downgraded to P3 — the ordering defect it names is real, small and honest.** Probes: `scripts/ws9-sched-order/q5.ts`, `q5b.ts`, `q5-out.txt` (untracked, on Hans's disk).

### BUG-258 — A cold start renders a BLANK SCREEN FOREVER when the `/auth/me` bootstrap request HANGS; nothing times it out and nothing tells the user — ✅ FIXED `2c69e3d`, DEVICE-VERIFIED AND PUSHED September 14, 2026 (D-WS9-241 B)

- **Date:** September 12, 2026 · **Surface:** `artifacts/kiwi/app/index.tsx` (the sole auth gate) + `contexts/AuthContext.ts` (`isBootstrapping`) + `app/_layout.tsx` (the QueryClient defaults) · **Found by:** chat-Claude, diagnosing why Expo Go loaded blank on Hans's phone — **noticed in testing something else (§28.1), adjacent to the change, not part of it.**
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN — RULED D-WS9-241 (B), September 13, 2026: a 10 s deadline on the bootstrap `/auth/me`; timeout / network / 5xx → a failure screen with the token KEPT (*Can't reach Kiwi* · *Try again* · *Sign out*); 401 → the existing cascade; a throwing `readToken()` → treated as no token → Welcome. Builds in the auth-core lane after D-WS9-239 Phase 1a lands.** · **Priority:** **P2** — ⚠️ **it becomes launch-shaped the moment real users are on mobile networks, which the September 12 Cloud Run deploy makes true.** · **Cross-ref:** **D-WS9-241** · **BUG-239** (the session-expiry route reaction — the SAME gate, the other failure mode: that entry fixed a token that DIED, this one is a bootstrap that never ANSWERS) · D-WS9-231 · the `TRUST_PROXY_HOPS` / cold-start measurements still owed on the deploy.
- **What was measured, September 12:** the api-server was not running — `Get-NetTCPConnection -LocalPort 3000 -State Listen` returned **no rows**, `curl.exe http://localhost:3000/api/healthz` returned nothing, and the laptop's IP was unchanged at the `.50` the app's `EXPO_PUBLIC_API_BASE_URL` names. Metro was up and had served the bundle; the phone showed **a blank screen with no error, indefinitely.** Starting the api-server and reloading the app — **one change, nothing else touched** — fixed it. That is the whole cause of THAT incident and it is operator state, not a defect.
- 🔴 **THE DEFECT IS WHAT THE APP DID ABOUT IT.** Code-verified, not reasoned: `index.tsx` is `if (isLoading) return null;` · `AuthContext.ts:69` is `isBootstrapping = !storageRead || (!!token && meQuery.isLoading)` · the root QueryClient sets `retry: false`. **So a request that RESOLVES — including a fast connection-refused — flips `isLoading` false, `user` is null, and `index.tsx` redirects to `/(auth)/welcome`. That path is fine. The unguarded path is a request that NEVER SETTLES: `meQuery.isLoading` stays true, `index.tsx` returns `null` forever, and the user sees an empty screen with no message, no spinner, and no way to retry short of force-quitting.** There is no timeout on the bootstrap and no failure branch above the gate.
- ⚠️ **MEASURED vs INFERRED, stated plainly:** everything in the paragraph above is read off the files. **What is INFERRED and NOT measured is that Hans's phone hit the hang path rather than the refuse path** — the likely reason is that Windows Firewall DROPS blocked inbound packets rather than rejecting them, so the phone's SYN went unanswered instead of getting an RST. **That is a hypothesis with a cheap check: point the app at an unreachable address, cold-start it, and watch whether it lands on `welcome` (refused) or stays blank (hung).** Do that check before designing the fix — if both paths can blank, the fix is bigger than a timeout.
- **Why it matters more now than last week:** a dropped-packet hang is the NORMAL failure of a phone on a weak cellular connection, in a tunnel, or on captive-portal wifi — not an edge case. Until September 12 the API was a LAN address and every user was Hans. **It is now on Cloud Run, reachable from anywhere, which means "the request never settles" is a real user's Tuesday.** The visible symptom is an app that appears completely broken with nothing on screen to explain it.
- 🔴 **AMENDED September 13, 2026 — A THIRD PATH, MEASURED (the web-export probe, row 3b):** on web, `expo-secure-store` is `export default {}` — `readToken()` REJECTS with `getValueWithKeyAsync is not a function`; **`AuthContext.ts:77 await readToken()` has no catch, so `setStorageRead(true)` never runs, `isBootstrapping` stays true, and `index.tsx` returns `null` forever.** Screenshot: solid `#faf6f0`. **So the gate blanks on THREE inputs, not one: a request that HANGS (the hypothesis above), a token READ that THROWS (measured), and — by the same missing catch — any native SecureStore failure** (Android Keystore errors after an OS update are a known class). **The fix shape is therefore not just a timeout: the storage read needs a catch that still flips `storageRead`, and the gate needs a failure state.** The web SecureStore fallback (a `lib/auth.ts` web branch onto localStorage / AsyncStorage) is a separate two-hour item and rides with this if web is ruled IN.
- **Not scoped, not commissioned.** The obvious shape is a bootstrap timeout plus a failure state above the gate (message + Retry), but the retry/backoff layer is already flagged as a WS9+ concern in `AuthContext.ts:219` and this should be designed with it rather than bolted on. ⚠️ **Whoever takes it: `app/**` is outside the test glob and a hook added to a screen needs a device pass (standing hazard).**

### BUG-261 — Sign-up collects phone + email-consent + SMS-consent and SENDS NONE OF THEM: `signup()` is still the four-argument shape — ✅ FIXED `431f935`, DEVICE-VERIFIED AND PUSHED September 14, 2026 (D-WS9-241 A). **Hans signed up with a phone and both consent boxes and Profile shows all three.**

- **Date:** September 13, 2026 · **Surface:** `app/(auth)/sign-up.tsx` (collects `phone`, `emailConsent`, `smsConsent` — lines 27–48, `ConsentRow` ×2 at 187–199) → `contexts/AuthContext.ts:135` `signup(email, password, firstName, lastName)` → `signupRequest({…})` · **Found by:** chat-Claude, checking the file after Hans reported the dev-build sign-up form "doesn't have the double consent" — **it does (both rows sit below the phone field, the SMS row only once a phone is typed); what it lacks is the WIRE.** Noticed in testing something else (§28.1).
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN — RULED D-WS9-241 (A), September 13, 2026: phone + both consents go on the SIGNUP wire, server-side, one write (`POST /auth/signup` schema + `user.create`); SMS consent without a phone is refused; `PATCH /me/profile` stays the edit path. The auth Phase 0 answered the open question: the server schema is non-strict and silently STRIPS the fields today. Builds in the auth-core lane after D-WS9-239 Phase 1a lands.** · **Priority:** **P2 — launch-gating IF any marketing email or SMS goes out at launch; P3 otherwise.** ⚠️ **"Email me my list" (row 3a) is TRANSACTIONAL and does not need this. "Weekly meal plan tips and Kiwi updates" is MARKETING and does.** · **Target:** the auth/sign-up surface — one small lane, mobile + possibly server · **Cross-ref:** migration `20260509005040_ws6_6a_2_ai_infrastructure_and_marketing_consent` (the columns exist) · BUG-235 (same auth surface; consider one lane) · the ToS/Privacy row on `kiwi_go_live_todos.md` (consent language must match what is actually recorded).
- **Measured:** the file's own comment at `sign-up.tsx:80` — *"Phone + consent flags collected here; the signup payload extension (server-side `User.phone` + consent storage) lands in WS5-firstrun-2 or WS6 … For now, we keep the existing 4-arg `signup()` shape."* The WS6 migration landed the columns; **nothing ever extended the client call.** `AuthContext.ts:137–140` types `signup` as exactly `(email, password, firstName, lastName)`.
- **Why it matters:** (1) **the UI lies** — a user who ticks or un-ticks a consent row believes a choice was recorded; none was (G5: nothing ships styled-but-dead). (2) **SMS marketing without recorded prior express written consent is a TCPA exposure** the moment the first marketing text is sent; the screen ASKS, so the record must exist. (3) The phone number itself is collected and discarded — a field the user filled in for nothing.
- **First check for whoever picks it up (not established):** whether `POST /api/auth/signup`'s Zod schema already accepts `phone` / `emailConsent` / `smsConsent`, or whether the server half is also unbuilt. **Twice-search before claiming either.**
- ⚠️ **Not a fix candidate:** hiding the rows. Hans's product intent is to collect these; the fix is the wire.

### BUG-263 — A typo in a spend-guard env var silently DISABLES the cap, and nothing says so at boot

- **Date:** September 13, 2026 · **Surface:** `artifacts/api-server/src/lib/spendGuard.ts` — `parseThreshold` · **Found by:** chat-Claude, auditing `6ff7d8c` against `app.ts`'s own precedent.
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED `da8969b` (September 13, 2026)** — `validateSpendGuardEnv(env, log?)` runs once at module load in `app.ts` beside the `TRUST_PROXY_HOPS` warn: `error` per set-but-unparseable var, plus an `info` one-liner giving the guard's effective state on every revision. Env parsing was refactored into pure `parseThresholdRaw` / `parseDisabledRaw` shared by the boot validator and the per-call read, so the two can never disagree. ⚠️ **CC extended the scope correctly: `AI_DISABLED="ture"` is the same silent-off class and now gets the same `error`.** **A test asserts the summary has exactly five keys and that `DATABASE_URL` / `JWT_SECRET` values present in the env never reach either log line.** · **Priority:** **was P3** · **Cross-ref:** BUG-262 (same file, same lane) · `app.ts` `parseTrustProxyHops` (the pattern this should match).
- **The asymmetry:** `AI_DAILY_CEILING_USD="$10"` or `"10 USD"` parses to `NaN` → `parseThreshold` returns `null` → **the check is disabled** and a deduped `warn` is emitted on the first AI call that reaches it. **Hans believes the cap is on; it is off.**
- ⚠️ **The project already ruled this class the other way.** `app.ts`, verbatim: *"A typo in a deploy variable must not silently widen who we trust"* — and `parseTrustProxyHops` falls back to the SAFE value. Here the fallback is the UNSAFE direction.
- **Why "refuse everything" is NOT the fix:** a typo must not take the app down either. **The fix is visibility, not a different fallback:** validate all three vars ONCE AT BOOT (module load in `app.ts` or `index.ts`, beside the existing `TRUST_PROXY_HOPS` warn) and log at **error** when a var is SET but unparseable — so it is in the first page of Cloud Run logs on the revision that introduced it, not buried in a `warn` on some later request.
- **Second, smaller instance in the same file:** `logCannotEvaluate` passes the call through when the prisma stub lacks `count`/`aggregate`. Correct for tests, and production always supplies the real client — **but it is a second silent-pass path and belongs in the same boot-time story.**


### BUG-264 — The Welcome screen still renders the RETIRED app icon: the first screen a new user sees is off-brand — ✅ FIXED `2c983d8` (LOCAL, September 14, 2026), NOT YET DEVICE-VERIFIED

- **Date:** September 13, 2026 · **Surface:** `artifacts/kiwi/app/(auth)/welcome.tsx:59` · **Found by:** Hans on the dev build, reported three times as *"old logo"*; chat-Claude finally read the file after he sent a screenshot.
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN** · **Priority:** **P2 — cosmetic, but it is the FIRST screen a new user sees and it is brand-inconsistent with every other surface.** · **Target:** the next mobile lane that opens `(auth)/` · **Cross-ref:** the Deep Kiwi mark wiring `f0b392c` · D-WS9-241 (C) (the lane that just added two sibling screens and correctly did not touch this one).
- ✅ **THE MEASUREMENT, AND IT RESOLVES A THREE-ROUND MISUNDERSTANDING: `welcome.tsx:59` hard-codes `require("../../assets/images/icon.png")` — the ORIGINAL asset, 851,304 bytes, mtime April 2026.** The mark lane's assets sit beside it untouched (`icon-1024.png`, `kiwi-mark-256.png`, `header-mark-28*.png`, all mtime September 13).
- ⚠️ **WHY IT WAS MISSED, AND IT IS NOT CC's ERROR:** `f0b392c`'s file set was `app.json` (icon / splash / adaptive / favicon) + `HomeHeader.tsx`. **Welcome renders its own `<Image>` from a hard-coded path and was in nobody's file set.** A brand asset reached three surfaces and there was a fourth.
- 🔴 **AND IT EXPLAINS HANS'S "old logo" REPORTS, WHICH CHAT-CLAUDE MISREAD TWICE AS BEING ABOUT THE SPLASH.** The splash is CORRECT and new — he verified it himself on the BUG-260 pass (*"a sage screen with a small kiwi mark in the middle"*). **Two different screens, one phrase.** ⚠️ **The general lesson: when a user names a screen, confirm WHICH FILE renders it before answering — a screenshot settled in one look what three rounds of prose did not.**
- **Fix shape (not a ruling):** point the hero at `kiwi-mark-256.png` (the bare mark — the screen already wraps it in `styles.iconCircle`, so the full icon tile's green background would double up). The stale comment two lines below (*"the Deep Kiwi vector mark is a go-live item"*) predates the mark and goes with it.
- 🔵 **Untouched and deliberately so: the `"Instacart, Whole Foods, or your store of choice"` feature line.** Its own header comment records it as **a Hans-ruled placeholder held VERBATIM, "do not edit or redline it,"** until the retailer path settles. ⚠️ **That condition has now been met — D-WS9-240's September 13 amendment ruled Instacart a FAST-FOLLOW with a "coming soon" stub at launch — so the hold is ripe for revisiting, by Hans, before store submission. It is NOT this entry's scope and nobody edits that line without him.**
