### BUG-265 — The verify-email page's "Open in the Kiwi app" link is a dead end: the page already spent the token, and a logged-out user never sees why — ✅ FIXED `306326e` (LOCAL, September 14, 2026)

- **Date:** September 13, 2026 · **Surface:** `artifacts/api-server/src/pages/verifyEmail.ts` (the app-link affordance) + `artifacts/kiwi/app/verify-email.tsx` + `app/_layout.tsx`'s `SessionGate` · **Found by:** the BUG-235 lane, answering the one code-read question its prompt asked; the page half was chat-Claude's follow-on read of `verifyEmail.ts`.
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN** · **Priority:** **P3 — narrow and self-limiting (the browser page does the real work and succeeds), but it ends a successful flow on a screen that says it failed.** · **Target:** a small lane with the error-copy work (D-WS9-241 D) or whatever next opens `src/pages/` · **Cross-ref:** **D-WS9-231** (the page) · **BUG-239** (`SessionGate`) · **BUG-233** (single-use tokens) · BUG-235.
- 🔴 **TWO MECHANISMS, BOTH VERIFIED IN CODE, AND THEY STACK.** **(a) The page verifies ON LOAD** — its script calls `verify()` immediately and POSTs to `/api/me/email/verify-change`. **So by the time anyone taps "Open in the Kiwi app", the token is SPENT** (BUG-233's ledger), the app's own call takes the opaque `400`, and `app/verify-email.tsx` renders *"Couldn't verify email"* — **immediately after the browser said "Email changed."** **(b) If that user is LOGGED OUT, they do not even get the wrong answer:** `SessionGate` fires `router.replace("/(auth)/sign-in")` and the verify call completes on an unmounted screen where `setState` is a no-op.
- **The (b) lines that decide it, quoted because they are not obvious:** `AuthContext.ts:69` — `isBootstrapping = !storageRead || (!!token && meQuery.isLoading)`, so for a logged-out user it flips false as soon as SecureStore is read, no query involved · `_layout.tsx:71` `if (isLoading || user) return;` **passes** · `_layout.tsx:77` `if (group === undefined || group === "(auth)") return;` **does not** — `segments[0]` is `"verify-email"` · `_layout.tsx:90` replaces to sign-in. ⚠️ **`verify-email.tsx`'s header comment says a logged-out user is *"bounced to auth by index.tsx"*. That is STALE — it predates BUG-239, and `SessionGate` now bounces them BEFORE the result can render.**
- ✅ **CHAT-CLAUDE'S RULING, September 13: DROP THE "Open in the Kiwi app" LINK FROM THE VERIFY-EMAIL PAGE.** The page has already completed the action, so the link can only take a user from a success to a failure. **The RESET page KEEPS its app link** — there the app screen does real work (it collects the new password) and the page does NOT auto-submit, so handing off is genuine. **This is the cheap fix and it closes both mechanisms for the live path.**
- 🔵 **What the ruling does NOT close, left open deliberately:** `app/verify-email.tsx` still sits at the ROOT, outside `(auth)/`, so ANY future deep link to a non-`(auth)` route hits the same eviction for a logged-out user. **The durable fix is a `SessionGate` allow-list of token-bearing public routes, and it is not worth a lane today** — after this ruling nothing reaches that screen logged-out.

### BUG-266 — The catalog tagger is EQUIPMENT-BLIND: a preheat window carries a long unattended oven cook, so the oven appears to bake two things at once and the meal reads short

- **Date:** September 14, 2026 · **Surface:** the `parallelGroup` tags written onto catalog meals by D-WS9-239 Phase 3 (the Encoding-B tagger's `firstDependent` answer; no code defect — the derivation and the scheduler both behave exactly as ruled) · **Found by:** the D-WS9-239 Phase 2 lane, spot-checking the two LARGEST shortenings **outside** its own acceptance sample, after that sample came back 0/49
- **Status:** 🟡 **KNOWN AND BOUNDED — the 5 worst instances are EXCLUDED from the Phase 3 write (they stay untagged and keep today’s serial time); this entry is the honest record of the ones the detector misses.** · **Priority:** **P3** — the error runs in the SHORT direction, which is the direction that matters (*"it’s a problem when a recipe says 45 minutes and 1.25 hrs later you’re eating"*), but it is ~0.5% of tagged dishes and every instance is one shape · **Target:** not commissioned. The durable fix is an equipment signal the step data does not carry today; revisit if the shelf numbers are ever used in marketing copy. · **Cross-ref:** **D-WS9-239** (the arc; Phase 2 measured it, Phase 3 excludes the 5) · Phase 0b, which NAMED this limit in advance — *"the dependency answer cannot see equipment"* · **D-WS9-235** (derived times) · **D-WS9-240** item 6 (the 30-minute supplement, sized off these shelf numbers).
- **The mechanism, in one dish:** White BBQ Chicken Pizza. A 15-minute `preheat`, a 35-minute *roast the garlic*, and a final pizza bake. The tagger answered that the preheat’s first dependent is **the final bake** — true of the preheat in isolation, and it makes the 35-minute roast a RIDER on the preheat’s kickoff. **The schedule then runs the garlic roast inside a 15-minute preheat, and the meal reads 116 → 54. ~13 dishonest minutes.** The physical fact the tagger cannot see is that there is one oven and the roast occupies it.
- **The measurement (Phase 2, 830 tagged dishes):** a word-based heuristic — *a preheat window carrying an unattended, oven-worded cook of ≥ 10 min* — found **7 candidates**; by eye **4 MAJOR** (White BBQ pizza 13 min, Honey Sriracha broccoli 10, Baked Chicken Alfredo 10, Roasted Vegetable Ziti 15), **1 minor** (par-baked baguette, 4), **2 false positives** (stovetop pasta worded *"finish in the oven"*). ≈**0.5% of tagged dishes, ≈48 dishonest minutes across the catalog, all one shape.**
- ⚠️ **WHY THIS ENTRY EXISTS EVEN THOUGH THE 5 ARE EXCLUDED: the heuristic is WORD-BASED, so an oven step that is not oven-worded is missed.** Even at 50% recall the population MAJOR rate is ≈1%, inside D-WS9-239’s ≤ 5% gate (and the seeded random 20 returned 0 MAJOR on 49 dishes). **An unknown small number of preheat-carries-oven-cook dishes therefore ship tagged and read short. That is the residual, stated rather than averaged away.**
- 🔴 **THE LESSON THAT GENERALISES, AND IT IS THE REASON THIS WAS CAUGHT AT ALL: A CLEAN ACCEPTANCE SAMPLE IS NOT EVIDENCE OF A CLEAN POPULATION WHEN THE SAMPLE IS SMALL AND THE DEFECT IS RARE.** The 20-meal draw contained 14 tagged dishes and returned 0 MAJOR; the lane **refused to bank that number**, went looking at the largest shortenings instead, and found a 13-minute MAJOR on the second dish it opened. **Sample verdicts bound the COMMON case. Rare, high-magnitude defects are found by sorting on the magnitude, not by drawing again.** Every future acceptance sample on shared data gets a tail check beside it.
- 🔵 **If it is ever fixed:** the honest fix is a step-level equipment token (`oven` / `stovetop` / `counter`) declared by the generator, which would also let the scheduler refuse to run two oven steps concurrently — a strictly larger piece of work than D-WS9-239 and NOT commissioned. **A word-list patch on the derivation is NOT the fix and should not be proposed as one: it is the same fitted-heuristic shape as refused variant A** (D-WS9-239).

---

### BUG-267 — Signup refuses SMS consent without a phone; `PATCH /me/profile` does not — so the invariant can be set, or broken, in one call from the edit path — ✅ FIXED `dca04a9` (LOCAL, September 14, 2026)

- **Date:** September 14, 2026 · **Surface:** `artifacts/api-server/src/routes/me.ts` (`PATCH /me/profile`) · contrast with `routes/auth.ts` `POST /auth/signup` as landed in `431f935` · **Found by:** the auth-core lane (D-WS9-241 A+B+E), which was told the signup phone rule *"mirrors the rule `PATCH /me/profile` already enforces"* and **reported that this is only half true rather than papering over it**
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN** · **Priority:** **P3 today, P2 the moment SMS sending exists.** No SMS is sent by anything (PRD §3.3 defers SMS; there is no Twilio account), so today the defect produces a data row, not a message. · **Target:** **fix-on-next-touch of `routes/me.ts`** — not its own lane. · **Cross-ref:** **D-WS9-241 (A)** (the ruling this completes) · **BUG-261** (closed by `431f935`) · **D-WS7-025** (the consent columns) · the pre-launch privacy-policy row (a consent claim in that document rests on this invariant).
- **What the lane measured, and both halves matter:** `PATCH /me/profile` enforces a phone **FORMAT** rule and nothing else. So **(a)** `PATCH /me/profile {marketingConsentSms: true}` on a row with a **null phone** is accepted today, and **(b)** `PATCH` can **null the phone while `marketingConsentSms` stays true.** Signup (as of `431f935`) refuses the pairing; the edit path does not. **Per the ruling, the lane left `PATCH` unchanged and reported the divergence — correct: a lane does not widen its own scope on a finding.**
- 🔴 **WHY (b) IS THE ONE THAT ACTUALLY MATTERS, AND IT IS NOT THE OBVIOUS ONE:** consent=true with no phone is inert — nothing can be sent. **The real failure is consent SURVIVING a phone change: the user consents for number A, nulls it, adds number B later, and number B silently inherits a consent that was never given for it.** That is a consent-integrity defect, not a validation gap, and it is exactly the class a privacy policy makes a claim about.
- ✅ **RULED (chat-Claude, September 14), so the next lane to open that file does not have to re-reason it — TWO rules, and the second is the one worth having:** **(1)** `PATCH /me/profile` refuses `marketingConsentSms: true` when the resulting row would have no phone — 400, the same shape signup gives. **(2)** 🔴 **CHANGING OR CLEARING THE PHONE CLEARS `marketingConsentSms` in the same write.** Consent is consent for a NUMBER, not for an account. **Both rules are evaluated against the ROW AFTER the patch, never against the request body alone** — a patch that supplies only one of the two fields is exactly how this was missed the first time.
- ✅ **ACCEPTED, not a defect — the lane’s client-side judgment call:** on sign-up, SMS consent without a phone is **dropped before the request** rather than sent and 400’d, because the form already clears that pairing when the phone empties. **The server 400 still guards every other caller, which is the part that has to be true.**
- ⚠️ **SECOND HALF OF THE SAME HOLE, found at the September 14 device pass and recorded here rather than as its own entry because it is one concern: THE PHONE RULE ITSELF ACCEPTS NUMBERS THAT CANNOT RECEIVE A TEXT.** `src/lib/phoneValidation.ts` is *"at least 7 digits anywhere in the string, at most 40 characters"* — deliberately permissive, born in `PATCH /me/profile` at WS7-2 — and `artifacts/kiwi/lib/phone.ts` mirrors it byte-for-byte (regex and cap diffed September 14: identical). **So `5551233` saves.** ✅ **That is the rule behaving exactly as D-WS9-241 A specified — the ruling said to reuse PATCH’s rule, "the SAME rule, not a second one," and the lane did.** 🔴 **But a consent recorded against an undialable number is as empty as a consent with no number at all, which is this entry’s whole subject.** 🔵 **RULED: do NOT tighten it now.** Nothing sends SMS, the permissive rule is shared with an edit path that has years of rows behind it, and a stricter rule is a product decision (which countries? formatting? verification?) rather than a validation tweak. **Settle it WITH the SMS build, alongside rules (1) and (2) above — and when it is settled, the two mirrored files change together.**

### BUG-269 — HANDS-ON steps are tagged as unattended `cook`, so `isUnattended` is true for work a human is standing over — and the scheduler overlaps it

- **Date:** September 14, 2026 · **Surface:** step data across the catalog (`phaseType` + `isTimingSensitive` on `RecipeInstructionStep`) → `isUnattended()` → `cookingScheduler.ts` · **Found by:** the rest/hold refinement lane, **while explaining why `preheat` scored 0 fixes / 3 losses in the adjacency window set** — i.e. found sideways, by something that was measuring a different thing
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN, UNMEASURED at population scale — a FOURTH named instance September 15: Beef Pho’s *"strain the broth + season"* (4 min, hands-on) typed `cook` / unattended, which is why BUG-270 Phase E REFUSED to re-tag it** · **Priority:** 🟡 **P2 — but it points the WRONG WAY, which is what makes it worth an entry:** the error makes a meal read **SHORT**, and *"an honest 38 beats a lying 45"* is a locked ruling of this project · **Target:** not commissioned; a read-only measurement first · **Cross-ref:** **D-WS9-239** (`isUnattended` is its whole foundation) · **D-WS9-235** (derived meal time) · **BUG-245** (the attendance hypothesis, refuted — this is a different mechanism) · **D-WS9-236** (hands-on labels).
- **The mechanism:** `isUnattended = phaseType ∈ {preheat, rest, hold} OR (phaseType === "cook" AND !isTimingSensitive)`. **A step like *"dredge the cutlet in flour"* or *"dip in egg wash"* is tagged `cook` with `isTimingSensitive: false` — so it reads as UNATTENDED, and the scheduler is free to run other work during it.** It is not unattended. A human is standing there with floury hands.
- **The three found (all in the 30 adjacency fires, so this is a floor and not a count): Onion Straws · Chicken Francese · Caesar** — each a *dredge / dip* step immediately after a `preheat`.
- 🔴 **WHY IT IS BIGGER THAN THE RULE THAT SURFACED IT: `isUnattended` is not just the adjacency rule’s input — it is the predicate the WHOLE SCHEDULER classifies on (D-WS9-239 Phase 1a).** Every mis-tagged step is a step the scheduler may overlap with another dish. **The adjacency rule only made three of them visible; the scheduler has been reading all of them since `f543183`.**
- ✅ **WHAT WAS DELIBERATELY NOT DONE, and the reasoning is the valuable part:** the lane proposed nothing and chat-Claude **refused to drop `preheat` from the adjacency window set to make the three symptoms disappear.** `preheat → fry` is a genuine continuation (heat the oil, then fry in it); the window side is physically right. **The defect is in the DATA, on the RIDER side. Removing `preheat` would have fitted the rule to a sample — exactly the move that got variant A refused — and would have hidden this entry instead of creating it.**
- 🔵 **THE MEASUREMENT OWED BEFORE ANY FIX, and it is cheap and read-only:** over the catalog, count steps with `phaseType === "cook" AND isTimingSensitive === false` whose TEXT reads hands-on (dredge, dip, coat, bread, toss, assemble, plate, stir constantly…), and report **how many minutes the scheduler currently overlaps on the strength of them.** That number decides whether this is a prompt fix (teach the generator that hands-on work is timing-sensitive), a derivation fix, or nothing. ⚠️ **A word-list is the DETECTOR for the measurement, never the fix** — BUG-266 is the standing example of a word-based heuristic being useful for finding and wrong for deciding.

### BUG-270 — The scheduler is PATH-BLIND: a dual-path dish’s from-scratch steps AND its store-bought alternates are walked serially, so 1,655 dishes store a time nobody cooks

- **Date:** September 14, 2026 · **Surface:** `scheduleCookingSequence` (`cookingScheduler.ts`) and every consumer that walks a dish’s step list for a time — `mealTiming.dishTotals` was flagged Σ-serial at D-WS9-239 Phase 0 and never chased · **Found by:** the 30-minute supplement lane, **sideways, while explaining why 86 of its 95 new meals read long** — the third defect this week found by a lane measuring something else · **Evidence:** `scripts/ws9-30min/paths_measure.ts`
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED — `f654432` (the scheduler) · `45db72f` (Phase D, the public re-stamp) · `72e1991` (Phase E, the 15-dish re-tag); all LOCAL September 15 ~00:45Z, push owed from Hans; every write VERIFIED FROM THE DB.** Hans ruled option B (~00:15Z): public population only — 1,117 meals / 1,379 dish rows written (1,031 catalog + 86 supplement); **148 private meals / 196 dish rows EXCLUDED under D-WS9-230, so the d235 harness now reads a KNOWN private residue (136 total-time / 148 any-field) — that is expected, not drift.** · **Priority:** 🔴 **P1.** The direction is conservative — meals read LONG — so it is not D-WS9-235’s *"lying 45"*, but it is the mirror of it: **a 39-minute dinner that reads 59 falls off the 45-shelf, makes Kiwi look slow, and is still a false number on more than half the catalog.** · **Target:** its own SERVER lane (instructed), then a d235 re-stamp over the catalog that is **Hans’s carve-out call** · **Cross-ref:** **D-WS9-235** (the derived pair this inflates) · **D-WS9-239** (whose shelf numbers this makes PROVISIONAL) · **D-WS9-240** item 6 (the 95 supplement meals, judged on inflated numbers) · **D-WS9-148** / WS9B (the store-bought toggle, which will need the OTHER path’s time) · **BUG-121** (dual-path interaction, flagged at Phase 0).
- **The mechanism, in one dish (the lane’s example, to be re-derived from live rows by the fix lane):** Buffalo Chicken Quesadillas — the DISH stores **53 = base 20 + scratch 25 + bought 8** (base + scratch alone is 45); the MEAL stores 59 because a 6-minute all-attended celery side is serialised behind it. ⚠️ **Chat-Claude’s first write of this line said "59 = 25 + 8 + 20", which does not sum — dish and meal conflated; the lane re-derived it from live rows.** The dish has a from-scratch path and a store-bought alternate; a cook does ONE. The scheduler did both.
- **Reach, MEASURED by the fix lane (corrects chat-Claude’s "1,349", which was the public count only): 1,655 dual-path dishes — 1,351 public catalog · 196 private `batch_generated` · 108 from the 95 supplement meals** — every one of their stored `estimatedTimeMinutes` included both paths; none was all-bought, zero half-tagged rows. ⚠️ **`d235 --dry` reading `moved 0` proves stored == derived and says NOTHING about this** — the derivation is self-consistent and wrong.
- 🔴 **CONSEQUENCE FOR NUMBERS ALREADY IN CANON: the shelf counts D-WS9-239 recorded as canonical (≤30 12 · ≤45 181 · ≤60 571, now 20 / 218 / 651 after the supplement) are UNDERSTATED. They are the honest derivation of an inflated step list. They are provisional until this lands and the catalog is re-stamped.** ✅ **And the 12.9-minute mean lever D-WS9-239 delivered is not diminished by this — it was measured on the same inflated base before and after, so it is real; this is a SECOND lever stacked on top, of unmeasured size.**

That is WS9B’s decision.** The filter lives in the scheduler’s input, once, so every consumer inherits it — not re-implemented per consumer.

Inherit the fix: `deriveMealTiming` / `stampMealTiming` incl. `dishTotals` (all five callers), the d235 harness, Cook Mode. Unaffected in fact: `deriveTimingFromOutlines` (no `pathKey`); not changed: `parallelGroupDerive` (tags, never sums), `prepWeekAssembly`, the mobile’s `remainingMinutes` (mobile never sees `pathKey`).

Both shapes are pinned in tests. ⚠️ **Reconcile at Phase D: the ignored-tag table reads `attended_window` 28 · `anomaly_guard` 34 · `lone_token` 1, which does not obviously equal the 12 + 3 + 0 above — ask what each counts (per meal vs per dish; pre-existing vs new).**

ACCEPTED.** **2 by more — Patty Melt 92 → 119, Tortellini 47 → 49 — and they expose a defect that is D-WS9-239’s, not this entry’s: the 1c CATALOG TAGGER tagged parallel windows over the FULL step list, bought steps included.** 15 groups catalog-wide (14 public, 1 supplement) have their window on a bought step (*"warm pre-sliced onions"*, 4 min) with base riders, while the scratch equivalent (*caramelize*, 38 min) sits untagged; drop the bought window and the riders go serial. ### BUG-292 — `home.test.ts`'s BUG-114 assertion fails between ~20:00 local and midnight in US timezones: the TEST computes the weekday with local `getDay()` while the route has used `getUTCDay()` since BUG-282's server fix

- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED — `a603656` (`lane-pfc` Part B.1), September 17 ~03:00Z, AUDITED PASS.** The test computes with `getUTCDay()`, matching the route. ✅ **Verified the honest way — INSIDE the failing window (02:48Z = 22:48 EDT), not asserted from outside it: the pre-fix shape reproduced 14/1 red, and the fix reads 15/0 across local, Los Angeles, Kiritimati, Pago Pago and Tokyo. No `TZ` pinned anywhere**, and the full server suite now passes in local EDT — which is the point, and it is why the 2,409 figure is finally TZ-independent. · **Priority:** **P3 (test-only; no user-facing effect)** · **Raised by:** the Block 3 lane, September 17, 2026, as a REFUTED line against the prompt's stated suite baseline.
- **What happens:** the server suite reports **2,407 pass + 2 skip under `TZ=UTC`** but **one failure in local time after ~20:00 EDT**, because the assertion derives its expected weekday locally while `routes/home.ts` switched to `getUTCDay()` in `1336fa6` (BUG-282's server half). **The code is right and the test is wrong** — which is the dangerous shape: a suite that goes red by wall-clock trains people to ignore it.
- ⚠️ **Own it honestly: this was introduced BY THIS ARC** (the post-pass server lane), not inherited. It went unnoticed because the lanes that touched it ran before 20:00.
- **Fix:** the test computes its expectation with `getUTCDay()`, matching the route. One line. ⚠️ **Do not "fix" it by pinning `TZ=UTC` in the test runner** — that hides the next instance of the same class.
- **Cross-ref:** BUG-282 · `1336fa6` · D-WS7-103 (`todayFor`).

### BUG-289 — “Get another plan option” FREEZES on the SECOND press: both dedupe guards key on `candidate.id`, the AI-minted string canon already condemned as useless for identity, so the second single-plan call collides with the first and the busy flag is never released

- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED — `068ef5c` (plan-flow Block 3 Part A), September 17 ~01:10Z, AUDITED PASS with chat-Claude re-reading the landed hook on disk.** The lane confirmed the diagnosis AND found a THIRD blocked release path I had missed: **the “settled with no card” effect was itself gated on `candidates.length > 0`, so a colliding id had NO path to clear the button** — the deadlock was total, not partial. Fix as ruled: `candidateIdentity()` (NFKC/trim/lowercase/collapse, title + sorted meal titles) now keys `hasCandidate` and the card key; `countedRef` is a fresh `Set` per run; **and the run, the dedupe and the flag moved into `hooks/useAnotherPlanOption.ts` where ONE settle effect (`isComplete || isError`) clears `busy` and reports `{delivered, error, reason}` once per run — the screen only places the card.** ✅ **Verified by chat-Claude: the settle effect is unconditional, and there is no reset race** — `reset()` calls `setState(IDLE)` while `isComplete` derives from `status === "success"`, so a fresh press cannot settle on the previous run's success. Break/restore: re-gating the settle on a delivered card turned 2 tests red (*“busy must be released by the settle, not the insert”* + the per-run dedupe), restored 7/7. **Device-unproven — four presses in a row is item 1 of the re-test.** · **Priority:** 🔴 **P1 — it breaks the flagship interaction of the screen this whole arc exists to build, and it is 100% reproducible on the second press** · **Raised by:** Hans's device pass, September 16, 2026 (item 7), twice, deterministically.
- **Symptom, Hans verbatim:** *“the screen froze with the skeleton of the new card at the bottom and 'get another plan' greyed out… I used Get Another Plan Option again, it worked. then i did not for me and get another plan, and it did the same thing again as it did the first time. froze with a skeleton.”* No error, no toast, no stall-watchdog fire — **the request SUCCEEDS; the client throws the result away.**
- 🔴 **ROOT CAUSE (chat-Claude, verified by reading the landed code, not inferred from the symptom):** `lib/wizard/planOptions.ts`'s `hasCandidate` compares `c.candidate.id === candidate.id`, and `app/plan-options.tsx`'s `countedRef` stores `fresh.id`. **`lib/wizardContentHash.ts`'s own header states the disqualifying fact: `candidate.id` is *“an AI-minted free string — not durable, can collide across generate calls, and regenerates on every call. It is therefore useless as an idempotency key.”* That finding is WHY BUG-030 built the content hash in the first place.** Separate single-plan calls re-mint the same id; the three-card first batch is distinct only *within one response*, which is exactly why press 1 works and press 2 dies.
- 🔴 **THE FREEZE ITSELF IS A SECOND, DEEPER DEFECT, and the more important lesson:** `countedRef.current.has(fresh.id)` early-returns **before** `setAnotherBusy(false)`, and that insert effect is the ONLY path clearing the flag on a successful run. **So any dedupe or early return in that effect deadlocks the UI permanently.** The fix is not only content-derived identity — it is that **the busy flag must be released by the code that knows the run SETTLED, never by the code that decides whether to render the result.**
- **Why a remount “fixes” it:** backing out and re-entering through “See previous options” mounts a fresh screen with an empty `countedRef`, so the next press succeeds and the one after collides again — which is precisely the cycle Hans observed and reported.
- ⚠️ **Chat-Claude's share of this:** the Block 2 prompt said *“appendCandidates (stream re-delivery deduped)”* without naming the key, and never carried BUG-030's lesson into a prompt that was building a brand-new identity path. **A prompt that introduces a dedupe must name the key it dedupes on.**
- **Fix:** Block 3 Part A — content-derived `candidateIdentity()` mirroring `computeWizardContentHash`'s normalisation; `countedRef` cleared per run (its real job is the stream's within-run catch-up); the busy flag owned by a settle handler; a regression test asserting **a run that yields a duplicate or no card still clears busy**.
- **Cross-ref:** **BUG-030** (why the content hash exists) · D-WS9-191 · BUG-284 (the last identity-keyed defect in this flow).

- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN — NOT COMMISSIONED** · **Priority:** **P2 — a silent cost and a silent non-determinism: a green run could be green because a live model answered** · **Target:** the test harness — either the runner does not load `.env` (a `.env.test` with no key, or an explicit `KIWI_TEST_NO_AI` guard in `getClient()` that throws under `NODE_ENV=test`), so an unstubbed AI call FAILS LOUDLY in the suite instead of succeeding live; plus the deps-seam pattern (`routes/plans.ts` `deps.computePlanMacros`) for any route that reaches an AI call · **Cross-ref:** BUG-274 (the instance) · §27.4 / §27.5 (a test that passes for the wrong reason).

The harness-level fix (a `.env.test` / a throwing `getClient()` under test) stays OPEN.**

- **Evidence:** 27 `eventType` literals are emitted across non-test `src`; `cook_meal` appears only in two READS; the probe counted 0 rows. `Meal.timesCooked` / `lastUsedAt` are 3 / 1,759 non-zero (canon's "0 / 337" is stale on both numbers; the only writer bumps `MealPlanTemplate.lastUsedAt`, not `Meal`); `MealPlanItem.timesCooked` / `lastCooked` and `MealPlanInstance.lastCooked` are all 0. **"Never served to this user" is therefore a JOIN, not a column:** `NOT EXISTS (MealPlanItem ⋈ Meal WHERE Meal.userId = :u AND Meal.sourceStoreMealId = catalog.id)`, with a title-match fallback for pre-lineage meals — the shape `buildRecentRotation` already uses over three plans.

### BUG-274 — EVERY user-created meal saves with NO macros: `POST /me/meals` sums dish macros the payload never carries and never calls the grounded estimator — against PRD §10.3.2 / §11.10 (macros computed at write time)

- **Date:** September 16, 2026 · **Surface:** Recipes → Meals → Add Meal → *Ask Kiwi for a meal* → Meal Builder → save → Meal Detail · **Found by:** Hans, device, two imports (*"Grilled chicken breast with rice pilaf and steamed green beans"*).
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED — `4ece0cf` + `8f19cdb` (LOCAL, September 16; lane-import + the Block 1 follow-up F2); DEVICE-VERIFIED by Hans the same day (macros present on a fresh Ask-Kiwi import; the save's latency read as acceptable — no figure taken)** · **Priority:** **P2 — Hans: *"these should get macros after save — one marketing angle and the tight timeline is trying to catch healthy new year people."*** · **Cross-ref:** BUG-273 (same import, the time) · BUG-253 (same import, the dish structure) · PRD §10.3.2 (*"macros computed from ingredients × USDA-equivalent nutrition data"*) · §11.10 (*"dish-level and meal-level macros computed at write time"*) · the macro arc (D-WS9-050…056, the USDA grounding the catalog path already uses).
- **The defect:** the imported meal shows no macros at all. **The PRD locks macros as a write-time computation for every path, and the catalog generators already run one — so this is a path that skips a computation that exists, not a missing feature.** ⚠️ **UNVERIFIED which of the three it is:** the import save never calls the macro computation, calls it and discards the result, or calls it with ingredients the USDA lookup cannot resolve and stores nothing rather than an estimate (§11.9 says estimate-and-flag, never blank). One read of the Ask-Kiwi save path settles it — Phase 0 of the redesign arc.
- ✅ **ROOT CAUSE SETTLED (redesign-arc Phase 0, September 16, read-only): NEVER CALLED — and the surface is every import and manual path, not only Ask Kiwi.** The save path is `POST /me/meals` → `materializeMeal` → `recomputeAndPersistMealMacros`, which SUMS the dishes' macros; the Ask-Kiwi parse produces none and the client adapter (`parsedMealToDraft.ts`) hard-zeroes them. The grounded estimator `estimateDishMacros` (`dishMacros.ts`, the `nutrition.ingredient_estimate` prompt, D-WS9-050) has three production callers — plan recalc (`planMacros.ts`, lazily when a dish is at zero AND in a plan), wizard expansion, and scripts — **not the meal save.** So a user meal stays 0/0/0/0 until it lands in a plan and `POST /plans/:id/recalc-macros` runs. **Fix shape (arc import block): call the estimator at save for any dish whose macros are zero — one AI call per such dish; the cost per call is the estimator's, not yet quoted here.** Same block as BUG-273.
- ✅ **FIXED — `4ece0cf` (lane-import) + `8f19cdb` (the Block 1 follow-up), September 16, AUDITED PASS; device-unproven.** The estimator is a standalone pre-pass (`estimateZeroMacroDishes`) that `POST /me/meals` runs BETWEEN ingredient resolution and the `$transaction` — every new dish with all-zero macros → `estimateDishMacros` in parallel, grounded from the resolved Ingredient rows, `skipConversionWriteback`, stamping macros + `macroGroundedPct` and emitting `dish_macros_estimated` exactly as plan recalc does; fail-soft on failure / throw / lookup miss / an 8 s deadline (dish stays at zero, `warn`, save proceeds); `materializeMeal` only consumes the pre-computed map and ignores it for store-fill (`target` set). 🔴 **Two placements that are rulings, not accidents:** (1) the estimator runs OUTSIDE the 15 s Neon transaction — an AI call inside it would hold the connection through model latency and roll back `LLMCallLog` rows on a failed save; (2) it reaches the route through a `MeRouterDeps.estimateDishMacros` seam (the `routes/plans.ts` `deps.computePlanMacros` pattern) — production defaults to the real implementation, the router suites inject a stub — because the hermetic suite loads `.env` and a default-on call would fire LIVE from `pnpm test` (BUG-279). Guards: estimator called for the zero-macro dish only; a throwing dep still returns 201; `["estimate", "tx-open"]` order asserted; break red / restore hash-matched. The spend guard is per-call inside `runAICall`, so every estimator call is covered. ⚠️ **Cost: one AI call per zero-macro dish at save time, and the save now waits on them (≤ 8 s, parallel) — the device pass measures it.**

### BUG-273 — The Meal Builder's import adapter collapses EVERY step onto dish[0] and drops `phaseType`, so every imported multi-dish meal (Ask Kiwi · URL · text · photo) derives the serial sum and mis-sequences in Cook Mode — BUG-253's real mechanism

- **Date:** September 16, 2026 · **Surface:** Recipes → Meals → Add Meal → *Ask Kiwi for a meal* → the saved meal's cook time · **Found by:** Hans, device, twice in a row.
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED — `388e465` (LOCAL, September 16, lane-import), DEVICE-VERIFIED by Hans the same day (his own three-dish import came back as three dishes with their own steps and an overlapped time)** · **Priority:** **P2 — the time is the number this arc exists to make honest, and the Playlist intake (D-WS9-244) will save meals through this path in bulk** · **Cross-ref:** 🔴 **BUG-253** (the one-dish flattening — NOT this; see below) · **BUG-245 / D-WS9-235** (derive-at-save with the scheduler's parallelism) · D-WS9-239 · BUG-274 (same import, no macros).
- **Hans, verbatim:** *"it gave me three dishes, not a great rice pilaf recipe (but I'll let it slide), and steamed green beans. it worked great, but the timing on the cook time is wrong. we just fixed that and this one came in with incorrect times — it looks like it went sequential on everything, so it didn't go through a process before it got mathed."*
- 🔴 **WHY THIS IS NOT BUG-253.** BUG-253's mechanism is *one dish → every step serial by construction → the sum*. **This import produced THREE dishes and the time still came out as the sum.** Two consequences: (1) the flattening BUG-253 recorded is not deterministic — the same prompt gave one dish on September 11 and three on September 16, so it is model variance, and BUG-253 is neither fixed nor reliably reproducible; (2) with three dishes the scheduler HAD something to overlap and did not, so **a second mechanism exists.** ⚠️ **UNVERIFIED HYPOTHESES, two, for Phase 0 to settle with one read each:** (a) the Ask-Kiwi save path does not run D-WS9-235's derivation at all and stores an authored or summed number; (b) it runs it, but the imported steps carry no `isTimingSensitive` / phase tags the scheduler's unattended predicate can act on, so nothing overlaps and the derivation legitimately returns the sum. **(b) would mean the fix is in the import prompt's step contract, not the save path.** Hans's *"didn't go through a process before it got mathed"* is hypothesis (a) in his words; it is recorded as a hypothesis, not a finding (§27).
- ✅ **ROOT CAUSE SETTLED (redesign-arc Phase 0, September 16, read-only) — hypothesis (a) REFUTED, (b) CONFIRMED with a sharper mechanism, and it is CLIENT-SIDE:** the save (`POST /me/meals` → `materializeMeal`) DOES run D-WS9-235's `stampMealTiming`. But before the save, `parsedMealToDraft.ts` flattens every sub-dish's steps into ONE meal-level `steps[]` (keeping `estimatedMinutes` + `isTimingSensitive`, DROPPING `phaseType` — the review type has no such field), the Meal Builder hydrates *"all steps onto dish[0]"* for multi-dish drafts, and `serializeNewDishesForSave` sends text / minutes / timing-sensitive only. The server accepts `phaseType` / `parallelGroup` (it defaults `phaseType` to `cook`); the scheduler only overlaps ACROSS dishes; with every step on one dish the derived total is the serial sum, legitimately. **The loss is entirely in the client adapter, and the same flatten pass serves URL / text / image imports — every user-imported multi-dish meal is affected, not only Ask Kiwi.** 🔴 **BUG-253 IS THIS DEFECT SEEN FROM THE OTHER SIDE:** the Ask-Kiwi prompt (`meal_builder.mode_a_parse`) asks for 1–5 sub-dishes with per-dish steps, `phaseType` and `isTimingSensitive`, and its rule 1 (*"and / with / alongside / served over → separate sub-dish"*) makes three dishes the INSTRUCTED behaviour; the DB shows every `directed` meal is multi-dish (0 / 2 / 3 by dish count = 1 / 2 / 3+). Hans's *"steps built like it's one dish"* on September 11 was the steps-on-dish[0] collapse, not a one-dish import. **One fix closes both: keep steps per dish with `phaseType` (and `parallelGroup` where present) through the adapter and the save serializer.** Same block as BUG-274.
- ✅ **BUILT — `388e465` (LOCAL, September 16, lane-import), AUDITED PASS; device-verified September 16 (item 8 of the migration-day pass).** Steps now nest under dishes in the draft/review types (`ReviewMealDish.steps`, `phaseType` + `parallelGroup` on the step — the builder's own `BuilderDish.steps` idiom, not a third one); BOTH adapters fixed (`parsedMealToDraft.ts` for Ask Kiwi and `canonicalToDraftMeal` in `recipeImport.ts`, which URL / image / text share); hydration is per dish (dish[0] only as a legacy fallback when no dish carries steps); the save serializer emits `phaseType` per step per dish; loaded-meal and picked-dish hydration carry `phaseType` too (an adjacent fix, disclosed: the edit PATCH otherwise relied on by-index preservation, which mis-tags on reorder). UI: the builder already rendered one step list per dish; a small per-step "move to next dish" control was added, no redesign. Tests with a three-sub-dish `mode_a_parse` fixture; break red / restore hash-matched. ⚠️ **`parallelGroup` rides the draft but NOT the wire — the client `SaveMealStep` type lacks the field (BUG-278); moot for Ask Kiwi, whose text-mode parse never emits it.** Client suite 1480/1480. **Device test owed after the sequence: a fresh three-dish Ask-Kiwi import → overlapped time.**

### BUG-272 — Nine catalog components carry a bought path that replaces nothing: bought-path steps with no scratch-path steps behind them

- **Date:** September 15, 2026 · **Surface:** the shared catalog — `Dish.componentRegistry` entries and their `RecipeInstructionStep` rows · **Found by:** the substitution lane's effort-class derivation (D-WS9-242), which could not classify them because there is no scratch path to read.
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN — NOT COMMISSIONED** · **Priority:** **P3 — 9 of 1,852 components (0.49%); nothing renders a path today (BUG-121 unbuilt)** · **Target:** the next catalog-quality pass, NOT a lane of its own · **Cross-ref:** **D-WS9-242** (the effort-class stamp; these are its `unresolvable` 9) · D-WS9-066 (a component is a scratch path the user may swap for a bought one) · BUG-121.
- **The defect:** a registry component whose steps are ALL tagged `pathKey: bought`. **A bought path is defined as the thing a cook buys INSTEAD of making that part of the dish — with no scratch steps there is no "instead", so the component offers a choice between a product and nothing.** The user-visible consequence once BUG-121 ships is a component that vanishes from the recipe when the from-scratch path is selected.
- **The nine, by dish and component:** Classic Chicken Noodle Soup (broth) · Broccoli Cheddar (×2) · Sopes (beans, ×3) · Panang (paste) · Meatballs (seasoning) · Pesto Panini (pesto). Full ids and step texts in `scripts/output/ws9-242/dry_out.txt` (gitignored, on Hans's disk).
- 🔵 **Two candidate fixes, neither ruled:** generate the missing scratch steps for the nine (a small AI run, catalog write, inside D-WS9-230's shared-catalog carve-out), or drop the registry entry and fold the product into the dish's base ingredients (a $0 data edit — and the same shape D-WS9-243 recommends for the supplement meals). ✅ **SETTLED IN PRINCIPLE September 15, 2026 — D-WS9-243 RULED the shortcut-as-plain-ingredient shape, so the SECOND option is the answer: drop the registry entry and fold the product into the dish's base ingredients. $0, no generation run.** Hans, on the same principle applied to the 95: *"for the obvious ones we can delete the manual cook option."* **The nine here are the mirror image of that case — a product with no from-scratch alternative behind it — and under the ruling a product with no alternative is an INGREDIENT, not a component.** ⚠️ **STILL NOT COMMISSIONED, and deliberately not bolted onto the km30 rerun lane (§3 one-workstream-at-a-time): these nine sit OUTSIDE the 95, so they are a different population with a different failure mode, and a $0 data edit on nine shared rows does not belong inside a lane carrying a generation gate and a spend breaker.** **Next catalog-quality pass. The write is inside D-WS9-230's shared-catalog carve-out and Hans's ruling above is the authorization it needs when someone picks it up.**
- 🔴 **THE COUNT IS NOW TEN, AND THE SHAPE IS STILL BEING GENERATED — September 15, 2026.** The km30 rerun under D-WS9-243 produced a fresh instance: **"Breakfast Burritos" salsa, a bought step with no scratch step behind it, 1 of 27 new components (3.7%).** ⚠️ **This matters more than the extra row: the `# Shortcut mode` prefix section does NOT prevent the defect, so fixing the ten existing components is a cleanup that will silently refill.** ✅ **The durable half is a PROMPT rule — a component must have a scratch path behind it or it is an ingredient, not a component — and it belongs in the generate prefix beside the quality-bar clause D-WS9-242 owes (the D-WS9-064 sentence that also never reached the prompt). Fix the rule and the data in one pass, not the data alone.**

### BUG-271 — The password-reset web page is unstyled — and D-WS9-231 made it the PRIMARY reset surface

- **Date:** September 14, 2026 · **Surface:** the api-server-hosted `/reset-password` page (D-WS9-231’s https fallback page) · **Found by:** Hans, completing the first real reset end to end (BUG-224’s closure).
- **Status:** 🔴 **OPEN — NOT COMMISSIONED** · **Priority:** **P3 — functional; polish before friends-and-family** · **Target:** the polish pass nearest launch, NOT the correctness block · **Cross-ref:** D-WS9-231 (why the page is primary) · BUG-224 · BUG-257 (the `/verify-email` sibling on the same page).
- **Hans, verbatim:** *"a page that had some green on it and was a mostly unstyled place to put a new password (2 fields for new password)."* It worked — the new password signed in on the phone.
- ⚠️ **Why it is not nothing: D-WS9-231 ruled the web page the PRIMARY reset surface, and reset is the flow most likely to be opened on a laptop by someone already locked out.** For that person it is the first Kiwi-branded thing they see during the incident, and an unstyled password form is exactly what a cautious user has been taught to distrust.
- 🔵 **Scope when commissioned:** colours and type matched to the values in `artifacts/kiwi/constants/tokens.ts` (the only truth for tokens), the Kiwi mark, a one-line heading, the two existing fields and their validation, a success state that sends the user back to the app, and the same treatment on the `/verify-email` hand-off. **No new routes, no new behaviour.** ⚠️ **Its own small lane — it lives in the api-server’s static page, not `app/**`, and must not ride into the BUG-270 or WS9B lanes.**
